### [1] [Monopole crystal and effective theory for a monopole-enhanced 3D U(1) gauge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15922)
*Alessandro Mariani*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a modified U(1) lattice gauge theory with monopole enhancement, showing that strong enhancement leads to a dense, antiferromagnetically ordered monopole ground state; despite density, an effective sine-Gordon description persists, and analytical predictions—first-order transitions driven by monopole charge dominance and an Ising-class transition with gauge coupling—are verified numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To deepen understanding of U(1) lattice gauge theory with monopole enhancement, particularly how monopole density and ordering affect confinement mechanisms and phase structure beyond the dilute-monopole regime.

Method: Combined analytical techniques—including adaptation of Polyakov’s argument to dense monopole backgrounds, effective field theory mapping to sine-Gordon, and Landau theory for transition classification—with numerical lattice simulations to map the phase diagram and test predictions.

Result: Discovery of a Néel-ordered (antiferromagnetic) monopole ground state at large monopole enhancement; derivation of an effective sine-Gordon model valid even in the dense regime; analytical prediction and numerical confirmation of a first-order transition governed by monopole charge selection and an Ising-universality transition driven by gauge coupling variation.

Conclusion: Monopole condensation in this enhanced U(1) theory exhibits rich non-perturbative structure: dense ordering does not invalidate effective low-energy descriptions, and the phase diagram features both first-order and continuous (Ising) transitions, reconciling topological excitations with conventional critical phenomena.

Abstract: Recently, a version of U(1) lattice gauge theory on the cubic lattice with a monopole enhancement term was considered in the literature. In this work, we further our understanding of this theory with a mixture of analytical and numerical methods. We show that for sufficiently large monopole enhancement, the monopoles are no longer dilute and they arrange themselves into an antiferromagnetic, Néel-ordered ground state. Nonetheless, a variant of Polyakov's argument for the dilute excitations over the dense ground state leads to an effective sine-Gordon model which is qualitatively similar to that of the standard theory. We also predict analytically the presence of first-order transitions as the monopole chemical potential is varied at fixed gauge coupling, corresponding to a change in the dominant monopole charge. Similarly, we predict a transition in the Ising universality class as the gauge coupling is varied. We then use numerical methods to study the phase diagram of the theory and check some of our predictions.

</details>


### [2] [Critical behavior of the bosonized Schwinger model with uniform matrix product states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16092)
*Takuya Okuda*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the critical mass of the continuum Schwinger model at θ=π using a bosonized Hamiltonian lattice formulation and VUMPS-based infinite MPS simulations, achieving high-precision agreement with staggered-fermion results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the critical mass (m/g)_c of the Schwinger model at θ=π — a nontrivial benchmark for quantum simulation and lattice field theory — via a dual bosonic formulation that avoids fermionic sign problems and enables exact charge-conjugation symmetry preservation.

Method: Hamiltonian lattice discretization of the bosonized (non-compact scalar) Schwinger model; variational uniform matrix product state (VUMPS) optimization on an infinite chain; oscillator basis truncation aligned with the free-limit reduced density matrix; Symanzik effective theory–constrained continuum extrapolation.

Result: (m/g)_c = 0.333 555(5), obtained from controlled infinite-volume, continuum-limit extrapolation with quantified statistical and systematic uncertainties.

Conclusion: The bosonized Hamiltonian MPS approach yields a high-precision, symmetry-exact determination of the θ=π critical point, validating its efficacy for strongly coupled 2D gauge theories and confirming consistency with fermionic lattice methods.

Abstract: Quantum electrodynamics in two spacetime dimensions, also known as the Schwinger model, admits a dual formulation in terms of a non-compact boson field. Using a Hamiltonian lattice discretization of the bosonized model, we compute the critical mass of the continuum Schwinger model at $\theta=\pi$. We numerically obtain the ground state on an infinite chain by the variational uniform matrix product state (VUMPS) algorithm, preserving charge conjugation exactly. As a truncated basis for the local Hilbert space, we employ the oscillator basis that diagonalizes the one-site reduced density matrix of the free limit. We constrain the fitting functions for the continuum extrapolation using the Symanzik effective theory for lattice corrections. Our result for the critical mass, $(m/g)_c = 0.333\,555(5)$, agrees with recent staggered-fermion determinations at comparable precision.

</details>


### [3] [Automatic O(a) Improvement for Wilson Twisted Mass Fermions in the Hamiltonian Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16658)
*Carsten Urbach*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper applies the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian framework to Wilson twisted mass fermions to establish automatic O(a) improvement at maximal twist and via mass/Wilson averaging, and derives the complete structure of O(a) counterterms needed for clover on-shell improvement—including a splitting of the clover term into two independent contributions in the Hamiltonian formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically analyze and prove O(a) improvement properties of Wilson twisted mass fermions using a Hamiltonian-based Symanzik framework—bypassing Euclidean effective action methods—and to clarify the structure of O(a) counterterms required for on-shell improvement, especially for clover-improved Wilson fermions.

Method: Constructs the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian directly from lattice Hamiltonian symmetries (not from Euclidean effective action); employs canonical transformations to eliminate on-shell redundant operators; performs operator classification and matching to identify independent O(a) counterterms.

Result: Rigorous proof of automatic O(a) improvement at maximal twist and via mass/Wilson average; derivation of full set of O(a) counterterms for clover on-shell improvement; demonstration that the isotropic Euclidean clover term splits into two distinct terms with independent coefficients in the Hamiltonian framework.

Conclusion: The Hamiltonian Symanzik approach provides a symmetry-driven, on-shell–oriented route to O(a) improvement, revealing finer operator structure than Euclidean formulations—particularly for clover terms—and confirms and extends known improvement mechanisms for twisted mass fermions.

Abstract: We apply the recently developed Symanzik effective Hamiltonian framework to Wilson twisted mass fermions. The effective Hamiltonian is constructed solely based on the symmetries of the lattice Hamiltonian without recourse to the Symanzik effective action in Euclidean space-time. We use it to prove automatic $O(a)$ improvement at maximal twist, as well as $O(a)$ improvement via so-called mass and Wilson average. Moreover, we derive the structure of $O(a)$ counterterms pre-requisite for clover on-shell improvement of Wilson fermions. We show how to remove on-shell redundant contributions using canonical transformations, and find that the well known clover term in isotropic Euclidean space time splits into two terms with independent coefficients.

</details>


### [4] [Numerical tests of chiral fermion weights with $η$-invariant phases in the 3450 model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16876)
*Tatsuhiro Misumi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper numerically tests a chiral fermion lattice prescription combining PV-regularized magnitude and APS η-invariant phase; it confirms high accuracy (<0.04% error) for torus determinants and induced mass (2.82089 vs. 2.82095), and validates APS phase cancellation in anomaly-free correlators on a cylinder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To validate a theoretically proposed prescription for defining chiral fermions on the lattice—specifically, coupling continuum chiral fermions to interpolated lattice gauge fields using a Pauli-Villars regularized magnitude and an Atiyah-Patodi-Singer η-invariant phase—that preserves anomalies and gauge invariance nonperturbatively.

Method: Numerical lattice simulation on a 16×16 torus and cylinder geometries using an anomaly-free 2D U(1) chiral gauge theory with charges (3,4)_L and (5,0)_R; exact analytic determinant used as benchmark; interpolation of gauge fields from lattice links; evaluation of Polyakov loops, vacuum polarization (induced mass), and a twelve-point correlator under generalized APS boundary conditions; comparison with and without the APS η-phase.

Result: At zero flux: Polyakov loops match exact values within 0.04%; omitting APS phase reduces them to ~3% of exact. Induced mass m/e = 2.82089 vs. exact 5/√π = 2.82095. At unit flux: twelve-point correlator magnitude agrees within 0.8%; APS phase errors reduced from 1.8–3.0 mrad to 0.2–0.5 mrad; gauge-extension-dependent phase shifts cancel to <0.2 mrad in the anomaly-free combination.

Conclusion: The APS η-invariant phase is essential for correctly reproducing chiral determinants, anomalies, and physical observables in lattice regularization; numerical results strongly support the consistency and viability of this prescription for nonperturbative chiral gauge theory simulations.

Abstract: We test numerically a recently proposed prescription for coupling continuum chiral fermions to interpolated lattice gauge fields, in which the fermion weight has a Pauli-Villars (PV) regularized magnitude and a phase given by an Atiyah-Patodi-Singer (APS) $\eta$-invariant in one higher dimension. We use the anomaly-free two-dimensional $U(1)$ theory with left-handed charges $3,4$ and right-handed charges $5,0$, whose torus determinant is known exactly. At zero magnetic flux, with gauge fields interpolated from lattice links, the Polyakov loops evaluated on a $16\times16$ grid of flat connections agree with the exact values within $0.04\%$, whereas omitting the APS phase substantially reduces them to about $3\%$ of the exact result. The vacuum-polarization response yields an induced mass $m/e=2.82089$, close to $5/\sqrt{\pi}=2.82095$. At unit flux, where zero modes make the determinant vanish, we calculate a twelve-point correlator on a cylinder with the generalized APS boundary condition. For three smooth backgrounds its magnitude agrees with the exact result within $0.8\%$. Without the $\eta$-invariant its phase deviates by $1.8$-$3.0$ mrad, and with it by $0.2$-$0.5$ mrad. The phase of each charged fermion changes by $15$-$46$ mrad when the gauge field is extended differently into the third dimension, but the change cancels to below $0.2$ mrad in the anomaly-free combination.

</details>


### [5] [$NΩ$ Dibaryon near Unitarity from Physical-Point Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16989)
*Liang Zhang,Yan Lyu,Sinya Aoki,Takumi Doi,Tetsuo Hatsuda,Song Zhang,Yu-Gang Ma*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the $N\Omega$ dibaryon interaction using lattice QCD at physical quark masses and finds an attractive interaction supporting a shallow bound state; binding energies are reported as $B_{n\Omega} \approx 0.61$ MeV and $B_{p\Omega} \approx 1.25$ MeV (including Coulomb), providing first-principles predictions for experimental searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The $N\Omega$ system is a promising dibaryon candidate due to absence of shared valence quarks, eliminating Pauli repulsion; thus, it offers a clean probe of strong-interaction dynamics beyond conventional baryons.

Method: The $^{5}S_{2}$ $N\Omega$ interaction is determined in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD at physical pion mass ($m_\pi \simeq 137~\text{MeV}$) using the HAL QCD method; binding energies are extracted via $T$-matrix pole analysis, with finite-charge-size Coulomb correction applied for the proton-$\Omega$ channel.

Result: The $N\Omega$ interaction is attractive over the resolved range and supports a shallow near-unitary bound state; binding energies are $B_{n\Omega} = 0.61\,(^{+0.41}_{-0.30})\,(^{+0.60}_{-0.18})~\text{MeV}$ and $B_{p\Omega} = 1.25\,(^{+0.46}_{-0.39})\,(^{+0.69}_{-0.25})~\text{MeV}$.

Conclusion: This work provides the first ab initio, physical-point lattice QCD prediction for the $N\Omega$ dibaryon binding, establishing a quantitative benchmark for femtoscopy and experimental dibaryon searches.

Abstract: The $N\Omega$ system is a promising dibaryon candidate because its constituents share no valence quarks and therefore avoid valence-quark Pauli repulsion. We determine the $^{5}S_{2}$ $N\Omega$ interaction in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD at physical quark masses ($m_\pi\simeq137~\text{MeV}$) using the HAL QCD method. The interaction is attractive over the resolved range and supports a shallow state near unitarity. A direct $T$-matrix pole analysis gives $B_{n\Omega} = 0.61\,(^{+0.41}_{-0.30})\,(^{+0.60}_{-0.18})~\text{MeV}$; including finite-charge-size Coulomb attraction yields $B_{p\Omega} = 1.25\,(^{+0.46}_{-0.39})\,(^{+0.69}_{-0.25})~\text{MeV}$. These results provide a first-principles benchmark for future $N\Omega$ femtoscopy and dibaryon searches.

</details>

### [6] [Weak null singularities for the Einstein--Vlasov system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15318)
*Dawei Shen,Yuefeng Song,Jingbo Wan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence and stability of weak null singularities for Einstein–Vlasov systems (massive and massless), showing continuous metric extendibility, bounded curvature and energy-momentum tensor at the singularity, non-square-integrable Christoffel symbols for singular data, and Lipschitz inextendibility under extra curvature assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Luk's vacuum weak null singularity results to the physically richer Einstein–Vlasov system without symmetry, addressing how matter fields affect singularity formation and regularity.

Method: Constructs spacetimes from characteristic initial data; introduces novel commutator vector fields to control derivative loss and avoid the most singular curvature component; uses double-null gauge and energy estimates adapted to Vlasov matter.

Result: Existence of spacetimes with weak null singular boundaries that admit continuous metric extensions; bounded, continuously extendible Ricci curvature and energy-momentum tensor; failure of local L² integrability of Christoffel symbols for genuinely singular data; Lipschitz inextendibility under additional curvature decay conditions.

Conclusion: Weak null singularities persist robustly in the Einstein–Vlasov system, with matter (both massive and massless) preserving key features of vacuum weak singularities—continuous metric extension and bounded curvature—but introducing new analytic challenges resolved via tailored vector field commutators.

Abstract: We study the formation and stability of weak null singularities for solutions to the massive and massless Einstein--Vlasov systems without symmetry assumptions. Extending previous results of Luk in the vacuum setting, we establish the existence of spacetimes arising from characteristic initial data that develop a singular null boundary while remaining extendible with a continuous metric. For both massive and massless particles, the energy-momentum tensor and Ricci curvature remain bounded and extend continuously to the singular boundary. For genuinely singular initial data, the Christoffel symbols in the original double-null coordinates fail to be locally square integrable near the boundary. We introduce new commutator vector fields that avoid both derivative loss and the most singular curvature component. Under additional curvature assumptions, we also prove Lipschitz inextendibility across the singular null boundary.

</details>


### [7] [Exact model of a gravitational wave and electromagnetic radiation in DHOST gravity theory: wave solutions and testable effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15414)
*Konstantin E. Osetrin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically investigates the coupled dynamics of gravitational waves and high-frequency electromagnetic fields in both General Relativity and DHOST gravity, confirming gravitationally induced waveguiding and deriving a scalar frequency drift effect that observationally distinguishes GR from DHOST Ia theories—even when metrics are identical.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable, non-perturbative signatures distinguishing General Relativity from degenerate higher-order scalar-tensor (DHOST) theories—particularly when their metric solutions coincide—by studying exact electromagnetic responses to gravitational waves.

Method: Uses an exact gravitational-wave spacetime in isotropic (null) wave coordinates to analytically integrate Maxwell's equations in full GR; then applies the resulting exact electromagnetic solution to the nonlinear field equations of DHOST class Ia gravity, analyzing how DHOST constraint algebra modifies electromagnetic wave parameters.

Result: Exact non-perturbative confirmation that gravitational waves act as waveguides for co-directional EM radiation and generate an effective pure-radiation source; DHOST Ia constraints induce measurable modifications to EM wave parameters, leading to a scalar-induced frequency drift.

Conclusion: The derived scalar frequency drift constitutes an observationally testable, theory-discriminating effect that remains viable even in the 'degenerate' regime where GR and DHOST Ia yield identical metric tensors—thus breaking the strong equivalence between them at the level of electromagnetic propagation.

Abstract: Within an analytical non-perturbative framework based on a single exact model, the coupled dynamics of gravitational waves and high-frequency electromagnetic fields is investigated in General Relativity and DHOST gravity. Using an exact gravitational-wave spacetime in an isotropic (null) wave coordinate system, Maxwell's equations are integrated analytically. This analysis provides an exact non-perturbative confirmation from the full field equations that a gravitational wave acts as a waveguide, forming a propagation channel for electromagnetic radiation co-directional with the gravitational wave and generating an effective pure-radiation source. Applying this exact electrodynamic solution to the nonlinear field equations of DHOST class~Ia gravity reveals that the algebraic structure of the DHOST constraint equations enforces a modification of the electromagnetic-wave parameters. Based on this mechanism, an observationally testable effect of scalar frequency drift is derived, providing a direct way to discriminate between General Relativity and modified gravity theories even when their metric tensors share identical functional forms.

</details>


### [8] [Machine learning and high accuracy: Neural preconditioners for computational gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16106)
*Thomas Osburn,Nils Vu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a hybrid machine learning method using a graph neural network (GNN) as a fast, generalizable preconditioner for solving Helmholtz-type sparse linear systems arising in gravitational and elastic wave modeling—achieving 2–15× fewer iterations than traditional preconditioners while maintaining high accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational wave modeling demands high numerical accuracy, making pure ML approaches unsuitable due to inherent noise floors; however, hybrid methods that embed ML into traditional numerical schemes can accelerate convergence without sacrificing precision.

Method: The authors train a graph neural network (GNN) to act as a preconditioner for iterative solvers of Helmholtz-type sparse linear systems. The GNN is trained directly on the sparse matrix structure using a novel loss function based on the true residual of the underlying iterative algorithm—not proxy losses like reconstruction or label prediction.

Result: For challenging parameter regimes in asymmetric-mass black hole binary and elastic wave simulations, the GNN preconditioner reduces iteration counts by 2–15× versus state-of-the-art traditional preconditioners (e.g., multigrid or ILU). It generalizes across problem instances with minimal retraining due to its graph-based, physics-agnostic architecture operating natively on sparse matrices.

Conclusion: Hybrid ML—specifically GNN-based preconditioning—is a viable, accurate, and scalable strategy for high-fidelity PDE solving in gravitational wave astrophysics and other wave-dominated physical domains, bridging the gap between speed and precision.

Abstract: Machine learning (ML) developments have empowered valuable calculations in many areas of theoretical physics. Certain topics involving moderate-to-low accuracy requirements have been particularly fortunate because many ML techniques, such as neural networks, introduce a noise floor that would be difficult to penetrate with stricter tolerances. For this reason, topics requiring high accuracy can be hesitant to pursue ML. One prominent example is gravitational wave modeling, which relies on traditional methods that accommodate the high sensitivities of detectors such as LISA. However, recent progress in applied mathematics has identified hybrid methods where fast-but-noisy ML mechanisms are inserted into otherwise traditional numerical schemes to accelerate convergence in a way that ultimately meets or exceeds the accuracy of standard methods. We develop an optimized hybrid ML method to tackle a class of challenging PDEs; specifically, we demonstrate ML implementations for gravitational waves from asymmetric-mass black hole binaries and elastic waves. These require solving sparse linear algebra systems with Helmholtz features that are especially difficult for traditional iterative methods. Our new approach is to train a graph neural network (GNN) as a significantly faster preconditioner using a novel loss function based on the true residual of the iterative algorithm. For the most difficult regions of parameter space, our neural preconditioner decreases iteration counts by 2-15x compared to traditional preconditioners. Because the GNN operates directly on the sparse matrix and needs only modest physical context, it generalizes beyond its training domain and can be adapted to new problems across physics by retraining rather than redesign. Hybrid ML methods such as this offer a new and compelling avenue to handle high accuracy and speed requirements in gravitational wave modeling and beyond.

</details>


### [9] [The uniqueness of smooth stationary black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16853)
*Yishen Huang,Jiongyue Li,Junbin Li,Xi-Ping Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that a smooth, asymptotically flat, stationary vacuum black hole spacetime with a single non-degenerate rotating horizon must be isometric to a subextremal Kerr solution—without requiring analyticity or smallness assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole uniqueness (no-hair) problem for stationary vacuum black holes under minimal geometric and physical assumptions, removing reliance on analyticity or perturbative regimes.

Method: Applies the Harrison transform to generate a family of Einstein–Maxwell solutions; uses null area comparison and the area-charge inequality to constrain geometric quantities on the limiting bifurcate sphere; normalizes ADM mass to M=1 and derives constant electromagnetic flux densities and unit area radius, enabling application of Ionescu–Klainerman’s rigidity theorem.

Result: The domain of outer communications of the given spacetime is isometric to that of a subextremal Kerr black hole.

Conclusion: Subextremal Kerr geometry is uniquely determined by smoothness, asymptotic flatness, stationarity, vacuum condition, and non-degenerate rotation—establishing unconditional uniqueness for single rotating black holes in 4D general relativity.

Abstract: We show that the domain of outer communications of a smooth, asymptotically flat, stationary vacuum spacetime describing a single rotating non-degenerate black hole is isometric to the domain of outer communications of a subextremal Kerr solution under reasonable geometric and physical assumptions, without analyticity or any smallness assumptions. The method is to apply Harrison transform to construct a family of solutions of Einstein--Maxwell equations. Combining null area comparison with the area-charge inequality, we show that, in the limiting transformed geometry, the bifurcate sphere has area radius one and constant electric and magnetic flux densities, with the original ADM mass normalized to $M=1$. These identities imply the technical assumptions of Ionescu--Klainerman, yielding the identification with Kerr.

</details>


### [10] [Gray-body factors: Method matters II, WKB vs GrayHawk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15889)
*Marco Calzà,Andrea Spina*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares two methods—numerical integration (GrayHawk) and WKB approximation—for computing gray-body factors across four black hole geometries and three field types; it finds excellent agreement at high frequencies/large angular momentum, but notable discrepancies at low frequencies and for nearly-extremal or multi-peak-potential black holes, with substantial differences in computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically assess the reliability, domain of validity, and computational trade-offs between fully numerical and WKB-based approaches for computing gray-body factors in black hole perturbation theory.

Method: Direct numerical integration of the radial scattering equation (via GrayHawk) versus the semi-analytic WKB approximation; applied to scalar, fermionic, and vector perturbations across four black hole spacetimes (including near-extremal cases); comparison includes gray-body factors, Hawking emission spectra, and Page evaporation factors.

Result: Excellent agreement between methods at high frequency and large l; significant and systematic discrepancies at low frequencies, especially for nearly-extremal black holes and effective potentials with multiple peaks; GrayHawk is more accurate but computationally expensive, while WKB is efficient but breaks down in non-asymptotic regimes.

Conclusion: The two methods are complementary: WKB suffices for asymptotic regimes, but numerical integration is essential for quantitative accuracy in low-frequency, near-extremal, or multi-peak scenarios—critical for precision Hawking radiation modeling and evaporation studies.

Abstract: We present a systematic comparison between two independent approaches for the computation of gray-body factors. A fully numerical integration of the radial scattering equation, as implemented in GrayHawk, and one based on the WKB approximation. We consider four black hole geometries and scalar, fermionic, and vector perturbations, comparing the corresponding gray-body factors, primary Hawking emission spectra, and Page evaporation factors. As expected for high-frequency and large $l$ regimes, the two methods provide excellent agreement. At low frequencies, however, systematic discrepancies emerge and become particularly pronounced for nearly-extremal black holes. Systematic discrepancies are also met when considering effective geometric potentials characterized by the presence of more than one peak. A significant difference between the two approaches is also found in their computational cost, highlighting their complementary nature.

</details>


### [11] [Joint Bayesian Inference of Dense Matter and f(Q) Gravity with Nuclear and Neutron Star Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14553)
*N. K. Patra,Sneha Pradhan,P.K. Sahoo,Kai Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper performs a joint Bayesian inference of nuclear matter and symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity parameters using a Taylor-expanded equation of state (EOS), incorporating nuclear physics constraints, GW170817 tidal deformability, and NICER mass–radius data; it finds $R_{1.4} = 11.28^{+0.35}_{-0.31}$ km, $\Lambda_{1.4} = 151.4^{+44.9}_{-31.2}$, and $M_{\rm max} = 2.22^{+0.29}_{-0.16}\,M_\odot$, highlighting weak direct correlations between gravity parameters and observables but strong internal degeneracies in the gravity sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To jointly constrain dense matter physics (via EOS) and modified gravity (symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$) in neutron stars, while rigorously propagating EOS uncertainties — essential for disentangling matter effects from gravitational modifications in multimessenger NS observations.

Method: Joint Bayesian inference using a linear $f(Q)$ model and a Taylor-expanded EOS around nuclear saturation density; incorporates constraints from nuclear saturation properties, chiral EFT neutron matter, heavy-ion collisions, empirical nuclear data, GW170817 tidal deformability posterior, and NICER mass–radius measurements.

Result: For a $1.4\\,M_\\odot$ NS: radius $R_{1.4}=11.28^{+0.35}_{-0.31}\\,\\mathrm{km}$, tidal deformability $\\Lambda_{1.4}=151.4^{+44.9}_{-31.2}$, and maximum mass $M_{\\rm max}=2.22^{+0.29}_{-0.16}\\,M_\\odot$; gravity parameters show weak direct correlations with stellar observables but strong internal degeneracy.

Conclusion: Accounting for EOS uncertainties is crucial for robustly probing symmetric teleparallel gravity with neutron star data; the analysis provides a unified framework to simultaneously constrain matter and gravity sectors and quantify their correlations using multimessenger and nuclear physics inputs.

Abstract: We investigate neutron star (NS) properties in symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity while consistently accounting for uncertainties in the dense matter equation of state (EOS). We focus on the linear $f(Q)$ model and perform a joint Bayesian inference of the nuclear matter and modified gravity parameters using a Taylor expansion EOS around nuclear saturation density. The analysis incorporates complementary constraints from nuclear saturation properties, chiral effective field theory calculations of pure neutron matter, heavy-ion collision, and empirical nuclear data, the GW170817 tidal deformability posterior, and NICER mass--radius measurements. The resulting posterior distributions simultaneously constrain the dense matter and gravity sectors and quantify their correlations within a common framework. For a canonical $1.4\,M_\odot$ NS, we obtain $R_{1.4}=11.28^{+0.35}_{-0.31}\,\mathrm{km}$ and $\Lambda_{1.4}=151.4^{+44.9}_{-31.2}$, while the maximum mass is constrained to $M_{\rm max}=2.22^{+0.29}_{-0.16}\,M_\odot$. The modified gravity parameters exhibit only weak direct correlations with most stellar observables, while a pronounced degeneracy remains within the gravity sector. Our results show that accounting for EOS uncertainties is essential for quantifying the interplay between dense matter physics and modified gravity and provide a systematic framework for confronting symmetric teleparallel gravity with nuclear and multimessenger NS observations.

</details>


### [12] [Optical and ADM Tomography from Directed Causal Arrival Times](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14554)
*Maxim Churilov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a local Lorentzian geometry reconstruction method from directed null arrival times and spacetime volume, under a given clock foliation and spatial gauge; it establishes exact algebraic inversion, minimal sampling requirements, robustness bounds, and a consistency test for noisy measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an operational, gauge-conditional tomography framework for reconstructing Lorentzian spacetime geometry from physically measurable quantities—namely, null arrival times and volume—without assuming a background spacetime or quantum-gravitational origin of clocks or measurement oracles.

Method: Uses null geodesic arrival data (one-way directed null times) and spacetime volume density within a fixed ADM foliation; derives the exact future null indicatrix and its algebraic inverse; employs semialgebraic geometry to formulate a consistency test; analyzes stability via short-time freezing and finite-resolution inverse; determines minimal sampling via dimension-counting of scalar measurements in d spatial dimensions.

Result: Shows that d(d+3)/2 one-way arrival measurements suffice and are necessary for stable local metric recovery; provides certified robustness and rejection margins under bounded arrival error a, probe scale ε, and volume error ζ; obtains optimal metric error bound O(√a + ζ); validates inversion and stability numerically and algebraically.

Conclusion: Establishes a rigorous, measurement-based, gauge-conditional tomography theorem for Lorentzian geometry—operational in principle, algebraically exact, and numerically verifiable—while explicitly excluding background-free quantum-theoretic inference of clocks or oracles.

Abstract: We formulate a local reconstruction of Lorentzian geometry from directed null arrival times and spacetime volume, assuming a supplied clock foliation and spatial coordinate gauge. Static arrival data recover the optical metric $\bar h=N^{-2}h$, while a normalized volume density fixes the remaining conformal scale up to a global constant. In the stationary case, the Randers arrival function separates optical geometry and shift. For time-dependent ADM metrics we derive the exact future null indicatrix, its algebraic inverse, and a minimal finite-direction sampling scheme: $d(d+3)/2$ one-way arrival measurements suffice in $d$ spatial dimensions, are necessary for stable local recovery within the specified scalar-measurement class, and admit an exact semialgebraic consistency test with certified robustness and rejection margins under bounded measurement error. A short-time freezing estimate and finite-resolution inverse give metric error $O(a/\epsilon+\epsilon+\zeta)$ for arrival error $a$, probe scale $\epsilon$, and volume error $\zeta$; optimizing yields $O(\sqrt a+\zeta)$. Algebraic and numerical checks reproduce the inversion and stability formulas. The result is an operational, gauge-conditional tomography theorem; it does not infer the clocks or arrival oracle from a background-free quantum theory.

</details>


### [13] [Energy exchange confined to the perturbations: the frozen dark sector confronted with Planck, DESI, supernovae and cosmic shear](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14556)
*M. G. Richarte,L.F. Guimarães,S.J. Landau,M. Leizerovich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests a 'frozen model' where dark matter and dark energy exchange energy only in perturbations (not background), keeping ΛCDM expansion history but modifying structure growth via a coupling parameter Σ. Using Planck CMB/lensing, DESI BAO, DES SNe, and cosmic shear (KiDS/DES), it finds mild preference for negative Σ (enhanced growth) when excluding shear, reducing H₀-related tensions—but including shear strongly disfavors coupling (Σ ≈ 0) and worsens the S₈ tension; ΛCDM remains favored overall when all probes are combined.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether a minimal coupled dark sector—where interaction affects only sub-horizon perturbations (preserving ΛCDM background expansion)—can simultaneously alleviate CMB–late-time tensions (e.g., H₀, S₈) without violating other observables.

Method: Bayesian parameter inference using Markov Chain Monte Carlo on cosmological datasets: Planck 2018 (TTTEEE+lensing), DESI DR2 BAO, DES 5-yr SNe (Dovekie), KiDS-Legacy + DES Y3 cosmic shear (growth-corrected for Σ); marginalization over standard ΛCDM parameters plus Σ; model comparison via Bayes factors (ln B).

Result: Planck alone prefers Σ = −0.22 ± 0.11; inclusion of BAO/SNe yields Σ = −0.24 ± 0.09 (ln B = 1.5 vs ΛCDM), but this preference vanishes when excluding strong-coupling regime; with shear, Σ = 0.01 ± 0.08, S₈ = 0.808 ± 0.025, ln B = −1.3 (strongly favors ΛCDM); Planck–shear tension increases from 1.9σ to 2.5–2.7σ; H₀ and age remain unchanged; SH0ES H₀ discrepancy persists at 4.4σ.

Conclusion: The frozen coupling model cannot resolve the S₈ tension and exacerbates it when cosmic shear is included; while it modestly alleviates some H₀-related tensions when shear is excluded, the overall evidence (especially from lensing and shear) disfavors nonzero Σ, reinforcing ΛCDM as the preferred baseline model.

Abstract: We present cosmological constraints on the frozen model, in which dark matter and dark energy exchange energy only at the level of the perturbations. The expansion history is that of flat $\Lambda$CDM, while on sub-horizon scales a dimensionless coupling $\Sigma$ enhances ($\Sigma<0$) or suppresses ($\Sigma>0$) the growth of the dark-matter density contrast. We use Planck 2018 CMB and lensing data, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations (BAO), the DES five-year supernovae (Dovekie recalibration), and the KiDS-Legacy and DES Year 3 cosmic-shear amplitudes, corrected for the model's growth. Planck alone gives $\Sigma=-0.22\pm0.11$, a preference driven by the lensing smoothing of the acoustic peaks and the low large-scale temperature power. Since BAO and supernovae do not constrain $\Sigma$, the coupling acts only through the Planck posterior, lowering the Planck--DESI tension from $1.9\sigma$ to $1.0$--$1.5\sigma$, and that between Planck plus supernovae and DESI from $2.4$--$2.5\sigma$ to $1.7$--$2.1\sigma$. Combining Planck, DESI and supernovae we find $\Sigma=-0.24\pm0.09$ and $\ln B=1.5$ relative to $\Lambda$CDM, a preference that vanishes when the strongest-coupling region is excluded. The enhanced growth, however, raises the Planck--shear tension from $1.9\sigma$ to $2.5$--$2.7\sigma$. With shear included, $\Sigma=0.01\pm0.08$ and $S_8=0.808\pm0.025$, while $\ln B=-1.3$ favors $\Lambda$CDM. $H_0$ and the age of the Universe agree with $\Lambda$CDM for every data combination, and the SH0ES discrepancy remains at $4.4\sigma$. Without shear, the favored coupling predicts half the $\Lambda$CDM integrated Sachs--Wolfe amplitude at $z=0.5$ and an enhanced shear signal in the lowest tomographic bins. These signatures and the redshift distribution of cluster counts test the coupling independently of the amplitude of the fluctuations.

</details>


### [14] [Spins do not add: why rotating stars do not help spin-up black holes in binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14610)
*Filippo Camilloni,Konrad Topolski,Keneth Miler,Luciano Rezzolla*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: In black hole–neutron star mergers, increasing the neutron star’s spin causes the final black hole’s spin to *decrease*, contrary to intuition and BH–BH behavior—due to tidal disruption–driven angular momentum loss via dynamical ejecta, rivaling GW radiation losses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the intuitive monotonic relationship between component spins and final black hole spin—established for black-hole binaries—holds for black hole–neutron star (BH–NS) systems, where matter effects (e.g., tides, disruption) introduce new physics.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations across a broad parameter space of BH–NS binaries with varying neutron star spins and mass ratios; analysis of angular momentum transport, tidal disruption dynamics, ejecta production, and gravitational-wave emission.

Result: The final black hole spin *decreases* with increasing neutron star spin—a reversal of the BH–BH trend; this is causally linked to enhanced tidal disruption at higher NS spin, leading to significant angular momentum loss through unbound dynamical ejecta, sometimes comparable to that carried away by gravitational waves.

Conclusion: Spin evolution in BH–NS mergers is fundamentally distinct from BH–BH mergers due to hydrodynamic and tidal effects; standard spin-remnant models ignoring matter dynamics are inadequate, implying greater complexity in stellar-mass black hole spin histories.

Abstract: It is well-known that in the merger of a black-hole binary with spins aligned with the orbital angular momentum, the spin of the final black hole always increases with the spin of the components. We show that this very intuitive behavior is violated in black hole--neutron star binaries, where the spin of the final black hole actually decreases as the spin of the neutron star increases. Using a large set of binaries with different spins and mass ratios, we identify the mechanism behind this surprising result in the nonlinear dynamics of the tidal disruption, which drives a substantial loss of angular momentum via dynamical ejecta, in some cases comparable to that radiated via gravitational waves. Our results highlight that the spin evolution of stellar-mass black holes involved in binary mergers is different from what normally assumed, far more complex, and interesting.

</details>


### [15] [Conic Tilings of Black Hole Images by Radial Closest Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14625)
*George N. Wong,Andrew Mummery*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes that photon trajectories in black hole images—parameterized by their closest-approach radius—form conic-section arcs in standard image coordinates for Kerr and generalized Plebanski-Demianski spacetimes; it proves a local slope test suffices to identify physical arcs under physically reasonable conditions (positive energy/mass, regular metric), leveraging PD field equations and principal null direction alignment, and derives a unifying polynomial for the critical curve.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To geometrically interpret black hole images as maps of radial photon propagation—decoupling image structure from emission models—by characterizing how light rays probe spacetime depth via their minimum radial coordinate (closest-approach radius).

Method: Analytical ray tracing in Kerr and Plebanski-Demianski (PD) spacetimes; derivation of image-coordinate expressions for contours of constant closest-approach radius r_min; use of radial geodesic equation and turning-point analysis; application of PD field equations and principal null direction properties to prove sufficiency of a local radial potential slope condition; construction of a unified polynomial encoding the critical curve.

Result: Contours of constant r_min are conic sections in Bardeen (Kerr) and natural local (PD) image coordinates; under positive mass/energy and regularity assumptions, the physical arc is selected by a local sign test on dV/dr at r_min; this fails across r=0 or in non-PD extensions like Ovcharenko-Podolsky; a single polynomial captures the critical curve in both Kerr and PD.

Conclusion: Black hole images encode radial photon dynamics geometrically: the critical curve and substructure are governed by conic geometry tied to r_min, enabling model-independent interpretation of observational data as a map of spacetime probing depth.

Abstract: Each point in a black hole image corresponds to a light ray, whose minimum radial coordinate when traced backward from the observer measures how deeply it probes the spacetime. We show that contours of constant closest-approach radius form arcs of conic sections in standard Bardeen image coordinates for Kerr, and in natural local image coordinates for finite-radius observers in the broader Plebanski-Demianski (PD) family of Einstein-Maxwell solutions. At each radius, the condition of vanishing radial velocity defines a complete conic; the physical contour consists of those points whose rays reach that radius before encountering an outer turning point. For positive Killing energy and positive mass, we prove that throughout connected positive-radius regions with positive radial metric function and allowed motion at both endpoints, a local test of the radial potential's slope is sufficient to select the physical arcs. This simplification follows from a coefficient identity imposed by the PD field equations and alignment of the principal null directions. We give explicit counterexamples in a domain crossing r=0 and in the separable Ovcharenko-Podolsky extension, where an outer turning point invalidates the local test. The conic family also yields a single polynomial equation containing the critical curve in Kerr and PD. These results turn the returning image into a geometric map of radial photon propagation, independent of the emission model. (This abstract has been abridged to meet the arXiv character limit.)

</details>


### [16] [Post-adiabatic effects sourced by radiation reaction for inspiraling, eccentric binaries in post-Newtonian theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14627)
*Rohit S. Chandramouli,Nijaid Arredondo,Lidia J. Gomes Da Silva,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses resummed multiple-scale analysis to derive first post-adiabatic corrections to the adiabatic approximation for eccentric binary evolution under radiation reaction, showing that waveform accuracy and dephasing depend critically on orbital phase at initialization — especially for highly eccentric, long-lived systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the accuracy limitations of the widely used adiabatic approximation for eccentric binaries, particularly how initialization phase affects secular evolution and gravitational-wave phasing under leading-order radiation reaction.

Method: Resummed multiple-scale analysis (closely related to near-identity averaging transformations) applied to orbit-averaged radiation-reaction-driven evolution; derivation of first post-adiabatic corrections to orbital elements and GW polarizations; gauge-invariant matching of waveforms at physical events; analysis of initial-condition dependence at radial turning points vs. generic phases.

Result: Secular equations for e and a receive no explicit first post-adiabatic source terms, but their solutions acquire such corrections via initial conditions; first post-adiabatic oscillations vanish at radial turning points, ensuring osculating and orbit-averaged energy/angular momentum agree up to second post-adiabatic order there; elsewhere, significant dephasing arises and is mitigated by first post-adiabatic corrections; dephasing sign changes across parameter space due to competing higher-order contributions.

Conclusion: Initialization phase fundamentally governs the accuracy of adiabatic and post-adiabatic approximations; neglecting it leads to uncontrolled errors in waveform phasing—especially critical for highly eccentric, long-duration binaries in gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: The adiabatic approximation, in which eccentric binaries evolve under orbit-averaged fluxes, is widely used in gravitational-wave and astrophysical modeling. We use resummed multiple-scale analysis, which is closely related to near-identity averaging transformations, to derive post-adiabatic corrections from leading-order radiation reaction and explain how the accuracy of the adiabatic approximation depends on the initial orbital phase. While the secular evolution equations for the eccentricity and semi-major axis receive no explicit first post-adiabatic corrections, their solutions acquire such corrections through the initial conditions. We also derive the change in the functional form of the gravitational-wave polarizations that cancels the gauge dependence of the orbital elements at first post-adiabatic order, ensuring agreement between waveforms evaluated at the same physical event in different coordinates. At a radial turning point, the first post-adiabatic oscillations of the semi-major axis and eccentricity vanish, so the initial osculating energy and angular momentum agree with their orbit-averaged values up to second post-adiabatic corrections. When initialized elsewhere along the orbit, the adiabatic approximation can accumulate significant dephasing, which the first post-adiabatic approximation can substantially reduce. Both dephasings can change sign across parameter space, consistent with competing contributions from successive orders. Accounting for initialization is therefore essential for assessing the accuracy of these approximations, particularly for long-lived, highly eccentric binaries.

</details>


### [17] [Gravitational observables from a post-Carrollian expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14644)
*Max Niedermaier,Mahdi Sedighi Jafari*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs exact ultralocal Dirac observables in Carroll gravity—a diffeomorphism-invariant ultrarelativistic limit of Einstein gravity—and uses them to build a local, gradient-expansion-based 'post-Carrollian' framework for Dirac observables in full Einstein gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the common belief that diffeomorphism invariance necessarily forbids local gravitational observables, by demonstrating explicit counterexamples in Carroll gravity and leveraging them for Einstein gravity.

Method: Constructs infinitely many exact ultralocal Dirac observables in velocity-dominated (electric) Carroll gravity; then develops a systematic 'post-Carrollian' expansion for Einstein gravity, organized by spatial gradient order or powers of 1/G_N, ensuring locality at each finite order.

Result: Proof that diffeomorphism invariance alone does not preclude local observables; explicit construction of ultralocal Dirac observables in Carroll gravity; derivation of a local, convergent (in appropriate regimes) gradient expansion for Dirac observables in Einstein gravity.

Conclusion: Locality of gravitational observables is compatible with full diffeomorphism invariance; Carroll gravity provides a tractable arena for defining exact local observables, and its structure informs a physically meaningful, local observable framework for Einstein gravity via a controlled post-Carrollian expansion.

Abstract: Einstein gravity, with a few exceptions, only admits nonlocal observables. This feature is often attributed to diffeomorphism invariance, since a gravitational field can thereby locally be transformed away. We show this attribution to be incorrect by constructing infinitely many exact ultralocal Dirac observables in (velocity dominated aka electric) Carroll gravity. The latter describes Einstein gravity's ultrarelativistic limit, is fully diffeomorphism invariant, and of independent interest in a variety of contexts. Starting from these Carroll observables a systematic construction of Dirac observables for Einstein gravity is outlined. The resulting "post-Carrollian" expansion is organized by the number of spatial gradients or fractional inverse powers of Newton's constant, where each partial sum is local.

</details>


### [18] [Binary black holes with non-orthogonal reference metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14647)
*Nishita Jadoo,Zachariah B. Etienne*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces and validates a novel non-orthogonal reference 3-metric implementation in the covariant moving-puncture formalism for numerical relativity, enabling smoothly derefined structured grids—demonstrated via convergence tests on Kasner spacetimes and stable, high-mode gravitational waveform extraction from binary black hole simulations without mesh refinement boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of prior numerical relativity simulations that used only orthogonal reference metrics, enabling flexible, multi-scale grid resolution (e.g., high resolution near black holes and low resolution in the wave zone) while maintaining stability and accuracy in the moving-puncture formalism.

Method: Developed a general covariant formulation for non-orthogonal reference 3-metrics; validated using exact Kasner initial data with a fisheye-style metric and full binary black hole evolutions; employed spheroidal modifications of spherical fisheye maps to anisotropically compress grid resolution along z for orbital-plane-aligned systems; extracted gravitational waveforms up to ℓ=8 on uniform grids without adaptive mesh refinement.

Result: Demonstrated spectral-like convergence to exact Kasner solution; stable BBH evolutions through inspiral–merger–ringdown despite large reference metric deformations; smooth, high-ℓ (up to 8) waveforms extracted on grids free of mesh refinement boundaries; reduced grid point count via spheroidal fisheye tuning without loss of accuracy.

Conclusion: Smoothly derefined structured grids enabled by general non-orthogonal reference metrics are viable and robust for high-fidelity BBH simulations in the moving-puncture formalism, offering improved scalability and flexibility over traditional orthogonal or AMR-based approaches.

Abstract: To our knowledge, numerical relativity (NR) simulations in the covariant moving-puncture formalism have only been performed with orthogonal reference metrics. Here, we demonstrate a new general non-orthogonal reference 3-metric implementation. We first validate this reference metric implementation by numerically evolving the exact Kasner initial data with a fisheye-style reference 3-metric, demonstrating numerical results that converge to the exact solution with increasing grid resolution. Next, we validate our approach by evolving binary black holes (BBHs) in the moving-puncture formulation, from inspiral through merger and ringdown. Even with a large deformation in the reference geometry, introduced to allow a large change in grid refinement to enable the simulation to span the relevant length scales, from high resolutions near the BHs to low resolutions in the wave zone, no pathologies are observed. Smooth waveforms for up to $\ell=8$ are extracted on a grid without mesh refinement boundaries. We demonstrate the flexibility of tuning structured grids through the reference metric by employing spheroidal modifications of the spherical fisheye map to reduce the number of grid points in the $z$ direction when the orbit is in the $xy$-plane. These results support the use of smoothly derefined structured grids for BBH evolutions.

</details>


### [19] [Ernst-Potential Driven Recovery of Kerr with Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14650)
*Veselin G. Filev,Stoytcho S. Yazadjiev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies artificial neural networks to directly minimize a regularized Ernst sigma-model action to recover the Kerr black hole solution, achieving high-accuracy metric reconstruction and mass prediction without solving Einstein's equations analytically or numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a practical, data-free, variational deep learning framework for reconstructing exact stationary black-hole geometries—specifically the Kerr solution—by leveraging symmetry reduction, coordinate compactification, and physics-informed neural network constraints.

Method: Uses physics-informed neural networks trained via direct minimization of a regularized Ernst sigma-model action; employs axial Killing reduction to avoid ergospheric divergence, compactified coordinates to cover the full exterior domain, and hard-coded boundary conditions (asymptotic flatness and axis regularity) in the network architecture.

Result: For dimensionless spins 0.1 ≤ a/M ≤ 0.99, median max relative error in g_ϕϕ < 7.2×10⁻⁴; when given only horizon rod length and angular momentum as inputs, networks predict mass with median relative error < 1.8×10⁻³.

Conclusion: Direct variational training with neural networks is a viable and accurate method for recovering rotating black-hole spacetimes, demonstrating feasibility for more complex stationary vacuum or electrovacuum solutions.

Abstract: We use artificial neural networks to recover the Kerr solution by directly minimizing a regularized Ernst sigma-model action. Reduction along the axial Killing direction avoids the divergence at the ergosphere. Compactified coordinates cover the entire black-hole exterior, while the network parametrization enforces the asymptotic data and axis regularity. For dimensionless spins $0.1 \le a/M \le 0.99$, the median maximum relative error in the recovered metric component $g_{\phi\phi}$ remains below $7.2\times10^{-4}$. When only the horizon rod length and angular momentum are prescribed, the networks also predict the mass with median relative errors below $1.8\times10^{-3}$. These results demonstrate the practicality of direct variational training for rotating black-hole geometries.

</details>


### [20] [Correlation Geometry and Emergent Space-Time from Stochastic Correlation Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14693)
*Antonino Marciano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a geometric framework linking correlation geometry to stochastic flows of correlation projectors, using unitary equivalence, Wilczek–Zee connections, and Stratonovich gradient flows; it derives Yang–Mills and Einstein–Hilbert-like terms from one-loop methods, establishes projector-based parallel transport, and identifies conditions for mapping to Ashtekar’s canonical gravity variables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous geometric and stochastic foundation for emergent spacetime and gravity from quantum correlation structures, bridging quantum information, gauge theory, and general relativity via correlation projectors and their dynamics.

Method: Uses measure-theoretic encoding of physical configurations on self-adjoint operator spaces, defines Wilczek–Zee connection via local orthonormal frames in spectrally separated correlation sectors, imposes projector constraints via Stratonovich gradient flow, applies local continuum approximation with massive fast modes, and employs standard one-loop field-theoretic methods to derive effective actions; analyzes cyclic projector traces for parallel transport and matches to Ashtekar variables via symplectic structure identification.

Result: Derives Yang–Mills-type gauge interactions and Einstein–Hilbert curvature terms from correlation geometry; proves convergence of cyclic projector trace to parallel-transport operator trace along smooth closed paths; identifies first-order frame action as symplectic on projector sector; specifies matching conditions for Ashtekar phase-space description and assumptions linking correlation geometry to induced interactions and emergent spacetime.

Conclusion: Correlation geometry—when equipped with stochastic projector flows and spectral separation—naturally encodes both gauge and gravitational dynamics; the framework provides a concrete pathway from quantum correlations to canonical gravity variables, contingent on specified matching conditions and open-system assumptions.

Abstract: We propose an effective framework relating correlation geometry to stochastic flows of correlation projectors. Physical configurations are encoded by measures on spaces of self-adjoint operators, with physical equivalence defined by unitary conjugation. Assuming a smooth, spectrally separated correlation sector, local orthonormal frames define a Wilczek--Zee connection, while a Stratonovich gradient-flow ansatz preserves the projector constraints. In a local continuum approximation with massive fast modes, standard one-loop methods generate Yang--Mills-type terms and curvature terms of Einstein--Hilbert form. Along a smooth closed path, the trace of a cyclic product of projectors converges to the trace of the parallel-transport operator as the discretization is refined. We examine possible geometric, disordered, and topological regimes and motivate the stochastic dynamics through an effective open-system description. The first-order frame action defines a symplectic structure on the projector sector, providing a starting point for its relation to gravitational canonical variables. We identify the matching conditions required for an Ashtekar phase-space description and the assumptions connecting correlation geometry to induced interactions and emergent spacetime.

</details>


### [21] [Quantum Fluctuations of the Stress-Energy Tensor via Noether's Theorem and the Connection to the Cosmological Constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14875)
*J. A. Crosse*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives quantum fluctuations of Noether’s current—specifically the stress-energy tensor—from first principles using Noether’s theorem, showing that off-shell Euler–Lagrange contributions generate a mass⁴-dependent vacuum energy term consistent with the cosmological constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the non-vanishing Euler-Lagrange term in Noether's current for quantum systems (which do not follow classical paths) and quantify its physical consequence—namely, a quantum contribution to the cosmological constant via stress-energy fluctuations.

Method: Applies Noether’s theorem off-shell to a complex scalar field with spacetime translational symmetry; isolates the quantum fluctuation term arising from the Euler-Lagrange equation not vanishing; computes the resulting correction to the stress-energy tensor.

Result: Derives a quantum fluctuation contribution to the stress-energy tensor proportional to m⁴, matching dimensional expectations for vacuum energy density and aligning with standard estimates of the cosmological constant.

Conclusion: The off-shell quantum deviation from classical trajectories generates an intrinsic, symmetry-derived contribution to the stress-energy tensor, providing a first-principles field-theoretic origin for a m⁴-dependent cosmological constant term.

Abstract: When deriving the expression for the conserved Noether's current one obtains a term that is an integral over the Euler-Lagrange equation. This term vanishes vanishes if the system evolves along the classical path. However, quantum particles do not necessarily follow the classical path and, hence, for a quantum system, the Euler-Lagrange term does not vanish. This extra term corresponds to a quantum fluctuation of the Noether's current owing to the system deviating from the classical path. Here, we compute the fluctuation of a quantum field directly from Noether's Theorem and, by considering a spacetime translational symmetry of a complex scalar field, we find the specific fluctuations of the Stress-Energy tensor. As the Stress-Energy tensor is the source term for Einstein's equations of General Relativity, these fluctuations can be seen to be a contribution to the Cosmological Constant. The final expression for this contribution is proportional to the mass of the field to the fourth power which is consistent with previously derived expressions for the Cosmological Constant.

</details>


### [22] [From Matched Black-Hole Scalar Charges to Gravitational-Wave Inspirals in a Massive Scalar--Gauss--Bonnet Effective Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14934)
*Jie Wu,Mengfei Sun,Jin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a matched description for scalar fields in scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity with finite mass, linking isolated black hole curvature-sourced fields to effective binary dynamics; it derives renormalized scalar charges, computes their impact on post-Newtonian conservative dynamics and gravitational-wave phasing—including dipole/quadrupole radiation thresholds and phase accumulation—and incorporates dispersive massive propagation and tidal responses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect the finite-mass scalar field response of isolated black holes in scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity to observable gravitational-wave signatures from binary black hole inspirals, particularly accounting for finite interaction range, Yukawa suppression, and massive-radiation kinematics.

Method: Constructs a matched asymptotic solution separating the massive scalar field into curvature-forced (distributed) and homogeneous Yukawa components; matches near- and far-zone solutions to determine the local static coefficient and renormalize it to a worldline coupling; incorporates leading matched scalar charges into leading post-Newtonian conservative dynamics and GW phasing for nonspinning quasicircular binaries; treats breathing/longitudinal tidal responses and dispersive massive propagation separately.

Result: The static coefficient has no linear dependence on scalar mass—first correction is quadratic; scalar mass modifies conservative dynamics, introduces distinct dipole and quadrupole radiation thresholds, alters accumulated inspiral phase, and yields measurable breathing and longitudinal polarizations; dispersive propagation is consistently separated.

Conclusion: The matched description successfully bridges BH-local scalar physics to BBH-source and GW-propagation observables in massive sGB gravity, enabling robust phenomenological tests of scalar-tensor theories with finite-range interactions using LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and future detectors.

Abstract: Massive scalar fields introduce a finite interaction range in scalar--Gauss--Bonnet (sGB) gravity and can modify the gravitational waves (GWs) emitted by binary black holes (BBHs). We develop a matched description that relates the curvature-sourced scalar field of an isolated black hole (BH) to the scalar charge entering the effective binary dynamics. The massive field contains a distributed curvature-forced contribution and a homogeneous Yukawa sector. By matching the near- and far-zone solutions, we determine the local static coefficient and derive its finite conversion to a renormalized worldline coupling. The static coefficient has no linear scalar-mass correction, and its first correction is quadratic. We use the leading matched charges in the leading post-Newtonian dynamics and GW phasing of nonspinning, quasicircular BBHs while retaining the finite Yukawa range and massive-radiation kinematics. The scalar mass modifies the conservative dynamics, produces distinct dipole and quadrupole radiation thresholds, and changes the accumulated inspiral phase. We also construct the associated breathing and longitudinal tidal responses and treat dispersive massive propagation separately. These results connect the finite-mass BH scalar response to source and propagation observables for BBH inspirals.

</details>


### [23] [Magnetized strange quark matter in hypersurface-homogeneous $f(R,T)$ cosmology: physical closure, isotropization and late-time observational constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15077)
*D. P. Tadas,S. V. Gore,S. P. Hatkar,S. D. Katore*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper revisits magnetized strange quark matter cosmologies in $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ gravity on hypersurface-homogeneous Bianchi I/Kantowski–Sachs/Bianchi III spacetimes, treating the Maxwell field exactly and avoiding kinematic ansatzes. It closes equations via bag-model EoS and magnetic flux freezing, includes dust/radiation, and fits observational data (cosmic chronometers, Pantheon+, DESI BAO) to constrain $\mu = 1.12^{+0.65}_{-0.58}$ and $H_0 = 69.1 \pm 3.2~\text{km s}^{-1}\text{Mpc}^{-1}$. Energy conditions hold; model slightly favors $\mu>0$, but Bayesian evidence is inconclusive versus $\Lambda$CDM; the fitted bag parameter $B_c^{1/4}=2.15~\text{meV}$ is an effective vacuum energy, and $\mu$ behaves as an effective late-time coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of prior works—namely reliance on inconsistent kinematic ansatzes for magnetized sources and absence of observational constraints—by formulating a fully consistent, observationally testable cosmological model of magnetized strange quark matter in $f(R,T)$ gravity.

Method: Exact integration of Einstein–Maxwell field equations in $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ gravity on hypersurface-homogeneous Bianchi I, Kantowski–Sachs, and Bianchi III backgrounds; minimal coupling of Maxwell field; closure via bag-model equation of state and magnetic flux freezing; inclusion of dust and radiation; analytical solutions in limiting regimes and numerical integration in general; joint MCMC likelihood analysis using cosmic chronometers (32), Pantheon+ SNe Ia (1590), and DESI DR2 BAO.

Result: $\mu = 1.12^{+0.65}_{-0.58}$, $H_0 = 69.1 \pm 3.2~\text{km s}^{-1}\text{Mpc}^{-1}$, present shear parameter $(\sigma/H)_0 \simeq 5.8\times10^{-11}$ for 1 nG primordial field; all energy conditions satisfied; $\Delta\mathrm{AIC} = -1.4$, $\Delta\mathrm{BIC} = +4.0$, Bayes factor inconclusive vs. $\Lambda$CDM; effective bag parameter $B_c^{1/4} = 2.15~\text{meV}$.

Conclusion: The $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ model with magnetized strange quark matter is theoretically self-consistent and observationally viable; $\mu > 0$ is weakly preferred but not decisively supported, and $\mu$ functions as an effective late-time phenomenological parameter rather than a fundamental coupling, sensitive to matter Lagrangian choice and data priors.

Abstract: Cosmological models of magnetized strange quark matter in $f(R,T)$ gravity have so far relied on kinematic ansatzes and lacked observational tests. We revisit them for $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ on a hypersurface-homogeneous background containing the locally rotationally symmetric Bianchi I, Kantowski--Sachs and Bianchi III geometries. Treated exactly, the Maxwell field couples minimally. Because kinematic ansatzes contradict a magnetized source, we close the field equations with the bag-model equation of state and magnetic flux freezing and, adding dust and radiation, solve them exactly in limiting cases and numerically in general. Without primordial shear, the field-induced anisotropy keeps, as in general relativity, a nearly constant radiation-era level for $\mu\ge0$, then decays as the model approaches de Sitter space; an assumed present field of 1 nG leaves $(\sigma/H)_0\simeq5.8\times10^{-11}$ today. A joint fit to 32 cosmic chronometers, 1590 Pantheon+ supernova light curves and baryon acoustic oscillations from the second data release of the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument gives $\mu=1.12^{+0.65}_{-0.58}$ and $H_0=69.1\pm3.2$~\kmsMpc, and the null, weak and dominant energy conditions hold throughout. Relative to the flat $\Lambda$ cold dark matter model, the Akaike (AIC) and Bayesian (BIC) information criteria give $\Delta\mathrm{AIC}=-1.4$ and $\Delta\mathrm{BIC}=+4.0$, and the Bayes factor is inconclusive. The fitted bag term, $B_c^{1/4}=2.15$ meV, is an effective vacuum energy, not the quantum chromodynamics bag constant. The preference for $\mu>0$ depends on the supernova sample and the prior, a constant coupling would shift matter--radiation equality, and the sign of $\mu$ depends on the matter Lagrangian; $\mu$ is therefore an effective late-time parameter.

</details>


### [24] [Astrometric effects of anisotropic gravitational wave backgrounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15206)
*Petar D. Dimitrov,Deyan P. Mihaylov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes astrometric correlation functions (generalized overlap reduction functions) for anisotropic stochastic gravitational wave backgrounds (SGWBs) across all six GW polarizations, providing closed-form expressions in the distant-source limit and highlighting qualitative differences between tensor, vector, and scalar modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable future astrometric detection of anisotropic, arbitrarily polarized SGWBs—particularly from supermassive black hole binaries—by extending the Hellings-Downs formalism to astrometry and beyond the isotropic tensor assumption.

Method: Derives generalized overlap reduction functions (ORFs) for anisotropic SGWBs in astrometry using the distant-light-source approximation; analytically computes correlations for all six polarization states (tensor+, ×, vector-x, y, scalar-longitudinal, breathing) following pulsar timing array methodology.

Result: Closed-form ORF expressions are obtained for all polarizations under the distant-source limit; tensor, vector, and scalar correlations exhibit qualitatively distinct angular dependence and symmetry properties; anisotropy breaks rotational invariance and introduces mode-dependent harmonic structure.

Conclusion: The derived generalized ORFs provide a foundational framework for detecting and characterizing anisotropic, multi-polarization SGWBs with future high-precision astrometric data (e.g., Gaia DR4+), enabling tests of gravity beyond general relativity and improved astrophysical source inference.

Abstract: Precise astrometric observations of distant objects such as stars and quasars can be used to probe gravitational wave signals at nanohertz frequencies. A stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB) produces correlated apparent motions of pairs of objects on the sky. That correlation as a function of their angular separation is the astrometric analog of the Hellings-Downs curve in the context of pulsar timing measurements. Upper limits on the SGWB have been obtained from very-long baseline interferometry and Gaia astrometry, and future Gaia data releases are expected to improve them. An astrophysical background from inspiraling supermassive black hole binaries is expected to be significantly anisotropic. In this work, we compute the astrometric correlation functions for an anisotropic SGWB, known as the generalized overlap reduction functions, for all six gravitational wave polarizations. We present closed-form expressions in all cases where the distant-light source limit can be applied, following the approach used for pulsar timing arrays. We find that the correlations differ qualitatively between the tensor, vector, and scalar polarizations. Finally, we discuss the applicability of these results in future astrometry searches for arbitrarily polarized anisotropic gravitational wave backgrounds.

</details>


### [25] [Quasinormal Modes of a Black Hole in a Dehnen-Type Dark Matter Halo with Radial Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15256)
*Guoyang Fu,Jiahao Zhou,Dan Zhang,Jian-Pin Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of scalar and axial gravitational perturbations for black holes embedded in Dehnen-type dark matter halos with nonzero radial pressure, finding that QNM real/imaginary parts show distinct redshift dependence on DM halo compactness $M/a_0$, governed primarily by the profile parameter $\gamma$; damping rate is more sensitive than frequency, and eikonal-limit behavior is captured by light-ring angular velocity $\Omega_c$ and Lyapunov exponent $\lambda$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic dark matter halo models—with nonvanishing radial pressure—affect black hole perturbative dynamics and quasinormal mode spectra, beyond standard vacuum or pressureless DM assumptions.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of quasinormal modes for scalar and axial gravitational perturbations in a black hole spacetime sourced by a Dehnen-type dark matter halo with radial pressure; analysis includes parameter dependence (especially $M/a_0$ and $\gamma$) and eikonal limit correspondence to null geodesic quantities ($\Omega_c$, $\lambda$).

Result: Real and imaginary parts of QNMs exhibit distinct redshift coefficients w.r.t. DM halo compactness $M/a_0$, unlike the zero-pressure case; effect magnitude is dominated by $\gamma$; damping rate shows stronger sensitivity to $M/a_0$ than oscillation frequency; eikonal-limit deviations align with $\Omega_c$ and $\lambda$.

Conclusion: Nonzero radial pressure in Dehnen-type DM halos meaningfully alters black hole QNM structure, implying that dark matter microphysics (e.g., pressure support) must be accounted for in precision gravitational-wave spectroscopy and tests of strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of scalar and (axial) gravitational perturbations of a black hole spacetime surrounded by a Dehnen-type dark matter (DM) halo with nonvanishing radial pressure. Our results reveal that the real and imaginary parts of the QNMs exhibit distinct redshift coefficients with respect to the DM halo compactness $M/a_0$, in contrast to the vanishing radial pressure case. The magnitude of this effect is dominated by the profile parameter $\gamma$, and the damping rate is more sensitive to the DM halo compactness than the oscillation frequency. In the eikonal limit, the redshift deviations are well captured by the light ring angular velocity $\Omega_c$ and the Lyapunov exponent $\lambda$.

</details>


### [26] [Conserved gravitational-wave Stress-Energy Tensor in General Metric Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15321)
*Xiao-Bin Lai,Yu-Zhi Fan,Yu-Qi Dong,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a conserved gravitational-wave stress-energy tensor for general metric theories—including those with vector fields—by augmenting the conventional tensor with vector-field contributions derived from the diffeomorphism identity; it unifies multiple derivation methods in the wave zone, yields a generally nonsymmetric but divergence-free tensor, and applies the framework to Einstein-æther theory to resolve prior discrepancies in orbital energy loss predictions for compact binaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in gravitational-wave stress-energy tensors for metric theories with additional fields (e.g., vector fields), particularly the non-conservation of the conventional tensor and conflicting energy-loss predictions in theories like Einstein-æther.

Method: Constructs a generalized GW stress-energy tensor by enforcing conservation via the diffeomorphism identity; incorporates vector-field contributions; performs short-wavelength averaging in a flat vacuum wave zone with constant background fields; compares perturbed field equations, perturbed action, Landau-Lifshitz, and Noether current approaches; applies result to Einstein-æther theory to compute orbital energy loss for adiabatic quasicircular binaries.

Result: Derives a completed, generally nonsymmetric stress-energy tensor $\tau^{\mu\nu}$ satisfying $\partial_\mu \tau^{\mu\nu} = 0$ and yielding energy flux $c\tau^{i0}$; shows equivalence of four derivation methods under stated conditions; computes consistent orbital energy loss rates in Einstein-æther theory, reconciling prior discrepancies.

Conclusion: A consistent, conserved GW stress-energy tensor can be constructed for general metric theories with vector fields, enabling robust radiation balance calculations and facilitating astrophysical and observational tests of gravity beyond GR.

Abstract: We construct a gravitational-wave (GW) stress-energy tensor for general metric theories and show that it is conserved after averaging in the wave zone. In metric theories involving a vector field, we find that the conventional GW stress-energy tensor is generally not conserved. Combining the diffeomorphism identity, we identify the vector-field contribution required in addition to the conventional GW stress-energy tensor.
