On this basis, in a flat vacuum wave zone with constant background fields and after short-wavelength averaging, the perturbed field equation, perturbed action, Landau-Lifshitz, and Noether current methods yield equivalent tensors. In this setting, the completed tensor is generally nonsymmetric and satisfies $\partial_\mu\tau^{\mu\nu}=0$, with energy flux $c\tau^{i0}$. It also does not depend on whether a covariant or contravariant vector is chosen as the fundamental variable. Applying this framework to Einstein-æther theory, we derive the orbital energy losses of an adiabatic quasicircular compact binary, and provide a possible explanation for the discrepancies among previous results in this theory. These results establish a consistent radiation balance that allows additional gravitational degrees of freedom to be connected to the evolution of astrophysical systems and to observational tests of gravity.

</details>


### [27] [FOORT: Flexible Object-Oriented Ray Tracing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15331)
*Daniel R. Mayerson,Seppe J. Staelens,Fabio Bacchini*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: FOORT is a flexible, open-source C++ ray-tracing code for imaging black holes and compact objects in arbitrary spacetimes—including analytical (e.g., Kerr, Schwarzschild) and numerical metrics—supporting horizons, naked singularities, and horizonless objects; it features conserved-quantity-free geodesic initialization, adaptive mesh refinement on user-defined diagnostics (e.g., photon rings), Python post-processing for images and visibilities, and scales efficiently with OpenMP threads.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a flexible, lightweight, and extensible ray-tracing framework capable of handling diverse spacetime geometries—including non-Kerr, numerical, and horizonless metrics—without relying on metric symmetries or conserved quantities, thereby enabling broader exploration of compact-object astrophysics and observational signatures.

Method: FOORT numerically integrates null geodesics backward from a camera in arbitrary 3+1 spacetimes using a coordinate-based ODE solver; geodesics are initialized without assuming Killing symmetries or conserved quantities (e.g., energy, angular momentum); it supports analytical and user-supplied numerical metrics via modular metric classes; employs adaptive mesh refinement guided by diagnostics (e.g., equatorial crossings, radius of closest approach); and outputs raw geodesic data processed by Python tools into images and interferometric visibilities.

Result: FOORT successfully reproduces analytic photon ring structure and disk images in Schwarzschild and Kerr spacetimes; demonstrates robust performance on non-Kerr metrics (e.g., boson stars, fuzzballs); achieves efficient parallel scaling with OpenMP; produces high-resolution images resolving higher-order photon rings via adaptive refinement; and generates synthetic VLBI visibility amplitudes compatible with observational analysis.

Conclusion: FOORT establishes a versatile, open, and computationally efficient foundation for relativistic ray tracing across general spacetimes, lowering barriers to testing exotic compact object models against current and future EHT- and VLBI-scale observations.

Abstract: We present FOORT (Flexible Object-Oriented Ray Tracing), an open C++ code for ray-tracing images of black holes and other compact objects in arbitrary spacetimes. FOORT is designed to offer flexibility and can handle horizons, naked singularities and numerical metrics, allowing it to ray-trace numerous different metrics including Kerr and other black holes, as well as horizonless objects such as fuzzballs and boson stars. New metrics, analytical or numerical, can be added with minimal effort, and geodesics are initialized at the camera without reference to conserved quantities, so no special structure of the metric is required. Besides the intensity observed from a geometrically and optically thin equatorial disk, FOORT is set up to track a number of diagnostics along each geodesic, such as the number of equatorial crossings and the radius of closest approach. Its adaptive mesh can refine the image on any of these diagnostics, efficiently resolving narrow features such as higher-order photon rings. Accompanying Python tools convert the output into images and interferometric visibility amplitudes. We describe the main features of the code, validate it against analytic results in the Schwarzschild and Kerr spacetimes, demonstrate it on several non-Kerr metrics, and characterize its scaling with the number of OpenMP threads. FOORT is lightweight, requiring only C++17 and OpenMP, and has already been used in a number of studies of black hole and compact-object imaging. The code is publicly available in a GitHub repository.

</details>


### [28] [Maximal force bounds in general relativity and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15405)
*Sanhitaa Guha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives upper bounds on gravitational force for stable, horizonless, compact objects in various gravity theories (d-dimensional GR, braneworld, pure Lovelock, Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet), using the absence of light rings as a stability criterion and considering anisotropic matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize and test the maximal force conjecture beyond 4D general relativity by examining how force bounds depend on spacetime dimensionality and higher-curvature or extra-dimensional modifications to gravity.

Method: Analyzes static, spherically symmetric, horizonless compact objects with anisotropic matter; employs photon-ring (light ring) structure as a proxy for stability—requiring no light rings—and derives force bounds via gravitational field equations and energy conditions in multiple gravity frameworks.

Result: Derives dimension- and theory-dependent upper bounds on gravitational force for stable horizonless compact objects; shows that bounds deviate from the standard $c^4/G$ value in higher dimensions and modified gravities, with explicit dependence on curvature corrections and number of dimensions.

Conclusion: The maximal force bound is not universal across gravitational theories; it is sensitive to spacetime dimensionality and higher-curvature/extra-dimensional corrections, supporting a contextual rather than absolute interpretation of the maximal force conjecture.

Abstract: Gibbons and Schiller proposed the maximal force conjecture in general relativity, according to which the maximum force is bounded by $c^4/G$. In this work, we derive upper bounds on the force for stable, self-gravitating, spatially regular and horizonless compact objects in $d$-dimensional general relativity, braneworld gravity, pure Lovelock gravity and Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity. Our analysis uses the connection between photon-ring structure and the stability of horizonless compact objects, adopting the absence of light rings as a criterion for stability. We investigate how the resulting force bounds depend on the spacetime dimension and on modifications to gravitational dynamics arising from extra dimensions and higher curvature corrections. We study how the bounds for such systems change under alternative higher curvature theories of gravity or in higher dimensions. Throughout this work, we consider a static and spherically symmetric spacetime sourced by anisotropic matter for which the radial and tangential pressures may differ.

</details>


### [29] [Exact transformation-optics representation of the Ellis wormhole and limits of finite-device scattering emulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15662)
*Abdullah Guvendi,Claudio Furtado,Omar Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the electromagnetic response of the ultrastatic Ellis wormhole, derives its exact isotropic material representation via Plebański correspondence, proves a fundamental lower bound on reflection mismatch for lossless finite single-port devices attempting to emulate it, and provides rigorous multipole scattering asymptotics and finite approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which electromagnetic features of the ideal two-ended Ellis wormhole can be physically realized by finite, passive, lossless devices — specifically addressing the fundamental limitations imposed by causality, topology, and energy conservation.

Method: Uses global isotropic coordinate transformation and Plebański's formalism to obtain an exact impedance-matched dielectric–magnetic medium; constructs the full vector-Maxwell operator; analyzes spectral properties and scattering (via Jost solutions, Wronskians, and multipole decomposition); applies complex analysis (reverse triangle inequality) to prove reflection mismatch bounds; derives low-frequency transmission scaling and finite-shell approximations with calibrated Lorentz constitutive models.

Result: Proves |R_id| < 1 for all real frequencies in the ideal wormhole, while any regular, lossless finite one-port must satisfy |R| = 1 — yielding a strict positive lower bound on |R − R_id|; obtains exact low-frequency transmission scaling |T_L|² ∝ (ωa/c)^(4L+2); shows total scattering cross section diverges logarithmically forward, transmission cross section → πa²; provides exact finite-band constitutive-error formula for passive Lorentz approximation.

Conclusion: The ideal Ellis wormhole’s electromagnetic response—particularly its nonzero transmission between asymptotic ends—is topologically and energetically forbidden for any finite, lossless, single-port device; this establishes a fundamental realizability gap; finite approximations can match scattering only within bounded frequency bands, quantified precisely by the derived constitutive-error formula.

Abstract: We derive a global isotropic representation of the ultrastatic Ellis wormhole and establish which features of its electromagnetic response can, and cannot, be reproduced by a finite device. An orientation-preserving coordinate map sends the signed proper-radial line to a punctured Euclidean chart with refractive index $n(\rho)=1+a^2/(4\rho^2)$. Through the Plebański correspondence, this gives the exact impedance-matched continuum medium $\varepsilon_r=\mu_r=n(\rho)\,\mathbb{1}$ and its full vector-Maxwell operator. For the ideal two-ended geometry, we derive the electromagnetic radial operator, establish its spectral properties, and express its scattering amplitudes through Jost solutions and Wronskians. Nonzero transmission into the second asymptotic end implies $|R_{\rm id}|<1$ at every real frequency, whereas a regular, lossless finite one-port has unit-modulus reflection. The reverse triangle inequality therefore yields a strictly positive lower bound on the complex reflection-amplitude mismatch. This theorem applies to the stated lossless single-port class and is not extended to lossy or multiport realizations. We compute converged multipole scattering data and establish the low-frequency threshold scaling $|T_L|^2\propto(\omega a/c)^{4L+2}$, with coefficients independently validated for $L=1,2,3,4$. The transmission cross section tends to $\pi a^2$, while the ideal profile's total scattering cross section has a logarithmic forward divergence. We also construct a smooth finite-shell target and derive an exact finite-band constitutive-error formula for a calibrated passive Lorentz model. The constitutive benchmark is kept distinct from scattering equivalence throughout.

</details>


### [30] [Highly spinning binary black holes: promise and pitfalls for parameterized tests of general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15721)
*Soumen Roy,Maria Haney,Justin Janquart*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how highly spinning, mass-asymmetric binary black hole systems enhance sensitivity to higher-post-Newtonian (PN) deviations from general relativity (GR), but shows that waveform model inaccuracies—especially in IMRPhenomXPHM-SpinTaylor, IMRPhenomXPNR, and SEOBNRv5PHM—induce systematic biases in inferred parameters, leading to false detections of GR violations via the TIGER framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve parameterized tests of GR using gravitational waves from highly spinning, mass-asymmetric binaries, which probe stronger-field, more dynamical gravity regimes where higher-PN deviations remain poorly constrained.

Method: Uses numerical relativity (NR) waveforms from the SXS catalog; performs Bayesian inference with three state-of-the-art phenomenological and effective-one-body waveform models (IMRPhenomXPHM-SpinTaylor, IMRPhenomXPNR, SEOBNRv5PHM); applies the TIGER framework—an IMRPhenomXPHM-based inspiral parameterized test of GR—to quantify apparent deviations induced by waveform systematics.

Result: Model-dependent biases in mass, spin magnitude, and spin orientation are observed even within GR; these biases translate into spurious, statistically significant deviations from GR in TIGER analyses, especially for high-SNR signals.

Conclusion: Highly spinning binaries are powerful probes of higher-PN physics, but current waveform models’ inaccuracies limit the reliability of GR tests—highlighting the need for improved waveform modeling before claiming new physics from parameterized tests.

Abstract: Highly spinning binary black holes exhibit complex inspiral dynamics and offer a powerful opportunity to probe gravity in a stronger-field and more dynamical regime than non-spinning binaries. Gravitational-wave signals from such systems enable stringent parameterized tests of deviations from the source dynamics predicted by general relativity. We show that highly spinning, mass-asymmetric binaries can provide enhanced sensitivity to deviations entering at higher post-Newtonian orders, which remain comparatively poorly constrained. Using NR simulations from the SXS catalog, we first recover the injected signals within general relativity using $\texttt{IMRPhenomXPHM-SpinTaylor}$, $\texttt{IMRPhenomXPNR}$, and $\texttt{SEOBNRv5PHM}$, finding model-dependent biases in the inferred masses, spins, and spin orientations. We then analyze the same signals with the $\texttt{IMRPhenomXPHM}$-based inspiral parameterized test of general relativity framework, TIGER, and find that these waveform-systematic biases can lead to apparent deviations from general relativity, which become significant for loud signals. Our results highlight that highly spinning binaries offer powerful tests of higher-order post-Newtonian deviations, but waveform inaccuracies can lead to false signs of new physics.

</details>


### [31] [Eccentricity detectability in Extreme Mass-Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15736)
*Maria Gonçalves Salgado,Lorenzo Speri,Elena M. Rossi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the trade-off between eccentricity-driven distinguishability and harmonic power loss in EMRI gravitational-wave detection with LISA, finding that eccentricity becomes detectable above ~0.05 but single-harmonic searches degrade beyond ~0.1; including ≥50% SNR harmonics mitigates loss but increases statistical cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how orbital eccentricity in extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) affects detectability by LISA — specifically, the competing effects of improved distinguishability from circular templates versus signal power dilution across harmonics.

Method: Semi-coherent detection framework applied to EMRIs with fixed total SNR = 50; evaluates eccentricity distinguishability threshold, single-harmonic sensitivity degradation, harmonic SNR recovery requirements, and statistical cost of multi-harmonic calibration; extended to two astrophysical EMRI population models.

Result: Eccentricity is distinguishable from zero for final eccentricities e_f ≳ 0.05; single-harmonic search sensitivity declines significantly beyond e_f ≈ 0.1; including sufficient harmonics to recover ≥50% of total SNR restores sensitivity in noise-free cases but inflates the number of free parameters for threshold calibration.

Conclusion: The interplay among eccentricity-driven template distinguishability, harmonic power redistribution, and statistical penalties of multi-harmonic searches must be carefully balanced in designing future EMRI detection pipelines for LISA.

Abstract: Extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) consist of a stellar-mass compact object orbiting a massive black hole and are among the primary targets for the future space-based gravitational-wave detector LISA. Their orbital eccentricity carries valuable information about their astrophysical origins, making its detection particularly important. However, increasing eccentricity has competing effects on detectability: it makes eccentric waveforms easier to distinguish from circular ones, but also redistributes signal power across multiple harmonics, reducing the power captured by single-harmonic searches. In this work, we quantify this trade-off within a semi-coherent framework for a source with fixed total SNR of $50$. Eccentricity becomes distinguishable from zero for final eccentricities $e_f \gtrsim 0.05$, while single-harmonic sensitivity declines beyond $e_f \simeq 0.1$. In noise-free analyses, including enough harmonics to recover at least $50\%$ of the total SNR avoids this degradation, but adds statistical freedom when calibrating detection thresholds. We apply the framework to two astrophysical EMRI populations, showing how these competing effects interact with their eccentricity distributions under simplified assumptions. The balance among eccentricity-driven distinguishability, harmonic power loss, and the statistical cost of additional harmonics is an important consideration for future EMRI search developments.

</details>


### [32] [Population inference of eccentric binary black holes using asymptotic constants of motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15738)
*Chiara Anselmo,Giulia Fumagalli,Davide Gerosa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using Newtonian orbital angular momentum—rather than eccentricity—as a consistent, asymptotically constant reference for gravitational-wave population inference of eccentric compact binaries, enabling astrophysically interpretable and frequency-agnostic modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard population analyses use a fixed reference frequency, leading to inconsistent evolutionary stages across sources; zero-frequency (past-time infinity) provides a mathematically well-defined common reference, but eccentricity diverges there (→1), whereas Newtonian orbital angular momentum remains finite and constant—making it suitable for consistent population modeling.

Method: Reformulates eccentric binary population inference by parameterizing systems in terms of the asymptotic constant of motion—the Newtonian orbital angular momentum—at zero frequency, instead of evolving eccentricity; applies this framework to simulated gravitational-wave data using Bayesian population inference.

Result: Demonstrates successful inference of eccentric populations using only asymptotic constants of motion; shows inferred distributions can be reliably propagated to any observational frequency (e.g., 10 Hz) for astrophysical comparison.

Conclusion: Using Newtonian orbital angular momentum as the zero-frequency reference enables self-consistent, evolution-invariant population inference for eccentric binaries—the first such approach—and bridges waveform modeling with astrophysical interpretation without frequency-dependent biases.

Abstract: In standard gravitational-wave population analyses of compact binaries, source parameters are defined at a reference frequency. For quantities that evolve during the inspiral, this means constructing a population of systems at different evolutionary stages, which complicates the astrophysical interpretation of the results. The past-time infinity, or zero-frequency, limit provides a mathematically well-defined reference where all sources enter the population fit on equal footing. This approach was first proposed for spin tilts, which evolve due to spin precession but converge to constant values as binaries are back-propagated. The orbital eccentricity also evolves throughout the inspiral and approaches unity in the zero-frequency limit for all binaries, making it unsuitable as a common reference point for population analyses. On the other hand, the Newtonian orbital angular momentum is an asymptotic constant of motion of the relativistic two-body problem and remains finite in this limit. In this paper, we make use of this known asymptotic behavior to reformulate eccentric population inference in terms of orbital angular momentum rather than eccentricity and demonstrate this approach on simulated gravitational-wave data. Our work constitutes the first example of eccentric population inference using only asymptotic constants of motion. Importantly, after the inference has been performed using this consistent reference, the results can be propagated to any preferred frequency for comparison with astrophysical predictions.

</details>


### [33] [Relaxation of Quantum Observables in a 4D Black Hole that forms from Collapse of a Null Shell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15746)
*Shohreh Gholizadeh Siahmazgi,Anıl Zenginoğlu,Paul R. Anderson,Alessandro Fabbri*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes vacuum polarization and luminosity differences between the 'in' vacuum and Unruh state for a massless scalar field in 4D null-shell black hole formation, finding late-time quartic decay of tail terms—slower than in 2D due to mode scattering—and confirms analytic tail behavior for luminosity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum field effects (vacuum polarization and luminosity) evolve during gravitational collapse in four dimensions, particularly how deviations from the stationary Unruh state relax over time, and to contrast with the simpler two-dimensional case where scattering is absent.

Method: Computes spherically symmetric mode contributions of a massless minimally coupled scalar field to $\langle \phi^2 \rangle_{\rm in} - \langle \phi^2 \rangle_U$ and $L_{\rm in} - L_U$ in the Schwarzschild region outside a contracting null shell; analyzes quasinormal mode dominance at intermediate times and tail behavior at late times; includes analytic verification of the late-time quartic tail for luminosity.

Result: The differences $\langle \phi^2 \rangle_{\rm in} - \langle \phi^2 \rangle_U$ and $L_{\rm in} - L_U$ vanish asymptotically, but with a late-time tail decaying as $t^{-4}$—significantly slower than in 2D—due to scattering of modes; quasinormal modes dominate at intermediate times; analytic calculation confirms the $t^{-4}$ tail for luminosity.

Conclusion: In 4D black hole formation from a null shell, quantum field relaxation to the Unruh state proceeds via quasinormal ringing followed by a scattering-induced $t^{-4}$ tail in both vacuum polarization and luminosity—highlighting dimensional dependence and the role of mode coupling in late-time dynamics.

Abstract: The vacuum polarization and luminosity are computed in a four-dimensional spacetime where a spherically symmetric null shell contracts to form a black hole. This is done by computing the contribution of the spherically symmetric modes of a massless minimally coupled scalar field to the quantities $\la \phi^2 \ra_{\rm in} - \la \phi^2 \ra_U$ and $L_{\rm in} - L_U$. Here the subscript ``in'' denotes the {\it in} vacuum state for the null shell spacetime and the subscript ``U'' denotes the Unruh state in Schwarzschild spacetime. The computation is done outside the null shell and outside the event horizon where the spacetime geometry is the Schwarzschild geometry. It is found that, as expected, these differences approach zero at late times. However, they do so significantly more slowly than in the two-dimensional case where there is no scattering of the mode functions. In both cases, the difference is dominated by a quasinormal mode at intermediate times and a tail term at late times that decays quartically in time. For the luminosity difference, an analytic calculation verifies the late-time result for the tail term.

</details>


### [34] [Integrable Singularity Black Holes without Inner Horizons in Asymptotically Safe Inspired Gravity and NFW Dark Matter Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15813)
*Milko Estrada*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a new UV-complete RG-improved black hole model in asymptotically safe gravity, where the running Newton coupling scales as G_I(r) ∼ r near the origin—yielding an integrable central singularity and eliminating the problematic inner horizon—then smoothly matches to standard GR at large r; it further couples this geometry self-consistently to an NFW dark matter profile via gravitational decoupling, preserving a single event horizon and producing a cored effective gravitational response without ad hoc profile modifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve predictability issues and mass inflation instabilities arising from dS cores and inner horizons in conventional RG-improved black holes, by constructing a regular, single-horizon geometry with an integrable central singularity.

Method: Relates the RG scale to a radially weighted quantum gravitational source in the Ricci scalar volume integrand, yielding a Bessel-type running Newton coupling G_I(r) ∼ r (UV) matched via Israel–Darmois junction conditions to an infrared limit G_II → G₀; incorporates NFW dark matter using the gravitational decoupling algorithm, ensuring separate conservation of quantum-gravitational and matter sectors.

Result: A regular black hole geometry with a single event horizon, no inner horizon, and integrable central singularity; when coupled to NFW dark matter, the effective matter contribution remains finite near r = 0 (due to G_I(r) ∼ r canceling ρ_NFW ∼ r⁻¹), yielding a cored gravitational potential without modifying the NFW profile; the event horizon shifts but retains single-horizon structure.

Conclusion: The proposed r-linear UV running of G provides a physically viable, predictive, and stable RG-improved black hole geometry; its shared coupling with NFW dark matter enables unified regularization of both black hole singularity and dark matter divergence, suggesting a common quantum-gravitational origin for UV completeness and galactic-scale core formation.

Abstract: We investigate an alternative ultraviolet (UV) structure for Renormalization Group improved (RG) black holes within a phenomenological framework inspired by asymptotically safe gravity. In several regular RG constructions, regularity at the origin is associated with a dS core and an additional inner horizon, raising issues of spacetime predictability and possible mass inflation instabilities. Motivated by this problem, we relate the RG scale to the radially weighted effective quantum gravitational source entering the volume integrand of the quantum corrected Ricci scalar. This prescription yields a Bessel type running Newton coupling with $G_I(r)\sim r$ in the UV, rather than the usual $G(r)\sim r^3$ behavior, and supports an integrable central singularity. The inner branch is matched through the Israel Darmois conditions to an exterior RG solution with $G_{II}\to G_0$ in the infrared, where $G_0$ is the Newton constant. Within the numerical range investigated, the resulting geometry has a single event horizon and no additional inner horizon. We then incorporate an NFW dark matter distribution coupled to the same running coupling. Since $\rho_{\rm NFW}\sim r^{-1}$ while $G_I(r)\sim r$ near the origin, the effective matter contribution entering the field equations remains finite, producing a cored effective behavior without modifying the NFW profile by hand. The gravitational decoupling algorithm keeps the quantum gravitational and dark matter sectors separately conserved within the same geometry. For the configurations considered, the NFW sector shifts the event horizon while preserving the single horizon structure. Thus, the same running coupling governs both the integrable UV black hole geometry and the softening of the central gravitational response of the NFW source.

</details>


### [35] [Einstein Reconstruction Scheme: Metric Reconstruction by Direct Inversion of the Einstein Operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15853)
*Kelvin Ka-Ho Lam,Dongjun Li,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: A new metric reconstruction scheme directly solves linearized Einstein equations for noncompact sources using a traceful radiation gauge and four transport equations, with the remaining six equations satisfied via uniquely determined homogeneous data—thereby fully closing the system and bridging Newman-Penrose and Hertz-potential formalisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome ambiguities in prior Newman-Penrose-based reconstruction methods—specifically, the undetermined homogeneous data—and to provide a complete, gauge-fixed, and physically grounded metric reconstruction for noncompact gravitational-wave sources.

Method: Formulates metric reconstruction in a traceful radiation gauge; hierarchically solves four first-order Einstein transport equations; fixes the remaining six Einstein equations by uniquely determining homogeneous initial/boundary data consistent with the full constraint and evolution structure of linearized gravity.

Result: A closed, well-posed reconstruction scheme that unambiguously determines the metric perturbation for noncompact sources without residual gauge freedom or undetermined data; establishes explicit equivalence between Newman-Penrose and Hertz-potential approaches.

Conclusion: The proposed scheme fully closes the linearized Einstein system for noncompact sources, resolves longstanding ambiguities in homogeneous data specification, and unifies major metric reconstruction paradigms in gravitational-wave physics.

Abstract: We propose a metric reconstruction scheme that computes the metric directly from the linearized Einstein equations for noncompact sources. Working in a traceful radiation gauge, we hierarchically solve four Einstein transport equations, with the remaining six enforced by suitable homogeneous data. Unlike previous Newman-Penrose approaches, we show how the homogeneous data are fixed, thereby closing the full set of Einstein equations. We conclude by relating our scheme to existing sourced reconstruction methods, thus establishing a connection between the Newman-Penrose and Hertz-potential approaches.

</details>


### [36] [Cubic Curvature Deformations to the Kerr Black Hole Shadow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15876)
*Alireza Allahyari,Shinji Mukohyama,Yi Pang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how cubic curvature corrections in effective field theory gravity alter the shadows of rotating black holes, using numerical ray-tracing of null geodesics on a perturbed Kerr metric; it finds spin- and coupling-dependent deviations in shadow size and shape, validating results against convergence tests and the Kerr limit.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We investigate the effects of cubic curvature corrections on the shadows of rotating black holes within the effective field theory of gravity. Starting from a perturbatively corrected Kerr metric incorporating two independent cubic curvature couplings, we construct black hole shadows through numerical integration of null geodesics, without assuming separability of the geodesic equations. Using a Hamiltonian ray-tracing approach, we systematically examine the dependence of the shadow geometry on the higher-curvature couplings, black hole spin, and observer inclination. We find that cubic curvature interactions can produce discernible deviations from the Kerr shadow, modifying its apparent size and shape. In particular, the two cubic interactions exhibit distinct effects on the shadow boundary, with the magnitude of the deviations depending sensitively on the coupling strengths and black hole spin. We validate our numerical results through independent convergence tests of the geodesic integration and shadow-boundary determination, and recover the analytical Kerr shadow in the vanishing-coupling limit. Our results provide a complementary probe of higher-curvature gravity in the strong-field regime and illustrate the potential of black hole shadow observations to constrain departures from general relativity.

Abstract: The non-Hermitian nature of the eigenvalue problem underlying black hole perturbations can induce the presence of exceptional points (EPs) in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum. EPs are points in parameter space where QNMs become degenerate. Near an EP, QNMs are resonantly excited, leaving imprints on the ringdown signal. In this work, we investigate the exceptional structure of scalar perturbations of the Kerr-Sen black hole, a rotating, charged solution of the low-energy limit of heterotic string theory, and compare it with the EP structure of the Kerr-Newman black hole in General Relativity. Starting from the EP associated with a massive scalar field in the Kerr limit, we trace exceptional lines in the parameter space spanned by the black hole spin, charge, and field mass. We locate these degeneracies directly by imposing double-root conditions on the QNM secular equation, thus avoiding the need for parameter space scans. We also investigate the hysteresis phenomenon, showing that encircling an EP in parameter space leads to an exchange of the associated QNMs.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Separation of variables applied to the neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger equation on Kerr geometry; identification of two commuting matrix angular operators and a physical first integral; reduction to a second-order radial ODE; reversible reconstruction of the full field from separated solutions.

Abstract: We separate the neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger equation on a Kerr background at finite rotation. Two commuting matrix angular operators and a physical first integral yield a second-order radial equation and reversible field reconstruction. The resulting equations give quasibound spectra, greybody factors, and helicity-resolved Hawking emission. For a matched weakly bound level, the leading rotational splitting has the opposite sign to that of a Dirac field. We derive its coefficient and identify its angular momentum origin.

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize null-coordinate formulations (e.g., Eddington-Finkelstein) to nonstationary rotating black holes where standard time coordinates fail to be null due to nonzero spin and time-varying mass, enabling well-behaved horizon descriptions and radiation modeling.

Result: Two independent Carter-type angular correction functions are required for outgoing/ingoing null congruences; the null one-forms are non-closed under mass variation, yielding two distinct closure equations; numerical solutions yield the Kerr-Vaidya metric in double-null form; standard null coordinates exhibit logarithmic divergence and vanishing determinant at the horizon; a regular Kruskal-type extension with time-dependent surface gravity is constructed.

Conclusion: The Kerr-Vaidya spacetime admits a well-defined double-null structure only when accounting for spin- and mass-variation–dependent angular corrections; the resulting Kruskal extension resolves horizon singularities and provides a robust framework for studying dynamical horizons and black-hole radiation in rotating, accreting systems.

Abstract: We construct a pair of null coordinates for the Kerr-Vaidya spacetime, a nonstationary rotating black-hole geometry whose mass varies with the retarded or advanced time. The metric is naturally written in Eddington-Finkelstein-type coordinates, but the corresponding time coordinate fails to be null when the spin is nonzero, so each null coordinate must acquire a correction of the Carter type that depends on the polar angle. We show that the outgoing and ingoing null congruences require two independent Carter-type functions. The canonical null one-forms obtained from the eikonal equation are not closed when the mass varies, and their closure conditions are different equations. For constant mass both closure equations reduce to the equation found by Argañaraz and Moreschi. We derive the two master equations together with the two integrating-factor equations, solve both sectors numerically, and obtain the Kerr-Vaidya metric in double-null form. We also show that the unextended null coordinates are singular at the horizon, where the advanced coordinate diverges logarithmically and the metric determinant vanishes. We construct the corresponding Kruskal-type extension with a time-dependent surface gravity and show that it is regular at the horizon.

</details>

Abstract: When two black holes merge, a common horizon appears around them as a single surface, which immediately splits into an outer and an inner one. Three numerical simulations, including an eccentric, precessing one, confirm the exponent to half a percent, and the reconstructed formation surface is marginally stable, as predicted.

### [42] [Exceptional point excitation by a plunging particle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16390)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We investigate the excitation of a second-order exceptional point (EP) in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum by a plunging particle. Starting from a gravitational action, we derive the master equation with a Gaussian bump modified potential. For the odd-parity mode $(\ell,m)=(2,1)$, we compute the waveform numerically and derive an analytic expression for the EP contribution to the ringdown waveform directly from the particle source. Comparison with the numerical waveform shows that the particle excites the EP response, which, together with its mirror contribution, dominates the intermediate ringdown stage. We use waveform fitting and Bayesian analysis to assess the limitations of conventional QNM templates for EP ringdown. In the fitting, the EP template recovers the complex frequency $\omega$ more accurately than the ordinary template. In Bayesian analysis, at a signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of $\rho=100$, the ordinary template yields a narrow frequency posterior whose $90\%$ credible region excludes the true EP frequency, whereas the broader EP-template region contains it. The Bayes factor nevertheless favors the ordinary template at lower SNRs and the EP template only at higher SNRs. ### [43] [Tricritical Dynamics Reveal Black Hole Interior Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16564)
*Fu-Guo Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs interior Kasner exponents of scalarized black holes using exterior radial perturbation growth rates, linking exterior dynamics to interior geometry near a tricritical point on the Reissner–Nordström–scalarized coexistence line.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge exterior observable dynamics (e.g., perturbation growth) with interior geometric properties (e.g., Kasner exponents) in scalarized black-hole spacetimes, especially near criticality where standard integration becomes numerically challenging.

Method: Analyzes linear radial perturbations on asymptotically flat and AdS black-hole backgrounds; relates the perturbation growth rate Ω_u to entropy-potential curvature and horizon scalar amplitude; uses static source expansion and zero-mode matching to fix constant C; eliminates amplitude to derive angular Kasner exponent p ∝ √(Ω_u/C); validates against inward numerical integration.

Result: Derives analytical relation p ∼ 2√(Ω_u/C) connecting exterior growth rate Ω_u to interior angular Kasner exponent p; confirms agreement within ~0.5% between reconstructed and independently integrated p near tricriticality.

Conclusion: Exterior linear perturbation data suffices to accurately reconstruct interior Kasner geometry in scalarized black holes near tricriticality, establishing a robust exterior–interior correspondence rooted in thermodynamic curvature and critical scaling.

Abstract: We reconstruct the interior Kasner exponents of a scalarized black-hole equilibrium from the growth rate of a radial perturbation outside the horizon. The equal-entropy coexistence line of Reissner--Nordström and scalarized phases ends at a tricritical point. We follow the intermediate equilibrium separating the two phases along the coexistence line. The radial growth rate $\Omega_u$ is proportional to the magnitude of the entropy-potential curvature. As the tricritical point is approached, the curvature vanishes as the fourth power of the horizon scalar amplitude. The interior logarithmic scalar gradient grows inversely with the horizon amplitude. Eliminating the amplitude connects exterior dynamics to interior geometry, giving the angular Kasner exponent $p\sim2\sqrt{\Omega_u/C}$. The static source expansion and the critical Reissner--Nordström zero mode fix $C$ before the scalarized interior is integrated. We compute the growth rate from linear radial perturbations on asymptotically flat and anti-de Sitter black-hole backgrounds. At the sampled equilibria closest to tricriticality, the reconstructed angular exponent agrees with the result of independent inward integration at about the $0.5\%$ level.

</details>


### [44] [Relational Cosmology: Covariant and Canonical Dressings, and the Hamiltonian of Internal Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16864)
*Francesco Sartini*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes relational dressings (covariant vs. canonical) and gauge-fixing in flat FLRW minisuperspace with a massless scalar, clarifying how clock choice breaks relational-time reparametrization covariance, enables a nontrivial relational Hamiltonian, and preserves dynamical equivalence under specific conditions—while highlighting open questions about quantum equivalence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual ambiguities between covariant and canonical relational approaches in constrained Hamiltonian systems—particularly how clock choice affects covariance, symmetry, and the emergence of a physical Hamiltonian—and to clarify conditions for dynamical equivalence between dressed and undressed formulations.

Method: Analytical comparison of covariant and canonical relational dressings with gauge-fixing reductions in the flat FLRW minisuperspace model; explicit construction of dressing maps, analysis of covariance vs. symmetry, derivation of relational Hamiltonians via Legendre map and relational evaluation, and examination of variational principles and phase-space structures.

Result: Clock fixing converts frame fields into background structures, breaking relational-time reparametrization covariance and enabling a nonvanishing relational Hamiltonian distinct from the vanishing Hamiltonian constraint; dressed equations reproduce original constrained dynamics iff the dressing satisfies consistency conditions; the relational generator defines a Hamiltonian flow even without time-translation symmetry; canonical complete observables and reduced Hamiltonians are recovered per clock frame via Legendre map and relational evaluation.

Conclusion: Covariant and canonical relational descriptions are classically compatible but differ in variational principles and intermediate phase spaces; their quantum equivalence remains unresolved.

Abstract: We compare covariant and canonical relational dressings with gauge-fixing reductions for the flat FLRW minisuperspace with a homogeneous massless scalar. We distinguish covariance of the dressing map from symmetries of the relational theory: fixing a clock makes its frame fields background structures, so its reorientations act as vector fields on relational field space rather than as spacetime diffeomorphisms. This loss of relational-time reparametrization covariance permits a nontrivial relational Hamiltonian, distinct from the undressed and dressed Hamiltonian constraints, which vanish on the constraint surface. We identify the conditions under which the dressed equations reproduce the original constrained dynamics. The relational generator defines a Hamiltonian flow even when relational-time translations are not symmetries of the dressed action. In each clock frame, it corresponds to the canonical complete observable and reduced Hamiltonian on the appropriate solution space, through the Legendre map and relational evaluation. The classical descriptions are compatible but retain distinct variational principles and intermediate phase spaces; their quantum equivalence remains an open question.

</details>


### [45] [Lurkers at the Threshold: GWTC-4.0's near-threshold events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16985)
*Daniel Williams,Michael Williams*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper performs a systematic parameter-estimation analysis on 42 marginal gravitational-wave candidates from GWTC-4.0 (p_astro ≥ 0.5 but FAR ≥ 1 yr⁻¹), using public data and software; 29 pass validation, revealing several astrophysically interesting events—including a possible NSBH merger and two with non-zero effective spin—while quantifying weak spin constraints via KL divergence and emphasizing robust validation for marginal detections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether scientifically valuable parameter estimates can be obtained for gravitational-wave candidates excluded from the official GWTC-4.0 catalogue due to marginal significance (FAR ≥ 1 yr⁻¹), despite having non-negligible astrophysical origin probability (p_astro ≥ 0.5).

Method: Applied a catalogue-style Bayesian parameter estimation pipeline using publicly available LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA strain data and open-source software (e.g., bilby, pesummary); implemented automated posterior validation (e.g., convergence diagnostics, prior-posterior consistency, noise subtraction checks); classified candidates based on validation outcomes and physical interpretability.

Result: 29/42 candidates passed validation; 7 were noise-like or artefact-contaminated; 6 yielded uninterpretable posteriors; identified GW230920_064709 (possible NSBH), GW230902_172430 and GW230823_142524 (non-zero χ_eff), with the latter showing mild precession evidence; KL divergence quantified weak spin constraints; full reproducible workflow and compatible posterior data released.

Conclusion: Marginal-significance GW candidates can yield meaningful astrophysical inferences when analyzed with rigorous, publicly accessible methods—but require stringent, automated validation to avoid spurious claims; this work establishes a blueprint for community-driven follow-up of sub-threshold events.

Abstract: We present the first systematic parameter-estimation analysis of 42 candidate gravitational-wave signals identified in GWTC-4.0 with a probability of astrophysical origin $p_\mathrm{astro} \geq 0.5$, but a false-alarm rate of at least $1\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$, which were therefore not selected for parameter estimation in the catalogue. We use a catalogue-style analysis workflow and publicly available strain data to obtain posterior samples for these candidates, of which 29 pass our validation criteria. Of the remaining 13 candidates, seven are consistent with noise or impacted by instrumental artefacts, while six yield results that cannot be reliably interpreted. We identify several interesting candidates: a possible neutron star-black hole merger, GW230920_064709, and two candidates with evidence for non-zero effective spin: GW230902_172430 and GW230823_142524. The latter also shows mild evidence of precession, although further analysis is needed to establish the robustness of this result. We quantify the information gained from these observations using the Kullback-Leibler divergence, finding that the inferred spin parameters are generally only weakly constrained. We present a reproducible analysis workflow that uses publicly available data and software, without access to LVK collaboration resources, and incorporates automated posterior-validation tools. We also provide a data release containing results in a format compatible with those from GWTC-4.0. Our results demonstrate that useful parameter constraints can be obtained for many candidates below the catalogue's parameter-estimation significance threshold, while highlighting the challenges of analysing marginal signals and the importance of careful validation.

</details>


### [46] [Spin to win: enhancing the quantum Otto cycle with the circular Unruh effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17027)
*Leo J. A. Parry,Dimitris Moustos,Benito A. Juárez-Aubry,Obinna Abah,Jorma Louko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a quantum Otto cycle using a qubit Unruh-DeWitt detector in uniform circular motion in Minkowski spacetime, interacting with a thermal scalar field; it derives motion-induced effective temperatures, shows violations of standard Carnot bounds (while respecting generalized ones), and demonstrates enhanced thermodynamic performance relative to inertial cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how relativistic motion—specifically uniform circular motion—affects quantum thermodynamic cycles, particularly whether non-inertial effects can enable超越 of classical thermodynamic bounds via motion-induced effective temperatures.

Method: Computes the transition rate of a uniformly rotating Unruh-DeWitt qubit coupled to a massless scalar field in a thermal state; derives an energy-gap-dependent effective temperature from detailed balance; analyzes work, heat, efficiency, and coefficient of performance (COP) for the quantum Otto cycle under isochoric and adiabatic strokes.

Result: The detector exhibits an effective temperature that deviates from the bath’s rest-frame temperature—higher for small qubit gaps, lower at high bath temperatures; engine efficiency and refrigerator COP exceed standard Carnot bounds (based on bath temperature) but obey generalized Carnot bounds defined by detector-effective temperatures; circular motion enables Carnot violation at lower speeds than inertial motion in the high-temperature limit.

Conclusion: Relativistic motion induces detector-specific thermodynamic parameters that redefine operational bounds; quantum thermal machines employing accelerated detectors can outperform inertial counterparts, demonstrating the necessity of frame-dependent effective temperatures in relativistic quantum thermodynamics.

Abstract: We study a quantum Otto cycle whose working medium is a qubit Unruh-DeWitt detector undergoing uniform circular motion in $(3+1)$-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, interacting with a massless scalar field in a thermal state during the isochoric strokes. We compute the detector's transition rate and determine its energy gap-dependent effective temperature, showing that the effective temperature can be higher than the ambient bath temperature in the small energy gap limit and lower in the high bath temperature limit. We then investigate how these motion-induced effective temperatures affect the operating conditions and performance of the quantum Otto cycle as a heat engine or a refrigerator. We show that the engine efficiency and refrigerator performance coefficient can exceed their standard Carnot bounds based on the bath's rest-frame temperatures, while remaining bounded by generalised Carnot limits expressed in terms of the effective temperatures experienced by the detector. In the high-temperature limit, the effective temperature coincides with that of an inertial detector moving through a thermal bath, and a comparison with the small-gap limit reveals that circular motion allows the standard Carnot bounds to be exceeded at lower speeds than in the inertial case.

</details>


### [47] [Rapid Multiparameter Gravitational-Wave Tests of General Relativity with Neural Posterior Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17038)
*Hong Su,Tao Yang,He Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: DINGO-TGR is a neural posterior estimation framework enabling fast, joint inference of binary-source parameters and six post-Newtonian (PN) phase deformations for multiparameter tests of general relativity; it uses importance sampling and PCA to overcome parameter correlations and computational bottlenecks, achieving ~40–60× speedup over conventional Bayesian sampling while validating GR consistency on real and synthetic events.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multiparameter gravitational-wave tests of GR are hindered by strong correlations among PN phase deformations and the high computational cost of traditional Bayesian inference.

Method: DINGO-TGR employs neural posterior estimation with IMRPhenomXPHM waveforms (including spin precession and higher-order modes), importance sampling for proposal reweighting, and PCA to identify dominant, well-constrained linear combinations of six PN deformations within the TIGER parametrization.

Result: Validated on 16 zero-noise GR and 12 beyond-GR injections; recovers GR-consistent posteriors and injected deformation shifts. Applied to GW150914 and GW250114: GW150914 shows a mild PCA-mode offset (p = 11.5%, not significant at 5% level); GW250114 is GR-consistent. Inference time reduced from ~177–238 hours to ~38 min–4.4 hours.

Conclusion: DINGO-TGR enables efficient, scalable, and statistically rigorous multiparameter GR tests, demonstrating feasibility of large-scale injection-based significance assessments using neural inference with importance sampling correction.

Abstract: Multiparameter gravitational-wave tests of general relativity (GR) are limited by strong correlations among post-Newtonian (PN) phase deformations and the computational cost of Bayesian inference. We present DINGO-TGR, a neural posterior estimation framework that jointly infers binary-source parameters and six PN phase deformations within the TIGER parametrization, using IMRPhenomXPHM waveforms with spin precession and higher-order modes. Importance sampling reweights the neural proposal samples, while principal component analysis (PCA) identifies two well-constrained combinations of PN deformations. We validate the framework using 16 zero-noise GR injections and 12 beyond-GR injections, recovering GR-consistent posteriors in the former and leading-mode shifts consistent with the injected deformations in the latter. For GW150914 and GW250114, our results agree with independent \texttt{bilby-tgr} analyses using \texttt{dynesty}. After training, inference including importance sampling takes approximately 38 minutes and 4.4 hours, respectively, compared with 177 and 238 hours for conventional sampling. GW150914 exhibits a mild offset from GR in the leading PCA mode, whereas GW250114 is consistent with GR. To assess the significance of the GW150914 offset, we analyze 1000 GW150914-like GR injections with independent Gaussian-noise realizations and fixed detector power spectral densities. The resulting empirical upper-tail $p$-value is $11.5\%$, indicating that the observed offset does not reject the adopted GR null model at the 5\% significance level. Our results demonstrate the potential of importance-sampling-corrected neural inference for efficient multiparameter GR tests and large-scale injection-based significance assessments.

</details>


### [48] [Gauge aspects of teleparallel gravity and Noether's second theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17147)
*Sebastian Brezina,Martin Krššák*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the gauge structure of TEGR using Noether's second theorem, identifying diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations as its fundamental gauge symmetries; it clarifies their distinct roles, confirms the uniqueness and GR-consistency of matter coupling—including for spinors—and discusses implications for translational symmetry in gauge-theoretic gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the gauge-theoretic foundations of TEGR by rigorously identifying its true gauge symmetries via Noether's second theorem, resolving ambiguities about the role of translations and matter coupling.

Method: Application of Noether's second theorem to the TEGR action to derive Noether identities and classify variational symmetries; analysis of the geometric and algebraic roles of diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations; examination of matter coupling consistency, especially for spinor fields.

Result: Diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations are the only gauge symmetries of TEGR; translations alone do not generate independent gauge identities; matter coupling—including minimal coupling for spinors—is uniquely fixed and matches that of general relativity.

Conclusion: TEGR is a genuine gauge theory with gauge group ISO(1,3) broken to O(1,3), where local Lorentz symmetry is essential and physical; its gauge structure is more subtle than often assumed, and its equivalence to GR extends fully to matter interactions.

Abstract: We examine the gauge structure of the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity (TEGR) from the perspective of Noether's second theorem. We show that diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations are the gauge symmetries of TEGR, as these are the only variational symmetries that give rise to Noether identities. We then discuss the distinct roles of these two symmetries and their implications for the gauge-theoretic interpretation of gravity. Finally, we discuss consequences of these observations for the role of translations in gauge theories of gravity and the coupling of matter in TEGR. In particular, we demonstrate that the matter coupling prescription is uniquely determined and coincides with that of general relativity, including in the case of spinor fields.

</details>


### [49] [Effects of Lensing by Sgr A* on Semicoherent Searches for Continuous Gravitational Waves. I. Signal Model and Mismatch Studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17153)
*Aditya Kumar Sharma,Alicia Calafat,Rodrigo Tenorio,David Keitel,Alicia M. Sintes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how gravitational lensing by Sgr A* affects the detection of continuous gravitational waves (CWs) from distant neutron stars, showing that lensing-induced interference and time-varying modulations degrade standard semicoherent search performance—especially for weak signals—and that sensitivity re-interpretation requires full lensed waveform injections, not simple magnification rescaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether standard CW searches (e.g., Hough-based semicoherent methods) remain effective for CWs lensed by Sgr A*, given that lensing induces time-evolving amplitude/phase modulations due to source motion and image interference—features absent in unlensed templates.

Method: The authors simulate lensed and unlensed CW signals injected into Gaussian noise, compute a semicoherent mismatch metric to quantify template mismatch under lensing, and perform a population study sampling astrophysically motivated distributions of transverse velocity and source–lens distance.

Result: Lensing enhances detectability for weak signals (broad negative mismatch distributions), but degrades it for loud signals in some configurations; smaller impact parameters improve recovery via magnification, while higher transverse velocities worsen it via faster modulation—especially at small impact parameters and short source–lens distances where interference dominates; population-averaged mismatches depend strongly on signal strength and are modestly sensitive to velocity/distance distributions.

Abstract: Continuous gravitational waves (CWs) from neutron stars beyond the Galactic Center can be lensed by the supermassive black hole in Sgr A$^*$. Since the signal lasts far longer than the lensing time delay, the two geometric-optics images overlap and interfere. Transverse source motion during the observation makes their magnifications and relative time delay evolve, producing evolving amplitude and phase modulations absent from standard CW templates. Using lensed and unlensed injections in Gaussian noise, we quantify their effect on a Hough-based semicoherent search through a semicoherent mismatch. Recovery is governed by the competition between lensing amplification and waveform modulation. Smaller impact parameters generally improve recovery through stronger magnification, while higher transverse velocities partially offset this gain through faster modulation, especially at small impact parameters and short source-lens distances, where image interference is appreciable. The response also depends on the intrinsic signal strength. Loud signals exhibit small fractional changes, with net losses in some configurations, whereas weaker signals yield broader mismatch distributions extending to more negative values, indicating greater enhancement. A population study, sampling lensing parameters from astrophysically motivated velocity and source-lens distance distributions, reproduces the strong dependence on signal strength. Differences between populations are modest, but high-velocity, short source-lens distance populations tend towards larger mismatches. Lensing effects therefore cannot be accounted for by rescaling search sensitivity with the magnification alone; reinterpreting directed Galactic Center search sensitivities and upper limits under the lensing hypothesis requires injection studies with the full lensed waveform across the lensing parameter space.

</details>

### [50] [Poles Are Not Enough: Zeros of Dyson-Schwinger Gluon Propagators in Yang-Mills Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16392)
*Marco Frasca,Anish Ghoshal,Stefan Groote*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a background-covariant thermal partition function for gluons using a normalized two-point function with multiple poles and interlaced zeros; inclusion of zero determinants—beyond simple pole fields—is essential to recover correct UV degrees of freedom (two polarizations per adjoint eigenvalue), and significantly affects thermodynamic observables like the critical temperature ratio $T_c/M_g$ and latent heat $L$, with quantitative shifts reported up to six poles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in reconstructing the source-free thermal partition function from a normalized two-point function with poles, and to correctly encode ultraviolet gluon degrees of freedom—specifically two transverse polarizations per adjoint mode—in a gauge-invariant, background-covariant framework.

Method: Constructs a Gaussian functional integral over the gluon field using a rational propagator with alternating poles and zeros; incorporates determinant contributions from both poles and interlaced zeros; applies the formalism to an elliptic Dyson-Schwinger gluon kernel in SU(3) Yang-Mills theory; computes thermodynamic quantities (coexistence temperature $T_c$, latent heat $L$, free-energy difference at fixed holonomy) under varying pole number $P$, comparing 'poles-only' vs. 'poles+zeros' prescriptions.

Result: A single-pole approximation already captures the first-order SU(3) deconfinement transition; the six-pole result changes $T_c/M_g$ by only +1.48% relative to the one-pole case; zero determinants increase $T_c/M_g$ by +2.31% and decrease $L/T_c^4$ by −12.4%; dimensional latent heat drops by −4.0% at fixed $M_g$; the fixed-holonomy free-energy difference asymptotically matches the two-polarization Weiss result only when zeros are included, whereas the poles-only prescription overestimates it by a factor of $(3P-1)/2$.

Conclusion: Interlaced zeros in the rational gluon propagator are indispensable for physical consistency: they restore correct UV polarization counting and quantitatively refine thermodynamics of the deconfinement transition—demonstrating that pole-only approximations are qualitatively and quantitatively inadequate.

Abstract: A normalized two-point function with several poles does not, on its own, fix the source-free thermal partition function. For the Gaussian measure of the original gluon field, a positive-residue rational propagator contributes determinants from its poles and from zeros interlaced between them. The zero factors distinguish this completion from a gas of independent pole fields and restore two ultraviolet polarizations per adjoint eigenvalue at every finite truncation. We apply the construction to a background-covariant completion of the elliptic Dyson-Schwinger gluon kernel. The first-order SU(3) transition is already present with a single pole, and the saturated six-pole result shifts $T_c/M_g$ by only $1.48\%$, where $T_c$ is the coexistence temperature and $M_g$ is the lowest pole mass. At six poles, the zero determinants raise $T_c/M_g$ by $2.31\%$ and lower $L/T_c^4$ by $12.4\%$, where $L$ is the latent heat and each prescription uses its own $T_c$. At fixed $M_g$ the dimensional latent heat decreases by $4.0\%$. The fixed-holonomy free-energy difference gives an ultraviolet test independent of the additive pressure normalization. At fixed pole number $P$, its asymptotic value agrees with the two-polarization Weiss result when the zeros are retained and is $(3P-1)/2$ times as large in the poles-only prescription.

</details>


### [51] [SuSy and CoDy Radiation from Evaporating Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14563)
*Giacomo Cacciapaglia,Francesco Sannino,Paola Simone,Vania Vellucci*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how physics beyond the Standard Model (e.g., supersymmetry, composite dynamics) and modified black hole geometries (e.g., polymerized, Hayward, Simpson–Visser) jointly affect photon and neutrino spectra from evaporating black holes, revealing distinctive, observable spectral signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and probe new particle physics (e.g., TeV-scale SUSY, composite dynamics) and quantum-gravity-motivated spacetime modifications via Hawking radiation spectra from primordial black holes.

Method: Implementation of three supersymmetric benchmarks and one composite dynamics scenario in the BlackHawk black hole evaporation code; comparison across Schwarzschild and four regular black hole geometries (polymerized, Hayward, Simpson–Visser); spectral analysis of instantaneous photon and neutrino emission, including radiative decays and multi-step cascades.

Result: New physics yields narrow (radiative) or broad (cascade) spectral features in photons/neutrinos; compressed spectra show no features; geometry–physics degeneracies arise in specific energy bands; enhancements extend into detectable ranges for current/future observatories (e.g., IceCube, CTA, SWGO).

Conclusion: Photon and neutrino spectra from evaporating black holes serve as complementary, high-sensitivity probes of both TeV-scale particle physics and quantum-spacetime geometry—enabling joint constraints inaccessible to colliders or gravitational-wave experiments alone.

Abstract: We investigate the interplay between physics beyond the Standard Model and modified spacetime geometries in shaping the photon and neutrino spectra from evaporating black holes. We implement three representative supersymmetric (SuSy) benchmarks and a composite dynamics (CoDy) scenario in BlackHawk. We consider both Schwarzschild black holes and regular black holes geometries, namely polymerized, Hayward, and Simpson--Visser black holes. New degrees of freedom at the TeV scale become accessible at Hawking temperatures corresponding to black hole masses of approximately $M_{\rm BH}\sim 10^{10}\,\mathrm{g}$. We show that physics beyond the Standard Model produces distinctive signatures in both the instantaneous secondary-photon and neutrino spectra. Radiative decays generate narrow spectral features with approximately temperature-independent relative amplitudes, while multistep decay cascades produce broad enhancements that become increasingly pronounced at higher temperatures. In contrast, compressed mass spectra exhibit no localized features. Crucially, we show that the effects of new particle physics and modified spacetime geometries can become intertwined in specific energy regimes, altering the resulting spectral signatures. Our results demonstrate that photon and neutrino spectra provide complementary probes of new particle physics and black hole geometry, with predicted spectral enhancements extending into the energy ranges accessible to current and future high-energy observatories.

</details>


### [52] [Strongly supercooled first-order phase transitions: monopole abundance, entropy production and homogeneity consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14593)
*Chen Zhang,Yue Niu,Xin Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines how strong supercooling in a first-order phase transition of a unified gauge theory can suppress monopole production in the early universe, using a Gaussian nucleation model and radiation-like energy transfer. It introduces three physically distinct but mathematically equivalent criteria for phase-transition completion (CMC, PVC, ATC), shows monopole abundance can be arbitrarily suppressed near the completion boundary, but argues that such extreme suppression violates FLRW homogeneity due to large Hubble-scale false-vacuum fluctuations—limiting viable parameter space and impacting gravitational wave predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological monopole problem in grand unified theories by exploring whether strong supercooling—while still allowing successful phase transition completion—can sufficiently suppress topological defect (monopole) formation beyond standard Kibble mechanism estimates.

Method: Analytical modeling of bubble nucleation via a Gaussian rate approximation; distinction and comparison of three completion criteria (comoving measure, physical volume, asymptotic thermodynamic); consistency analysis of FLRW homogeneity using Hubble-scale root-mean-square fluctuations of the false-vacuum fraction; entropy and energy budget tracking under radiation-dominated cosmological evolution.

Result: The three completion criteria coincide exactly on a common boundary in parameter space; monopole abundance scales inversely with proximity to that boundary and can be arbitrarily suppressed *mathematically*, but homogeneity breaks down (FLRW approximation fails) when false-vacuum fluctuations exceed O(1) at Hubble scale—imposing a physical lower bound on achievable suppression relative to Kibble estimate.

Conclusion: Strong supercooling cannot arbitrarily suppress monopoles without violating the foundational homogeneity assumption of standard cosmology; thus, there is a self-consistency–limited floor to monopole suppression, with direct consequences for predicted stochastic gravitational wave spectra from such transitions.

Abstract: We investigate whether monopole production in the early universe from a first-order phase transition in a unified gauge theory can be significantly suppressed if the phase transition is subject to strong supercooling but still completes successfully. This is facilitated by adopting a Gaussian nucleation model and assuming the false vacuum energy released is transferred to sectors which behave effectively as radiation in terms of cosmological evolution. We distinguish three criteria of phase-transition completion: the comoving measure completion (CMC) characterized by the eventual vanishing of the false-vacuum fraction (FVF), the physical volume completion (PVC) characterized by the eventual vanishing of the physical volume of false vacuum, and the asymptotic thermodynamic completion (ATC), which refers to the finiteness of total entropy production. Assuming an idealized Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) cosmology, it is shown that the three criteria share exactly the same completion boundary, away from which they are completely equivalent. It is further shown that monopole abundance can be arbitrarily suppressed for parameter points approaching the completion boundary from the successful side. Nevertheless, for parameter points too close to the completion boundary, justification of the self-consistency of a homogeneous FLRW cosmology is difficult. We propose the use of the Hubble-scale FVF root-mean-square fluctuation to diagnose the homogeneity consistency issue, and find that arbitrarily strong suppression of monopole abundance relative to a reasonable Kibble estimate is not justifiable in terms of homogeneity consistency. These findings also have implications for gravitational wave production in strongly supercooled phase transitions.

</details>


### [53] [Cosmological Magnetic Fluctuations: Positivity Bounds and QCD Contributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14595)
*Ahmadullah Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes model-independent bounds on thermal cosmological magnetic fluctuations using spectral moments and the fluctuation-dissipation relation, and computes a leading-order hadronic QCD contribution—dominated by the ρ-resonance—to such fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain cosmological magnetic fluctuations from thermal sources in a model-independent way, while quantifying a concrete physical contribution (hadronic QCD) to assess the relevance and tightness of those bounds.

Method: 1) Derives rigorous upper and lower bounds on the equilibrium magnetic power spectrum using spectral positivity, convexity/concavity of thermal kernels, and the fluctuation–dissipation relation—relying only on two spectral moments; 2) Computes the leading electromagnetic-order hadronic contribution to magnetic fluctuations via QCD, focusing on the dominant ρ-resonance in the timelike region; benchmarks bounds against QCD, e⁺e⁻ plasma, and cold collisional plasma spectra.

Result: Tight, moment-based bounds on magnetic power spectra are obtained—applicable per current component and strengthened by known frequency thresholds; the ρ-resonance is identified as the dominant timelike hadronic contribution, with quantitative estimates provided; all test cases satisfy the derived bounds.

Conclusion: Model-independent spectral bounds provide robust constraints on thermal cosmological magnetic fluctuations; the hadronic QCD contribution—especially from the ρ resonance—is significant and physically relevant, validating the utility of the bounding framework.

Abstract: We study thermal cosmological magnetic fluctuations in two complementary ways. First, we bound the equilibrium magnetic power spectrum in terms of two spectral moments. The proof uses the fluctuation--dissipation relation, spectral positivity, and the convexity and concavity of the thermal kernels. The bounds do not require a model for the spectral shape. They also apply to individual current contributions, using their own moments, and a known frequency threshold strengthens the upper bound. Second, we calculate a hadronic QCD contribution to cosmological magnetic fluctuations at leading electromagnetic order. We estimate the \(\rho\)-resonance contribution, which dominates the timelike hadronic contribution in our comparison. Examples from QCD, an electron--positron plasma and a cold collisional plasma test the bounds. The spectra describe fluctuations at the chosen cosmological epoch.

</details>


### [54] [The NANOGrav 15-year data set: A Limit on Continuous Spin Gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14613)
*Chinmay Ambasht,Olivia Bitcon,Tanner Trickle,NANOGrav Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses pulsar timing array data (NANOGrav 15-year) to constrain the spin scale ρ_g of a continuous spin graviton, finding ρ_g < 1.23×10⁻²³ eV at 95% credibility, leveraging the enhanced sensitivity of low-frequency (nanohertz) gravitational wave probes to CSP effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continuous spin particles (CSPs), including a possible CSP graviton, reduce to standard massless particles in the ρ → 0 limit; thus, experimental bounds on the spin scale ρ are essential to test whether known particles like the graviton could fundamentally be CSPs.

Method: Bayesian inference applied to NANOGrav 15-year pulsar timing array data, exploiting the ρ/ω scaling of CSP physical effects (with ω ~ nanohertz) to enhance sensitivity to deviations from standard GR predictions induced by a continuous spin graviton.

Result: The graviton spin scale is constrained to ρ_g < 1.23 × 10⁻²³ eV at 95% credible level.

Conclusion: Pulsar timing arrays provide uniquely strong constraints on continuous spin gravitons due to their nanohertz-band sensitivity; the result disfavors large ρ_g values and supports consistency with standard helicity-2 gravitons, but leaves room for extremely small CSP deformations.

Abstract: Continuous spin particles (CSPs) are massless particles characterized by a dimensionful spin scale, $\rho$, which, when non-zero, changes their helicity under Lorentz boosts. Since CSPs reduce to ordinary particles when $\rho \rightarrow 0$, the photon or graviton could be CSPs and only appear to behave as ordinary particles due to a small value of $\rho$. Therefore, it is important to determine what value of $\rho$ is experimentally allowed. Recent work has shown that the physical effects of CSPs scale as powers of $\rho/\omega$ when $\rho \ll \omega$, where $\omega$ is a frequency scale, making low-frequency probes particularly beneficial for highlighting these phenomena. In this paper we use pulsar timing arrays to set constraints on the spin scale of the continuous spin graviton. Pulsar timing arrays are uniquely suited for this application as they have exceptional sensitivity to low, nanohertz frequency gravitational waves, as demonstrated by their recent evidence for a stochastic gravitational wave background. Using standard Bayesian data analysis techniques, we find that the NANOGrav 15-year data set constrains the graviton spin scale to $\rho_\text{g} < 1.23 \times 10^{-23} \, \text{eV} $ at the 95% credible level.

</details>


### [55] [$N_{\mathrm{eff}}$, Radiative Dirac Neutrinos, and Flavor Physics from Leptoquarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14616)
*Patricio Escalona,Guillermo Gambini,Vítor Pereira,C. A. de S. Pires,Farinaldo S. Queiroz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a two-scalar-leptoquark + three-RHN model that simultaneously explains B⁺→K⁺νν̄ anomaly (Belle II), generates radiative Dirac neutrino masses, satisfies μ→eγ and ΔN_eff constraints, and shows cosmological N_eff bounds can exceed collider limits on leptoquark mass (~12–14 TeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify explanations of flavor anomalies (especially B⁺→K⁺νν̄), rare meson decays, and Dirac neutrino mass generation within a consistent leptoquark framework while respecting LFV and cosmological constraints.

Method: Constructs a scalar leptoquark model with two mixed leptoquarks (S₁ and S₃) and three right-handed neutrinos; computes one-loop radiative Dirac neutrino mass, analyzes contributions to B⁺→K⁺νν̄ (via leptoquark exchange), derives constraints from μ→eγ (photonic penguin), and evaluates impact on effective relativistic degrees of freedom (ΔN_eff) via late-time decays of heavy neutrinos or leptoquarks.
