Result: The model fits the Belle II B⁺→K⁺νν̄ central value; viable parameter space requires leptoquark masses ~12–14 TeV — below which B-anomaly is overpredicted, above which SM-like prediction falls short of data; μ→eγ and ΔN_eff impose competitive/complementary lower bounds; N_eff constraints can be stricter than current LHC direct search limits.

Conclusion: A minimal mixed scalar leptoquark model with RH neutrinos successfully links neutrino mass origin, B-anomalies, and cosmology; highlights the role of precision cosmology (N_eff) as a probe of multi-TeV leptoquark scales beyond collider reach.

Abstract: Leptoquarks provide a common framework for addressing flavor anomalies, rare meson decays, and neutrino-mass generation. We study a model with two mixed scalar leptoquarks and three right-handed neutrinos that generates radiative Dirac neutrino masses and contributes to $B^+\to K^+\nu\bar\nu$, charged-lepton flavor violation, and the effective number of relativistic species. We show that the model can accommodate the Belle~II measurement of $B^+\to K^+\nu\bar\nu$ while satisfying the $\mu\to e\gamma$ bound and the cosmological constraint on $\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}}$. When both leptoquark masses exceed approximately $12$--$14\,\mathrm{TeV}$, the new contribution to the $B$-meson decay becomes too small, and the prediction approaches the SM value, which lies below the Belle~II measurement. Interestingly, the lower bounds on the leptoquark mass scale derived from $N_{\mathrm{eff}}$ can be stronger than those from direct collider searches.

</details>


### [56] [New Physics in Ultra-High-Energy Neutrino Flavor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14624)
*Samiur R. Mir,Carlos A. Argüelles,Vedran Brdar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how ultra-high-energy (UHE) neutrino flavor measurements—enabled by next-generation radio-detection experiments using Earth-skimming tau neutrinos—can probe Lorentz invariance violation (LIV) with unprecedented sensitivity, surpassing current bounds by up to 20 orders of magnitude for dimension-4+ LIV operators; three-flavor observables further enhance sensitivity by up to 10× over tau-only channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore physics beyond the Standard Model—particularly energy-dependent effects like Lorentz invariance violation—that become significant at ultra-high neutrino energies (>100 PeV), where existing flavor measurements (e.g., IceCube’s astrophysical neutrino data) lack sensitivity.

Method: The authors perform a theoretical sensitivity study of projected UHE neutrino flavor composition measurements (100 PeV–10 EeV), focusing on in-ice radio detection of Earth-skimming tau neutrinos and extending to full three-flavor observables; they adopt LIV as a benchmark model, computing its impact on neutrino oscillations and flavor ratios via energy-dependent modifications to the dispersion relation and Hamiltonian.

Result: UHE flavor ratio measurements will set the strongest constraints on dimension-4 and higher LIV operators—improving upon all current experimental and astrophysical bounds by up to 20 orders of magnitude; inclusion of three-flavor observables (beyond tau-only) yields an additional factor of ~10 improvement in sensitivity.

Conclusion: Ultra-high-energy neutrino flavor measurements represent a uniquely powerful and complementary probe of Planck-scale physics such as LIV, with next-generation radio arrays poised to open a new window into quantum gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: Neutrino flavor measurements test physics beyond the Standard Model that modifies neutrino production, propagation, or detection. IceCube has measured the flavor composition of the astrophysical neutrino flux up to PeV energies. Next-generation experiments will extend flavor measurements to the ultra-high-energy (UHE) regime, $100\,\mathrm{PeV}$-$10\,\mathrm{EeV}$, by combining in-ice radio detection with Earth-skimming tau neutrinos. We present the first study of the new-physics reach of these projected UHE flavor measurements. We take Lorentz invariance violation (LIV) as a benchmark, since its effects grow as a power of the neutrino energy. We find that UHE flavor ratios will be the most sensitive probe of LIV for operators of dimension four and higher, improving on current bounds from all sectors by up to twenty orders of magnitude. Three-flavor observables improve this sensitivity by up to an order of magnitude over Earth-skimming tau neutrinos alone.

</details>


### [57] [Quantum Computing Enhanced Search for Wavelike Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14629)
*Yunjia Bao,Yu Cheng,Liang Jiang,Lian-Tao Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a quantum computing-enhanced sensing protocol using Grover search to detect weak, stochastic wavelike dark matter signals—particularly axions—in the 10–100 µeV mass range; it mitigates QSP errors via phase stabilization through long integration and sign-alternating QSP angles, achieving projected sensitivity beyond current astrophysical limits and reaching the QCD axion band with 10⁶ entangled transmon qubits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The central challenge is detecting weak, stochastic wavelike dark matter (e.g., axions) embedded in broad frequency bands, where conventional sensing suffers from low signal-to-noise and quantum signal processing (QSP) errors due to dark matter field fluctuations and algorithmic approximations.

Method: The authors adapt the Grover search algorithm for quantum-enhanced dark matter sensing: (i) employ long integration time to stabilize accumulated sensing phase and suppress QSP errors from DM field fluctuations; (ii) alternate signs of QSP rotation angles between oracle calls to cancel coherent buildup of intrinsic QSP approximation errors; (iii) benchmark the protocol on a transmon-based quantum processor and extrapolate sensitivity scaling with qubit count.

Result: The combined strategy yields projected sensitivity surpassing existing astrophysical bounds for axion dark matter in the 10–100 µeV mass range; with 10⁶ entangled transmon qubits, sensitivity reaches the theoretically predicted QCD axion band; the protocol is platform-agnostic and adaptable to other wavelike DM detectors.

Conclusion: Quantum search algorithms—specifically Grover-enhanced sensing with error-suppressing dynamical controls—provide a viable, scalable pathway to probe unexplored wavelike dark matter parameter space, offering an accelerated discovery channel complementary to classical approaches.

Abstract: Identifying a weak, stochastic signal hidden within a broad frequency band is a central challenge in the search for wavelike dark matter. We explore quantum computing enhanced sensing based on the Grover search algorithm to address this problem and find that a long integration time stabilizes the accumulated sensing phase, reducing quantum signal processing (QSP) errors induced by dark matter fluctuations. We further propose alternating the signs of the QSP angles between oracle calls to suppress the coherent accumulation of the intrinsic QSP approximation errors during the search. Combining these strategies in a transmon-based benchmark, we obtain a projected sensitivity to axion dark matter beyond existing astrophysical limits. This opens access to substantial unexplored parameter space across the $10$-$100\,\mu\mathrm{eV}$ mass range and offers an accelerated discovery channel for wavelike dark matter. With $10^6$ entangled sensing qubits, the projected sensitivity reaches the QCD axion band. Although illustrated with transmons, the protocol can be adapted to other experimental platforms for wavelike dark matter detection.

</details>


### [58] [A Grover-Accelerated Search for Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14641)
*Zachary Bogorad,Roni Harnik*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using Grover's quantum search algorithm to accelerate wave-like dark matter detection by treating dark matter as a coherent quantum phase gate, enabling a √N speedup in frequency-bin searches despite finite coherence time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the sensitivity limitations of classical wave-like dark matter searches imposed by finite coherence time τ, and to explore whether quantum computational techniques can fundamentally enhance detection efficiency across many coherence times.

Method: The authors model dispersive coupling between wave-like dark matter and a quantum sensor, demonstrate that dark matter induces a coherent O(1) phase shift over integration times ≫ τ, formalize its role as a repeatable quantum phase gate, design a protocol to transfer and digitize this phase onto a qubit register prepared in a superposition of frequencies, and apply Grover’s algorithm to search over N_bins frequency bins.

Result: Grover’s algorithm achieves a provable √N_bins quantum speedup for dark matter frequency scanning; the scheme is theoretically viable with no fundamental quantum mechanical obstruction—even when integration time vastly exceeds the dark matter coherence time τ.

Conclusion: Wave-like dark matter can function as a coherent, repeatable quantum gate, enabling parametric quantum acceleration of its detection; while experimentally challenging, the approach establishes a new paradigm at the intersection of quantum sensing, quantum computation, and fundamental physics.

Abstract: We show that Grover's algorithm can be used to parametrically accelerate wave-like dark matter searches. Despite having a finite coherence time $\tau$, wavelike dark matter can imprint a coherent $O(1)$ phase onto a sensor through a dispersive coupling over an integration time which significantly exceeds $\tau$. As such, we show that dark matter can be used as a repeatable quantum phase gate in a quantum device. If the sensor is prepared in a superposition of frequencies, we show that this phase can be transferred to a qubit register and digitized. The qubits are thus available for quantum computational processing in which dark matter can take part as a coherent gate. We show that Grover's algorithm can be used to speed up a search for dark matter in $N_{\rm bins}$ frequency bins by a factor of $\sqrt{N_{\rm bins}}$. Although the technological challenges to implementing such a search remain substantial, this demonstrates that there is no fundamental obstruction to accelerating wave-like dark matter searches with quantum computational techniques over many coherence times.

</details>


### [59] [Earth-sized tests of long-range neutrino interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14642)
*Dyer F. Cardenas,Mauricio Bustamante*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper sets new 90% CL upper limits on flavor-dependent neutrino interactions mediated by ultralight $U(1)'$ bosons (masses $10^{-15}$–$10^{-9}$ eV), using exact path-integrated matter potentials for atmospheric neutrinos in IceCube DeepCore data—improving upon prior constant/local-density approximations and surpassing equivalence principle bounds below $10^{-11}$–$10^{-10}$ eV for eight symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on new long-range, flavor-discriminating forces acting on neutrinos—mediated by ultralight $U(1)'$ bosons—by accurately modeling the spatially varying matter potential experienced by atmospheric neutrinos traversing Earth, beyond previous approximations (constant or locally density-scaled potentials).

Method: Computed the exact flavor-dependent interaction potential at every point along atmospheric neutrino trajectories through Earth, using the IceCube Earth density model and production-height distribution; performed a likelihood analysis of the 8-year IceCube DeepCore public dataset with full systematic uncertainties; derived 90% CL upper limits on couplings for 14 $U(1)'$ symmetries, focusing on those distinguishing $\nu_\mu$ and $\nu_\tau$.

Result: New 90% CL upper limits on couplings for eight $U(1)'$ symmetries (those with $\nu_\mu$–$\nu_\tau$ potential differences) over mediator masses $10^{-15}$–$10^{-9}$ eV; limits are stronger than equivalence principle tests below $10^{-11}$–$10^{-10}$ eV (depending on symmetry); holding the potential fixed at detector value weakens limits by factors ~1–3.

Conclusion: Exact path-integrated potential calculation significantly improves sensitivity to ultralight flavor-dependent neutrino forces; IceCube DeepCore provides world-leading constraints in the sub-$10^{-10}$ eV mediator mass range for specific $U(1)'$ models, demonstrating the power of atmospheric neutrino oscillations as probes of new long-range interactions.

Abstract: Neutrinos may experience a new long-range force that distinguishes their flavors, mediated by an ultralight neutral boson of a new $U(1)'$ gauge symmetry and sourced by the electrons and nucleons of ordinary matter. Atmospheric neutrinos that cross the Earth would then feel a flavor-dependent potential that alters their oscillations. When the range of the force is about one Earth radius or less, this potential varies from point to point along each neutrino's path, since it comes mostly from the matter near the neutrino. Earlier limits approximated it, either as constant along the path or as following the local density of matter. We compute it exactly at every point of each path, for the Earth model of the IceCube analysis and over the distribution of heights at which the neutrinos are produced, and use it to analyze the 8-yr public IceCube DeepCore sample with the systematic uncertainties of that analysis. For mediator masses from $10^{-15}$ to $10^{-9}$ eV, where the Earth alone sources the potential, we set upper limits at 90% confidence level on the coupling of eight of 14 $U(1)'$ symmetries, those whose potential differs between $\nu_\mu$ and $\nu_\tau$. A potential held at its value at the detector would weaken our limits by factors of about 1-3. Below about $10^{-11}$-$10^{-10}$ eV, depending on the symmetry, our limits are stronger than those from tests of the equivalence principle.

</details>


### [60] [Lepton flavor violation at mass dimension eight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14669)
*Julian Heeck,Mikheil Sokhashvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies 'accidentally protected' dimension-8 lepton-flavor-violating operators in the SMEFT that can dominate over dimension-6 ones at tree level in certain UV completions, expanding the phenomenologically viable space for LFV searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the hierarchy assumption between dimension-6 and dimension-8 lepton-flavor-violating (LFV) operators in the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT), challenging the conventional expectation that d=6 operators always dominate d=8 ones.

Method: Systematic derivation of all tree-level UV completions (using heavy scalars and fermions) for LFV operators up to mass dimension 8; classification of completions by their leading LFV dimension and identification of those where d=8 operators arise at tree level while d=6 contributions are loop-suppressed or absent due to symmetry or coupling structure.

Result: Discovery of UV completions that exclusively generate d=8 LFV operators at tree level, with d=6 counterparts suppressed by loop factors (e.g., 1/(16π²)) and/or forbidden by unrelated Yukawa couplings — i.e., 'accidentally protected' d=8 scenarios.

Conclusion: Dimension-8 LFV operators are not generically subleading; certain well-motivated UV models predict them as leading signals, thereby broadening the scope of experimentally testable LFV phenomena beyond standard d=6 expectations.

Abstract: Operators that violate lepton flavor first arise at mass dimension $d=5$ in the Standard Model Effective Field Theory, while charged lepton flavor violation first appears at $d=6$, inducing the typical exotic particle decays currently probed by experiments. Operators at $d=8$ carry the same quantum numbers as those at $d=6$ and are thus seemingly doomed to be subleading corrections. After (re)deriving the complete set of tree-level UV completions for lepton-flavor-violating $d\leq 8$ operators via heavy scalars and fermions, we show that some UV completions exclusively generate $d=8$ flavor violation at tree level, with $d=6$ contributions suppressed by loop factors and, in some cases, unrelated couplings. Such accidentally protected $d=8$ scenarios enlarge the set of testable lepton-flavor-violating operators.

</details>


### [61] [Symmetry adapted tensor networks and quantum classical geometric learning for hadronic tomography: a proton construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14711)
*Dustin Keller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a calibrated, symmetry-resolved tensor-network model of the proton's GTMD $F_{1,1}$ (valence-$u$, zero skewness), integrating experimental, lattice, and phenomenological inputs via a unified light-front state; it reveals strong constraints on forward transverse response but significant freedom in the nonforward $(x, \Delta_\perp)$-dependence, and designs the framework for systematic extension to neutrons, deuterons, and higher-spin nuclei.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build a first-principles–informed, experimentally and lattice-calibrated representation of the proton's generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distribution $F_{1,1}$ that respects QCD symmetries (color singlet, exchange statistics, $J_z$, $I_z$) and operator conventions across diverse inputs, while enabling scalable generalization to other hadrons.

Method: Constructs a tree tensor network encoding a symmetry-resolved light-front wavefunction, incorporating soft-subtracted GTMD definition, one-loop nonsinglet matching, NLO collinear evolution, and nonperturbative joint closure; calibrates against ART25 transverse projections, HERA inclusive DIS, lattice matrix elements, elastic form factors, and static properties; uses classical and quantum geometric nodes for transverse response modeling; verifies quantum circuits as feature maps and state encodings.

Result: Forward inputs constrain transverse response by ~81% at $x=0.4$, yet the full $(x, \Delta_\perp)$-dependence remains underdetermined—e.g., a symmetry-allowed deformation shifts $F_{1,1}(x=0.4, \Delta_\perp \\sim 0.5\\,\\mathrm{GeV})$ from 0.588 to 0.378 GeV$^{-2}$; dense momentum-space surfaces and phase-space maps quantify this freedom; quantum circuit capacity and cost are benchmarked alongside classical components.

Conclusion: The tensor-network architecture provides a consistent, extensible, and symmetry-embedded framework for GTMD modeling—unifying disparate data under shared operator definitions—and establishes a practical pathway toward ab initio descriptions of GTMDs in neutrons, deuterons, and spin-3/2 nuclei where symmetry constraints proliferate.

Abstract: We construct a calibrated model of the proton generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distribution (GTMD) $F_{1,1}$ in the real, staple-even valence-$u$ sector at zero skewness. Experimental measurements, lattice matrix elements, and phenomenological distributions enter through a single shared light-front state, organized as a symmetry-resolved tree tensor network in which color-singlet structure, exchange statistics, and the additive $J_z$ and $I_z$ charges are built in. Classical and quantum geometric nodes represent the residual transverse response. The GTMD is defined with soft subtraction, one-loop nonsinglet matching, NLO collinear evolution, and an explicit nonperturbative joint closure, so that each input is used with its own operator convention and scale. Calibrating against correlated ART25 transverse projections, HERA inclusive data, and lattice, elastic, and static information gives a simple picture. The forward inputs narrow the attainable transverse response by about $81\%$ at $x=0.4$. The simultaneous dependence on quark momentum and momentum transfer, however, is untouched by any of them: a deformation that leaves every present input invariant moves the nonforward value from $0.588$ to $0.378\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$. Dense momentum-space surfaces and finite-transfer-window phase-space maps show the structure this freedom controls. The tensor-network organization is chosen with composition in mind. The same shared subunits, operator hierarchy, and qualification protocol extend to the neutron, the spin-1 deuteron, and spin-$3/2$ nuclei, where the number of symmetry and exchange constraints grows quickly and a structural representation becomes the practical route. The quantum circuits are verified feature maps and state encodings; their capacity and cost are measured alongside the classical blocks.

</details>


### [62] [Three-gluon vertex planar degeneracy: a perturbative explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14896)
*Daniel O. R. Azevedo,Nahuel Barrios,Marcela Peláez,Nicolás Wschebor*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explains the planar degeneracy of the three-gluon vertex in Yang-Mills theory perturbatively using the Curci-Ferrari model in Landau gauge, showing that tree-level dominance and flat one-loop corrections account for the degeneracy; symmetric approximation errors peak at ~3%, mainly near soft-gluon regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a perturbative explanation for the observed planar degeneracy of the three-gluon vertex — previously seen in lattice and Dyson-Schwinger studies — within a controlled, infrared-safe framework.

Method: Uses the Curci-Ferrari (CF) model in Landau gauge with an infrared-safe renormalization scheme; performs a one-loop calculation of the three-gluon vertex for arbitrary kinematics in D=3 and D=4.

Result: Tree-level form factor dominates due to suppression of perturbative corrections; one-loop corrections are remarkably flat across phase space; symmetric approximation error peaks at ~3% in both dimensions, largest near soft-gluon configurations due to tamed UV collinear divergences.

Conclusion: Planar degeneracy arises naturally from perturbative dynamics within the mass-extended Faddeev-Popov framework, validating its capacity to capture nontrivial infrared QCD behavior analytically.

Abstract: We provide a perturbative explanation for the planar degeneracy of the three-gluon vertex in Yang-Mills theory, using the Curci-Ferrari (CF) model in the Landau gauge. By employing an infrared safe renormalization scheme whose coupling remains regular at all scales, we perform a one-loop calculation for arbitrary kinematics in both three and four dimensions. Our results demonstrate that the observed planar degeneracy can be understood naturally due to perturbative reasons: i) the tree-level form factor dominates the vertex structure simply because perturbative corrections are suppressed; ii) the one loop corrections to this form factor remain remarkably flat across the allowed phase space. We quantify the deviations from the symmetric configuration, finding that the mean error of the symmetric approximation, in the case of the tree-level form factor, peaks at approximately 3% both in three and four dimensions, with the most significant departures occurring near soft-gluon configurations due to tamed ultraviolet collinear divergences. This work explains previous lattice and Dyson-Schwinger studies while reinforcing the capacity of the mass-extended Faddeev-Popov Lagrangian to capture complex infrared QCD dynamics through perturbative analytical techniques.

</details>


### [63] [Suppressed Direct Detection of Dark Matter from a Non-Invertible Selection Rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15030)
*Riasat Sheikh,Koji Tsumura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a non-invertible selection rule to explain suppression of dark matter–nucleon scattering cross-section, yielding a suppressed but predictable signal for direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct detection experiments have found no viable signal, suggesting the dark matter–nucleon scattering cross-section is suppressed; the paper seeks to explain the origin of this suppression.

Method: Introduces a mechanism based on a non-invertible selection rule and supports it with numerical analysis.

Result: Demonstrates that a suppressed yet theoretically predictable dark matter–nucleon scattering cross-section is achievable.

Conclusion: A non-invertible selection rule provides a viable theoretical origin for the observed suppression in direct detection experiments.

Abstract: Although the existence of dark matter is well established, its particle nature is still unknown. Direct detection experiments have been struggling to find any viable signal, implying that the dark matter--nucleon scattering cross-section must be suppressed. Here we propose a mechanism to explain the origin of this suppression by considering a non-invertible selection rule. Our numerical analysis shows that a suppressed yet predictable dark matter--nucleon scattering cross section is possible.

</details>


### [64] [Novel method for measuring the isovector partner of X(3872)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15158)
*Qiu-Lin Tan,Mao-Jun Yan,Chun-Sheng An,Cheng-Rong Deng,Xiao-Hai Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the triangle singularity (TS) mechanism in B⁰ → πK̄D⁰D*⁰ decays to enhance experimental sensitivity to the predicted isovector partner W_{c1} of X(3872), leveraging D_{s1}(2536)D̄* rescattering and coupled-channel D D̄* dynamics to generate distinctive invariant mass structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The predicted isovector partner W_{c1} of X(3872) is expected to produce only a weak structure near the charged D D̄* threshold, making its experimental identification difficult; hence, an enhanced probe mechanism is needed.

Method: The authors employ the triangle singularity mechanism in B⁰ → πK D̄⁰D*⁰ decays, modeling the D_{s1}(2536) D̄* triangle rescattering and incorporating coupled-channel D D̄* scattering amplitudes solved via the Lippmann-Schwinger equation.

Result: The interplay between triangle singularity and coupled-channel dynamics generates characteristic structures in the K D̄⁰D*⁰ invariant mass distributions; notably, the charged-channel contribution exhibits a distinct peak-like structure sensitive to the nearby W_{c1} pole.

Conclusion: The triangle singularity mechanism significantly enhances sensitivity to the otherwise weak W_{c1} signal, providing a viable and complementary strategy for its experimental search at Belle II and LHCb.

Abstract: The predicted isovector partner of the $X(3872)$, denoted as $W_{c1}$, is expected to produce only a relatively weak structure near the charged $D \bar{D}^*$ threshold, making its experimental identification challenging. In this work, we explore the triangle singularity (TS) mechanism as a means of enhancing the sensitivity to the $W_{c1}$ in the decays $B^0 \to \pi K \bar{D}^0 D^{*0}$. We investigate the $D_{s1}(2536) \bar{D}^*$ triangle rescattering mechanism, incorporating the coupled-channel $D \bar{D}^*$ scattering amplitudes obtained from the Lippmann-Schwinger equation. The interplay between the TS and the coupled-channel dynamics produces characteristic structures in the $K \bar{D}^0 D^{*0}$ invariant mass distributions associated with the $X(3872)$ and its isovector partner $W_{c1}$. In particular, the charged-channel contribution exhibits a distinct structure sensitive to the nearby $W_{c1}$ pole. Our results demonstrate that the TS mechanism provides a promising way to probe the otherwise weak manifestation of the $W_{c1}$, offering a complementary experimental strategy for searching for this predicted state at Belle II and LHCb.

</details>


### [65] [Rapidly Spinning Primordial Black Holes from Confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15169)
*Michael Zantedeschi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that confinement mechanisms—both electric (quark–antiquark) and magnetic dual (monopole–antimonopole)—in the early universe can produce rapidly spinning primordial black holes (PBHs) via relativistic capture; spin distribution peaks linearly toward extremality (mean dimensionless spin a ≈ 2/3), verified numerically, and missed encounters may yield secondary near-extremal PBHs and a testable stochastic gravitational-wave background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of highly spinning primordial black holes and identify a robust, model-independent observational signature—specifically their spin distribution—that can be probed by gravitational-wave astronomy.

Method: Analytical modeling of confinement-driven dynamics (flux-tube attraction, cosmological tides, string bending) for heavy particle pairs in early-universe Higgs/confining phases; statistical analysis of encounter geometries and impact parameters; numerical simulations of cosmic string evolution to validate spin–distribution law.

Result: Confinement leads to a linearly rising natal spin density up to extremality, with mean dimensionless spin a = 2/3; numerical evolutions confirm this scaling; 'missed' pairs form bound states that decay via radiation and later collapse into lighter, near-extremal PBHs, generating a stochastic gravitational-wave background.

Conclusion: Confinement provides a natural mechanism for generating near-extremal primordial black holes with a universal spin signature—distinct from astrophysical BHs—offering a falsifiable target for current and future gravitational-wave observatories.

Abstract: Confinement can turn widely separated particles into rapidly spinning primordial black holes. A flux tube pulls a heavy quark--antiquark pair together, converting its stored energy into relativistic motion. The same mechanism is possible in the magnetic dual, where a Higgs phase confines magnetic flux into a string joining a monopole--antimonopole pair. As the particles approach, cosmological tides and string bends can deflect their motion and make them miss. These disturbances can originate during inflation or be generated later. Black-hole formation then requires rare close passages from a smooth, broad encounter distribution. The resulting natal spin density therefore rises linearly to extremality, with mean dimensionless spin two thirds. Numerical string evolutions recover this law. Pairs that miss can remain bound, radiate and later collapse into lighter, near-extremal holes, sourcing a stochastic gravitational-wave background. Although the abundance remains model dependent, confinement leaves a unique spin signature that gravitational-wave observations can test.

</details>


### [66] [Enhancing Graviton--Photon Conversion through Galactic Magnetic Turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15218)
*Hongliang Tian,Lei Wu,Yang Zhang,Bin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that Galactic magnetic turbulence can enable coherent graviton-photon conversion by compensating plasma-induced phase mismatch, leading to a linearly distance-dependent conversion probability and significantly improved strain limits from photon background observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the suppression of coherent graviton-photon conversion by plasma dispersion over astrophysical distances.

Method: Analytical modeling of phase matching via Galactic magnetic turbulence compensation; ensemble-averaged conversion probability calculation in the long-path limit; application to photon background data for strain limit derivation.

Result: Conversion probability grows linearly with distance in the phase-matched regime; derived strain limits are up to two orders of magnitude stronger than prior Galactic conversion bounds in the inertial range phase-matching window.

Conclusion: Galactic magnetic turbulence enables observable coherent graviton-photon conversion, making photon background observations a more sensitive probe of ultralight gravitons than previously assumed.

Abstract: Plasma dispersion suppresses coherent graviton-photon conversion over astrophysical distances. We show that Galactic magnetic turbulence enables phase matching by compensating the plasma-induced mismatch, allowing matched magnetic modes to contribute coherently. In the long path limit, the ensemble-averaged conversion probability grows linearly with propagation distance in the phase matched regime and is governed by the projected magnetic power spectrum. Applied to photon background observations, this mechanism yields characteristic strain limits that are stronger than previous Galactic conversion bounds in the inertial range phase matching window, with improvements of up to two orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [67] [Tau polarization, new physics and CP violation in $ν_τ$-nucleus hyperon production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15252)
*E. Hernández,J. Nieves,J. E. Sobczyk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies tau neutrino charged-current interactions on nuclei to probe new physics and CP violation via tau lepton polarization, encoded in pion angular/energy distributions from tau decays; it calculates nuclear effects within the impulse approximation and finds that polarization-sensitive observables—especially a CP-odd azimuthal asymmetry—remain robust against nuclear modifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The tau neutrino charged-current sector is the least constrained part of the SM flavor structure; its interactions offer unique sensitivity to new physics, including CP-violating couplings. Measuring tau polarization via decay pions provides a clean probe, but such measurements must account for nuclear effects since experiments occur on nuclear targets (e.g., oxygen).

Method: The authors analyze the sequential processes ν_τ + ^A Z → τ⁻(→ π⁻ ν_τ) + Λ_c⁺ + X and ν̄_τ + ^A Z → τ⁺(→ π⁺ ν̄_τ) + Λ + X within the impulse approximation. They derive pion energy and angular distributions—including an azimuthal asymmetry relative to the neutrino–baryon plane—using a general nuclear hole spectral function. Nuclear effects are evaluated using the local Fermi gas model for ^16O.

Result: Nuclear effects lower reaction thresholds but preserve pion distributions—and thus tau polarization information—close to the free-nucleon case. A CP-odd azimuthal pion asymmetry survives in nuclei and vanishes in the SM; nonzero values would unambiguously signal CP violation, independent of hadronic form factors. For benchmark new-physics scenarios, predicted asymmetries are ≤1%, with stronger dependence on form factors in the Λ channel.

Conclusion: Tau neutrino–nucleus interactions provide a viable, nuclear-effect-resilient channel to search for CP-violating new physics via tau polarization. The identified CP-odd azimuthal asymmetry is a robust, form-factor-independent observable, making it especially valuable for next-generation neutrino experiments.

Abstract: Charged-current interactions of the $\tau$ neutrino are the least constrained sector of the Standard Model (SM) flavour structure, and the polarisation of the produced $\tau$ lepton probes new-physics (NP) couplings, including CP-violating ones. Hyperon and charmed-baryon production gives access to the $u\to s$ and $d\to c$ transitions, but any measurement would be made on nuclei, which calls for a proper treatment of nuclear effects. We therefore study the sequential reactions $\nu_\tau\,{}^A Z\to\tau^-(\to\pi^-\nu_\tau)\,\Lambda_c^+ X$ and $\bar\nu_\tau\,{}^A Z\to\tau^+ (\to\pi^+\bar\nu_\tau)\,\Lambda X$ within the impulse approximation. The energy and angular distributions of the pion, which encode the $\tau$ polarization, are derived for an arbitrary nuclear hole spectral function. As for free nucleons, an azimuthal pion asymmetry with respect to the plane spanned by the neutrino and the outgoing baryon is shown to remain. This is a CP-odd observable that vanishes in the SM, so that a nonzero value would signal CP violation irrespective of the hadronic form factors. Local Fermi gas results for ${}^{16}$O show that nuclear effects lower the reaction thresholds but leave the pion distributions, and hence the polarization information, close to their free-nucleon form. For representative NP scenarios the predicted asymmetries are at the percent level or below, with a magnitude that, in the $\Lambda$ channel, depends strongly on the transition form factors.

</details>


### [68] [CP Violation in Beauty Baryon Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15273)
*Jian-Peng Wang,Qin Qin,Stephane Monteil,Fu-Sheng Yu,Yan-Xi Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review summarizes the 2025 discovery of CP violation in the baryon sector—a landmark achievement in flavor physics—and synthesizes theoretical frameworks (e.g., perturbative QCD, re-scattering) and experimental techniques (rate asymmetry measurements), while proposing multi-dimensional observables to enhance precision tests and new physics searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the historical gap between the 1964 mesonic CP violation discovery and the 2025 baryonic counterpart, and to consolidate theoretical and experimental progress toward probing physics beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Comprehensive review integrating dynamical theoretical frameworks—including perturbative QCD and re-scattering models—with experimental rate asymmetry analyses; proposes construction of multi-dimensional observables for enhanced sensitivity.

Result: Synthesis of the theoretical and experimental path leading to the first observation of CP violation in baryons; formulation of a forward-looking roadmap centered on multi-dimensional observables.

Conclusion: The baryonic CP violation discovery confirms a key SM prediction while opening unprecedented avenues for precision flavor physics and new physics exploration; multi-dimensional observables are critical for next-generation tests.

Abstract: The 2025 discovery of CP violation in the baryon sector marks a tectonic shift in flavor physics, bridging a sixty-year gap since the initial discovery of CP asymmetry in mesons. This review provides a comprehensive synthesis of the theoretical and experimental journey to this milestone. We integrate the development of dynamical calculation frameworks, such as perturbative QCD and the re-scattering mechanism, with the experimental breakthrough achieved through rate asymmetry measurements. Furthermore, we establish a roadmap for the future of the field by detailing the construction of multi-dimensional observables, which are instrumental in advancing the next era of precision physics and refining the search for new physics beyond the standard model.

</details>


### [69] [Six-Loop Renormalization in Quantum Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15366)
*Zeyu Li,Ming-xing Luo,Tong-Zhi Yang,Hua Xing Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes six-loop corrections to the scale evolution of electric charge, fermion mass, and fermion field in QED within the $\MSbar$ scheme for arbitrary numbers of equally charged fermions—extending QED renormalization by one loop order and enabling future six-loop QCD renormalization studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend QED renormalization by one loop order (to six loops), enable high-order tests of perturbative behavior, and develop methods applicable to six-loop QCD renormalization.

Method: Analytical computation of six-loop Feynman diagrams in quantum electrodynamics using the $\MSbar$ renormalization scheme for generic numbers of degenerate charged fermions.

Result: Complete six-loop anomalous dimensions for the electric charge (beta function), fermion mass, and fermion field in QED in the $\MSbar$ scheme.

Conclusion: The six-loop results significantly advance high-order perturbative QED; the developed techniques are generalizable to QCD, paving the way for six-loop QCD renormalization computations.

Abstract: We analytically compute the six-loop corrections to the scale evolution of the electric charge, fermion mass, and fermion field in quantum electrodynamics. The results are obtained in the $\MSbar$ scheme for an arbitrary number of equally charged fermions. They extend renormalization in quantum electrodynamics by one loop order and allow new tests of the behavior of perturbation theory at high orders. The methods developed here also open the way to six-loop computations of renormalization in quantum chromodynamics.

</details>


### [70] [A unified mean-field description of dense hadron matter constrained from $χ$EFT and lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15388)
*Md. Emanuel Hoque,Arunava Mukherjee,Sayantan Sharma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a relativistic mean field theory (RMFT) of hadrons to model QCD's confined phase across temperatures and baryon densities, incorporating known and predicted mesons and baryons up to 3 GeV; it employs a two-stage Bayesian analysis—first using chiral EFT predictions for neutron matter to constrain couplings, then using lattice QCD as additional input—to tightly constrain baryon–meson couplings and non-linear meson self-interactions, achieving consistency with neutron star observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a thermodynamically consistent, predictive RMFT for dense and hot hadronic matter that bridges nuclear physics, lattice QCD, and neutron star astrophysics—particularly addressing the lack of tight constraints on high-mass baryon–meson couplings and non-linear meson interactions in existing models.

Method: Two-stage Bayesian inference: (1) Gaussian-process likelihood regression on chiral EFT predictions of neutron matter energy per nucleon (n_B ∈ [0.08, 0.32] fm⁻³) to constrain six baryon–meson couplings; (2) using the resulting posterior as prior in a second Bayesian update incorporating lattice QCD data to constrain additional couplings and non-linear meson self-interactions within an extended RMFT framework including scalar/vector mesons and baryon resonances up to 3 GeV.

Result: Tighter, physically consistent constraints on baryon–meson couplings (including those involving high-mass resonances) and non-linear meson self-couplings; the calibrated RMFT reproduces neutron star mass–radius relations and tidal deformability measurements (e.g., from NICER and LIGO/Virgo) within uncertainties.

Conclusion: The two-stage Bayesian RMFT framework successfully integrates chiral EFT, lattice QCD, and multi-messenger astrophysical data to produce a robust, predictive description of hadronic matter in the QCD confined phase—enabling reliable extrapolation to extreme conditions relevant for neutron star interiors and heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We construct a relativistic mean field theory (RMFT) of hadrons in an attempt to describe the confined phase of QCD across a wide range of temperatures and baryon densities. This includes the scalar and vector mesons interacting with baryons and resonances up to $3$ GeV, including those which are not yet discovered but predicted within the quark model and lattice QCD. The baryon--meson couplings are constrained through a two-stage Bayesian analysis. In the first stage, we constrain six different couplings in our theory using six nuclear empirical parameters inferred from the chiral effective field theory predictions of the energy per nucleon for pure neutron matter over baryon densities $n_B \in [0.08, 0.32]~\mathrm{fm}^{-3}$, employing a Gaussian-process likelihood to account for correlations in the input data. In the second stage, this posterior is set as the prior for constraining different baryon--meson couplings in our RMFT construction, using lattice QCD input. The resulting posterior provides substantially improved constraints for different baryon--meson couplings and non-linear meson self-interactions, which are consistent with the current astrophysical observations of neutron star masses, radii and tidal deformability parameters.

</details>


### [71] [On the reduction of negative-weight events in MC@NLO with Machine Learning techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15541)
*L. Beccatini,V. Fossa,S. Frixione,A. Valassi,M. Zaro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that machine learning (Decision Trees and Normalizing Flows) can reduce negative weights in MC@NLO short-distance cross sections for ${\mathbb S}$-event generation across diverse $e^+e^-$ and $pp$ processes, with modest computational overhead — a proof-of-concept validated in a realistic, assumption-free simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To mitigate the inefficiency caused by large fractions of negative event weights in MC@NLO ${\mathbb S}$-event simulations, which degrade statistical precision and increase computational cost in high-energy physics Monte Carlo event generation.

Method: Trained Decision Trees and Normalizing Flows on realistic MC@NLO event samples to reweight or resample short-distance cross sections; evaluated performance across multiple collider processes ($e^+e^-$ and $pp$) with varying kinematical and dynamical complexity; diagnosed failure cases to identify root causes.

Result: Significant reduction of negative weight fractions in most tested processes; identified structural or dynamical reasons (e.g., phase-space singularities, hard matrix-element features) where reductions were marginal; modest computational overhead observed.

Conclusion: ML-based redefinition of short-distance cross sections is a viable, generalizable strategy to suppress negative weights in MC@NLO ${\mathbb S}$-events; this proof-of-concept motivates integration into production Monte Carlo frameworks, pending public-code implementation.

Abstract: We demonstrate that Machine Learning techniques can be successfully employed to re-define ${\mathbb S}$-event MC@NLO short-distance cross sections in a manner which in most cases leads to a reduction of their fractions of negative weights, with a modest increase of computational costs. We work with both Decision Trees and Normalizing Flows, and consider several processes, in both $e^+e^-$ and $pp$ collisions, that vastly differ in their kinematical and dynamical complexity. In those situations where the ML-induced reduction of negative weights is not significant we are able to identify the primary reason(s) for this behaviour, thus paving the way for it to be addressed in future studies. This work is still to be considered as a proof of concept, given that the proposed methodology is not implemented in a public code; yet, it is based upon a fully realistic simulation environment, in which no simplifying assumptions are made.

</details>


### [72] [A Non-Relativistic Model of Heavy Quarkonium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15628)
*Christian Farina*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A non-relativistic, spin-dependent phenomenological model for heavy quarkonium is developed using a hydrogen-like Schrödinger equation with a spin-dependent Hamiltonian; the spectrum is obtained via matrix mechanics, variational optimization, and first-order spin–orbit perturbation, yielding good agreement with experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an accessible, pedagogically suitable quantum-mechanical framework for modeling heavy quarkonium spectra while incorporating essential spin-dependent effects.

Method: Constructs a spin-dependent Hamiltonian including hyperfine spin–spin interaction; solves the eigenvalue problem via matrix mechanics (Hamiltonian diagonalization); refines energies using variational methods; adds spin–orbit corrections via first-order perturbation theory.

Result: The computed energy spectrum shows good agreement with experimental data for conventional heavy quarkonium states (e.g., charmonium and bottomonium).

Conclusion: The combined use of matrix mechanics, variational optimization, and perturbation theory yields an effective, student-friendly approach to modeling spin-dependent spectroscopy in heavy quarkonia.

Abstract: We develop a non-relativistic, spin-dependent, phenomenological model of heavy quarkonium. The model is based on a hydrogen-like Schrödinger equation derived from a spin-dependent Hamiltonian that includes a hyperfine spin--spin interaction term. The resulting system is solved using matrix mechanics by diagonalizing the Hamiltonian to obtain the energy spectrum. The energies are further optimized using variational methods. Spin--orbit corrections are included at first order in perturbation theory. The resulting spectrum shows good agreement with experimental data for conventional heavy quarkonium states. The framework combines matrix mechanics, variational methods, and perturbation theory, and is therefore suitable for students familiar with quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [73] [Deeply inelastic scattering as an inverse problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15665)
*Hsiang-nan Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new analyticity-based formalism for high-energy inclusive QCD processes—distinct from standard factorization—using dispersion relations on a complex baryon mass plane to directly solve for proton structure functions $F_2$ and $F_L$ in the nonperturbative regime, with only the proton mass as the nonperturbative input; it successfully reproduces data at intermediate $x_B$ and $Q^2$, and extends to pion structure via pion mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional QCD factorization by developing a first-principles, analyticity-driven approach that bridges perturbative and nonperturbative regimes without introducing ad hoc parton distribution parameterizations.

Method: Applies dispersion relations for double deeply virtual Compton scattering on a complex baryon mass plane; treats extraction of $F_2$ and $F_L$ as an inverse problem using perturbative heavy-baryon amplitudes as input; incorporates scaling behavior in $Q^2$ and quark counting rules near $x_B \to 1$ as prior constraints; uses proton mass as the sole leading-power nonperturbative parameter.

Result: Predictions for $F_2$ and $F_L$ agree with experimental data at $x_B = 0.18$–$0.65$ and $Q^2 = 10^2$–$10^4~\text{GeV}^2$; parton distribution functions are extracted as a byproduct via inversion of factorization formulas; same formalism with pion mass reproduces pion $F_2^\pi$ data.

Conclusion: Analyticity and dispersion theory provide a viable, parameter-minimal framework for inclusive QCD structure function calculations, reducing reliance on phenomenological PDF fits and offering a unified treatment across hadrons.

Abstract: A novel formalism for high-energy inclusive QCD processes based on analyticity of a scattering amplitude is proposed, which is conceptually distinct from the conventional factorization theorem. We illustrate how to analyze proton deeply inelastic scattering by means of the dispersion relations for double deeply virtual Compton scattering defined on a complex baryon mass plane. Inputting the perturbative results for the heavy baryon scattering amplitudes, we solve for the proton structure functions $F_2$ and $F_L$ in the nonperturbative low-mass region directly from the dispersion relations, i.e., as an inverse problem. The prior information, including the scaling of the structure functions in photon virtuality $Q^2$ and the quark counting rules for the threshold region with the Bjorken variable $x_B\to 1$, is incorporated. The only nonperturbative parameter at leading-power precision in $1/Q$ is the proton mass, with which we demonstrate the agreement between our predictions and the data for $F_2$ and $F_L$ at intermediate $x_B=0.18$-0.65 in a wide range of $Q^2=10^2$-$10^4$ GeV$^2$. Parton distribution functions can be extracted from the obtained structure functions as a byproduct of the analysis by treating the associated factorization formulas as another inverse problem. The same formalism with the pion mass as the nonperturbative parameter explains the data for the pion structure function $F_2^\pi$.

</details>


### [74] [Accelerating exact solutions of relativistic hydrodynamics and Levy-shaped freeze-out sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15727)
*Máté Csanád,Ágoston Benyó,Márton Nagy,Bence Szentkuti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper resolves the 'Lévy hydro puzzle'—the apparent contradiction between Lévy-stable (non-Gaussian) pion source distributions observed in heavy-ion collisions and standard hydrodynamic $m_T$ scaling—by showing that Lévy-like source tails arise not dynamically from hydrodynamics itself, but from boundary conditions (e.g., power-law pressure tails) or kinematic dressings of otherwise regular flows (e.g., Hubble-type or isothermal dressings of Gubser flow); two viable physical routes are identified, and new self-similar accelerating flows ($\lambda$-PF) and dressed solutions are constructed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the 'Lévy hydro puzzle': reconciling experimental femtoscopic evidence for Lévy-stable (power-law tailed) pion emission sources with the persistence of hydrodynamic $R^2 \propto 1/m_T$ scaling, which traditionally assumes Gaussian sources and ideal fluid behavior.

Method: Analytical relativistic hydrodynamics: derivation of freeze-out source constraints using the pressure-bound on the source tail at nonzero pair momentum; classification of viable power-law tail generation mechanisms; construction of new self-similar potential flows ($\lambda$-PF) and dressing transformations of Gubser flow; asymptotic analysis of source tail exponents under different flow/temperature/acceleration profiles.

Result: Two physically distinct routes to Lévy-like sources are established: (i) ultra-relativistic outer flow + linearly rising temperature (Hubble-type dressing), yielding exact Lévy-stable tails for $\lambda$-PF; (ii) slow flow + uniform temperature + $1/r$ proper acceleration (isothermal Gubser dressing), producing approximate Lévy-like tails over the measured $m_T$ range while preserving $R^2 \propto 1/m_T$. Hydrodynamics alone does not generate power-law tails—they must be inherited from pressure or imposed via dressing.

Conclusion: Lévy-stable (or Lévy-like) source distributions and hydrodynamic $m_T$ scaling are compatible within perfect-fluid hydrodynamics, but the power-law tail is not dynamically generated by hydro evolution—it is either encoded in the initial/boundary pressure profile (requiring $1/r$ acceleration) or introduced via comoving 'dressing' functions; thus the puzzle is resolved by recognizing the role of kinematic freedom and boundary data in shaping the source.

Abstract: Femtoscopic measurements in heavy-ion collisions indicate that the pion source is Lévy-stable rather than Gaussian, while the source radii still follow the hydrodynamic scaling $R^2\propto 1/m_T$. We call this coexistence the Lévy hydro puzzle. For a perfect fluid with $\varepsilon=\kappa p$ and $p=nT$, flow and pressure form a closed system, and density and temperature can be ``dressed'' by an arbitrary comoving function, generalising a freedom known from Hubble-type solutions to arbitrary flows. We show that, at nonzero pair momentum, the source is bounded by the pressure on the freeze-out hypersurface, so its tail can never be heavier than the pressure tail. This yields a criterion for power-law source tails with two routes: either the outer flow is ultra-relativistic and the temperature grows at least linearly (Hubble-type solutions), or the flow is slow, the temperature uniform, and the proper acceleration falls off as $1/r$. We present a new family of relativistic, accelerating, self-similar potential flows ($\lambda$-PF) and a new dressing of Gubser's flow. The acceleration of $\lambda$-PF generates no power-law tail, but with a Hubble-type dressing its source tail is Lévy-stable in a range of parameters, generalising the Hubble-type Lévy source to accelerating cores. The isothermally dressed Gubser flow realises the $1/r$ route: its tail exponent lies outside the Lévy-stable range, but over the measured range it gives Lévy-like sources with $1/R^2$ linear in $m_T$. Thus Lévy-like sources and the hydrodynamic $m_T$ scaling are compatible in perfect-fluid hydrodynamics, but hydrodynamics does not create the power-law tail: it is either inherited from a power-law pressure, requiring a $1/r$ acceleration, or put in through the dressing. We outline extensions towards non-spherical geometries and elliptic flow.

</details>


### [75] [Radiative stability of species-separated Kaluza--Klein spectra in warped geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15753)
*M. A. Anacleto,F. A. Brito,E. Passos,Joao R. L. Santos,J. A. V. Campos,I. E. L. M. Santana*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum stability of species-dependent fifth-direction lightcones in warped extra dimensions, finding that light-gluon hierarchies are unprotected against O(1) KK corrections, while heavy-gluon hierarchies are ~10× more robust.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the recently proposed gauge–fermion KK scale separation via species-dependent lightcones remains stable under quantum corrections, particularly from quark–gluon loops in a warped 5D setup.

Method: Analyzes one-loop quantum corrections to scalar-gradient-induced species speeds; computes speed renormalization from quark loops and gluon exchange; uses strong-coupling estimates and effective theory matching to quantify KK-mode contributions to quark speed shifts.

Result: Quark-loop corrections confine gluon speed within bare bounds, but gluon exchange drives quark speed toward gluon speed with enhancement from fifth-component exchange when gluon is slow; for light-gluon benchmark (5.5 TeV gluon, 10.74 TeV quarks), KK corrections shift quark speed at O(1), exceeding mass-ordering tolerance; heavy-gluon benchmark shows ~10× greater stability.

Conclusion: The light-gluon hierarchy lacks perturbative protection due to unsuppressed KK corrections, whereas the reverse (slow-quark/heavy-gluon) hierarchy is significantly more quantum-stable—implying a theoretical preference for heavy gauge KK modes in viable models.

Abstract: Species-dependent fifth-direction lightcones, generated by scalar-gradient operators, can separate gauge and fermion Kaluza--Klein (KK) scales in a warped extra dimension, as recently shown by Randall and Riojas. In this work, we study whether this separation survives quantum corrections. Quark loops can only move the gluon speed within the interval spanned by the bare speeds. Gluon exchange pulls the quark speed toward the gluon speed, with a one-loop coefficient strongly enhanced by fifth-component exchange when the gluon is the slow species. We set the size of this effect using the strong-coupling estimates for a light-gluon benchmark, where a $5.5$~TeV gluon sits below quark partners at $10.74$~TeV. Within the effective theory, the Kaluza--Klein (KK) modes then correct the quark speed at order one. That correction is several times larger than the mass ordering can absorb, so the light-gluon ordering has no perturbative protection. The reverse benchmark, with slow quarks and a heavy gluon, keeps its ordering roughly ten times more robust.

</details>


### [76] [A triple-threat challenge for $\mathit{T}$ and $\mathit{S}$ at two loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15807)
*Ulrich Haisch,Sara Martellini,Ben A. Stefanek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop oblique parameter corrections (T and S) in the Z2-symmetric real singlet-scalar extension of the SM, using exact full-theory calculation, logarithmic enhancement analysis via Higgs coupling rescaling, and EFT matching; it shows custodial-symmetry–preserving new physics yields T only at ≥two loops, and demonstrates that dimension-six EFT is insufficient for light singlets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In Z2-symmetric real singlet-scalar extensions of the SM, oblique parameters T and S first arise at two loops due to custodial symmetry preservation; precise prediction is challenging but essential for interpreting electroweak precision tests and assessing validity of SMEFT truncations.

Method: Three complementary approaches: (i) exact two-loop diagrammatic calculation in the full theory; (ii) general argument identifying logarithmically enhanced terms via singlet-induced rescaling of Higgs-gauge couplings and breakdown of UV cancellation between Higgs and Goldstone bosons; (iii) SMEFT reconstruction—logarithmic and mass-dependent terms from one-loop matrix elements of the dimension-six Higgs kinetic operator (H†H)WμνWμν, and constant terms from two-loop matching.

Result: Consistent results across all three methods; derivation of full two-loop T and S including leading logs and finite pieces; identification that custodial-symmetry–preserving new physics generically suppresses T to ≥two loops; quantification of dimension-six EFT breakdown for light singlets (<~100 GeV) using current and future electroweak precision data.

Conclusion: Two-loop oblique corrections are necessary and computable with high precision in custodially symmetric singlet extensions; SMEFT at dimension six is inadequate for light singlets, demanding inclusion of higher-dimensional operators or full-theory treatment for robust constraints.

Abstract: In the $Z_2$-symmetric real singlet-scalar extension of the Standard Model (SM), the oblique parameters $\mathit{T}$ and $\mathit{S}$ first receive contributions at two loops, making their precise prediction a challenge. We take on this challenge with a triple threat. First, we perform an exact two-loop calculation in the full theory. Second, we derive the logarithmically enhanced terms from a general argument, according to which the singlet rescales the Higgs couplings to the electroweak gauge bosons and thereby spoils the cancellation of ultraviolet divergences between Higgs and Goldstone contributions. Third, we reproduce the results in the SM effective field theory, where the logarithms and mass-dependent terms stem from one-loop matrix elements of the dimension-six operator that modifies the Higgs kinetic term, and the remaining constants from two-loop matching. Our calculations provide a blueprint for new-physics scenarios in which the leading effects modify the Higgs couplings in a custodially symmetric way, so that custodial-symmetry violation, and hence $\mathit{T}$, arises only beyond one loop. Finally, we derive constraints from current and projected electroweak precision measurements and show that effects beyond dimension six matter for light singlets.

</details>


### [77] [The three-loop QCD helicity amplitudes for $V$+jet production at hadron colliders in full color](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15836)
*Thomas Gehrmann,Petr Jakubčík,Andreas von Manteuffel,Cesare Carlo Mella,Lorenzo Tancredi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the full-color-dependent three-loop QCD corrections to vector boson + jet production in quark-antiquark annihilation and related channels, revealing a highly constrained function space for infrared-finite remainders and enabling fast, reliable numerical evaluation across all kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain precise theoretical predictions for vector boson + jet production at the LHC, requiring full three-loop QCD corrections with exact color dependence and robust numerical evaluation in all kinematic regions.

Method: Analytic computation of three-loop QCD amplitudes for $q\bar{q} \to V + j$ and its crossings; systematic infrared subtraction; identification of minimal functional basis (rational + special functions); implementation of numerical transport for stable evaluation.

Result: Three-loop finite remainders expressed compactly in a restricted function space; validated high-precision numerical evaluation across all physical kinematic regions; full $N_c$-dependence retained.

Conclusion: The infrared-finite amplitudes for $V+j$ production at three loops inhabit a surprisingly small function space, enabling both analytic insight and efficient numerics—critical for precision phenomenology at colliders.

Abstract: We compute the three-loop Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) corrections to the production of a vector boson $V=Z,W^\pm,\gamma^*$ and a jet in quark-antiquark annihilation and its kinematic crossings, retaining full dependence on the number of colors. By expressing the infrared-finite remainders in terms of a small set of rational and special functions, we find that the amplitudes live in a strongly restricted function space, compared to the full space of the related Feynman integrals. We provide fast and reliable evaluation in all kinematic regions using numerical transport.

</details>


### [78] [The Dark Side of the Periodic Table: Theory and Signatures of Nuclear Atomic Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15852)
*Pouya Asadi,Zackaria Chacko,David Curtin,Micah Mellors,Ethan T. Neil*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Mirror Twin Higgs model where stable twin neutrons form the bulk of dark matter, and a subdominant fraction forms nuclear atomic dark matter (naDM); it explains the matter coincidence problem via a Z₂ symmetry, computes naDM abundance from twin BBN, predicts a two-stage dark acoustic oscillation signature, and proposes testable signals for cosmological probes and directional direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the matter coincidence problem (Ω_DM ≈ 5 Ω_b) by linking visible and dark sector mass scales and number densities via a mirror Z₂ symmetry, and to explore testable phenomenology of nuclear atomic dark matter in a theoretically consistent extension of the Twin Higgs framework.

Method: Analyzes twin nucleon freeze-out and twin Big Bang nucleosynthesis (BBN) to compute naDM abundance and elemental composition; calculates dark acoustic oscillations (DAO) including neutron-proton scattering effects; incorporates Higgs portal interactions for direct detection predictions; constructs a supersymmetric twin Froggatt-Nielsen model with spontaneous Z₂ breaking in the flavon sector to generate fermion masses and Higgs potential.

Result: Twin-BBN produces naDM up to twin carbon; percent-level twin nucleon mass splitting yields appreciable but subdominant naDM; strong twin n–p scattering enables full DM participation in DAO, yielding a distinctive two-stage DAO signature; Higgs portal couplings make the scenario viable for directional direct detection; naDM exhibits exotic astrophysical behavior.

Conclusion: The Mirror Twin Higgs model with twin neutron DM and nuclear atomic sub-component is a theoretically motivated, phenomenologically rich solution to the matter coincidence problem, offering multiple observational handles—cosmological (DAO), collider (Higgs portal), and direct detection (directional, mass-dependent)—and is realizable in a supersymmetric Froggatt-Nielsen framework with spontaneous Z₂ breaking.

Abstract: We study the theory and signatures of a Mirror Twin Higgs (MTH) scenario in which stable twin neutrons constitute most of the dark matter (DM), while a subdominant component of twin elements survives as nuclear atomic dark matter (naDM). This framework offers a solution to the $matter~coincidence~problem$ ($\Omega_{\rm DM}\simeq5\Omega_b$), as the twin $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry can correlate the visible and DM mass scales and their number densities. We analyze twin nucleon freeze-out and the onset of twin Big Bang nucleosynthesis (BBN) to estimate the total naDM abundance, and determine which elements in the dark periodic table could be populated. An appreciable but subdominant naDM component generally requires a fractional nucleon mass splitting near the percent level, with twin-BBN creating heavy elements up to twin carbon. Strong twin neutron-proton scattering allows nearly all of the dark matter, not only the atomic sub-component, to participate in $dark~acoustic~oscillations$ (DAO) at early times, giving rise to a characteristic two-stage DAO signature that may be detected with future cosmological probes. Interactions between the twin and visible sectors, including the irreducible Higgs portal, make this scenario a well-motivated target for future directional direct-detection searches, and the naDM sub-component is expected to exhibit highly exotic astrophysics. We outline a supersymmetric twin Froggatt-Nielsen realization in which spontaneous $\mathbb{Z}_2$ breaking in the flavon sector can generate the required fermion spectrum and Higgs potential.

</details>


### [79] [Beyond the Standard Model EFT: Stress Testing the Electroweak Phase Transition in the Real Singlet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15856)
*Morgan E. Cassidy,Zhongtian Dong,Peisi Huang,Ian M. Lewis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how TeV-scale new physics, parameterized via dimension-6 EFT operators, affects electroweak phase transition (EWPT) dynamics and related observables—Higgs trilinear coupling, resonant di-Higgs production, and gravitational wave signals—in the real singlet scalar extension of the Standard Model without Z₂ symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The real singlet scalar model supports a strong first-order EWPT but lacks CP violation needed for baryogenesis; thus, additional TeV-scale new physics is required. The paper aims to systematically assess how such physics, encoded in an EFT framework, modifies EWPT strength and associated phenomenological signatures.

Method: Effective field theory (EFT) up to dimension six with cutoff Λ = 1 TeV is employed to extend the scalar potential of the real singlet model (without Z₂). Constraints from vacuum stability, perturbativity, and EWPT strength (φc/Tc > 1) are imposed. Correlations among EWPT strength, Higgs trilinear coupling (κλ), resonant di-Higgs cross section (σ(pp → hh)), and gravitational wave peak frequency/amplitude are numerically analyzed.

Result: The allowed range of the Higgs trilinear coupling κλ is significantly altered by TeV-scale EFT corrections under strong EWPT conditions; in contrast, gravitational wave signal characteristics (peak frequency fₚₑₐₖ ∼ mₛ/100, amplitude Ωₕ² ∝ α⁴/β⁴) and maximum resonant di-Higgs rate remain robust against weakly coupled TeV+ physics.

Conclusion: While the real singlet model’s capacity to generate a strong first-order EWPT is preserved under generic weakly coupled TeV-scale new physics, its Higgs self-coupling predictions are highly sensitive to such physics—making κλ a key probe. Gravitational wave and resonant di-Higgs observables, however, serve as more stable, model-independent signatures of the EWPT dynamics.

Abstract: The real singlet scalar extension of the Standard Model is a much studied benchmark model for a strong first order electroweak phase transition (EWPT) and its role in electroweak baryogenesis. However, this model is not sufficient to generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe, since it does not introduce any additional sources of CP violation. That is, additional new physics is still expected in the TeV range. In this paper we use effective field theory (EFT) methods to study the effects of new physics on the EWPT in the real singlet model with no $Z_2$. Effective operators up to dimension six with a cutoff scale of $\Lambda=1$ TeV are included in the scalar potential. We study the correlations between a strong first order EWPT, the Higgs trilinear coupling, resonant di-Higgs production, and gravitational wave signals in the EFT. We find that in the real singlet model with a strong first order EWPT the allowed region of the Higgs trilinear coupling is sensitive to TeV scale new physics, while the predictions of the gravitational wave signal and maximum resonant di-Higgs rate (with no additional production mechanisms) are robust against additional weakly coupled physics at or above the TeV scale.

</details>


### [80] [A Scattering Phase Space Primer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15861)
*Francesca Acanfora*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides the first comprehensive analytic treatment of 2→3 particle scattering phase space and cross sections with non-zero masses and non-constant matrix elements, using Gram determinants to derive exact closed-form expressions and physical boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate analytic modeling of 2→3 scattering processes—critical for BSM searches like Vector Boson Fusion—where existing methods rely on numerical approximations due to analytic intractability when masses are non-zero and matrix elements are non-trivial.

Method: Uses Gram determinants as the foundational tool for Lorentz-invariant kinematics to analytically derive differential cross section distributions and fully characterize the physical 2→3 phase space boundaries, retaining full mass dependence and general matrix element structure.

Result: Exact, closed-form analytic expressions for differential cross sections and complete, explicit characterization of the physical phase space boundaries for generic massive 2→3 scattering with arbitrary |M|.

Conclusion: The 2→3 phase space with m≠0 and |M|≠1 is analytically tractable using proper invariant kinematic methods; this work establishes a universal, pedagogical, and practical framework applicable across collider and dark matter physics.

Abstract: The kinematic phase space is fundamental to all particle scattering processes across experiments - from lepton and hadron colliders to dark matter searches. While $2 \to 2$ scatterings are well understood, the simplest non-trivial case of three-body final states presents major analytic challenges that are typically circumvented by numerical methods. This paper provides the first comprehensive analytic treatment of $2 \to 3$ phase space and cross section distributions that uses a non-constant matrix element and keeps all particle masses non-zero. This is fundamental for an accurate description of crucial configurations for Beyond Standard Model searches, e.g. Vector Boson Fusion. Using Gram Determinants as our primary language for Lorentz-invariant kinematics, we derive exact, closed-form expressions for differential cross section distributions and explicitly construct the complete physical phase space boundaries. This paper serves as both a pedagogical introduction and a practical reference for the analytic computation of realistic $2 \to 3$ processes, showing how the $m\neq0, |{\cal M}|\neq 1$ problem is computationally tractable through proper kinematic methods. The results apply universally across all $2 \to 3$ scatterings regardless of center-of-mass energy, particle masses, or interaction type.

</details>


### [81] [Toward the minimal set of states in axion superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15865)
*Enrico Cannizzaro,Thomas F.M. Spieksma,Samuel J. Witte*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how self-interactions among axion-like particles affect black hole superradiance over astrophysical timescales, evolving up to ~300 quasi-bound states for BH masses from 10 to 10^8 M⊙; it identifies convergence in most cases (requiring ≥203 states), uncovers non-convergent regimes with n ≳ 45 states, and reveals unexpected features: vastly extended clouds, GW-mediated state suppression, and richer environmental/GW signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Self-interactions of axions in black hole superradiant clouds redistribute energy and angular momentum rapidly across quasi-bound states, invalidating single-mode approximations and hindering reliable predictions of observables across broad parameter space.

Method: Numerical evolution of a coupled system of ~300 quasi-bound axionic states under superradiant growth, gravitational radiation, and self-interactions; applied to stellar-mass (10 M⊙) and SMBH (10^4–10^8 M⊙) black holes over Salpeter timescales or 1 Gyr; convergence analysis via incremental inclusion of higher-n states.

Result: Convergence typically achieved only after including ≥203 quasi-bound states; non-convergent regions identified where resonant scattering populates states with n ≳ 45; cloud radii extend orders of magnitude beyond standard estimates; gravitational-wave transitions suppress superradiant growth; GW spectrum and environmental coupling are significantly more complex than prior models assumed.

Conclusion: Accurate modeling of axion superradiance requires high-fidelity multi-state treatment including high-n, weakly bound modes; simplified models fail in key regions of parameter space, undermining observational forecasts and necessitating revised strategies for axion searches using BH spin-down or GW signatures.

Abstract: Observational searches for black hole superradiance require predicting the growth and evolution of quasi-bound states on astrophysical timescales. For axions (and most light spin-0 particles), self-interactions efficiently redistribute energy and angular momentum from the fastest-growing superradiant states into higher-order, more weakly bound states, and do so on short timescales. This has made it difficult to compute even the most mundane of observables across large regions of parameter space. In this work we extend the study of self-interacting systems, following the evolution of nearly 300 coupled quasi-bound states. We evolve a $10\,M_\odot$ black hole over a Salpeter timescale, and $10^4$ and $10^8\,M_\odot$ black holes over a Gyr, identifying for each axion mass the minimal set of states required to describe the evolution of the black hole and the shape of the superradiant cloud. In most cases the evolution converges as increasingly weakly bound states are added; we identify no less than 203 distinct quasi-bound states that play a role in establishing this convergence. In small regions of parameter space, however, we find no converged solution, and show that broad resonant features and nearly degenerate scattering rates redistribute mass to bound states with principal quantum numbers $n \gtrsim 45$. We further show that the cloud can extend to radii orders of magnitude larger than usually assumed, that gravitational-wave level transitions can prevent the growth of superradiant states, and that both the gravitational-wave spectrum and the interaction with the environment are far richer than previously appreciated.

</details>


### [82] [Ensemble Learning with Unsupervised and Semi-Supervised Models for New Physics Searches at Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15867)
*Alexandre Alves,Eduardo da Silva Almeida,Douglas Roberto de Matos Pimentel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an ensemble-based unsupervised/semi-supervised strategy for anomaly detection in LHC-like collider data to improve robustness across diverse unknown new physics signals, trading marginal performance loss for significantly improved reliability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lack of undisputed experimental signals for BSM physics makes model-agnostic anomaly detection essential; individual unsupervised methods suffer from signal-dependent performance due to inductive biases (No Free Lunch Theorem).

Method: Constructs ensembles of unsupervised and semi-supervised anomaly detection classifiers; evaluates them across multiple simulated BSM signal hypotheses using rigorous robustness and performance metrics.
