Result: Ensemble models consistently deliver robust and reliable classification across diverse signal types, outperforming individual models in reliability—though often with slight average AUC or significance loss—thereby increasing confidence in detecting genuinely unknown physics.

Conclusion: Ensemble learning mitigates the signal-dependence limitation of single unsupervised algorithms, providing a more model-independent, trustworthy framework for new physics searches at colliders.

Abstract: New physics beyond the Standard Model is widely believed to exist motivated by key limitations of the current theory. However, in the absence of undisputed experimental signals, there is little guidance on what type of extended physics model should be built. To address the challenge of training machine learning classifiers to spot unknown phenomena, many algorithms have been applied to the task of either identifying anomalous events relative to background expectations or cluster collision events together in classes of similarity. However, as in the supervised case, the no free lunch theorem assures that the performance of unsupervised models depends on the specific signal hypothesis and their inductive biases, and the successful identification of a given signal by a particular algorithm does not guarantee comparable performance for other signals. In this study, we take a step toward a more model-independent unsupervised and semi-supervised search strategy by combining classifiers into ensembles. Our goal is addressing two complementary aspects of unsupervised ensemble learning - overall performance and robustness of the classification task from a thorough assessment of ensemble models. We show that, despite sacrificing some performance relative to the best individual model in the majority of cases, the ensembles provide robust and reliable classification predictions across several signal hypotheses, increasing confidence that genuinely unknown new physics will not pass unnoticed.

</details>


### [83] [Discovering new physics with semitauonic $b$-hadron decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15869)
*Markus Prim,Dean J. Robinson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews theoretical and experimental aspects of lepton flavor universality violation (LFUV) anomalies in beauty hadron decays, assessing current status, challenges for future precision tests, and strategies to confirm or exclude new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observed anomalous lepton flavor universality violation in semileptonic B-meson decays suggests possible new physics beyond the Standard Model; a rigorous, unbiased interpretation requires careful treatment of theory uncertainties, experimental systematics, and consistent effective field theory frameworks.

Method: Systematic review and critical assessment of theoretical frameworks (e.g., SMEFT, WET), experimental analyses (LHCb, Belle II, ATLAS, CMS), global fits to b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ and b → cτν data, and evaluation of key systematic limitations including QCD effects, non-local contributions, and parametrization dependencies.

Result: Current anomalies—particularly in R(K), R(K*), and R(D*)—remain statistically significant but not yet conclusive; tensions persist between different observables and experiments; theory uncertainties (e.g., charm-loop contributions, form factors) and experimental correlations limit discrimination power.

Conclusion: Definitive confirmation or exclusion of new physics requires improved theoretical control (especially non-perturbative and long-distance effects), enhanced experimental precision with full correlation matrices, and synergistic global analyses across complementary channels and experiments.

Abstract: Anomalous lepton flavor universality violation (LFUV) observed in semileptonic decays of beauty hadrons may be the first signal of new physics (NP) beyond the Standard Model. We review the essential theoretical concepts and experimental considerations required for self-consistent and unbiased NP interpretations of these LFUV anomalies. We examine the status of present-day analyses, and assess the core challenges to the future precision studies that will be required to conclusively establish or reject an NP signal, as well as promising strategies to resolve them.

</details>


### [84] [Scalar and pseudoscalar glueballonia: do they bind?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15873)
*Narmin Nasibova,Francesco Giacosa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates molecular two-glueball bound states ('glueballonia') in pure Yang-Mills theory, focusing on scalar and pseudoscalar channels; using a nonrelativistic Yukawa-plus-contact potential calibrated via dilaton effective theory and lattice input, it finds robust binding for scalar-scalar and scalar-pseudoscalar configurations—both stable in the YM vacuum—while other channels (e.g., pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar) are attractive but unstable; Higgs-Higgs binding is excluded as a consistency check.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether bound states of two glueballs—'glueballonia'—can exist in pure Yang-Mills theory, motivated by analogies to meson-meson molecules and the need to understand the spectrum and stability of exotic gluonic matter beyond single-glueball states.

Method: Employs a nonrelativistic effective potential approach near threshold: an attractive Yukawa potential (from t/u-channel glueball exchange) plus a smeared contact term; for scalar-scalar systems, parameters are fixed via the scalar dilaton effective theory constrained by lattice-YM data; for scalar-pseudoscalar, extends minimally with u-channel pseudoscalar exchange; solves for binding condition (critical attraction) and assesses stability against decay into lighter glueballs.

Result: Scalar-scalar glueballonium binds robustly; scalar-pseudoscalar glueballonium also binds; both are stable in pure YM vacuum; pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar, scalar-tensor, and tensor-tensor channels are attractive but yield unstable bound states due to allowed decays; Higgs-Higgs bound state is excluded, validating the formalism.

Conclusion: Molecular glueball bound states—specifically scalar and scalar-pseudoscalar glueballonia—are viable and stable in pure Yang-Mills theory, implying a richer gluonic spectrum than previously assumed; their existence is theoretically robust under parameter variation and consistent with lattice constraints.

Abstract: We investigate the existence of molecular two-glueball bound states, so-called glueballonia, in pure Yang-Mills (YM) theory. We mainly concentrate on the scalar and pseudoscalar channels, thus studying scalar-scalar and scalar-pseudoscalar bound states, respectively. Near the onset of binding, when a bound state first appears just below threshold, a nonrelativistic framework is justified. We employ an attractive scalar-exchange Yukawa potential together with a smeared contact term contribution and determine the critical attraction required for binding. For two scalar glueballs, we use the scalar dilaton effective theory to determine the parameters of the non-relativistic potential: the Yukawa interaction is generated by t- and u-channels involving the exchange of one scalar glueball, while the repulsion emerges by the s-channel annihilation and contact interaction. Using lattice-YM input to fix the dilaton parameters, we find that the attraction is strong enough: the scalar glueballonium binds, and the result is quite robust with respect to parameter variations. In a minimal scalar-pseudoscalar extension, the Yukawa attraction is again given by the t-channel scalar glueball, while the crossed u-channel pseudoscalar exchange induces an attractive local contribution, making the pseudoscalar glueballonium also bind in pure gauge. Importantly, both the scalar and pseudoscalar glueballonia are stable in the YM vacuum. Next, we briefly comment on the pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar, scalar-tensor, and tensor-tensor channels: all these channels are attractive too and could form bound states, which, however, would be unstable in pure YM due to decay into two lighter glueballs. Finally, as a cross-check, our formalism confirms that Higgs-Higgs bound state does not form.

</details>


### [85] [Dual-species atom interferometry for axion dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15874)
*Maike Reckermann,John Carlton,Michael Werner,Agnese Mariotti,Guillermo A. Perez-Lobato,Nathaniel Sherrill,Naceur Gaaloul,Dennis Schlippert,Elina Fuchs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a dual-species atom interferometer to suppress gravity gradient noise and enhance sensitivity to axion dark matter via atomic mass differences, enabling competitive or superior constraints on axion–gluon coupling compared to current laboratory experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Axion dark matter can induce oscillations in atomic masses and transition frequencies, detectable by atom interferometers; however, gravity gradient noise (GGN) severely limits sensitivity.

Method: Design and analysis of a dual-species atom interferometry sequence exploiting differential phase response to GGN and enhanced signal from atomic mass difference; includes comprehensive noise modeling and comparison with single-species gradiometers (e.g., VLBAI, AICE).

Result: A 10 m dual-species interferometer achieves up to 1000× GGN suppression and surpasses the axion-gluon coupling sensitivity of a 100 m single-species gradiometer; VLBAI (10 m) reaches near-state-of-the-art lab limits, while AICE (100 m) improves sensitivity by up to two orders of magnitude for axion masses 10⁻¹⁹–3×10⁻¹⁵ eV/c².

Conclusion: Dual-species atom interferometry is a highly promising pathway for probing QCD axions in the ultralight mass range, with near-term experiments (VLBAI, AICE) poised to exceed existing laboratory constraints.

Abstract: Atom interferometers are sensitive to oscillations of the masses and transition frequencies of atoms, which can be induced by axion dark matter. The main limitation is the sensitivity to gravity gradient noise (GGN). We propose a dual-species atom interferometry sequence that provides a suppression of GGN by up to three orders of magnitude and a sensitivity enhancement via the mass difference of the two atoms. We show that, under realistic experimental assumptions, a 10m-baseline dual-species atom interferometer is more sensitive to the axion gluon coupling than a 100m single-species gradiometer. Including a comprehensive noise analysis, we demonstrate that the Very Long Baseline Atom Interferometer (VLBAI) in Hannover with a baseline of 10m can probe the axion decay constant at levels almost competitive with leading laboratory constraints. The proposed 100m-baseline Atom Interferometer CERN Experiment (AICE) can extend this sensitivity by up to two orders of magnitude, surpassing laboratory constraints for axion masses of $10^{-19}\;\text{eV}/c^2 \lesssim m_a \lesssim 3\times10^{-15}\;\text{eV}/c^2$.

</details>


### [86] [Dark Matter Velocimetry with Quantum Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15878)
*Miguel Angel Soto Alcaraz,Doojin Kim,Kyoungchul Kong,Myeonghun Park*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using two-node quantum sensor networks to probe the Galactic dark matter halo's velocity distribution via spatial phase coherence, showing that Earth's rotation breaks degeneracies between isotropic (SHM) and radially anisotropic (Gaia-inspired SHM++) halo models, enabling tests of Milky Way assembly history.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between smooth, isotropic dark matter halo models (e.g., Standard Halo Model) and structured, anisotropic models (e.g., Gaia-informed SHM++) using quantum sensor networks—thereby turning a dark matter detection into a probe of Galactic structure and formation history.

Method: Analyzes a minimal two-node quantum sensor network performing nonlocal exchange measurements to sample the characteristic function of the dark matter velocity distribution; incorporates daily/annual modulation, Earth’s rotation-induced baseline scanning, postselection, depolarization, and instrumental offset; employs likelihood analysis with signal-odd controls to separate astrophysical signal from flat backgrounds.

Result: Annual modulation alone cannot distinguish centered SHM from SHM++ due to identical phase waveforms when signal amplitude is unknown; Earth’s rotation breaks this degeneracy by varying the sensor baseline orientation relative to the Galactic frame, revealing direction-dependent coherence signatures of radial anisotropy.

Conclusion: Two-node quantum sensor networks—leveraging Earth’s rotation and modulation spectroscopy—can discriminate local dark matter velocity distributions, providing a new astrophysical probe of the Milky Way’s accretion history and halo substructure.

Abstract: Quantum sensor networks can use the spatial phase coherence of wavelike dark matter to probe not only particle properties but also the motion and structure of the Galactic halo. We explore this idea for the minimal two-node network, in which a nonlocal exchange measurement samples the characteristic function of the velocity distribution. We compare the Standard Halo Model (SHM) with a Gaia-inspired radially anisotropic component and their mixture (SHM++), taking into account daily and annual modulation, postselection, depolarizing events, and a flat instrumental offset. We show that annual modulation at one fixed local sidereal time has the same phase waveform for the centered SHM and Gaia-inspired SHM++ models, leaving them degenerate when the overall signal amplitude is unknown. Earth's rotation breaks this degeneracy by scanning the baseline through Galactic directions and exposing the direction-dependent coherence of an anisotropic population. Distinguishing these velocity distributions would test whether the local halo is smooth and nearly isotropic or contains a radially biased accreted component, turning a quantum-network detection into a probe of Milky Way structure. Our likelihood analysis shows how modulation and signal-odd controls separate this information from flat backgrounds and provides a path from model discrimination toward measurement of the local velocity distribution.

</details>


### [87] [Chains of Vector-like Leptons at Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15893)
*Tao Han,Tong Arthur Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vector-like leptons (VLLs) in chain flavor models, analyzing their production, decay, and discovery potential at current and future colliders (LHC, FCC-hh, lepton colliders); it links VLL mass splittings to fermion mass hierarchies and shows single VLL production via vector boson-lepton fusion directly probes new Yukawa couplings and hopping parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test chain flavor models that explain SM fermion mass hierarchy and mixing via vector-like lepton (VLL) multiplets whose mass differences encode the power-law pattern of SM fermion masses.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological study of VLL production (pair and single via vector boson-lepton fusion) and decay; collider sensitivity estimation using cross section calculations and kinematic thresholds for HL-LHC, FCC-hh, and e+e−/μ+μ− colliders.

Result: HL-LHC can probe chain scales up to 800–900 GeV; FCC-hh exceeds 2.5 TeV; lepton colliders enable clean VLL pair discovery at threshold and extend reach via Z-lepton fusion down to lower masses due to reduced kinematic threshold.

Conclusion: VLL chains are testable at near-future colliders; single production via vector boson-lepton fusion provides a direct probe of the new Yukawa couplings and hopping parameters underlying fermion mass generation in chain flavor models.

Abstract: Vector-like leptons (VLLs) are predicted in the chain flavor models, which possess characteristic features to explain the mass hierarchy and mixing of the fermions in the Standard Model (SM). We investigate the production and decay of the VLL chains and quantify the discovery potential at the LHC, FCC-hh, and lepton colliders. The resonant mass sets the new physics scale responsible for the fermion mass generation. The observation of the VLL multiplets associated with each lepton flavor yields the chain structure. The mass difference among the VLL multiplets determines the hopping parameter that gives the power-law pattern for the observed SM fermion masses. The single VLL production via vector boson-lepton fusion directly measures the partial width, equivalently the new Yukawa coupling and the VLL mass hopping parameter. At hadron colliders, the leading production mechanism is pair production via their gauge interaction. We find that HL-LHC may have the sensitivity to reach the chain scale of $800-900\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and FCC-hh reaches $>2.5\,\mathrm{TeV}$. In $e^+e^-$ and $\mu^+\mu^-$ colliders, VLL pair production is a clear discovery channel once the threshold is reached. Single production via $Z$-lepton fusion becomes more accessible owing to a lower mass threshold and extends signal discovery to the collider kinematic limit.

</details>


### [88] [Probing gluon saturation with forward heavy-flavor correlations in proton-nucleus collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15897)
*Farid Salazar,Björn Schenke,Wenbin Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates heavy-meson pair azimuthal correlations in p+p and p+Pb collisions using the Color Glass Condensate framework, incorporating small-x evolution and Sudakov resummation; it successfully describes LHCb D-Dbar and b-hadron data and predicts both suppression and broadening of the away-side peak in p+Pb vs. p+p—key signatures of gluon saturation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe gluon saturation via heavy-flavor azimuthal correlations, leveraging heavy quarks' sensitivity to small-x gluons, mass-induced suppression of collinear radiation, and faithful momentum inheritance by heavy mesons—offering cleaner access to partonic kinematics than light hadrons.

Method: First full-azimuth calculation of heavy-meson (D-Dbar, b-hadron) pair correlations within the Color Glass Condensate effective theory, combining JIMWLK/BK small-x evolution initialized from HERA-constrained gluon distributions with Sudakov resummation near ΔΦ = π.

Result: Good agreement with LHCb measurements of D-Dbar and b-hadron correlations; prediction of ~15% away-side yield suppression and 10–25% Gaussian peak broadening in p+Pb relative to p+p at ΔΦ ≈ π.

Conclusion: Heavy-flavor azimuthal correlations—especially their away-side broadening—are a robust, complementary probe of gluon saturation, distinct from light-flavor observables, and experimentally viable at the LHC.

Abstract: We investigate heavy-flavor azimuthal correlations as probes of gluon saturation in high-energy $p$+$p$ and $p$+Pb collisions within the Color Glass Condensate effective theory. Heavy flavor has several advantages: heavy-quark production is especially sensitive to the small-$x$ gluon distribution of the target, while the quark mass suppresses collinear radiation. Further, the heavy meson carries a large fraction of the momentum of its parent quark, so that the meson pair follows the partonic kinematics more closely than for light hadrons. We present the first calculation of heavy-meson pair correlations over the full azimuthal range that combines small-$x$ evolution from an initial condition constrained by HERA data with Sudakov resummation near the back-to-back limit. It provides a good description of the $D$-$\bar D$ and $b$-hadron pair correlations measured by LHCb. In contrast to the light-flavor case, we predict, in addition to a suppression of the away-side yield in $p$+Pb relative to $p$+$p$ collisions by about $15\%$, a broadening of the peak by approximately $10\%$ to $25\%$ for Gaussian fits near $\Delta\Phi=\pi$, which makes heavy-flavor correlations a promising observable in the search for gluon saturation.

</details>


### [89] [Polarized gluon TMD fragmentation into spin-1 quarkonium at NLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15918)
*Alessia Bongallino,Miguel G. Echevarria,Samuel F. Romera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the full set of gluon transverse-momentum-dependent fragmentation functions (TMDFFs) into spin-1 quarkonium (e.g., J/ψ) at next-to-leading order (NLO) in QCD, including all gluon and quarkonium polarization states and relevant NRQCD color-octet channels; results validate known collinear limits and LO TMDFFs, and provide novel NLO polarized TMDFFs essential for quarkonium polarization studies at EIC and e+e− colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precision studies of quarkonium polarization in high-energy collisions—particularly in jets at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and e+e− annihilation—by providing the complete, perturbatively calculable gluon TMDFFs into spin-1 quarkonia, which require full spin and color structure beyond collinear approximations.

Method: Perturbative QCD calculation within the NRQCD factorization framework at NLO (O(α_s^2)) in α_s and up to leading order in the heavy-quark velocity v; includes all polarization configurations of the fragmenting gluon and J^{PC}=1^{--} quarkonium; incorporates color-octet channels {}^3S_1^{[8]}, {}^1S_0^{[8]}, and {}^3P_J^{[8]}; exploits spin-1 symmetry constraints for generality.

Result: Complete set of gluon TMDFFs into spin-1 quarkonium at NLO, including previously unknown polarized TMDFFs and their full transverse-momentum dependence; consistency verified with known collinear fragmentation functions (up to δ(1−z) subtleties) and LO TMDFFs; unpolarized NLO TMDFFs cross-checked against prior work.

Conclusion: The computed NLO gluon TMDFFs constitute the first complete, systematically consistent perturbative input for spin-resolved quarkonium production in TMD-sensitive processes; the formalism extends to generic spin-1 hadron fragmentation, enhancing predictive power for quarkonium polarization observables at current and future colliders.

Abstract: We compute the complete set of gluon transverse-momentum-dependent fragmentation functions (TMDFFs) into quarkonium through next-to-leading order in perturbative QCD. We account for all polarization configurations of both the fragmenting gluon and the produced $J^{\rm PC}=1^{--}$ quarkonium. Within NRQCD, we include all color-octet channels that contribute through $\mathcal O(\alpha_s^2)$ at the considered order in the heavy-quark relative velocity $v$, namely ${}^3S_1^{[8]}$, ${}^1S_0^{[8]}$ and ${}^3P_J^{[8]}$. Several structures of the calculation follow only from the spin-1 nature of the final-state hadron, so our framework applies more generally to the fragmentation of a gluon into any spin-1 hadron. Our results agree with all known collinear fragmentation functions, up to differences in $\delta(1-z)$ terms that we discuss, and with the TMDFFs at LO, and we cross-check the unpolarized TMDFFs at NLO against previous calculations. Most of the polarized TMDFFs, and their full transverse-momentum dependence, are presented here for the first time. They provide the perturbative input for studies of quarkonium polarization in jets, at the EIC and in $e^+e^-$ annihilation.

</details>


### [90] [The Curious Case of Anapole Dark Matter at LZ: Minimal, Elastic and Testable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15957)
*Sourav Bera,Suvam Maharana,Poulami Mondal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that stable Majorana fermions interacting via the anapole moment—a momentum-dependent electromagnetic interaction—can explain a single high-energy nuclear-recoil event observed by LZ, while simultaneously satisfying cosmological dark matter abundance, collider, and indirect detection constraints; it is testable with future LZ data and XLZD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a minimal, electromagnetically interacting dark matter candidate consistent with the anomalous high-energy LZ recoil event and standard cosmological/phenomenological constraints.

Method: Theoretical modeling of elastic scattering of anapole-coupled Majorana fermion dark matter off nuclei, including calculation of the momentum-dependent differential recoil spectrum, comparison with LZ event data and background expectations, relic density computation via thermal freeze-out, and consistency checks against LHC searches and gamma-ray indirect detection limits.

Result: Anapole dark matter with mass ~300 GeV and specific coupling strength explains the LZ high-energy event, yields correct relic abundance, and evades current experimental bounds.

Conclusion: Elastically scattering anapole dark matter is a viable, minimal, and testable explanation for the LZ anomaly and the dark matter abundance, with clear predictions for upcoming experiments.

Abstract: Majorana fermions can carry neither charge nor dipole moments, leaving the anapole moment as their leading electromagnetic interaction. A stable Majorana fermion is therefore a minimal realization of electromagnetically interacting dark matter. The momentum dependence of the anapole interaction produces a harder recoil spectrum than standard momentum-independent interactions. We show that this makes elastically scattering anapole dark matter a viable interpretation of the single high-energy nuclear-recoil event recorded by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment, in a region where almost no background is expected. Curiously, the same coupling that fits the LZ data also yields the observed dark matter abundance at a mass of about 300 GeV, and is consistent with collider searches and indirect detection. The scenario is testable, predicting further events at lower energies, which the full LZ run will probe and a next-generation detector such as XLZD will decisively test.

</details>


### [91] [Matching 2HDM to HEFT in triple Higgs boson production from WBF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15979)
*L. Barreiro,D. Domenech,D. Garcia-Tejada,M. Herrero,S. Salazar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper matches HEFT coefficients to 2HDM parameters for triple Higgs production via WW→hhh, capturing non-decoupling effects from heavy Higgs bosons (H, A, H±), and identifies a large deviation from SM predictions—especially under misalignment (c_{β−α} ≠ 0)—as a potential collider signature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically capture non-decoupling contributions from heavy 2HDM scalars (H, A, H±) to the WW → hhh scattering amplitude, which persist even when those states are too heavy to be directly produced, by matching to HEFT and identifying observable deviations from the SM.

Method: Analytic matching of the full 2HDM tree-level WW → hhh scattering amplitude onto the HEFT Lagrangian in the limit m_H, m_A, m_H± ≫ m_h; solving for HEFT Wilson coefficients a, b, c, κ₃, κ₄ in terms of 2HDM parameters c_{β−α}, tan β, and m₁₂; comparing with double Higgs results; computing σ(WW → hhh) using matched coefficients.

Result: Explicit analytic expressions for HEFT coefficients a, b, c, κ₃, κ₄ in terms of c_{β−α}, tan β, m₁₂; demonstration that non-decoupling effects scale as O(1) rather than suppressed by inverse powers of heavy masses; numerical cross section shows large enhancement over SM when c_{β−α} ≠ 0 (misalignment), even for very heavy H/A/H±.

Conclusion: The HEFT parameterization with coefficients matched to 2HDM encodes the dominant non-decoupling physics of heavy Higgs sectors in triple Higgs production; the resulting sizable σ(WW → hhh) deviation from SM—driven by misalignment—is a robust, collider-accessible probe of otherwise inaccessible heavy 2HDM states.

Abstract: In this work we compare the Higgs Effective Field Theory (HEFT) predictions with the Two Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM) predictions for triple Higgs production via $WW \rightarrow hhh$ scattering, under the hypothesis of very heavy Higgs bosons $H$, $A$, and $H^\pm$, and assuming the lightest one $h$ has the observed mass in the experiments, $m_h \simeq 125$ GeV. By requiring the matching of the two corresponding scattering amplitudes for this $WW \rightarrow hhh$ process in the limit of very heavy mass for these bosons, we find the solutions for the leading order HEFT coefficients. Concretely, we determine the coefficients $a$, $b$, $c$, $\kappa_3$ and $\kappa_4$ defining the effective interactions $WWh$, $WWhh$, $WWhhh$, $hhh$ and $hhhh$, respectively, in terms of the 2HDM input parameters, $c_{\beta-\alpha}$, $\tan \beta$ and $m_{12}$. We will also compare these solutions with the corresponding solutions found for the case of double Higgs production. The solutions presented here for the matched coefficients summarize the non-decoupling effects from the heavy modes, $H$, $A$ and $H^\pm$ in the scattering process $WW \rightarrow hhh$ and provide the most relevant contributions to the cross section $\sigma(WW \rightarrow hhh)$ that do not decrease as inverse powers of the heavy masses. In the last part, we will also discuss the numerical predictions of the cross section $\sigma(WW \rightarrow hhh)$ in terms of the HEFT-matched coefficients. The focus of this discussion is set on the big gap between the HEFT-matched prediction and the SM prediction due to the non-decoupling behaviour occurring whenever misalignment is assumed in the 2HDM, i.e. for $c_{\beta-\alpha} \neq 0$. This big gap could serve as potential signals of the 2HDM heavy modes, $H$, $A$ and $H^\pm$, at future high energy colliders, even if they are too heavy and cannot be produced at these colliders.

</details>


### [92] [KNO functions for dipole-dipole scattering from large Pomeron loops: log-normal tails and a test in exactly solvable models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16081)
*Michael Roa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reexamines multiplicity distributions derived from resummed Pomeron loops in QCD using BK evolution and AGK cutting rules, showing that KNO-scaling functions have log-normal (not Gaussian) tails expressible via the Lambert W function; it demonstrates—via exactly solvable 0D models—that while amplitude resummation reproduces exact S-matrix well, the associated AGK-derived multiplicity distributions fail at high energy due to a fundamental decoupling between rare (amplitude-determining) and typical (multiplicity-determining) cascade configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To critically assess the validity of deriving multiplicity distributions from resummed Pomeron loop series (based on BK + AGK), particularly the assumed KNO scaling and entropy relation, by confronting analytical predictions with exact results from solvable zero-dimensional models.

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis of the BK and dipole-dipole KNO functions using Gaussian approximation of the loop series and Lambert W function representation; derivation of log-normal tail behavior; comparison with two exactly solvable unitary toy models in zero transverse dimensions, where exact S-matrix and multiplicity distributions are known.

Result: KNO functions exhibit log-normal tails: lnΨ_BK ∼ −½ ln²ξ and lnΨ_dd ∼ −¼ ln²ξ, expressible in closed form via Lambert W; KNO scaling requires discarding power-like coefficient factors, leading to non-unique summation and sign-changing alternatives; in 0D models, resummed amplitude matches exact S-matrix within 2.5–10%, but AGK-derived multiplicity fails beyond dilute regime—exact mean multiplicity grows as a power of rapidity (no KNO scaling), entropy grows as ½ ln⟨n⟩, and multiplicity is governed by typical, not rare, configurations.

Conclusion: The agreement between resummed amplitudes and exact S-matrices does not imply reliability of associated AGK-based multiplicity distributions; KNO scaling and the entropy-log-gluon-structure-function relation break down at high energy due to the distinct statistical origins of amplitude (rare events) and multiplicity (typical events), invalidating the standard loop-resummation + AGK prescription for multiplicity.

Abstract: The sum of large Pomeron loops in QCD, based on the $t$-channel unitarity and on the solution of the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation in the saturation region, has been combined with the AGK (Abramovsky, Gribov and Kancheli) cutting rules to obtain multiplicity distributions with KNO (Koba, Nielsen and Olesen) scaling and an entropy equal to the logarithm of the gluon structure function. We reexamine these distributions for the BK amplitude and for dipole-dipole scattering. The KNO functions that follow from the Gaussian representation of the asymptotic series have log-normal and not Gaussian tails, $\ln\Psi_{\rm BK}\simeq-\frac{1}{2}\ln^2\xi$ and $\ln\Psi_{\rm dd}\simeq-\frac{1}{4}\ln^2\xi$, and we give them in closed form in terms of the Lambert function. They are log-normal mixtures of the distributions of one and two BFKL cascades, and the summation is not unique. The KNO scaling requires that power-like factors in the coefficients of the series be discarded, and the alternative contour representation leads to functions that change sign. We then test the approach in two exactly solvable models in zero transverse dimensions. In the unitary toy model we confirm that the resummed series reproduces the exact $S$-matrix to within 2.5-10%, while the $S$-matrix changes by many orders of magnitude. Nevertheless, the multiplicity distribution obtained from the same series with the AGK rules agrees with the exact one only as long as both cascades are dilute. At higher energies the exact mean grows as a power of rapidity, the KNO scaling is absent, and the entropy in the rest frame of the target grows as one half of the logarithm of the mean multiplicity. The amplitude is determined by rare configurations of the cascades and the multiplicity by the typical ones, so that the agreement of the amplitudes does not validate the multiplicity distributions.

</details>


### [93] [Connecting Electroweak Fine-Tuning, Tens-of-GeV Dark Matter, and Exotic Higgs Decays in the GNMSSM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16191)
*Zhaoxia Heng,Haijing Zhou,Fei Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a natural parameter region in the GNMSSM where electroweak fine-tuning is mild (Δ ≤ 40), a ~10–20 GeV singlino serves as viable dark matter, and the SM-like Higgs exhibits sizable exotic decays h → aₛaₛ (7–25% BR), with two SM-like Higgs scenarios (h₁ or h₂) both consistent with LZ 2024 constraints; LHC already excludes part of the parameter space, but substantial regions—especially in the h₂ case—remain testable at HL-LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile electroweak naturalness, a light singlino dark matter candidate (~tens of GeV), and observable exotic Higgs decays within the GNMSSM, addressing tensions between fine-tuning, direct detection limits, and collider constraints.

Method: Parameter scan in the GNMSSM imposing constraints from electroweak fine-tuning (Δ ≤ 40), singlino DM relic density and direct detection (LZ 2024), Higgs signal strengths, and LHC searches for h → aₛaₛ → bbτ⁺τ⁻; classification into h₁-case (lightest CP-even Higgs SM-like) and h₂-case (second-lightest SM-like).

Result: A natural region emerges with m_S̃ < m_B̃ < m_H̃ and μ_tot ∈ [200, 500] GeV; Br(h → aₛaₛ) = 7–25% for m_aₛ ≈ 10–20 GeV; LZ-compatible solutions exist in both h₁ and h₂ cases; LHC excludes ~12% (h₁) and ~10% (h₂) of samples for 12 ≤ m_aₛ ≤ 15.5 GeV; significant h₂-case parameter space survives and is accessible at HL-LHC.

Conclusion: The GNMSSM can simultaneously realize natural electroweak symmetry breaking, viable singlino dark matter, and observable exotic Higgs decays; the h₂-case offers a particularly promising and testable target for HL-LHC and future DM experiments.

Abstract: In the general next-to-minimal supersymmetric standard model (GNMSSM), we connect electroweak fine-tuning, a singlino dark matter candidate with mass around tens of GeV, and Exotic Higgs Decays, identifying a natural region characterized by the mass hierarchy $m_{\tilde{S}}<m_{\tilde{B}}<m_{\tilde{H}}$ and $\mu_{\rm tot}\in [200, 500]$ GeV. For fine-tuning measure $\Delta \leq 40$, the branching ratio $\mathrm{Br}(h\to a_sa_s)$ falls in the range 7\%$-$25\%, with the pseudoscalar mass $m_{a_s}$ lying mostly between 10 and 20 GeV. This region splits into two viable scenarios consistent with LZ 2024 direct detection: one where the lightest CP-even Higgs $h_1$ is SM-like ($h_1$ case), and another where the second-lightest $h_2$ is SM-like ($h_2$ case). LHC searches for $h\to a_sa_s \to b\bar{b}\tau^+\tau^-$ exclude 12\% (10\%) of samples with 12 GeV $\leq m_{a_s} \leq$ 15.5 GeV in the $h_1$ ($h_2$) case, while a substantial fraction remains accessible at the HL-LHC in the $h_2$ case. Our analysis unifies naturalness, dark matter, and observable exotic Higgs decays, yielding concrete targets for future experiments.

</details>


### [94] [Probing the Molecular Nature of $D_{sJ}(2860)$ and $D_{s3}(2860)$ through Their Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16276)
*Qing Lu,Dan Dan Feng,Cai Cheng,Yin Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the charmed-strange resonances D_{sJ}(2860) and D_{s3}(2860) are molecular states—specifically S-wave D₁K or P-wave D*K*—by analyzing their strong decays using an effective Lagrangian approach and LHCb data from B_s⁰ → D̄⁰K⁻π⁺. It finds that assigning both as P-wave D*K* molecules best explains the partial width ratio R for D_{s3}(2860) and predicts R ≈ 1.26–1.29 for D_{sJ}(2860), favoring this interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the internal structure of the observed charmed-strange resonances D_{sJ}(2860) and D_{s3}(2860), whose quantum numbers and nature (e.g., conventional c\bar{s} mesons vs. hadronic molecules) remain ambiguous; specifically, to test molecular interpretations against LHCb angular and width measurements.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach to compute strong decays of candidate molecular configurations: S-wave D₁K and P-wave D*K* for D_{sJ}(2860), and P-wave D*K* for D_{s3}(2860); constrained by fitting LHCb data on B_s⁰ → D̄⁰K⁻π⁺ angular distributions and decay widths to extract molecular coupling constants and cutoff Λ; subsequent prediction of total widths and partial width ratios R = Γ(D*K)/Γ(DK).

Result: The S-wave D₁K assignment for D_{sJ}(2860) marginally better fits its total width, but the combined D₁K + D*K* assignment is ruled out by predicted R_{D_{s3}(2860)} exceeding experimental upper limits; in contrast, the pure P-wave D*K* assignment for both states yields R_{D_{s3}(2860)} consistent with data and predicts R_{D_{sJ}(2860)} ≈ 1.26–1.29.

Conclusion: The P-wave D*K* molecular interpretation is favored for both D_{sJ}(2860) and D_{s3}(2860), as it consistently reproduces the experimentally constrained partial width ratio for D_{s3}(2860) and yields a testable prediction for D_{sJ}(2860); future measurement of R_{D_{sJ}(2860)} will provide a decisive test.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the molecular interpretations of $D_{sJ}(2860)$ and $D_{s3}(2860)$ by studying their strong decays within the effective Lagrangian approach. We consider two possible configurations for $D_{sJ}(2860)$: an $S$-wave $D_1K$ molecule and a $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecule, while treating $D_{s3}(2860)$ as a $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecule. By fitting the LHCb-measured angular distribution and decay widths in $B_s^0\to\bar{D}^0K^-\pi^+$, we determine the molecular coupling constants and cutoff parameter $\Lambda$. Using these parameters, we calculate the total decay widths and the ratios $R$ of the partial widths into the $D^*K$ and $DK$ channels. For $D_{sJ}(2860)$, the $S$-wave $D_1K$ assignment provides a slightly better description of the measured total width than the $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ assignment. However, the combined $D_1K$ and $D^*K^*$ assignments for $D_{sJ}(2860)$ and $D_{s3}(2860)$, respectively, are disfavored by the predicted $R_{D_{s3}(2860)}$, which exceeds the experimental upper limit. In contrast, assigning both states as $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecules yields good agreement with the measured $R_{D_{s3}(2860)}$ and predicts $R_{D_{sJ}(2860)}\simeq 1.26$--$1.29$. These results favor the $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecular interpretation for both states, which can be further tested through future measurements of $R_{D_{sJ}(2860)}$.

</details>


### [95] [High Energy Asymptotics of Quark-Gluon Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16298)
*Jian Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives infrared evolution equations for twist-3 quark-gluon correlation functions (Qiu-Sterman and Boer-Mulders) at double-logarithmic accuracy, revealing shared small-x intercepts with twist-2 counterparts but additional 1/ln(1/x) suppression from coherent gluon color structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the high-energy (small-x) behavior of quantum interference between partonic configurations in the proton—specifically flavor-nonsinglet quark-gluon correlations governing transverse spin phenomena—and to enable reliable extrapolations for Electron Ion Collider experiments.

Method: Recursive factorization at double logarithmic accuracy, incorporating coherent gluon insertion; derivation of infrared evolution equations for twist-3 Qiu-Sterman and chiral-odd Boer-Mulders functions.

Result: Twist-3 quark-gluon correlation functions share the leading small-x intercepts of their twist-2 analogues but exhibit an extra 1/ln(1/x) suppression arising from the color structure of the coherent gluon insertion.

Conclusion: The derived evolution equations provide a quantitative basis for small-x extrapolations of transverse-momentum-dependent and spin-dependent quark-gluon correlations, critical for interpreting future EIC data.

Abstract: The internal structure of the proton is encoded not only in single parton densities, but also in the quantum interference between distinct partonic configurations. We study the high energy behavior of this interference by focusing on flavor-nonsinglet quark-gluon correlations relevant to transverse spin phenomena. By establishing recursive factorization at double logarithmic accuracy in the presence of a coherent gluon insertion, we derive infrared evolution equations for the Qiu-Sterman function and its chiral odd Boer-Mulders counterpart. We find that these twist-3 functions share the leading small $x$ intercepts of their respective twist-2 counterparts, but acquire an additional $1/\ln(1/x)$ suppression due to the color structure of the coherent gluon insertion. These results may guide small $x$ extrapolations of quark-gluon correlations at the Electron Ion Collider.

</details>


### [96] [Disentangling the Effects of Phase-Specific Transport Properties on Elliptic Flow Centrality Dependence in the AMPT Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16394)
*Yao Zhang,Xipei Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the AMPT model to disentangle contributions of partonic and hadronic phases to elliptic flow $v_2$ in Au+Au collisions at 200 GeV, finding that hadronic rescattering dominates $v_2$ in peripheral collisions and that increasing hadronic cross sections improves agreement with STAR data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate the distinct roles of partonic and hadronic transport properties—specifically shear viscosity $\eta/s$, phase transition temperature $T_{tr}$, and hadronic scattering cross sections—in shaping charged hadron elliptic flow $v_2$ across collision centrality.

Method: Systematic variation of key transport parameters within the AMPT model: (i) shear viscosity over entropy density $\eta/s$ in the partonic phase, (ii) parton-hadron phase transition temperature $T_{tr}$, and (iii) hadronic scattering cross sections; centrality-dependent $v_2$ is computed and compared to STAR experimental data.

Result: Hadronic rescattering contributes increasingly to $v_2$ with decreasing centrality (i.e., more peripheral collisions), becoming dominant there; scaling hadronic cross sections by ×4 significantly enhances $v_2$ in peripheral collisions and improves agreement with STAR data, while the observed flattening of $v_2$ at high centrality suggests a larger effective hadronic cross section.

Conclusion: The hadronic phase plays a crucial and centrality-dependent role in anisotropic flow, especially in peripheral (small-system-like) heavy-ion collisions; interpreting $v_2$ requires careful accounting of hadronic transport—not just partonic QGP properties.

Abstract: The centrality dependence of charged hadron elliptic flow $v_{2}$ in Au+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=200~\rm{GeV}$ is studied using the AMPT model with phase-specific control of partonic and hadronic transport properties. By separately varying the shear viscosity $\eta/s$, phase transition temperature $T_{tr}$, and hadronic scattering cross sections, we isolate their distinct effects on anisotropic flow. Hadronic rescattering is found to contribute increasingly to $v_{2}$ with centrality, with the contribution becoming dominant in peripheral collisions. Scaling up hadronic cross sections by a factor of four substantially amplifies $v_{2}$ in peripheral collisions and improves agreement with STAR data, suggesting that the flattening of $v_{2}$ at high centrality may reflect a larger effective hadronic cross section and highlighting the significant role of the hadronic phase in small systems.

</details>


### [97] [Probing the internal structure of the $ΩΩ$ dibaryon via its weak decays and lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16508)
*Qi-Fang Lü,Deng-Wang Tang,Yu-Bing Dong,Peng-Nian Shen,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the weak decay properties of the ΩΩ dibaryon using an effective Lagrangian approach, computing widths for three- and four-body final states across a range of binding energies (1–120 MeV); it finds distinct decay patterns and lifetime trends—e.g., dominant three-body channels shift with binding energy, double-weak-vertex contributions are negligible, and lifetimes increase from ~10⁻¹⁰ s to ~10⁻⁹ s—providing key discriminators between compact hexaquark and molecular structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the internal structure of the ΩΩ dibaryon and guide experimental searches at RHIC and LHC by establishing theoretically predicted weak decay signatures that distinguish between compact hexaquark and weakly bound molecular interpretations.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach; computation of partial and total decay widths for three-body (e.g., ΩΛK̄, ΩΞπ, ΞΞK̄) and four-body (e.g., ΛK̄ΛK̄) final states; inclusion of single- and double-weak-vertex diagrams; systematic variation of binding energy (1–120 MeV).

Result: For weakly bound states (Eb = 1, 20 MeV), three-body decays dominate, with channel hierarchy shifting from ΩΛK̄ → ΩΞπ; for deeply bound states (Eb = 100, 120 MeV), ΩΛK̄ is kinematically forbidden and ΩΞπ/ΞΞK̄ dominate; ΛK̄ΛK̄ is negligible; total lifetime increases from ~10⁻¹⁰ s to ~10⁻⁹ s with binding energy.

Conclusion: The binding-energy-dependent decay patterns and lifetimes provide robust theoretical benchmarks for experimental identification of the ΩΩ dibaryon and serve as clear discriminators between compact hexaquark and molecular structural models.

Abstract: The weak decay properties of the $\Omega\Omega$ dibaryon play a crucial role in probing its internal structure and guiding experimental efforts. Using an effective Lagrangian approach, we systematically study its weak decays and lifetime by computing the widths for both three- and four-body final states, with amplitudes receiving contributions from single- and double-weak-vertex diagrams. We investigate the dependence of the decay properties on the binding energy ranging from a few MeV to over $100$ MeV. For weakly bound states ($E_b = 1$ and $20$ MeV), the three-body channels $\Omega \Lambda \bar{K}$, $\Omega \Xi \pi$, and $\Xi \Xi \bar{K}$ exhaust essentially the entire width, with the largest channel shifting from $\Omega \Lambda \bar{K}$ at $E_b=1$ MeV to $\Omega \Xi \pi$ at $E_b=20$ MeV. For deeply bound states ($E_b = 100$ and $120$ MeV), the $\Omega \Lambda \bar{K}$ channel is forbidden, and the dominant modes are $\Omega \Xi \pi$ and $\Xi \Xi \bar{K}$, while the double-weak-vertex channel $\Lambda \bar{K} \Lambda \bar{K}$ is found to be negligibly small. As the binding energy increases, the total lifetime extends from the order of $10^{-10}~\mathrm{s}$ to $10^{-9}~\mathrm{s}$. These distinct differences in decay patterns and lifetimes provide valuable theoretical reference values for experimental searches at RHIC and the LHC, and offer a clear discriminator between the compact hexaquark and weakly bound molecular interpretations.

</details>


### [98] [A perturbative construction of a modular $S_3$ model for quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16545)
*Gabriele Battimelli,Davide Meloni*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an $S_3$ modular-invariant supersymmetric flavour model for quarks, using only the complex modulus τ (and its gCP symmetry) as the source of flavour structure and CP violation—no flavons or fine-tuned superpotential coefficients. It reproduces 8 quark observables with just 10 free parameters (including Re(τ), Im(τ)), achieving the minimal parameter count among known $S_3$ modular quark models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a predictive, minimally tuned supersymmetric flavour model for quarks using modular invariance—eliminating flavons and arbitrary couplings while explaining mass hierarchies and CP violation solely via modular forms and the gCP-symmetric modulus τ.

Method: Builds a Γ₂ ≅ S₃ modular-invariant supersymmetric effective theory where quark masses arise from modular forms of different weights; imposes generalized CP (gCP) symmetry to constrain τ and ensure it is the sole source of CP violation; fits 8 dimensionless observables (4 mass ratios + 4 CKM parameters) using 10 real parameters (including τ’s real and imaginary parts).

Result: Reproduces all 8 quark-sector observables within 1σ experimental uncertainty using only 10 free real parameters; establishes τ as the unique source of CP violation and flavour structure; achieves the lowest parameter count for any S₃ modular quark model reported to date.

Conclusion: The model demonstrates that modular invariance with gCP symmetry provides a highly economical and predictive framework for quark flavour, requiring no flavons or ad hoc couplings—setting a new benchmark for minimality and testability in modular flavour model building.

Abstract: We present a modular-invariant supersymmetric model of flavour for quark masses and mixing, based on the group $\Gamma_2\cong S_3$. Hierarchies in the quark sector follow from the symmetric structure of the model, as they are generated by modular forms of different weights in the mass matrices, and no severe tuning of free superpotential coefficients is needed. Moreover, no flavons are employed besides the modulus $\tau$ which is, given the gCP symmetry imposed on the model, the only source of CP violation. 8 dimensionless observables (4 mass ratios and 4 CKM parameters) are reproduced by making use of 10 free model parameters (including real and imaginary part of the modulus) within the $1\sigma$ range making our model, to the best of our knowledge, the $S_3$ modular model for quarks with the fewest free parameters.

</details>


### [99] [Inelastic Scalar Dark Matter in a Gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ Model and its LZ Phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16553)
*Changjie Dai,Linyun He,Yonghua Wang,Wei Chao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic scalar dark matter model within a gauged U(1)_{B-L} framework to explain the high-energy recoil candidate observed by LZ, featuring two real scalar DM components (φ₁, φ₂) from a complex scalar Φ, enabling exothermic and endothermic scattering; it computes relic abundance consistency and identifies best-fit parameter regions via three-bin likelihood analysis of nuclear recoil spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the high-energy nuclear recoil candidate reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment using a theoretically motivated, minimal extension of the Standard Model — specifically, inelastic scalar dark matter in a gauged U(1)_{B-L} model — which naturally accommodates both exothermic and endothermic scattering while addressing relic density and direct detection constraints.

Method: Constructs a U(1)_{B-L} gauge model with two complex scalars: S (for symmetry breaking) and Φ (whose real components φ₁ and φ₂ constitute two-component scalar DM); computes thermal freeze-out dynamics for both components; determines viable parameter space satisfying Planck-measured relic abundance; calculates differential nuclear recoil spectra for xenon targets; performs a three-bin binned likelihood analysis on LZ data to identify best-fit points in both exothermic and endothermic regimes.

Result: Finds that φ₁ and φ₂ attain comparable abundances at freeze-out; identifies physically allowed parameter regions reproducing Ωh² ≈ 0.12; obtains statistically significant best-fit points for the LZ excess in both exothermic (long-lived φ₁ coexisting with φ₂) and endothermic (dominant φ₂ abundance) scenarios; predicts distinguishable spectral shapes between the two cases.

Conclusion: The two-component inelastic scalar DM in the gauged U(1)_{B-L} model is a viable and predictive explanation for the LZ high-energy excess; it reconciles thermal production, direct detection phenomenology, and gauge coupling unification motivations; future high-statistics LZ or XENONnT data can discriminate between exothermic and endothermic interpretations via spectral shape analysis.

Abstract: We study inelastic scalar dark matter (DM) in a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model motivated by the high-energy recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ). The model introduces two complex scalars, $\Phi$ and $S$, charged under $U(1)_{B-L}$. The scalar $S$ is responsible for spontaneous symmetry breaking, while the two real components of $\Phi$, $\phi_1$ and $\phi_2$, serve as DM. This two-component DM framework accommodates both exothermic and endothermic scenarios. In the exothermic scenario, the lifetime of the heavier state $\phi_1$ exceeds the age of the Universe, and both states contribute to the present-day DM abundance. In the endothermic scenario, the abundance comes almost entirely from the lighter state $\phi_2$. We investigate the thermal history of the dark sector and find that the two scalars have approximately equal abundances at freeze-out. We identify parameter regions that reproduce the observed relic abundance. We calculate the nuclear recoil spectra, perform a three-bin likelihood analysis, and obtain the best-fit points interpreting the LZ signal in both scenarios.

</details>


### [100] [Low-lying doubly charmed strange baryons via QCD sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16576)
*Hui-Min Yang,Hua-Xing Chen,Xuan Luo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates masses and radiative transition widths of doubly heavy baryons (cc and bb) using QCD sum rules and light-cone sum rules, predicting hyperfine splittings and decay widths for spin-3/2 → spin-1/2 γ transitions, with implications for experimental searches at LHCb and Belle II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine the masses and electromagnetic radiative transition properties of low-lying doubly heavy baryons (Ξ_cc, Ω_cc, and their bottom analogs), especially to support experimental identification of yet-unobserved spin-3/2 excited states via γ decays.

Method: QCD sum rules and light-cone sum rules (LCSR) are employed with mixed interpolating currents; electromagnetic form factors are derived within LCSR framework; magnetic-dipole (M1) and electric-quadrupole (E2) contributions are separately evaluated; uncertainties are estimated via standard parameter variations.

Result: Predicted masses: Ξ_cc = 3.62⁺⁰·¹⁴₋₀.₁₅ GeV, Ξ*_cc = 3.72⁺⁰·¹⁴₋₀.₁₃ GeV, Ω_cc = 3.72⁺⁰·¹³₋₀.₁₅ GeV, Ω*_cc = 3.82⁺⁰·¹³₋₀.₁₅ GeV; bb counterparts ~10.27–10.34 GeV; hyperfine splittings ~101 MeV (Ξ_cc) and ~99 MeV (Ω_cc); radiative widths: Ξ*_cc⁺⁺→Ξ_cc⁺⁺γ = 5.25⁺⁸·⁵⁷₋₃.₆₂ keV, others in sub-keV range; M1 dominates; E2 negligible; light- and heavy-quark photon emission interfere significantly.

Conclusion: The theoretical predictions—masses, hyperfine splittings, and radiative widths—are consistent with known doubly charmed baryons and provide quantitative benchmarks for discovering spin-3/2 doubly heavy baryons via radiative decays at current and future high-energy experiments.

Abstract: We systematically investigate the masses of the low-lying doubly heavy baryons and their $B^*(3/2^+)\to B(1/2^+)\gamma$ radiative transitions within QCD sum rules and light-cone sum rules (LCSR). By constructing mixed interpolating currents, we obtain $M_{\Xi_{cc}}=3.62^{+0.14}_{-0.15}$ GeV, $M_{\Xi_{cc}^*}=3.72^{+0.14}_{-0.13}$ GeV, $M_{\Omega_{cc}}=3.72^{+0.13}_{-0.15}$ GeV, and $M_{\Omega_{cc}^*}=3.82^{+0.13}_{-0.15}$ GeV, together with their doubly bottom partners around $10.27$--$10.34$ GeV. The extracted masses are compatible with the observed $\Xi_{cc}$ and $\Omega_{cc}$ states and predict hyperfine splittings of about $101^{+27}_{-11}$ MeV and $99^{+11}_{-12}$ MeV for the $\Xi_{cc}$ and $\Omega_{cc}$ systems, respectively. Using these inputs, we derive the LCSR for the electromagnetic form factors and obtain radiative widths $\Gamma(\Xi_{cc}^{*++}\to\Xi_{cc}^{++}\gamma)=5.25^{+8.57}_{-3.62}$ keV, $\Gamma(\Xi_{cc}^{*+}\to\Xi_{cc}^{+}\gamma)=0.31^{+0.84}_{-0.25}$ keV, and $\Gamma(\Omega_{cc}^{*+}\to\Omega_{cc}^{+}\gamma)=0.41^{+0.67}_{-0.30}$ keV, while the corresponding doubly bottom widths are at the $0.002$--$0.01$ keV level. We find that the magnetic-dipole contribution dominates, whereas the electric-quadrupole form factor is numerically small. The light- and heavy-quark photon-emission terms can interfere or enhance significantly, and the heavy-quark contribution cannot be neglected in radiative transitions. These results provide useful theoretical guidance for future searches for spin-$3/2$ doubly heavy baryons through radiative decay modes at LHCb, Belle II, and other experimental facilities.

</details>


### [101] [Extended $SO(10)$-inspired leptogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16604)
*Pasquale Di Bari,Xubin Hu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes hierarchical Dirac neutrino mass structures in seesaw models and shows they generically yield a highly hierarchical heavy neutrino spectrum; it identifies the intermediate-heavy neutrino (N₂) as the dominant source for thermal leptogenesis—capable of explaining baryon asymmetry—while relaxing constraints on light neutrino mass ordering, particularly enabling inverted ordering solutions with m₁ ≳ 10⁻⁵ eV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether hierarchical Dirac neutrino masses in seesaw models robustly lead to viable N₂-leptogenesis without fine-tuning, and to assess implications for neutrino mass ordering (especially inverted hierarchy) and minimal light neutrino mass bounds.

Method: Analytical derivation of heavy neutrino mass eigenvalues under hierarchical Dirac mass assumptions; consistency checks with current low-energy neutrino oscillation data (mixing angles, mass-squared differences); comparison of N₁- vs. N₂-dominated leptogenesis efficiency; extension of SO(10)-inspired scenarios by relaxing CKM-like mismatch assumptions between neutrino and charged-lepton Dirac mass matrices.

Result: Hierarchical Dirac masses inevitably produce hierarchical heavy neutrinos; N₂ mass naturally falls in the ~10⁹–10¹² GeV range suitable for successful thermal leptogenesis; N₁ contribution is negligible; inverted neutrino mass ordering becomes viable; lower bound on lightest neutrino mass relaxes from ~10⁻³ eV to ~10⁻⁵ eV; strong thermal leptogenesis still excludes inverted ordering.

Conclusion: N₂-leptogenesis is a generic, robust outcome of hierarchical seesaw models—not reliant on fine-tuned or SO(10)-specific assumptions—broadening its theoretical plausibility and compatibility with inverted neutrino mass ordering (except under stringent strong thermal conditions).

Abstract: We consider seesaw models with hierarchical Dirac neutrino masses. We show that, barring well known fine-tuned crossing level solutions and given current low energy neutrino experimental data, these always lead to a very hierarchical heavy neutrino spectrum. If the Dirac neutrino masses are assumed not to significantly differ from the up-quark masses, then the intermediate seesaw neutrino ($N_2$) mass falls in the correct range for its decays to explain the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the universe via ($N_2$-)leptogenesis, while the lightest seesaw neutrino decays give a negligible contribution. We show that these conclusions are valid independently of any additional condition on the Dirac neutrino mass matrix. In this way we extend the usual $SO(10)$-inspired leptogenesis scenario, assuming the mismatch between neutrino and charged lepton Dirac mass matrices at the level of the CKM matrix. We find that solutions for inverted ordered neutrino masses become viable. We still find a lower bound on the lightest neutrino mass, $m_1 \gtrsim 10^{-5}\,{\rm eV}$, but this is relaxed by two orders of magnitude. We also consider the special subset of solutions realising strong thermal leptogenesis within this extended scenario, finding that inverted ordering remains incompatible. Our results show how the emergence of the $N_2$-leptogenesis scenario within seesaw models is much more general than thought so far.

</details>


### [102] [Particle diffusion in magnetized QCD matter with a realistic equation of state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16656)
*Arnab Sarkar,Manu Kurian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives relativistic magnetohydrodynamic equations for particle number diffusion in a hot, interacting QCD medium under an external magnetic field, using a quasiparticle-based effective covariant description and Chapman–Enskog expansion; it finds that interactions suppress magnetic-field-induced anisotropy in diffusion, offering a framework for dissipative transport in magnetized QCD matter relevant to heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent theoretical framework for dissipative particle transport—specifically number diffusion—in magnetized, interacting hot QCD matter, motivated by the need for realistic hydrodynamic modeling of heavy-ion collisions where strong magnetic fields and medium interactions coexist.

Method: Starts from an effective covariant quasiparticle description of the hot QCD medium; applies a Chapman–Enskog-like iterative expansion of the distribution function to derive the second-order relativistic magnetohydrodynamic evolution equation for the particle number diffusion current; obtains the Navier–Stokes limit and analyzes modifications to transport coefficients due to magnetic field and interactions.

Result: The magnetic field introduces new tensorial structures into the diffusion current, altering transport properties; medium interactions suppress the magnetic-field-induced anisotropy in particle diffusion; explicit expressions for diffusion current and associated transport coefficients are obtained in both second-order and Navier–Stokes limits.

Conclusion: A self-consistent relativistic magnetohydrodynamic framework for particle number diffusion in interacting, magnetized QCD matter is established; the suppression of magnetic anisotropy by interactions highlights the crucial role of scattering processes, making this framework essential for refining hydrodynamic simulations of heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: In this study, we investigate particle number diffusion in an interacting hot QCD medium in the presence of an external magnetic field. Starting from an effective covariant description of the medium in terms of quasiparticle degrees of freedom, we employ a Chapman Enskog-like iterative expansion of the distribution function to derive the second-order relativistic magnetohydrodynamic evolution equation for the number diffusion current. The magnetic field introduces additional tensorial structures into the diffusion dynamics, thereby modifying the transport properties of the medium. We further obtain the corresponding Navier-Stokes limit and examine the influence of medium interactions and the magnetic field on the diffusion current and its associated transport coefficients. Our results show that medium interactions suppress the magnetic field-induced anisotropy in particle diffusion. This study provides a consistent framework for investigating dissipative particle transport in magnetized, interacting QCD matter and may serve as an important ingredient in developing realistic hydrodynamic descriptions of heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [103] [Next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to prompt $Υ(1S)$ pair hadroproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16686)
*Zhi-Guo He,Xiao-Bo Jin,Bernd A.Kniehl*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates next-to-leading-order QCD predictions for prompt Υ(1S) pair hadroproduction in the color-singlet channel, including feed-down from higher Υ states, and compares them with CMS data at 8 and 13 TeV; it finds moderate K-factors for the total cross section but highly differential K-factors (0.2–20), good overall agreement with data except at extreme kinematic edges, and identifies residual discrepancies as potential signatures of double parton scattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise theoretical predictions for Υ(1S) pair production to test QCD dynamics, constrain nonperturbative NRQCD matrix elements, and establish a baseline for isolating double parton scattering (DPS) contributions—particularly via the key DPS parameter σ_eff.

Method: Next-to-leading-order (NLO) calculation within the nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization framework, focusing on color-singlet contributions and including feed-down from Υ(2S) and Υ(3S); differential distributions (m_ΥΥ, p_T^ΥΥ, |Δy|, p_T^Υ) are computed and compared to CMS data at √s = 8 and 13 TeV.

Result: Integrated cross section receives mildly negative NLO corrections (K ≈ 0.9); differential K factors vary strongly: ~0.6 at high m_ΥΥ ≤ 100 GeV, ~0.2 at large |Δy| ≤ 4, and up to ~20 at high p_T^Υ ≤ 30 GeV; predictions agree well with CMS data except in outermost m_ΥΥ and |Δy| bins, where deficits suggest DPS contributions.

Conclusion: The NLO NRQCD color-singlet calculation provides a solid leading-order baseline for Υ pair production; the observed kinematic discrepancies at phase-space boundaries support the presence of DPS, enabling future extraction of σ_eff—the effective cross section characterizing double parton interactions.

Abstract: Working at next-to-leading order in nonrelativistic-QCD factorization, we study prompt $\Upsilon(1S)$ pair hadroproduction, including feed-down from the excited $\Upsilon(2S)$ and $\Upsilon(3S)$ states and focusing on the color singlet contributions, which are known to be dominant. Besides the integrated cross section $\sigma^{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$, we predict distributions in the invariant mass $m_{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$, transverse momentum $p_T^{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$, and rapidity separation $|\Delta y|$ of the $\Upsilon(1S)$ pair, as well as the one in the transverse momentum $p_T^{\Upsilon}$ of either $\Upsilon(1S)$ meson, and compare them with measurements by the CMS Collaboration at the CERN LHC with center-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s}=8$ and $13~\mathrm{TeV}$ as far as they go. The QCD corrections to $\sigma^{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$ turn out to be mildly negative, with $K$ factors of about 0.9. By contrast, the differential cross sections receive $K$ factors as small as 0.6 and 0.2 at large values of $m_{\Upsilon\Upsilon}\le 100$ GeV and $|\Delta y|\le4$, respectively, and as large as 20 for large values of $p_T^{\Upsilon}\le30$ GeV. Our predictions agree well with the data, except in the outermost bins of $m_{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$ and $|\Delta y|$, where they fall short of the data, leaving room for contributions from double parton scattering as expected. Our analysis thus provides a first solid basis for the extraction of the key parameter $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ of double parton scattering.

</details>


### [104] [Dark matter evolution in stellar objects: A Boltzmann transport approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16689)
*Yen-Hsun Lin,Meng-Ru Wu,Seokhoon Yun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the Boltzmann transport equation to model dark matter evolution in stars—particularly the Sun—showing that DM often fails to thermalize, leading to spatially extended distributions and strongly suppressed annihilation rates; it further applies this framework to inelastic Higgsino DM, revealing enhanced sensitivity to mass splitting due to thermal nuclear motion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of captured dark matter in stars beyond the classical isothermal approximation, especially for weakly scattering or inelastic DM scenarios where thermalization fails and nuclear thermal motion matters.

Method: Solves the Boltzmann transport equation (BTE) averaged over orbital motion, formulated as a master equation in energy and angular momentum; includes capture, bound-state scattering, annihilation, and evaporation; applied to solar DM capture and inelastic Higgsino models with thermal nuclear effects.

Result: Captured DM in the Sun typically does not thermalize within its lifetime when nucleus-DM scattering is rare, resulting in a more extended phase-space distribution and annihilation rates suppressed by many orders of magnitude relative to isothermal predictions; for inelastic Higgsinos, thermal nuclear motion opens new capture channels, tightening constraints on the mass splitting by ~10–100 keV.

Conclusion: Classical isothermal assumptions significantly overestimate DM annihilation in stars for non-thermalizing DM; accurate BTE treatment is essential for interpreting indirect detection limits and collider/underground experiment results—especially for inelastic or low-scattering cross-section scenarios.

Abstract: Dark matter (DM) captured by stars offers a way to search for physics beyond the Standard Model. We study the evolution of captured DM with the Boltzmann transport equation (BTE), which follows both the abundance and the phase-space distribution of the bound particles from capture toward a stationary state. Averaging over orbital motion, we write the BTE as a master equation in energy and angular momentum that includes capture, scattering between bound orbits, annihilation and evaporation. Applying it to the Sun, we find that captured DM does not thermalize within the solar age when its scattering on nuclei is rare. The bound DM then remains much more extended than the isothermal distribution assumed in the classical treatment, and its annihilation rate can be suppressed by many orders of magnitude. We also apply the BTE to the inelastic Higgsino interpretation of the recent LUX-ZEPLIN result, which involves two DM components coupled through inelastic scattering. The thermal motion of solar nuclei opens capture channels that are closed for nuclei at rest and strengthens the constraint on the mass splitting by tens of keV.

</details>


### [105] [Residual Entanglement vs. Residual Coherence: A Wave Packet Approach in Neutrino Oscillation with Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16709)
*Lekhashri Konwar,Abhishek Kumar Jha,Bhavna Yadav*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum correlations in three-flavor neutrino oscillations using a wave-packet formalism (beyond plane-wave approximation) in constant matter density, computing residual $l_1$-norm coherence, Concurrence Fill, and Three-$\pi$ entanglement in terms of transition probabilities for an initial muon neutrino state; it applies the analysis to DUNE and extended baselines, finding wave-packet width–dependent decoherence, matter-induced modulation of oscillation features, dominance of coherence over entanglement, and asymptotic insensitivity to $L$ and $\delta_{\rm CP}$ at large distances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the standard plane-wave approximation and systematically quantify how realistic wave-packet effects—especially decoherence—and matter interactions jointly influence quantum correlations (coherence and entanglement) in long-baseline three-flavor neutrino oscillations.

Method: Employed a relativistic Gaussian wave-packet formalism for three-flavor neutrinos propagating in constant matter density; analytically expressed residual $l_1$-norm coherence, Concurrence Fill, and Three-$\pi$ entanglement in terms of neutrino transition probabilities; numerically evaluated these measures for the DUNE experimental setup and extended baselines up to asymptotic regimes; performed parameter scans in the $L$–$\delta_{\rm CP}$ plane.

Result: Finite wave-packet widths suppress and smooth oscillations, with stronger damping for smaller widths; matter effects shift amplitudes and extrema of oscillatory features; residual $l_1$-norm coherence dominates over Concurrence Fill and Three-$\pi$ entanglement; all measures decay toward asymptotic constants at large $L$, earlier for narrower wave packets; residual $l_1$-norm coherence shows weak $\delta_{\rm CP}$ dependence, while entanglement measures exhibit greater $\delta_{\rm CP}$ sensitivity at DUNE baseline; beyond a characteristic baseline, all three measures become independent of both $L$ and $\delta_{\rm CP}$.

Conclusion: Wave-packet decoherence—not just matter effects—fundamentally governs the suppression and eventual loss of quantum correlations in long-baseline neutrino oscillations; residual $l_1$-norm coherence is the most robust and dominant correlation measure across propagation distances, whereas entanglement measures are more sensitive to CP phase but vanish faster; the asymptotic constancy of all measures signals complete environmental decoherence, rendering quantum correlations unobservable at sufficiently large $L$.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate quantum correlations in three-flavor neutrino oscillations beyond the standard plane-wave (PW) description by employing a wave-packet formalism in the presence of constant matter density. We study three quantum correlation measures, namely the residual $l_1$-norm coherence, Concurrence Fill, and Three-$\pi$ entanglement, and express them in terms of transition probabilities for an initial muon-flavor state. The analysis is performed for the DUNE setup and extended beyond its baseline to study the interplay between wave-packet decoherence and matter effects over long distances. For the DUNE baseline, finite wave-packet widths suppress and smooth the oscillations relative to the PW case, with smaller widths causing stronger damping, while matter effects modify the amplitudes and positions of the oscillation features. Among the considered measures, residual $l_1$-norm coherence dominates over the entanglement measures. At very long propagation distances, wave-packet decoherence progressively washes out the oscillatory behavior, driving the oscillation probabilities and quantum correlation measures toward averaged values, with smaller wave-packet widths leading to earlier damping. Further, in the $L-\delta_{\rm CP}$ plane, residual $l_1$-norm coherence exhibits weak $\delta_{\rm CP}$ dependence, while Concurrence Fill and Three-$\pi$ entanglement show greater sensitivity at the DUNE baseline. Beyond a characteristic baseline in the extended propagation regime, all three measures approach constant values, independent of $L$ and $\delta_{\rm CP}$.

</details>


### [106] [Solar Neutrinos at the ESSνSB experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16807)
*ESSnuSB Collaboration,J. Aguilar,D. Barčot,E. Baussan,A. K. Bhattacharyya,M. Blennow,M. Bogomilov,B. Bolling,E. Bouquerel,F. Bramati,A. Branca,G. Brunetti,I. Bustinduy,C. J. Carlile,J. Cederkall,T. W. Choi,S. Choubey,P. Christiansen,I. Christodoulou,E. Cristaldo Morales,P. Cupiał,D. D'Ago,H. Danared,J. P. A. M. de André,E. A. Díaz,M. Dracos,I. Efthymiopoulos,T. Ekelöf,M. Eshraqi,G. Fanourakis,A. Farricker,E. Fasoula,T. Fukuda,S. Gago,N. Gazis,Th. Geralis,M. Ghosh,A. Giarnetti,G. Gokbulut,C. Hagner,L. Halić,S. G. Hernández,J. Hiegel,M. Hooft,K. E. Iversen,N. Jachowicz,M. Jakkapu,M. Jensen,I. Karakoulias,E. Kasimi,A. Kayis Topaksu,B. Kliček,K. Kordas,B. Kovač,A. Leisos,A. Longhin,M. López,C. Maiano,S. Marangoni,J. G. Marcos,C. Marrelli,D. Meloni,M. Mezzetto,N. Milas,J. L. Muñoz,K. Niewczas,M. Oglakci,T. Ohlsson,M. Olvegård,A. Opanasenko,M. Pari,D. Patrzalek,G. Petkov,Ch. Petridou,P. Poussot,A. Psallidas,F. Pupilli,M. L. Reguera,D. Saiang,E. Salehi,D. Sampsonidis,A. Scanu,C. Schwab,F. Sordo,G. Stavropoulos,M. Stipčević,R. Tarkeshian,F. Terranova,T. Tolba,M. Topp-Mugglestone,E. Trachanas,R. Tsenov,A. Tsirigotis,S. E. Tzamarias,M. Vanderpoorten,G. Vankova-Kirilova,N. Vassilopoulos,S. Vihonen,J. Wurtz,V. Zeter,O. Zormpa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the ESSnuSB water-Cherenkov detector's sensitivity to two solar neutrino signatures—the MSW-induced spectral upturn in ^8B ν_e survival probability and the first direct detection of hep neutrinos—accounting for systematic uncertainties and dominant backgrounds (muon spallation, ^222Rn); it finds that 90% spallation rejection is insufficient for 5σ discovery within 20 years, and ~99% rejection is required.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the ESSnuSB WC detector can achieve groundbreaking solar neutrino physics—specifically, resolving the MSW spectral upturn in P_ee(E_ν) and making the first direct observation of the hep neutrino flux—despite challenging environmental backgrounds and systematic limitations.

Method: Shape-driven statistical analysis with floating normalization; incorporation of realistic detector systematics (energy resolution, flux normalization), background models (cosmic-ray muon spallation, ^222Rn), and exposure-dependent sensitivity calculations; benchmarked against Super-Kamiokande and JUNO spallation rejection performance.

Result: With 90% spallation rejection, neither the MSW upturn nor the hep signal reaches 5σ significance in 20 years; detailed hep sensitivity shows that discovery-level significance requires ~99% spallation rejection efficiency and low recoil energy threshold jointly.

Conclusion: ESSnuSB has strong potential for solar neutrino physics, but achieving definitive discovery of either signature demands unprecedented spallation background suppression (~99%), highlighting a critical experimental challenge for next-generation WC detectors.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of the ESSnuSB large underground water-Cherenkov (WC) detector to two key solar neutrino signatures: the MSW spectral upturn in the $^8$B electron neutrino survival probability $P_{ee}(E_\nu)$, and the first direct observation of the rare hep neutrino flux. The analysis incorporates detector systematic uncertainties (energy resolution and flux normalization) and models environmental backgrounds from cosmic-ray muon spallation and $^{222}$Rn contamination.
