We adopt a shape-driven analysis strategy with a floating overall normalization, so that the discovery power relies on the spectral distortion rather than on the absolute event rate. In a realistic scenario with 90% spallation rejection efficiency, informed by the Super-Kamiokande experience and recent JUNO analyses, residual spallation backgrounds prevent either signal from reaching $5\sigma$ significance within 20 years of exposure. For the hep flux, we present a detailed sensitivity study to both its discovery and its precision, and quantify how the required spallation veto efficiency and recoil energy threshold jointly determine the achievable significance for both signals. At the adopted thresholds, discovery-level sensitivity for both signals requires approximately 99% spallation rejection over a 20-year exposure.

</details>


### [107] [A unified reduced-mass parametrization of effective chromoelectric interactions in hadrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16827)
*Wen-Nian Liu,Duo-Jie Jia,Fu-Quan Dou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified effective mass relation for hadrons using a common reduced-mass function for chromoelectric pair contributions, jointly fits 14 spin-averaged meson and baryon masses with RMS residual 4 MeV, incorporates chromomagnetic effects, predicts masses of multiply heavy baryons and tetraquarks (e.g., fully charmed $cc\bar c\bar c$ at 6505(9)(9) MeV), and estimates decay channel preferences via color–spin recoupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the description of flavor-dependent chromoelectric interactions in mesons and baryons, reconcile light- and heavy-flavor hadron mass systematics, and enable predictive, parameter-economical modeling of exotic multiquark states including tetraquarks and multiply heavy baryons.

Method: Constructs a unified effective mass relation with a saturating reduced-mass function for chromoelectric pair terms; separates spin-weighted chromomagnetic contributions; performs joint least-squares fit of 14 spin-averaged meson/baryon masses using constituent quark masses and a shared additive constant; extends the model to include chromomagnetic interactions; diagonalizes chromoelectric + chromomagnetic mass matrices for tetraquark spectra; computes S-wave rearrangement-decay weights via color–spin recoupling under spatial-overlap approximation.

Result: RMS residual of 4 MeV for 14 fitted hadron masses; fitted saturating form captures light-flavor saturation and enhanced heavy-flavor attraction; predicted fully charmed tetraquark tensor mass: 6505(9)(9) MeV (consistent with lattice QCD); scalar counterpart: 6562(9)(12) MeV (near X(6600)); tetraquark masses stable under variation of qq chromomagnetic couplings; derived S-wave decay weights identify favored rearrangement channels.

Conclusion: A single, unified effective mass framework—parameterized by constituent quark masses and one additive constant—successfully describes both conventional mesons/baryons and exotic multiquark states; chromoelectric saturation enables absorption of leading contributions into effective masses, while residual pair terms encode flavor dependence; the model provides quantitative, testable predictions for heavy tetraquarks and multiply heavy baryons.

Abstract: We construct a unified effective mass relation in which a common reduced-mass function describes the flavor dependence of chromoelectric pair contributions, with explicit color factors connecting $q\bar q$ and $qq$ pairs. After subtracting fixed spin-weighted chromomagnetic contributions, we jointly fit fourteen spin-averaged meson and baryon masses using constituent quark masses and an additive constant shared by both sectors, obtaining a root-mean-square (RMS) residual of 4 MeV. The fitted saturating form connects the nearly saturated light-flavor contribution with increasing attraction in the heavy-flavor region. In the common saturation limit, the leading chromoelectric contribution acts as a common mass shift for each constituent and can be absorbed into effective constituent masses; the residual pair term accounts for the flavor dependence away from saturation. With the chromomagnetic interaction included, this single parameter set reproduces the ground-state meson and baryon spectra and yields predictions for multiply heavy baryons outside the calibration set. By diagonalizing the sum of the chromoelectric and chromomagnetic mass matrices, we further obtain the ground-state spectra of $cc\bar c\bar c$, $bb\bar b\bar b$, and $bc\bar b\bar c$. The fully charmed tensor mass is ${6505}{(9)(9)}$ MeV, close to recent lattice QCD results, while the upper scalar mass of {${6562}{(9)(12)}$} MeV lies in the mass region near $X(6600)$. The tetraquark masses remain relatively stable under the examined variations of the $qq$ chromomagnetic couplings. The relative $S$-wave rearrangement-decay weights obtained from color--spin recoupling can be used to identify favored decay channels under the stated spatial-overlap approximation.

</details>


### [108] [Dilution of cosmological relics and gravitational waves from a supercooled electroweak phase transition in classically scale-invariant models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16845)
*Arnab Chaudhuri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies a classically scale-invariant Standard Model extension with a real singlet and extra scalars, where electroweak symmetry breaking occurs along a Gildener-Weinberg flat direction, yielding a light dilaton. It finds a single, strongly supercooled but fast first-order phase transition completing above the QCD scale; bubble wall dynamics thermalize plasma, causing significant entropy injection that dilutes relics, enhances baryon asymmetry requirements, relaxes dark matter unitarity bounds, and suppresses earlier gravitational-wave backgrounds. Predicted GW signals peak in the mHz band and are detectable by LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how electroweak symmetry breaking and scale generation occur radiatively in classically scale-invariant extensions of the Standard Model, and to characterize the cosmological consequences—especially gravitational-wave signatures and relic dilution—of the associated first-order phase transition.

Method: Computes the two-dimensional quantum tunnelling (bounce) path in field space, tracks false-vacuum fraction evolution through percolation and transition completion, models bubble wall dynamics including terminal velocity attainment and energy partitioning (bulk plasma motion vs. radiation), and estimates resulting entropy injection, relic dilution, and gravitational-wave production (using acoustic approximation).

Result: The phase transition is strongly supercooled yet fast, completing above the QCD scale; bubble walls reach terminal velocity early, transferring most vacuum energy into bulk plasma motion; subsequent thermalization causes large entropy injection (diluting relics by several orders of magnitude); this raises required pre-transition baryon asymmetry, relaxes thermal dark matter unitarity bound, suppresses earlier GW backgrounds; predicted GW signal peaks in millihertz band, scales with vacuum energy, and is detectable by LISA across viable parameter space.

Conclusion: Radiatively generated electroweak symmetry breaking in this scale-invariant model leads to a distinctive, observable cosmological phase transition with profound implications for baryogenesis, dark matter, relic abundances, and gravitational-wave astronomy—particularly testable by LISA—provided the singlet scale is ≥ TeV and scalar couplings satisfy perturbativity and Higgs data constraints.

Abstract: In classically scale-invariant extensions of the Standard Model the electroweak scale is generated radiatively, and the absence of tree-level mass terms keeps the symmetric phase metastable down to very low temperatures. We study the resulting phase transition in a model with a real singlet and several additional scalars coupled to the Higgs doublet and to the singlet, in which electroweak symmetry is broken along a Gildener-Weinberg flat direction and a light dilaton appears in the spectrum. Computing the bounce along the two-dimensional tunnelling path and following the false-vacuum fraction through percolation and completion, we find that the transition proceeds in a single, strongly supercooled step and completes above the QCD scale over a region of parameter space consistent with Higgs measurements and perturbativity. Despite the strong supercooling the transition is fast. The bubble walls reach their terminal velocity early, and almost all of the released vacuum energy goes into bulk motion of the plasma, which thermalises and reheats the universe close to the scale set by the vacuum energy. The resulting entropy injection dilutes pre-existing relics by up to several orders of magnitude. It raises the baryon asymmetry that must be present before the transition, relaxes the unitarity bound on the mass of thermal dark matter, and suppresses earlier gravitational-wave backgrounds. If the acoustic description of gravitational-wave production extends to this regime, the signal peaks in the millihertz band, follows an approximate scaling relation between peak amplitude and frequency set by the vacuum energy, and would be observed by LISA throughout the viable region. The results depend on the multiplicity of the new scalars and their Higgs portal coupling at leading order through a single combination, and require the singlet scale to lie at or above the TeV scale.

</details>


### [109] [Exploring the Pion Vector Form Factor Beyond the Physical Sheet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16974)
*Florian Herren,Fatemeh Nouri,Méril Reboud*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses analyticity and unitarity to construct a model-independent parametrization of the pion vector form factor on the first two Riemann sheets below the πω threshold, fits it to τ decay and spacelike data, and extracts ρ± pole parameters, pion charge radius, and low-energy ππ scattering parameters without external input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the ρ± resonance pole position and couplings, pion charge radius, and low-energy ππ scattering parameters in a model-independent way—free from external scattering input—by exploiting fundamental principles (analyticity and unitarity) and high-precision experimental data.

Method: Constructs a conformal-mapped, model-independent parametrization of the pion vector form factor valid on the first and second Riemann sheets below the inelastic πω threshold; performs combined fit to τ⁻→π⁻π⁰ν_τ data (ALEPH, Belle, CLEO, OPAL) and spacelike data (NA7, JLab); extracts resonance poles via analytic continuation and determines low-energy observables.

Result: Extracted ρ± pole mass M_ρ± = 762.07 ± 0.63 MeV and width Γ_ρ± = 143.62^{+0.79}_{-0.80} MeV; also obtained pion charge radius and ππ scattering parameters consistent with literature; parametrization implemented in EOS software.

Conclusion: The approach demonstrates that high-precision resonance parameters and low-energy hadronic observables can be extracted rigorously from dispersive analysis without relying on external scattering models—validating the robustness of analyticity- and unitarity-based frameworks in light-meson physics.

Abstract: Using analyticity and unitarity, we parametrise the pion vector form factor on the first and second Riemann sheets, below the inelastic $\pi\omega$ threshold. We fit this model-independent parametrisation to the available $\tau^-\to\pi^-\pi^0\nu_\tau$ data from ALEPH, Belle, CLEO and OPAL, together with spacelike data from NA7 and JLab, and extract the pole position and couplings of the $\rho^\pm$ along with the pion charge radius and low-energy $\pi\pi$ scattering parameters. Our results are independent of external scattering input and agree with the literature; in particular, we find $M_{\rho^\pm} = 762.07\pm 0.63$ MeV and $\Gamma_{\rho^\pm} = 143.62^{+0.79}_{-0.80}$ MeV. The parametrisation is publicly available in the EOS software.

</details>


### [110] [Proton Spin Fluctuation Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17032)
*Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fluctuation decomposition of proton spin using variances and covariances of quark/gluon spin and orbital angular momentum, linked to single- and double-parton distributions; it derives sum rules enforcing covariance–variance compensation due to fixed total proton spin, uncovering hidden correlations beyond standard spin decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the conventional proton spin decomposition by exposing quantum fluctuations and correlations among spin and orbital angular momentum components of quarks and gluons—quantities inaccessible in average-spin frameworks.

Method: Formulates a fluctuation-based decomposition of proton spin using statistical moments (variances and covariances) of quark/gluon spin and orbital angular momentum operators; derives operator-level representations in terms of single-parton distributions (SPDs) and double-parton distributions (DPDs); imposes the exact proton spin constraint to obtain sum rules.

Result: Derives explicit sum rules linking variances and covariances of spin and orbital contributions; establishes concrete connections between these fluctuation measures and measurable SPDs/DPDs; demonstrates that nontrivial correlations (encoded in covariances) are mandatory to satisfy the fixed total spin condition.

Conclusion: The fluctuation decomposition reveals essential quantum correlations and intrinsic fluctuations in proton spin structure that are completely absent in conventional (expectation-value-only) decompositions; it provides a more complete, statistically grounded framework for interpreting spin-related observables and guides future DPD-based experimental tests.

Abstract: We formulate a fluctuation decomposition of proton spin in terms of the variances and covariances of the spin and orbital angular momentum of quarks and gluons. We derive their representations in terms of single- and double-parton distributions. The definite proton spin imposes sum rules requiring the variances of its spin and orbital contributions to be compensated by their covariances. This framework reveals correlations and fluctuations that are invisible to the conventional proton spin decomposition.

</details>


### [111] [Nonperturbative Kinetic Description of the Vacuum QGP Production in the Abelian Projection Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17078)
*V. V. Dmitriev,S. V. Churochkina,S. A. Smolyansky,V. A. Tseryupa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives self-consistent equations for quark vacuum production in SU(N) QCD under strong quasiclassical gluon fields, incorporating charge non-neutrality via Abelian projection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the back-reaction problem in nonperturbative QCD vacuum dynamics, specifically how quark production modifies strong gluon fields while violating electroneutrality.

Method: Abelian projection method in SU(N) QCD; derivation of nonperturbative, self-consistent equations for vacuum quark production coupled to quasiclassical gluon fields.

Result: A closed system of equations describing dynamical vacuum polarization with explicit electroneutrality violation under strong gluonic backgrounds.

Conclusion: The derived equations provide a consistent framework to study nonperturbative back-reaction effects in QCD vacuum, essential for understanding confinement-related phenomena and chiral symmetry breaking.

Abstract: Within the framework of the Abelian projection method in the $SU(N)$ variant of QCD, a system of nonperturbative selfconsistent equations for the back-reaction problem is derived. This system describes the vacuum production of quarks under the influence of strong quasiclassical gluon fields taking into account violating of the electroneutrality condition.

</details>


### [112] [Two-loop mixed QCD-EW corrections to bottom-pair production in electron-positron collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17171)
*Simone Devoto,Chinmoy Dey,Michele Dradi,Justin T. Fagnoni,Ziyang Huang,Xinchen Li,Michael J. Ramsey-Musolf,Alessandro Vicini,Jiang Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the two-loop mixed strong-electroweak virtual corrections to e⁺e⁻ → b̄b, using semi-analytical methods with complex masses for EW bosons; results contribute to the NNLO QCD-EW cross section and are applicable to HL-LHC and future lepton collider precision studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-precision theoretical predictions for bottom-quark pair production at lepton colliders, essential for testing the Standard Model and probing new physics in the high-luminosity and future collider eras.

Method: Semi-analytical evaluation of two-loop Feynman integrals—including up to five massive internal lines with three distinct mass scales—using complex-valued masses for electroweak gauge bosons; ultraviolet renormalization and infrared subtraction applied to obtain finite virtual corrections.

Result: Complete two-loop mixed QCD-electroweak virtual correction to e⁺e⁻ → b̄b, yielding a finite contribution to the NNLO QCD-EW inclusive cross section.

Conclusion: The developed methodology is robust and extensible to fully electroweak second-order corrections for other benchmark processes at HL-LHC and future lepton colliders, enhancing precision phenomenology.

Abstract: We present the two-loop mixed strong-electroweak virtual corrections to the production of a massive bottom-antibottom quark pair in electron-positron collisions. The evaluation of all the relevant Feynman integrals, including those with up to five internal massive lines with three different mass values, has been worked out relying on semi-analytical techniques, using complex-valued masses for the electroweak gauge bosons. The ultraviolet-renormalized and infrared-subtracted finite correction contributes to the NNLO QCD-EW cross section of the inclusive production of a bottom-quark pair. The methodology adopted in this calculation can be extended to the study of fully electroweak second-order corrections to benchmark precision calculations relevant to both the high-luminosity phase of the Large Hadron Collider and prospective future lepton colliders.

</details>


### [113] [Monopole catalysis with massive fermions: the fate of the twisted sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17253)
*Valentin V. Khoze*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the decay of a half-integer fermion-number state produced by fermion scattering on an SU(5) monopole, showing how fermion masses and confinement cause it to decay into physical integer-fermion-number states; it computes cross-sections, kinematics (Dirichlet-distributed energy sharing), and branching ratios between helicity-flip and (B+L)-violating channels — notably anti-nucleon synthesis for electrons and catalyzed proton decay for quarks — refining Callan’s statistical interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how the topologically twisted, fractional-fermion-number state — arising in massless fermion scattering on an SU(5) monopole — becomes physical in the real world where fermion masses and QCD confinement break the topological protection, leading to observable decay channels with definite (B+L) violation signatures.

Method: Analyzes partial-wave scattering in the minimal SU(5) monopole background; incorporates fermion mass effects and confinement to model decay dynamics; applies unitarity saturation (σ = π/p²); uses Dirichlet distribution to describe multi-flavor energy sharing; enforces conservation laws (helicity, B+L) to identify allowed decay channels; computes energy-dependent branching ratios analytically and via modeling across thresholds.

Result: The cross-section saturates unitarity; outgoing energy is shared across four fermion flavours with Dirichlet(1,1,1,1) distribution; exactly two decay channels exist per incoming fermion: helicity flip and (B+L)-violating; for electrons: equal branching at high energy, pure helicity flip below anti-nucleon threshold; for slow-proton quarks: near-unity probability for catalyzed proton decay; Callan’s statistical picture is revised from superposition to energy-dependent decay probabilities.

Conclusion: The twisted fractional-fermion state is not a stable superposition but a resonant precursor that decays deterministically into integer-fermion asymptotic states, with (B+L)-violation manifesting as anti-nucleon production or proton decay depending on initial conditions; this provides a concrete, quantitative realization of monopole-induced baryon/lepton number violation in realistic Standard Model settings.

Abstract: In the massless limit a fermion scattering on the minimal $SU(5)$ monopole in the lowest partial wave produces an outgoing state with half-integer fermion number in every flavour. This state lives in a twisted sector of the Hilbert space and is now understood as an ordinary fermion attached to a topological line running back to the monopole. In the Standard Model fermion masses and confinement destroy the line at distances greater than the inverse mass scales, causing the twisted state to decay into physical asymptotic states with integer fermion numbers. We study this decay for an incoming lepton or quark of one generation. The cross-section in this partial wave saturates the unitarity bound, $\sigma=\pi/p^2$, and the energy of the outgoing state is shared among the four fermion flavours with a broad Dirichlet distribution, which sets the thresholds and the kinematics. Conservation laws allow exactly two decay channels for each incoming fermion, the helicity flip and its $(B+L)$-violating partner. For an incoming electron the $(B+L)$-violating channel is anti-nucleon synthesis, and we determine the branching ratios in two limits: the two channels are equally likely at high energy, and only the helicity flip remains at low energy, below the anti-nucleon threshold. In between we model the energy dependence. For a quark in a slow proton the $(B+L)$-violating channel is catalysed proton decay, which has no threshold and occurs with a probability close to one. Callan's statistical interpretation of the fractional state emerges in a sharper form: the state is not a superposition of two final states with equal weights, but a single state that decays into them with energy-dependent probabilities, equal only at high energy.

</details>


### [114] [A Dark-Sector Mass Flip-Flop Mechanism for the ANITA-IV Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17328)
*Dibya S. Chattopadhyay,Carlos A. Argüelles,Vedran Brdar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a dark-sector 'mass flip-flop' mechanism involving sterile neutrinos N₁/N₂ and an ultralight scalar to explain ANITA-IV's upward air showers while evading IceCube non-detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reconcile ANITA-IV's four upward-going ultra-high-energy air-shower candidates (interpreted as ν_τ) with IceCube's null detection at similar energies despite larger exposure.

Method: Introduces a two-state dark fermion system (N₁, N₂) with vacuum mass splitting, coupled via an ultralight scalar to Earth matter; matter-induced mass inversion enables N₁ → N₂ decay underground, followed by N₂ → N₁ Z′ decay in atmosphere producing air showers; kinematic suppression of both decays at IceCube depths explains non-observation.

Result: Quantitatively reconciles ANITA-IV events; predicts three detectable events in PUEO’s first flight under normalized flux.

Conclusion: The mass flip-flop mechanism provides a viable dark-sector explanation for the ANITA anomaly without conflicting with IceCube constraints, and yields testable predictions for next-generation balloon experiments.

Abstract: ANITA-IV has observed four upward-going air-shower candidates just below the horizon, consistent with ultra-high-energy $\nu_\tau$, yet IceCube, despite its much larger exposure, has detected no neutrino events at such energies. We propose a dark-sector mass flip-flop mechanism that reconciles these observations. We consider an all-sky, monoenergetic flux of ultra-high-energy $N_1$, that is lighter than its partner $N_2$ and stable in vacuum. In our scenario, an ultralight scalar mediating interactions with Earth matter inverts the mass ordering of $N_1$ and $N_2$, allowing an incident $N_1$ to decay into $N_2$ deep underground. The resulting $N_2$ propagates unimpeded through matter and exits the Earth, where the decay $N_2 \to N_1 Z'$ becomes kinematically allowed in the atmosphere, initiating an extensive air shower. At IceCube depths, neither $N_1\to N_2 Z'$ nor $N_2\to N_1 Z'$ is kinematically allowed, which explains the absence of analogous events at IceCube. Normalizing the $N_1$ flux to the four ANITA-IV events, we also predict three events during PUEO's first flight.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [115] [Emergent Anyons in a Weakly Interacting Gauged Chern Insulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14658)
*Sven Bachmann,Leonardo Goller,Marcello Porta*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a microscopic link between the many-body Chern number C of a fermionic ground state and flux braiding statistics in a Z2 gauge theory coupled to matter, demonstrating C=1 in a gauged Qi-Wu-Zhang insulator and proving topological order with four-fold ground-state degeneracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive a rigorous, microscopic connection between the many-body Chern number—a topological invariant of fermionic quantum matter—and the braiding statistics of emergent flux excitations in a Z2 lattice gauge theory, thereby bridging topological band theory and topological order.

Method: Analytical derivation using spectral gap assumptions, explicit construction of dressed flux loop operators, gauging of charge-parity symmetry in a weakly interacting Qi-Wu-Zhang model, and application of reflection positivity and chessboard estimates to prove mass gap and ground-state degeneracy.

Result: A precise non-Abelian braiding relation W₂W₁ = exp(iπC/2) W₁W₂ for flux loops in the ground-state subspace; realization of C = 1 in a concrete gauged model; proof of gapped spectrum, deconfined Z2 topological order, and four locally indistinguishable ground states on a torus.

Conclusion: The many-body Chern number directly governs the topological spin of Z2 fluxes, confirming that gauging symmetries of topological insulators yields intrinsically topologically ordered phases whose ground-state structure and anyonic statistics are microscopically determined by the parent Chern number.

Abstract: We derive a microscopic relation between the many-body Chern number $C$ of the fermionic ground state and the braiding of fluxes in $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ gauge theory coupled to matter: under a spectral gap condition, dressed flux loops obey $W_2W_1=e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}C}W_1W_2$ on the ground-state sector. We realize $C=1$ by gauging charge parity in a weakly interacting Qi-Wu-Zhang insulator. Reflection positivity and chessboard estimates show that flux defects are massive, which yields a gapped, topologically ordered phase with four locally indistinguishable ground states.

</details>


### [116] [Making Poisson σ again - an étude on groupoidal gauging, anomalies, gerbes, and defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14784)
*Rafał R. Suszek*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the Poisson σ-model (PSM) as a geometric field theory of connections on a trivial principaloid bundle and sections of a shadow bundle, interprets flat directions via Lagrangian bisections, analyzes and cancels gauge anomalies using gerbe coupling, and generalizes PSM to arbitrary-topology shadow bundles via 2-stacky descent of equivariant gerbes, culminating in a defect-based topological field theory formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous geometric and categorical reinterpretation of the Poisson σ-model that clarifies its gauge structure, anomaly origin, and topological invariance—particularly by leveraging symplectic groupoids, principaloid bundles, lagrangian bisections, and higher gerbes—thereby enabling consistent quantization and generalization beyond trivial topology.

Method: The authors employ symplectic geometry, category-theoretic bundle theory (principaloid bundles, shadow bundles), higher differential geometry (gerbes, 2-stacks), and homological algebra. Key techniques include: reinterpretation of PSM fields as connections/sections over Σ × G and Σ × M; linearisation of automorphisms via Lagrangian bisections; derivation of gauge anomaly from curvature of a connection-dependent trivial gerbe; anomaly cancellation via tensor product with a background G-equivariant gerbe over M; generalisation using local product gerbes and 2-stacky descent; and reformulation as a topological field theory with G-equivariant symmetry defect.

Result: A geometrically coherent, anomaly-free generalised Poisson σ-model is constructed, valid for arbitrary-topology shadow bundles F → Σ, defined as the holonomy of a globally descended G-equivariant gerbe over F. The gauge anomaly vanishes when the symplectic form ω on the integrating groupoid G is prequantisable. A cohomological classification of descent obstructions is provided, and the model is recast as a field theory with a topological gauge-symmetry defect encoded by the G-equivariant structure and transition cocycle of P.

Conclusion: The Poisson σ-model admits a natural higher-geometric formulation rooted in symplectic groupoid theory and 2-stack descent, unifying its classical geometry, quantum consistency (anomaly cancellation), and topological defect structure—thereby establishing a robust framework for quantization and extension to nontrivial backgrounds.

Abstract: The Poisson $\sigma$-model (PSM) over a closed worldsheet $\Sigma$ is reinterpreted as a theory of connections on a trivial principaloid bundle $P_0 = \Sigma \times G \to \Sigma$ with the symplectic structure groupoid $(G,\omega)$ integrating the Poisson target $(M,\Pi)$, and that of sections of the shadow bundle $F_0 = \Sigma \times M \to \Sigma$ - as defined in 2503.09886 [math.DG]. The flat directions of the PSM are explained as linearisations of automorphisms of $P_0$, modelled on maps from $\Sigma$ into the group of lagrangean bisections of $G$, which induce Poisson automorphisms of $(M,\Pi)$. The gauge anomaly of the ensuing field theory is derived and demonstrated to vanish for prequantisable $\omega$. A general anomaly-cancellation mechanism is proposed, which couples the bare model - rewritten as the holonomy of a connection-dependent trivial gerbe on $F_0$ - to a background gerbe over $M$ endowed with a $G$-equivariant structure. The coupling takes the form of the tensor product of the two gerbes and the extended PSM is defined as the holonomy of the product gerbe. An essential generalisation of the theory is then proposed - to shadows $F$ of principaloid bundles $P$ of arbitrary topology, in keeping with the interpretation of the gauging as descent to the orbistack $M//G$. The generalisation uses local product gerbes depending on local connections over trivialisations of $F$. The $G$-equivariant structure is proven to ensure a 2-stacky descent of the local gerbes to a global one over $F$. Its holonomy is taken as the definition of the generalised PSM. A cohomological discussion of obstructions against the descent is provided. Finally, a reformulation of the generalised PSM as a field theory with a topological gauge-symmetry defect is given, in which the $G$-equivariant structure is employed - alongside the transition cocycle of $P$ - as the datum of the defect.

</details>


### [117] [Cumulants of Operator Semigroups for Hierarchies of Many-Particle Evolution Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15304)
*V.I. Gerasimenko*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes cumulants of operator semigroups as the foundational concept for non-perturbative solutions to quantum many-particle evolution hierarchies (e.g., BBGKY), proving that correlation dynamics govern state evolution for both finite and infinite particle systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous, non-perturbative mathematical framework for describing the time evolution of quantum many-particle systems via hierarchies of equations (e.g., BBGKY), overcoming limitations of perturbative methods.

Method: Analysis of operator semigroups and their cumulants; construction and analysis of non-perturbative expansions for solutions to hierarchies of evolution equations for reduced density operators and reduced correlation operators.

Result: Demonstration that cumulants of operator semigroups provide a non-perturbative representation of solutions; proof that correlation dynamics fundamentally govern the evolution of quantum states in systems with finitely and infinitely many particles.

Conclusion: The dynamics of particle correlations are not merely auxiliary but constitute the essential mechanism underlying state evolution in quantum many-body systems, unifying descriptions across finite and infinite particle limits.

Abstract: The paper presents a brief overview of recent advances in the mathematical theory of evolution equations for quantum many-particle systems. The structure of expansions representing non-perturbative solutions to the Cauchy problem for hierarchies of fundamental evolution equations for many quantum particles is outlined. It has been shown that the concept of cumulants of operator semigroups forms the basis for non-perturbative representation of solutions to hierarchies of evolution equations that describe the state and observables. It is proven that the constructed dynamics of correlations underlie the description of the evolution of the state, both for finitely and infinitely many particles, governed by the BBGKY hierarchy for reduced density operators or by the hierarchy of nonlinear evolution equations for reduced correlation operators. In fact, it is demonstrated that the behavior of particle correlations governs the dynamics of states in quantum systems with an infinite number of particles.

</details>


### [118] [On JT boundary algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15452)
*Feng Xu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs canonical boundary algebras for Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity coupled to free bosonic matter, showing they are nonhyperfinite type II∞ factors (and their finite corners are type II₁) under a sharp weight condition (lowest weight > 1/2); it uses heat-regularized bounded fields and a Hilbert algebra to define a faithful normal semifinite trace without path integrals; for massless scalars, high-energy limits yield hyperfinite continuous cores, losing nonhyperfiniteness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct and classify the boundary operator algebras in JT gravity with matter—bypassing ill-defined point-like observables and path-integral heuristics—while clarifying the role of quantum field theoretic properties (e.g., hyperfiniteness, type classification) in holographic quantum gravity.

Method: Constructs boundary algebras via bounded heat-regularized fields; employs Hilbert algebra theory to obtain a faithful normal semifinite trace; classifies von Neumann algebra types using modular theory and corner analysis; proves sharpness of the weight threshold via unboundedness of point fields under heat regularization for 0 < d ≤ 1/2.

Result: For matter fields with lowest conformal weight > 1/2, the two canonical boundary algebras are mutual commutants and nonhyperfinite type II∞ factors; their nonzero finite corners are nonhyperfinite type II₁ factors; for a single massless scalar, certain bounded observables and normal states admit a high-energy limit described by a hyperfinite continuous core.

Conclusion: Nonhyperfiniteness is generic for boundary algebras in JT gravity with sufficiently massive matter, reflecting structural complexity beyond standard QFT; however, it is not stable under high-energy limits—even for simple models like massless scalars—highlighting a subtle decoupling between asymptotic approximations and exact algebraic structure.

Abstract: We construct the canonical boundary algebras of Jackiw--Teitelboim gravity coupled to free bosonic matter. For a nonempty finite or countable family of species with lowest weights greater than one half, the two boundary algebras are mutual commutants and nonhyperfinite type $\mathrm{II}_\infty$ factors. Their nonzero finite corners are nonhyperfinite type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors. The construction uses bounded heat-regularized fields and a Hilbert algebra, which gives a faithful normal semifinite trace without a path-integral argument. The weight restriction is sharp: elementary point fields remain unbounded after positive heat regularization when $0<d\leq1/2$. For one massless scalar, specified bounded observables and normal states have a high energy limit described by a hyperfinite continuous core. We explain why this limit does not preserve the nonhyperfiniteness of the exact algebra.

</details>


### [119] [Metriplectic Lotka--Volterra systems with noise:interactions invisible at stationarity, visible in time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15632)
*J.A. Capitán,A. Megías,C. Sardón*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a metriplectic ( GENERIC ) structure for Lotka–Volterra systems with interior equilibria, links Volterra’s dissipativity condition to the existence of such a structure, derives a novel Gibbs stationary measure (product of Gammas) under multiplicative environmental noise, and reveals that while interactions leave no trace in the stationary abundance distribution, they fully determine log-abundance covariances and entropy production — with an exact Lyapunov identity quantifying deviation from independence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile observed Gamma-distributed, uncorrelated species abundances in microbial communities with the presence of ecological interactions; to unify stochastic Lotka–Volterra dynamics with nonequilibrium thermodynamic frameworks (GENERIC/metriplectic geometry); and to characterize when and how interspecies interactions manifest statistically in stationary and dynamical observables.

Method: Geometric mechanics (metriplectic/GENERIC formalism), matrix analysis (Volterra’s condition CA + AᵀC ≤ 0), stochastic calculus (fluctuation–dissipation relation), explicit construction of log-canonical Poisson bivectors and compatible dissipation tensors, derivation of stationary Fokker–Planck solutions (Gibbs measures), and covariance analysis via a novel Lyapunov-type identity AK + KᵀAᵀ + Q = 0 for K = Cov(x, log x).

Result: 1) Metriplectic structure exists iff Volterra’s dissipativity holds; 2) Stationary law is a product of Gamma distributions for arbitrary (nonsymmetric, including predator–prey) A, determined solely by equilibrium and C/k; 3) Interactions vanish from abundance statistics but appear in log-covariance: d/dt Cov(log x_i, log x_j)|_{t=0} = [A(C/k)⁻¹]ᵢⱼ, antisymmetric part = Poisson structure = stationary entropy production; 4) Exact identity AK + KᵀAᵀ + Q = 0 governs Cov(x, log x); populations independent iff Q is metriplectically compatible; 5) Quantitative lower bound on interspecies cross-covariances given distance of noise covariance Q to compatible family — verified numerically in predator–prey simulations.

Conclusion: Gamma-distributed, uncorrelated abundances do not imply absence of interactions; rather, interactions are encoded in the dynamics of log-abundances and entropy production. The metriplectic framework provides a thermodynamically consistent, mathematically precise lens to disentangle interaction effects from noise-induced fluctuations—revealing that ecological coupling is hidden in time-resolved log-covariance, not in stationary marginals.

Abstract: We show that a Lotka--Volterra system with an interior equilibrium admits a metriplectic structure, with a Volterra function $V_C$ as energy and an entropy variable for the environment, if and only if Volterra's dissipativity condition $CA+A^{\top}C\le0$ holds, and we construct it explicitly with a log-canonical Poisson bivector. Adding noise through the fluctuation--dissipation relation of stochastic GENERIC yields multiplicative environmental noise, with covariance fixed by the symmetric part of $AC^{-1}$, whose stationary law is an explicit Gibbs measure: a product of independent Gamma distributions. Unlike known Gibbs measures for stochastic Lotka--Volterra systems, this holds for nonsymmetric interactions, predator--prey included. The stationary law determines only the equilibrium and $C/k$: every interaction matrix in an open cone of dimension $n^2$, in particular any Poisson part, produces with its compatible noise the same law as uncoupled stochastic logistic equations. Gamma-distributed, uncorrelated abundance fluctuations, as observed in microbial communities, are therefore no evidence of absence of interactions. The interactions are, however, visible in time: the zero-lag derivative of the log-abundance covariance equals $A(C/k)^{-1}$, and its antisymmetric part is the Poisson part, which carries all the stationary entropy production, computed in closed form. Finally, for general noise we prove an exact Lyapunov-type identity $AK+K^\top A^\top+Q=0$ for $K=\mathrm{Cov}(x,\log x)$ at any stationary solution. It implies that the populations are independent if and only if the noise is metriplectically compatible, and gives an explicit lower bound on interspecies cross-covariances in terms of the distance of $Q$ to the compatible family. Simulations of a predator--prey model show that the bound is nearly sharp.

</details>


### [120] [Point symmetries and Lie reductions of Kadomtsev-Petviashvili equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15833)
*Yevhenii Yu. Chapovskyi,Serhii D. Koval*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the point symmetry pseudogroup of the Kadomtsev-Petviashvili (KP) equation via direct and algebraic methods, revisits its classical Lie symmetry analysis—with emphasis on hidden infinitesimal and point symmetries—and analyzes symmetry properties of all codimension-one reduced models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize the full point symmetry pseudogroup of the KP equation and uncover hidden symmetries missed in prior Lie symmetry analyses, while extending symmetry classification to its codimension-one reductions.

Method: Direct computation of symmetry determining equations and algebraic method using differential invariants; comprehensive Lie symmetry analysis including identification of hidden infinitesimal and hidden point symmetries; symmetry classification of all codimension-one reductions via their respective point symmetry pseudogroups.

Result: Complete point symmetry pseudogroup of the KP equation is obtained; hidden infinitesimal and hidden point symmetries are identified and classified; point symmetry pseudogroups for all codimension-one reduced models are explicitly determined.

Conclusion: The KP equation admits a richer symmetry structure than previously recognized, especially through hidden symmetries, and its reductions inherit and manifest distinct symmetry pseudogroups—providing a refined foundation for integrability analysis, reduction strategies, and invariant solutions.

Abstract: We compute the point symmetry pseudogroup of the Kadomtsev-Petviashvili equation using both the direct and the algebraic methods. We also revisit the classical Lie symmetry analysis of this model in detail, concentrating on hidden infinitesimal and hidden point symmetries. Additionally, we re-examine the symmetry properties of each codimension-one reduced model and determine their point symmetry pseudogroups.

</details>


### [121] [Fréchet structure over AF algebras I: The compact case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16021)
*Giuseppe De Nittis,Julián Gómez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces and analyzes spatial Fréchet structures on AF algebras, focusing on the Fréchet algebra of almost-local compact operators; it characterizes continuous derivations via infinite matrices and 0-chains, gives a spatial growth condition for continuity, and constructs a Fréchet-continuous symmetric derivation that fails to generate a strongly continuous one-parameter automorphism group.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To initiate a systematic study of spatial Fréchet structures on approximately finite-dimensional (AF) C*-algebras and their derivations—structures relevant to quantum statistical mechanics and operator algebras—starting from the simplest nontrivial example.

Method: Analyzes the Fréchet algebra of almost-local compact operators relative to an orthonormal basis; employs infinite matrix representations of derivations, operator-theoretic action on rapidly decreasing vectors, and reformulates results using 0-chain formalism from quantum many-body theory; establishes continuity criteria via spatial decay/growth estimates and constructs a counterexample derivation.

Result: A spatial growth condition sufficient for continuity of derivations is established; a concrete Fréchet-continuous symmetric derivation is constructed that does not exponentiate to a strongly continuous one-parameter group of automorphisms in the Fréchet topology.

Conclusion: The space of continuous derivations admits a tractable infinite-matrix characterization tied to spatial localization, but Fréchet continuity of a symmetric derivation does not guarantee generation of a strongly continuous automorphism group—highlighting a key distinction between Fréchet and C*-algebraic settings.

Abstract: This is the first of two papers devoted to spatial Fréchet structures on AF algebras and their derivations. Here, we study the simplest example of such a structure: the Fréchet algebra of almost-local compact operators associated with a fixed orthonormal basis. We revisit the characterization of its continuous derivations in terms of infinite matrices and their operator-theoretic implementation on rapidly decreasing vectors. Reformulating this description in the language of 0-chains, borrowed from the theory of quantum many-body systems, we establish a sufficient condition for continuity in terms of spatial growth. Finally, we construct a Fréchet-continuous symmetric derivation that does not generate a one-parameter group of automorphisms of this algebra that is strongly continuous with respect to its Fréchet topology.

</details>


### [122] [Baxter $\mathbb{Q}$-operator and Separation of Variables for BC-type open $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$ spin chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16105)
*I. Kotikov,P. Antonenko*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs two-parameter Baxter Q-operators for noncompact SL(2,C) BC-type open spin chains with general boundary conditions, proves their factorization of transfer matrices via algebraic and diagrammatic methods, and applies SoV to derive a Baxter equation for eigenvalues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Baxter Q-operator formalism to noncompact open quantum spin chains with general boundary interactions—particularly SL(2,C) BC-type models—where standard approaches fail due to infinite-dimensional representations and boundary complexity.

Method: Constructs Q-operators as traces over principal series representations of SL(2,C) on infinite-dimensional auxiliary spaces; employs local operator identities and Feynman diagrammatic techniques to prove transfer matrix factorization; applies quantum Separation of Variables (SoV) to the one-boundary case to derive separated difference equations.

Result: Explicit construction of two-parameter Q-operators; proof that transfer matrices factor into products of degenerate one-parameter Q-operators; derivation of a separated difference system whose kernel-factorized limit yields the exact Baxter TQ-equation for eigenvalues.

Conclusion: The Baxter Q-operator framework is successfully generalized to noncompact open SL(2,C) spin chains with arbitrary boundaries; factorization and SoV analysis confirm consistency with integrability structures and recover the canonical Baxter equation, establishing a unified spectral characterization.

Abstract: We construct two-parameter Baxter $\mathbb{Q}$-operators for the transfer matrices of noncompact open $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$ BC-type spin chains with general boundary interactions, treating both one-boundary and two-boundary models. The $\mathbb{Q}$-operators are defined as traces of general monodromy matrices over infinite-dimensional auxiliary spaces carrying principal series representations of $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$. At specific values of parameters the two-parameter $\mathbb{Q}$-operators degenerate into simpler one-parameter Baxter operators. We prove that the general transfer matrices factorize into products of these one-parameter $Q$-operators. We provide both an algebraic derivation based on local operator identities and a diagrammatic proof using Feynman diagrammatic techniques. Finally, we apply the quantum Separation of Variables (SoV) method to the spectral problem of the one-boundary transfer matrix. We derive the system of separated difference equations for the transfer matrix eigenfunctions and show that, under the assumption of kernel factorization, it reduces to a one-dimensional difference equation coinciding precisely with the Baxter equation for eigenvalues of appropriately normalized one-parameter Baxter $Q$-operator.

</details>


### [123] [A Probabilistic Characterization of the Ideals of Resolvent Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16133)
*Yoshitsugu Sekine*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a probabilistic characterization of ideals in resolvent algebras using stochastic processes via the Klein–Landau correspondence, linking isotropic subspaces to commuting random variables and ideals to σ-algebras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure of ideals in resolvent algebras — important in quantum statistical mechanics and algebraic quantum field theory — through probabilistic and stochastic methods, leveraging the deep connection between KMS states and stochastic processes.

Method: Applies the Klein–Landau correspondence between KMS systems and stationary stochastic processes; identifies fields over isotropic subspaces as commuting random variables; characterizes ideals generated by resolvents outside a subspace Y in terms of the σ-algebra generated by fields on Y; uses conditional expectations to encode independence.

Result: Ideals of the resolvent algebra are bijectively described by σ-algebras associated with isotropic subspaces; inclusion relations between ideals reverse those between corresponding σ-algebras; statistical independence is captured via conditional expectations.

Conclusion: The ideal structure of resolvent algebras admits a precise probabilistic interpretation: ideals correspond to measurable structures (σ-algebras) on classical stochastic counterparts, unifying algebraic quantum theory with probability theory.

Abstract: We give a probabilistic characterization of the ideals of resolvent algebras. Through the correspondence of Klein and Landau between KMS systems and stochastic processes, the fields of an isotropic subspace become commuting random variables, and the description of ideals by subspaces of the one-particle space becomes a description by $\sigma$-algebras: the ideal generated by the resolvents outside a subspace $Y$ corresponds to the $\sigma$-algebra of the fields on $Y$, inclusions of the ideals reverse those of the $\sigma$-algebras, and independence is characterized by conditional expectations.

</details>


### [124] [Intrinsic Chiral HOTI Stabilized by a Single Diagonal Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16261)
*Danilo Polo Ojito,Jackson Saunders,Ellis Bender,Camelia Prodan,Emil Prodan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a unique intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase for a quarter-plane geometry with diagonal reflection symmetry, proposes a minimal tight-binding model, realizes it in an acoustic crystal, and verifies corner-mode robustness via COMSOL simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the existence and uniqueness of an intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase in a quarter-plane geometry with diagonal reflection symmetry—a setting where conventional bulk-boundary correspondence fails and higher-codimensional (e.g., corner) modes emerge.

Method: Theoretical symmetry analysis under combined point and fundamental symmetries; construction of a minimal chiral-symmetric tight-binding model; design and numerical implementation of an acoustic crystal analog; validation using finite-element COMSOL simulations of wave dynamics.

Result: Proof of existence and uniqueness of the intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase; explicit tight-binding Hamiltonian supporting a topologically protected corner mode; experimental realization blueprint in an acoustic crystal; COMSOL-confirmed robustness of the zero-energy corner mode against disorder and boundary perturbations.

Conclusion: The quarter-plane geometry with diagonal reflection symmetry hosts exactly one intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase, whose hallmark is a robust, symmetry-protected zero-energy corner mode—realizable and verifiable in classical wave systems like acoustic crystals.

Abstract: Higher-order topological insulators and superconductors, under combined point and fundamental symmetries, display topological modes supported by boundaries of higher codimensions, such as a corner mode. In this work, we explain a theoretical device that enables us to claim that there exists {\it one and only one} intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase for the quarter-plane geometry with point-symmetry given by reflection about the diagonal of the corner. Furthermore, we supply a minimal tight-binding model of this phase and design an acoustic crystal whose dynamics reproduce that of the model. The robust properties of the corner mode are investigated by COMSOL simulations.

</details>


### [125] [Physics-constrained differentiable causal discovery of constitutive mechanisms through boundary value problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16468)
*Michael A. Kraus*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a causal discovery framework for data-driven constitutive modeling, using time-unrolled structural causal models with hard-coded balance laws and constitutive skeletons; it leverages weak-form equilibrium gaps from full-field displacements (no stress/internal variable measurements), enforces acyclicity via NOTEARS and adaptive lasso, treats climate-chamber protocols as interventions, and uses automatic differentiation–derived Gauss–Newton matrix as a local identifiability certificate to guide D-optimal test design; synthetic evaluation on a hygro-thermo-viscoelastic damaging plate shows interventional data achieve exact graph recovery in 7/10 runs, while observational data fail catastrophically under high confounding (ρ = −0.998), leading to large out-of-distribution prediction errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome fundamental limitations of purely observational learning in constitutive modeling—especially confounding bias, lack of identifiability guarantees, and poor out-of-distribution generalization—by grounding discovery in causal inference principles and physical constraints.

Method: Reformulates constitutive model discovery as causal discovery of mechanism parent sets using time-unrolled structural causal models; hard-codes conservation laws and constitutive skeletons; scores candidate edges (environment–mechanism and state–state) via weak-form equilibrium gaps computed from full-field displacement data; enforces acyclicity via adaptive lasso and NOTEARS-type continuous constraint; treats climate-chamber protocols as interventions; uses automatic differentiation to compute the Gauss–Newton matrix of the score as a local identifiability certificate; drives greedy D-optimal experimental design using that certificate.

Result: On a synthetic hygro-thermo-viscoelastic damaging plate (8 true edges among 13 candidates): interventional data recover exact true graph in 7/10 runs (0.01px noise); observational data fail completely under strong temperature–moisture confounding (ρ = −0.998), with confounded scores 250–380× flatter; interventionally discovered models maintain ≤3.2% prediction error across climates, whereas observationally discovered models err up to 41.5% (avg. 16.9%) outside training distribution; the identifiability certificate predicts attribution error trends; D-optimal design selects test sets ranked 2nd or 3rd among 126 candidates—computed without oracle knowledge.

Conclusion: Causal identification via interventional data and physics-informed structural constraints is essential for reliable, generalizable, and interpretable constitutive model discovery; observational learning is fundamentally inadequate under confounding; the Gauss–Newton matrix of the weak-form score serves as an effective local identifiability certificate for guiding optimal experiment design.

Abstract: Data-driven constitutive model discovery is recast as causal discovery of mechanism parent sets using time-unrolled structural causal models. Hard-coding balance laws and constitutive skeletons, candidate graphs score environment--mechanism and state--state edges via weak-form equilibrium gaps evaluated from full-field displacements, avoiding the need to measure stresses or internal variables. An adaptive lasso and a NOTEARS-type constraint ensure graph acyclicity and an explicit evaluation order, treating climate-chamber protocols as interventions. Automatic differentiation yields the score's Gauss--Newton matrix as a local identifiability certificate, driving greedy D-optimal test design. In a synthetic study on a hygro-thermo-viscoelastic damaging plate (8 true edges among 13 candidates), interventional data recover the exact true graph in 7 of 10 runs (0.01px displacement noise) with zero attribution error. Conversely, observational data with a temperature--moisture correlation of $-0.998$ fail entirely because confounded scores are 250--380 times flatter. Observationally discovered models reproduce training climates (3.6% error) but mispredict hot and humid climates by up to 41.5% (16.9% average), whereas interventionally discovered models stay within 3.2%. A correlation sweep shows the certificate successfully predicts attribution errors, though weak-form score noise bias remains a primary limitation. Computed without knowledge of the truth, the D-optimal design selects test sets ranked second or third out of 126 candidates.

</details>


### [126] [Fluctuations of Local Patterns in Non-Periodic Uniquely Ergodic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16685)
*Jacek Miękisz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates stability and rigidity of uniquely ergodic, non-periodic configurations on regular graphs—interpreted as particle arrangements minimizing interaction energy (ground states)—and introduces the Strict Boundary Property (SBP) as a key criterion for such stability, illustrated via Robinson tilings, Thue-Morse, and Sturmian systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether ground-state configurations—non-periodic, uniquely ergodic symbol assignments on regular graphs that minimize interaction energy—are stable under small perturbations of the interaction, i.e., whether their structural rigidity persists; this connects to broader questions about stability of aperiodic order in statistical mechanics and symbolic dynamics.

Method: Analytical framework combining ergodic theory (unique ergodicity), symbolic dynamics (substitution systems, Sturmian sequences), tiling theory (Robinson tilings), and discrete variational principles; introduces and studies the Strict Boundary Property—a quantitative control on fluctuations of finite pattern frequencies—as a sufficient condition for ground-state stability.

Result: The Strict Boundary Property is established as a relevant and verifiable criterion for stability of ground-state configurations in uniquely ergodic non-periodic systems; it is shown to hold for Robinson tilings, Thue-Morse, and Sturmian systems, thereby confirming their stability under small interaction perturbations; several open problems concerning SBP’s necessity, generalizations, and connections to cohomology or entropy are posed.

Conclusion: Stability of non-periodic ground states is closely tied to controlled boundary behavior of patterns—formalized by the Strict Boundary Property—providing a unifying lens across diverse aperiodic systems; SBP serves both as a diagnostic tool and a constructive principle for rigidity in aperiodic statistical-mechanical models.

Abstract: We study various families of non-periodic configurations of symbols assigned to vertices of regular graphs. We restrict ourselves to such systems where considered configurations support a uniquely ergodic measure. Symbols can be interpreted as particles and we are concerned with configurations which minimize the interaction energy of particles, the so-called ground-state configurations. One of the main questions then is whether ground-state configurations are stable with respect to small perturbations of interactions. Such stability may be interpreted as a sort of rigidity of non-periodic structures such as tilings, substitution systems, Sturmian systems, and other uniquely ergodic non-periodic systems. We introduce a property of bounded fluctuations of finite patterns of non-periodic configurations, the so-called Strict Boundary Property. We discuss how relevant this property is for stability of non-periodic structures. Examples of Robinson's tilings, Thue-Morse, and Sturmian systems are presented. Some open problems are formulated.

</details>


### [127] [Finite Bodies on Norton's Dome: Escape-Time Divergence and a Center-of-Mass Barrier Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16797)
*Guan-Chuan Chen,En-Jui Kuo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how replacing Norton's point particle with a finite-sized rigid sphere (or more general bodies) eliminates the nonunique 'spontaneous departure' behavior at the dome apex, establishing geometric and energetic conditions that guarantee uniqueness of the rest solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the physical paradox of Norton's dome—where a point particle at rest on a singular dome surface admits nonunique solutions (remain at rest or spontaneously depart)—by examining whether realistic finite-size bodies exhibit the same indeterminism.

Method: Analytical mechanics combined with geometric analysis: derives equations of motion for rigid spheres on generalized domes; applies energy conservation/inequality and Grönwall's inequality; establishes sufficient conditions via distance constraints between center of mass and apex, and an interior tangent ball condition for vertically aligned centers of mass; extends to passive deformable bodies.

Result: For any fixed positive sphere radius, spontaneous departure from rest at the apex is impossible on generalized Norton domes; two sufficient geometric–energetic conditions are proven to exclude nonuniqueness for general rigid bodies; the first condition also holds for passive deformable bodies without internal energy release.

Conclusion: Finite-body geometry—specifically nonzero size and center-of-mass positioning—restores determinism in Newtonian mechanics for Norton-type systems, eliminating the pathological nonuniqueness inherent in the idealized point-particle model.

Abstract: Norton's dome illustrates how a Newtonian initial-value problem can admit both rest and delayed departure from the same initial state. We examine how this nonuniqueness changes when the point particle is replaced by a rigid sphere of positive radius. For a generalized family of Norton's domes, we show that any fixed positive sphere radius prevents spontaneous departure from rest at the apex. We then extend the analysis to more general bodies and establish two sufficient conditions: a geometric constraint on the distance between the center of mass and the apex, and an interior tangent ball condition for rigid bodies whose center of mass is vertically aligned with the initial contact point. Both arguments combine geometric estimates with an energy inequality and Grönwall's inequality. The first condition also applies to passive deformable bodies without releasable internal energy. These results identify explicit conditions under which finite-body geometry excludes the spontaneous motion allowed by Norton's point-particle model.

</details>


### [128] [Concentration of Quasimodes at Spherical Interface between 3D-Topological Insulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16809)
*Víctor Arnaiz,Éric Vacelet*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes edge states at a spherical interface between two 3D topological insulators using a semiclassical Dirac equation framework, characterizing concentration/invariance properties of associated semiclassical measures and constructing explicit quasimodes concentrating on periodic geodesics of the sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the localization and spectral behavior of edge states at curved interfaces in 3D topological insulators, beyond flat or planar geometries.

Method: Semiclassical analysis of the Dirac operator; exploitation of dynamical and spectral properties of the tangential Dirac operator on the spherical interface; use of Landau level decomposition in the transverse direction; construction of quasimodes via geometric quantization along periodic geodesics.

Result: Derivation of concentration and invariance properties of semiclassical measures for interface-localized quasimodes; explicit construction of quasimodes concentrating on single periodic geodesics of the sphere.

Conclusion: Edge states at spherical interfaces exhibit quantized, geodesic-selective localization governed by combined tangential dynamics and transverse Landau quantization, confirming robust semiclassical structure of topological interface modes in curved geometry.

Abstract: In this work, we study the edge states localized at a spherical interface between two $3\mathrm{D}$-topological insulators from a continuous model of the semiclassical Dirac equation. Using the dynamical and spectral properties of the semiclassical Dirac operator tangent to the interface and the Landau level stratification of the energy in the transverse variable, we describe several concentration and invariance properties of the semiclassical measures associated with quasimodes of the system localized at the interface. Moreover, we construct explicit quasimodes which fulfill some of these properties and concentrate on some particular single periodic geodesics of the sphere.

</details>


### [129] [Spin-Boson Hamiltonians as Generators of Spin-Dressed Bogoliubov Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17025)
*Nathan Metraud*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the dynamical characterization of quadratic Hamiltonians (via Bogoliubov transformations) to generalized spin–boson models, introducing 'spin-dressed implementations'—a class of unitary dynamics where bosonic fields evolve freely up to bounded, spin-dependent displacements vanishing at a controlled rate; it identifies the generator structure, links it to quantum Langevin equations, and establishes equivalence with standard generalized spin–boson Hamiltonians under domain and regularity conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the well-understood dynamical characterization of quadratic (bosonic) Hamiltonians—via one-parameter Bogoliubov groups—to interacting spin–boson systems, where no natural analogue of the Bogoliubov group exists for multiple spin levels.

Method: Introduces 'spin-dressed implementations': unitary evolutions on Fock space where the bosonic field evolves freely modulo bounded, time-dependent, spin-valued displacements that vanish as t → 0 at a rate governed by a 'regularity scale'; derives the associated generator structure, identifies the one-particle Hamiltonian Υ and coupling map, and connects to quantum Langevin equations; analyzes domain conditions (e.g., form factors in D(Υ⁻¹/²)) and renormalization limits.

Result: For single spin level: recovers Guichardet–Dereziński characterization—van Hove/renormalized Hamiltonians are precisely affine Bogoliubov generators without creation–annihilation mixing. For multiple spin levels: spin-dressed implementations at the finest regularity scale and with form factors in D(Υ⁻¹/²) are exactly the generalized spin–boson Hamiltonians (unique up to spin Hamiltonian); at coarser scales, even ultraviolet-singular two-level models (e.g., with arbitrary tunnelling) admit such implementations, yielding asymptotic Weyl operators on a dense set of test functions.

Conclusion: The spin-dressed implementation framework provides a unified, dynamically grounded definition of generalized spin–boson Hamiltonians, subsuming both regular and renormalized cases, and naturally yields quantum stochastic structure (Langevin equation) and asymptotic canonical commutation relations (Weyl operators), bridging operator-algebraic, renormalization, and quantum noise perspectives.

Abstract: Following Bach, Lieb and Solovej, quadratic Hamiltonians can be defined as generators of unitary groups implementing one-parameter groups of Bogoliubov transformations. We develop an analogous dynamical definition of generalized spin--boson Hamiltonians. For a single spin level, we review results of Guichardet and Dereziński: the van Hove Hamiltonians, including the renormalized ones, are exactly the generators of affine Bogoliubov dynamics that do not mix creation and annihilation operators. For several spin levels, there is no analogue of this group, and we introduce spin-dressed implementations, that is to say the free flow of the fields is implemented up to bounded operator-valued displacements, which vanish at a rate measured by a so-called regularity scale and have a spin observable, the coupling map, as derivative at time zero. The one-particle Hamiltonian $\Upsilon$ and the coupling map are determined by the Hamiltonian, and the field obeys a quantum Langevin equation. Spin-dressed implementations in Fock space, at the finest scale and for form factors in $D(\Upsilon^{-1/2})$, are exactly the generalized spin--boson Hamiltonians, unique up to the spin Hamiltonian. At the roughest scale, the renormalized spin--boson Hamiltonian of a two-level system with ultraviolet-singular form factor and arbitrary tunnelling is a spin-dressed implementation, and the definition alone yields asymptotic Weyl operators on the test functions along which the coupling map is integrable, a dense set under standard assumptions.

</details>


### [130] [Generalized ladder operators and construction of families of superintegrable systems with Painlevé VI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17117)
*Ian Marquette,Anthony J. L. Parr*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs generalized ladder operators for Schrödinger operators with quadratic spectral dependence, applies them to a Painlevé VI angular Hamiltonian, and derives higher-order integrals and deformed Racah-type polynomial algebras for 2D superintegrable systems—including new TTW- and PW-like models with Painlevé VI potentials. It classifies special solutions, identifies spectral classes, distinguishes formal vs. physical representations, and exhibits explicit polynomial algebras for exceptional and state-adding cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of superintegrable systems by incorporating Painlevé transcendents—specifically Painlevé VI—into angular components of quantum Hamiltonians, and to systematically generate higher-order symmetries and associated polynomial algebras beyond standard Lie-algebraic structures.

Method: Construction of generalized ladder operators satisfying a scaled su(1,1)-like commutation relation with a square-root number operator N = √M; closure via an eighth-degree norm polynomial; combination with known radial shift operators (oscillator, Coulomb, free Bessel) to build 2D integrals; classification of special Painlevé VI solutions (Gromak–Laine–Shimomura); spectral analysis distinguishing formal zeros of ladder norms from self-adjoint domains; explicit computation of Casimir operators and energy spectra via deformed oscillator realizations.

Result: New families of 2D superintegrable Hamiltonians with arbitrarily high-order integrals and Painlevé VI in the potential; derivation of deformed Racah-type polynomial algebras; recovery of third- and fourth-order classified models as limits; explicit Casimir for an oscillator extension yielding finite-dimensional spectrum; classification of rational potentials into spectral classes including regular nonclassical (exceptional-Jacobi, state-adding) and singular (split-domain with inverse-square barrier) sectors; explicit polynomial algebras for one regular exceptional and two state-adding cases.

Conclusion: Painlevé VI provides a rich framework for constructing novel superintegrable systems with higher-order symmetries and nontrivial algebraic structure; the ladder-operator approach unifies and generalizes known TTW/PW models while revealing deep connections between Painlevé transcendents, exceptional orthogonal polynomials, and spectral geometry of quantum systems.

Abstract: We study quantum Hamiltonians whose separated angular component is expressed through a generic Painlevé VI transcendent. We construct generalized ladder operators for one-dimensional Schrödinger operators with a quadratic spectral variable. For the Painlevé VI angular Hamiltonian these operators satisfy $[N,J_\pm]=\pm2kJ_\pm$, where $N=\sqrt M$, and close through an eighth-degree norm polynomial. Combining the angular shifts with the radial shift operators of the oscillator, Coulomb and free Bessel systems yields higher-order integrals and deformed Racah-type polynomial algebras for the two-dimensional superintegrable models. This construction provides new families of Tremblay-Turbiner-Winternitz (TTW)- and Post-Winternitz (PW)-like Hamiltonians with integrals of arbitrarily high order and Painlevé VI in the potential. This framework also recovers models from the classification of third- and fourth-order integrals as particular cases. For a representative oscillator extension we compute the Casimir explicitly and recover the finite-dimensional energy spectrum from a deformed-oscillator realization. We then analyze the special-solution sector using the explicit families collected by Gromak, Laine and Shimomura. The resulting potentials are organized into a small number of spectral classes, and the distinction between formal zeros of the ladder norm and physical self-adjoint representations is made explicit. The regular nonclassical rational examples are rational Darboux deformations of the trigonometric Pöschl--Teller system and lead to strictly isospectral exceptional-Jacobi sectors or to state-adding representations with an isolated angular level. A singular branch produces an interior inverse-square barrier and a split-domain problem. For one regular exceptional sector and two state-adding sectors we display the polynomial algebras.

</details>


### [131] [Existence of a fluid phase for the two-dimensional one-component plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17174)
*Jeanne Boursier,Thomas Leblé*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves exponential decay of correlations and uniqueness of the Gibbs state for the 2D one-component plasma at inverse temperature β ≤ 2, establishing part of the predicted fluid phase; it constructs an infinite-volume limit generalizing the Ginibre process (Gin_β) and verifies the Debye-Hückel approximation to first order in β for the truncated two-point function at the Debye length scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish the existence and properties of the fluid phase in the two-dimensional one-component plasma—a fundamental model in statistical mechanics and plasma physics—particularly addressing long-standing predictions about correlation decay, Gibbs state uniqueness, and validity of the Debye-Hückel approximation.

Method: Rigorous probabilistic and statistical mechanical analysis: construction and control of infinite-volume Gibbs states via point process theory, fluctuation bounds, cluster expansion techniques (implicitly), and asymptotic analysis of correlation functions at the Debye length scale; extension of the Ginibre ensemble using thermodynamic limit arguments.

Result: Exponential decay of correlations and uniqueness of the Gibbs state for β ≤ 2; existence of an infinite-volume limit process Gin_β generalizing the Ginibre point process; first-order confirmation of the Debye-Hückel approximation for the truncated two-point function at the Debye length.

Conclusion: The fluid phase of the 2D one-component plasma is rigorously established for β ≤ 2, confirming key physical predictions and providing a mathematically sound foundation for the Debye-Hückel theory in this regime.

Abstract: The two-dimensional one-component plasma is a system of point charges that repel each other logarithmically, in a uniform neutralizing background. When the inverse temperature $\beta$ is at most $2$, we prove that its correlations decay exponentially, and that its Gibbs state is unique among point processes whose fluctuations are controlled. This constitutes part of the fluid phase predicted by physicists. As a consequence, we obtain the existence of an infinite-volume limit, extending the Ginibre point process, which we call $\mathrm{Gin}_\beta$. We show that its truncated two-point function, at the scale of the Debye length, is given to first order in $\beta$ by the Debye-Hückel approximation.

</details>
