### [132] [Benchmarking Machine-Learning Top Taggers from JetClass to a Full-Simulation $Z'\to t\bar{t}$ Search: Transfer, Extrapolation, Sensitivity and Inference Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15274)
*Manisha Kumari,Amal Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates seven machine-learning top taggers on realistic CMS Open Data for a Z'→tt̄ search, showing that benchmark AUC rankings do not predict discovery significance—particle transformers (ParT, UParT, GloParT) extend mass reach by 400 GeV due to robustness at high pT and full jet merging; pileup has minimal impact; lightweight CNNs offer competitive performance at low computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Machine-learning top taggers are typically benchmarked on idealized fast-simulation samples without pileup and in narrow pT ranges, limiting their predictive power for real collider searches; this study aims to assess tagger performance under realistic experimental conditions—including Geant4 simulation, pileup, and broad kinematic coverage—to guide optimal tagger selection for boosted tt̄ resonance searches.

Method: Seven taggers—BDT, two image-based CNNs, ParticleNet, and three particle transformers (ParT, UParT, GloParT)—are trained identically on JetClass. They are then applied without retraining to a Z'→tt̄ search using 2016 CMS Open Data simulated with Geant4 and realistic pileup, covering m_Z' = 1.2–2.5 TeV. Performance is evaluated via jet-level discovery significance, ROC AUC, mass reach extension, and computational cost; robustness to pileup and high-pT extrapolation is tested.

Result: Taggers with similar AUC differ by up to 3× in jet-level discovery significance; particle transformers extend mass reach by 400 GeV, especially for fully merged tops and at pT beyond training range; pileup affects all taggers minimally; a small CNN achieves competitive discrimination at far lower compute cost; ranking gaps persist under retraining and within statistical uncertainties.

Conclusion: Benchmark AUC alone is insufficient for selecting top taggers in physics analyses; optimal choice must balance kinematic coverage, analysis working point, discovery sensitivity, and computational cost—particle transformers excel in high-mass, high-pT regimes, while lightweight models offer efficient alternatives where sensitivity permits.

Abstract: Searches for heavy resonances decaying to \(t\bar{t}\) test physics beyond the Standard Model at the TeV scale, and their reach depends on how well boosted top jets are identified. Machine-learning top taggers are usually compared on fast-simulation samples without pileup and in a narrow jet-\(p_T\) window, so a benchmark ranking does not directly show how much each tagger adds to a real search. This study trains seven taggers under identical conditions on JetClass. They are a boosted decision tree, two image-based networks, ParticleNet, and three particle transformers (ParT, UParT, and GloParT). The taggers are then applied without retraining to a \(Z'\to t\bar{t}\) search with \(m_{Z'}\) between 1.2 and 2.5~TeV, using 2016 CMS Open Data simulated with Geant4 and realistic pileup. The overall benchmark order of the taggers is preserved. However, taggers with similar area under the ROC curve (AUC) differ by up to a factor of three in jet-level discovery significance. The particle transformers extend the mass reach of the search by \(400~\mathrm{GeV}\), and their lead grows with resonance mass. This gain is seen in fully merged top jets and is associated with stable performance at jet \(p_T\) above the training range. Pileup has only a minor effect on all taggers. A small convolutional network achieves competitive discrimination at a much lower computational cost. The gaps between tagger groups hold under retraining and within statistical uncertainties. The results show that a tagger must be chosen on its kinematic coverage, the working point of the analysis, its discovery sensitivity and its cost, and not on its benchmark score alone.

</details>


### [133] [Amplitude analysis of the $Λ_c^+\to n K^0_{\mathrm{S}} π^+$ decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14932)
*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,L. P. An,Q. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,X. K. Cai,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,X. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,E. N. Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,J. Chen,J. D. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,T. G. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. Q. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,Z. J. Dong,K. B. Du,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,C. F. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,K. X. Fan,Y. H. Fan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First amplitude analysis of Λc+ → n K⁰S π+ decay using 4.5 fb⁻¹ BESIII data; identifies four intermediate resonances (Λ(1830)⁰, Λ(2000)⁰, N(1440)⁺, N(1650)⁺) and measures their product branching fractions and decay asymmetry parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the dynamics of charmed baryon decays and improve understanding of excited hyperon and nucleon resonance contributions in Λc+ hadronic decays, which are poorly constrained experimentally.

Method: Amplitude analysis of Λc+ → n K⁰S π+ using e⁺e⁻ collision data (4.600–4.699 GeV, 4.5 fb⁻¹) collected by BESIII; employs a model with coherent sum of resonant amplitudes including spin-parity hypotheses and angular distributions to extract resonance contributions and asymmetry parameters.

Result: Four intermediate resonances identified: Λ(1830)⁰, Λ(2000)⁰, N(1440)⁺, N(1650)⁺; product branching fractions measured with statistical/systematic/normalization uncertainties; decay asymmetry parameters α determined for each channel, indicating significant polarization effects for Λ(1830)⁰π⁺ and Λ(2000)⁰π⁺.

Conclusion: This analysis provides the first quantitative evidence for multiple resonant pathways in Λc+ → n K⁰S π+, constraining production mechanisms and resonance properties; asymmetry measurements suggest nontrivial spin alignment, offering input for QCD-inspired models and future lattice QCD comparisons.

Abstract: We perform the first amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon hadronic decay $\Lambda_c^+\to n K^0_{\mathrm{S}} \pi^+$. The analysis uses $e^+ e^-$ annihilation data corresponding to a total integrated luminosity of 4.5~$\rm{fb}^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII spectrometer at center-of-mass energies between 4.600 GeV and 4.699~GeV. The amplitude analysis identifies four intermediate resonances $\Lambda(1830)^{0}$, $\Lambda(2000)^{0}$, $N(1440)^{+}$ and $N(1650)^{+}$. We measure the products of branching fractions: $$\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow \Lambda(1830)^{0}\pip)\cdot \mathcal{B}(\Lambda(1830)^{0} \rightarrow n \Ks) = (2.4\pm0.7\pm0.5\pm0.1)\times 10^{-3}, \mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow \Lambda(2000)^{0}\pip)\cdot \mathcal{B}(\Lambda(2000)^{0} \rightarrow n \Ks) = (5.4\pm1.7\pm1.1\pm0.3)\times 10^{-3}, \mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow N(1440)^{+}\Ks)\cdot \mathcal{B}(N(1440)^{+} \rightarrow n \pip) = (4.0\pm2.0\pm1.5\pm0.2)\times 10^{-3}, \mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow N(1650)^{+}\Ks)\cdot \mathcal{B}(N(1650)^{+} \rightarrow n \pip) = (5.2\pm1.1\pm0.8\pm0.3)\times 10^{-3},$$ where the first uncertainties are statistical, the second are systematic, and the third are related to the normalizing branching fraction $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+\to n K^0_{\mathrm{S}} \pi^+)$. In addition, we measure the decay asymmetry parameters: $$\alpha_{\Lambda(1830)^{0}\pip} = \phantom{-}0.93^{+0.07}_{-0.72}\pm0.07,\alpha_{\Lambda(2000)^{0}\pip} = -0.91^{+0.56}_{-0.09}\pm0.14,\alpha_{N(1440)^{+}\Ks} = -0.13\pm0.43\pm0.35,\alpha_{N(1650)^{+}\Ks} = -0.42\pm0.45\pm0.29.$$

</details>


### [134] [Adaptive Matrix-Based Denoising Algorithm Using Local Spatial Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15544)
*Zhaoli Guo,Qingyu Li,Baolin Zhang,Jiarui Zhao,Aonan Zhu,Liang Peng †,Huilin Li †*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes an adaptive matrix-based denoising algorithm using local spatial support for real-time background suppression in the HLT of high-luminosity colliders, validated on STCF's MDC with >90% background rejection and >98% true-hit retention under realistic conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To meet stringent online data-processing requirements of the high-level trigger (HLT) under high-event-rate and high-background conditions at high-luminosity collider facilities—specifically, low/predictable latency, high precision, and high throughput for upstream denoising.

Method: An adaptive matrix-based denoising algorithm leveraging local spatial support among detector hits; implemented and tested on the Main Drift Chamber (MDC) of the Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF); evaluated using OSCAR-simulated events across five charged-final-state physics channels.

Result: Background rejection >90% for discrete point-cluster backgrounds at low-to-moderate background levels; true-hit retention >98% even when detector efficiency drops to 90%; satisfies HLT constraints on latency, precision, and throughput.

Conclusion: The algorithm is a general, model-agnostic approach for online denoising in regularly structured detectors, as it relies solely on local spatial hit relationships—not predefined track parameters or physics models—making it suitable for diverse high-luminosity collider experiments.

Abstract: To address the online data-processing requirements of the high-level trigger (HLT) under high-event-rate and high-background conditions in high-luminosity collider facilities, this paper proposes an adaptive matrix-based denoising algorithm using local spatial support. The algorithm is implemented and tested in the main drift chamber (MDC) of the Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF). Based on tests with five charged-final-state physics channels simulated using the Offline Software of the Super Tau Charm Facility (OSCAR), the rejection rate for discrete point-cluster backgrounds exceeds 90% at low to moderate background levels, while the true-hit retention ratio remains above 98%. Even when the detector efficiency is reduced to 90%, the true-hit retention ratio remains above 98%. These results demonstrate that the proposed algorithm satisfies the stringent online-processing constraints of an HLT upstream denoising module, including low and predictable average processing time, high precision, and high throughput. Because its filtering criterion is derived from local spatial relationships among detector hits rather than predefined track parameters or a model of a specific physical process, the proposed algorithm provides a general research approach for online denoising in regularly structured detectors used in high-luminosity collider facilities.

</details>


### [135] [Observation of the $η$ $\to$ $μ^+μ^-π^+π^-$ decay and an improved measurement of the $η$ $\to$ $μ^+μ^-μ^+μ^-$ branching fraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15973)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS reports first observation of the rare decay η → μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻ and improves precision of the branching fraction ratio for η → μ⁺μ⁻μ⁺μ⁻/η → μ⁺μ⁻ using 62.6 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV pp collision data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe rare η decays sensitive to beyond-Standard-Model physics and improve precision on poorly measured multi-lepton branching fractions.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data at √s = 13.6 TeV collected by CMS in 2022–2023; signal extraction via invariant mass fits and background modeling; systematic uncertainties evaluated from control regions and simulation.

Result: First observation of η → μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻ with BR ratio B(η→μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻)/B(η→μ⁺μ⁻) = (2.89 ± 0.48 stat +0.42−0.29 syst) × 10⁻³; improved measurement of B(η→μ⁺μ⁻μ⁺μ⁻)/B(η→μ⁺μ⁻) = (0.86 ± 0.06 stat +0.09−0.08 syst) × 10⁻³, 44% more precise than prior result.

Conclusion: These measurements provide new constraints on low-energy QCD dynamics and potential contributions from light new particles or anomalous gauge couplings; the η → μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻ observation opens a channel for future CP-violation and form-factor studies.

Abstract: An observation of the rare decay $\eta$ $\to$ $\mu^+\mu^-\pi^+\pi^-$ is reported from proton-proton collision data at a center-of-mass energy of 13.6 TeV, collected by the CMS experiment at the CERN LHC in 2022 and 2023, and corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 62.6 fb$^{-1}$. In addition, the branching fraction ratio of $\eta$ $\to$ $\mu^+\mu^-\mu^+\mu^-$ relative to $\eta$ $\to$ $\mu^+\mu^-$ is measured with a precision improved by 44% compared with the previous measurement. The measured branching fraction ratios are $\mathcal{B}(\eta \to\mu^+\mu^-\pi^+\pi^-)/\mathcal{B}(\eta\to\mu^+\mu^-)$ = [2.89 $\pm$ 0.48 (stat) $^{+0.42}_{-0.29}$ (syst)]$\times$ 10$^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{B}(\eta\to\mu^+\mu^-\mu^+\mu^-)/\mathcal{B}(\eta\to\mu^+\mu^-)$ = [0.86 $\pm$ 0.06 (stat) $^{+0.09}_{-0.08}$ (syst)]$\times$10$^{-3}$.

</details>


### [136] [Multi-approach Standard Model Effective Field Theory analyses of top-quark associated production in leptonic final states at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16347)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents SMEFT interpretations of top-quark associated production processes using ATLAS Run 2 data (140 fb⁻¹ at 13 TeV), performing a simultaneous likelihood fit across nine final states to constrain 33 dimension-six Wilson coefficients; no significant deviation from the SM is observed, and a process-specific analysis strategy yields superior sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on beyond-Standard-Model physics via top-quark interactions by performing a comprehensive, coherent SMEFT interpretation of multiple associated production channels using full Run 2 ATLAS data.

Method: Simultaneous binned likelihood fit across nine t-associated processes (e.g., ttγ, ttZ, ttH, tZq, tWZ, etc.), with harmonized object reconstruction and unified detector-level SMEFT response modeling; two strategies compared: (1) generic object-counting categorization, (2) orthogonal, process-optimized regions.

Result: 95% CL limits set on 33 real and complex dimension-six SMEFT Wilson coefficients (including top-boson, four-quark, top-lepton operators); no significant deviation from SM; process-targeted strategy achieves better sensitivity for most coefficients.

Conclusion: Coherent multi-process SMEFT interpretation is feasible and powerful; the study demonstrates that dedicated, process-optimized analyses outperform generic categorizations in sensitivity, guiding future SMEFT program design.

Abstract: Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) interpretations of measurements of top-quark associated production processes in leptonic final states are presented using the full Run 2 proton-proton collision dataset recorded by the ATLAS detector at the Large Hadron Collider at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 140 fb$^{-1}$. A simultaneous likelihood fit is performed across final states sensitive to $t\bar{t}\gamma$, $t\bar{t}Z$, $t\bar{t}W$, $t\bar{t}H$, $t\bar{t}t\bar{t}$, $tZq$, $tWZ$, $tHq$, and $tHW$ production, with harmonised reconstructed final-state objects and a coherent treatment of detector-level SMEFT effects across all contributing processes. Two complementary analysis strategies are investigated: an approach employing a more generic event categorisation based on counting reconstructed objects, and a more complex approach combining orthogonal regions optimised to specifically identify individual processes, as employed by previous dedicated measurements. Constraints are obtained on the Wilson coefficients of dimension-six SMEFT operators affecting top-quark interactions, including top-boson, four-quark and top-lepton operators, together with complex operator contributions. The results do not show a significant deviation from the Standard Model expectation and 95% confidence interval limits are placed in a simultaneous fit to 33 SMEFT Wilson coefficients. The comparison of the two analysis strategies provides new insight into the design of increasingly complex SMEFT interpretations. The second, more complex, approach targeting individual processes results in the best sensitivity to the majority of Wilson coefficients.

</details>


### [137] [Light-Meson Spectroscopy at COMPASS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16397)
*David Spülbeck*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports resonance parameter determinations in diffractively produced ωπ⁰π⁻, Kₛ⁰K⁻, and π⁻π⁺π⁻η final states; observes the 1⁻⁺ hybrid candidate π₁(1600) in multiple decay channels; identifies an unexpected 0⁻ strange-sector state; and performs the first event-based likelihood fit of a double-Regge model using η⁽′⁾π data to constrain non-resonant production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe exotic meson spectroscopy—particularly the lightest spin-exotic hybrid meson π₁(1600) with J^PC = 1⁻⁺—and test quark model predictions by analyzing diffractive production of multi-meson final states; additionally, to characterize non-resonant double-Regge exchange mechanisms in light-meson production.

Method: Partial-wave analysis (PWA) of high-statistics diffractive production data; amplitude analysis including resonant and non-resonant components; event-based maximum-likelihood fit employing a double-Regge exchange model for high-mass η⁽′⁾π systems.

Result: Observation of the π₁(1600) in b₁(1235)π, f₁(1285)π, and η′π decay modes; identification of a supernumerary 0⁻ state in the strange sector inconsistent with conventional quark-model expectations; first successful event-based likelihood fit of a double-Regge model to η⁽′⁾π data, constraining non-resonant contributions.

Conclusion: Evidence supports the π₁(1600) as a strong hybrid meson candidate; the excess 0⁻ state suggests physics beyond the naive quark model; the double-Regge analysis provides a quantitative framework for disentangling resonant and non-resonant dynamics in light-meson spectroscopy.

Abstract: We report on our latest findings from studying the diffractively produced $\omega\pi^0\pi^-$, $K_S^0K^-$, and $\pi^-\pi^+\pi^-\eta$ final states and determining the associated resonance parameters. A particular focus is on the signal of the lightest hybrid meson candidate with spin-exotic quantum numbers $J^{PC}=1^{-+}$, i.e. the $\pi_1(1600)$, which we observe in different decay channels including $b_1(1235)\pi$, $f_1(1285)\pi$, and $\eta^\prime\pi$. Furthermore, we highlight the observation of a supernumerary state with respect to predictions of quark models in the strange sector with $J^P=0^-$. In addition, the analysis of the non-resonant double-Regge exchange process using $\eta^{(\prime)}\pi$ data in the high-mass region is discussed. It is the first event-based likelihood fit of a double-Regge model and improves our understanding of non-resonant production mechanisms in light-meson spectroscopy.

</details>


### [138] [Performance of the ATLAS tau trigger in Run 3 and future prospects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16454)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper details the ATLAS experiment's hadronic tau lepton trigger algorithms and performance in Run 3 (13.6 TeV), reporting high plateau efficiencies (90–98%), data-derived correction factors (0.95–1.05, ±2–3% uncertainty) for Monte Carlo simulation, and early results from improved trigger test chains using 2026 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure robust, efficient, and well-calibrated selection of events containing hadronically decaying tau leptons at the trigger level—critical for physics analyses involving Higgs bosons, supersymmetry, and other BSM processes—in the higher-luminosity, higher-pileup environment of LHC Run 3.

Method: Design and deployment of tau-specific trigger algorithms in ATLAS; measurement of trigger efficiency in data (2022–2023) using tag-and-probe with tau-enriched samples; derivation of data/MC correction factors; evaluation of upgraded trigger candidates using early 2026 test-chain data.

Result: Measured plateau efficiencies of 90–98% for single-tau triggers; correction factors of 0.95–1.05 with 2–3% uncertainty across a broad pT range; demonstrable improvements in identification performance from new test-chain algorithms.

Conclusion: The current hadronic tau trigger performs reliably in Run 3, with well-understood and precisely calibrated efficiency; proposed algorithmic enhancements show promise for future runs, particularly under increased pileup and luminosity.

Abstract: The algorithms and selections used at the trigger level in the ATLAS experiment to select events with hadronically decaying tau leptons are described. The performance of the hadronic tau trigger is presented using proton-proton collision data recorded by the ATLAS experiment in Run 3 at $\sqrt{s} = 13.6$ TeV at the Large Hadron Collider. In the plateau region of the efficiency curve for a given single tau trigger, efficiency varies between 90-98%. Correction factors for the simulated tau trigger efficiency in Monte Carlo samples were derived using 2022 and 2023 data enriched with hadronically decaying tau lepton final states, and these range from 0.95-1.05 with an uncertainty of 2-3% over a wide range of the transverse momentum of the hadronic tau candidates. In addition, potential improvements and their performance for the identification of hadronic tau candidates at trigger level are discussed using data recorded in 2026, having been implemented in selected tau trigger test chains.

</details>


### [139] [New measurement of BR$(K^+\rightarrowπ^+ν\barν)$ at the NA62 experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16530)
*Silvia Martellotti on behalf of NA62 Collaboration,A. T. Akmete,R. Aliberti,S. Alibocus,F. Ambrosino,R. Ammendola,B. Angelucci,A. Antonelli,G. Anzivino,R. Arcidiacono,T. Bache,A. Baeva,D. Baigarashev,L. Bandiera,M. Barbanera,V. Bautin,G. Belleri,J. Bernhard,A. Biagioni,L. Bician,C. Biino,A. Bizzeti,T. Blazek,B. Bloch-Devaux,P. Boboc,V. Bonaiuto,M. Boretto,A. M. Bragadireanu,A. Briano Olvera,D. Britton,F. Brizioli,M. B. Brunetti,D. Bryman,F. Bucci,N. Canale,T. Capussela,J. Carmignani,A. Ceccucci,P. Cenci,M. Ceoletta,V. Cerny,C. Cerri,X. Chang,B. Checcucci,C. Chiarini,M. Cirkovic,A. Conovaloff,J. Cook,P. Cooper,E. Cortina Gil,M. Corvino,F. Costantini,D. Coward,P. Cretaro,G. D'Agostini,J. B. Dainton,P. Dalpiaz,H. Danielsson,B. De Martino,N. De Simone,M. D'Errico,D. Di Filippo,L. Di Lella,A. E. D\'ıaz Rodarte,N. Doble,V. Duk,F. Duval,B. Döbrich,D. Emelyanov,J. Engelfried,T. Enik,N. Estrada-Tristan,V. Falaleev,R. Fantechi,V. Fascianelli,P. Fedeli,L. Federici,S. Fedotov,A. Filippi,R. Fiorenza,M. Fiorini,M. Francesconi,O. Frezza,J. R. Fry,J. Fu,A. Fucci,L. Fulton,M. D. Galati,E. Gamberini,L. M. García Martín,L. Gatignon,G. Georgiev,S. A. Ghinescu,A. Gianoli,R. Giordano,M. Giorgi,S. Giudici,F. Gonnella,K. Gorshanov,E. Goudzovski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: NA62 experiment at CERN reports a new measurement of the ultra-rare decay $K^+\rightarrow\pi^+\nu\bar\nu$ using the 2023–2024 dataset, doubling statistics and achieving 5σ sensitivity to the Standard Model prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The decay $K^+\\rightarrow\\pi^+\\nu\\bar\\nu$ has an extremely small, precisely predicted Standard Model branching ratio (~$10^{-10}$), making it highly sensitive to indirect contributions from new physics at very high mass scales.

Method: Analysis of the enlarged 2023–2024 dataset collected by the NA62 experiment at the CERN SPS, employing high-resolution kinematic reconstruction and stringent background suppression to isolate the signal.

Result: A new measurement of the $K^+\\rightarrow\\pi^+\\nu\\bar\\nu$ branching ratio is presented; the doubled dataset yields 5$\\sigma$ expected sensitivity to the Standard Model expectation.

Conclusion: This result significantly advances the precision test of the SM in the quark-flavor sector and enhances discovery potential for new physics beyond the reach of direct collider searches.

Abstract: The decay $K^+\rightarrow\pi^+\nu\bar\nu$ with a very precisely predicted branching ratio of less than $10^{-10}$ is one of the best candidates to reveal indirect effects of new physics at the highest mass scales. The NA62 experiment at the CERN SPS is designed to measure the branching ratio of the $K^+\rightarrow\pi^+\nu\bar\nu$ decay. A new measurement of its branching ratio based on the 20230-2024 dataset is presented. This doubles the total dataset, leading to a 5$\sigma$ expected sensitivity for the Standard Model process.

</details>

### [140] [When Nonlocal Total Derivatives Are Not Variationally Trivial](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14612)
*Carlos Heredia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates total time derivatives in infinite-order and nonlocal Lagrangian mechanics, showing that while they remain variationally trivial in the formal infinite-order setting, they do not necessarily vanish in the nonlocal Euler–Lagrange expression—even when the Lagrangian is an exact total derivative $L = DW$—and provides a smooth convolution-type counterexample.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the variational role of total time derivatives beyond finite-order Lagrangian mechanics, especially in infinite-order and nonlocal settings where standard variational principles may break down.

Method: Analytical study of variational properties of total time derivatives in formal infinite-order jet bundles and nonlocal functional spaces; construction of a smooth convolution-based nonlocal Lagrangian that is an exact total derivative but yields a nonvanishing Euler–Lagrange expression.

Result: Demonstration that exactness $L = DW$ does not imply vanishing of the nonlocal Euler–Lagrange expression; explicit smooth convolution-type counterexample invalidating the direct extension of the 'boundary term' intuition to nonlocal theories.

Conclusion: Variational triviality of total derivatives is not preserved under nonlocality, revealing a fundamental distinction between local/infinite-order and genuinely nonlocal Lagrangian mechanics.

Abstract: We study total time derivatives in infinite-order and nonlocal Lagrangian mechanics. In the formal infinite-order setting, such terms remain variationally trivial. For nonlocal functionals on trajectories, however, exactness in the form $L=DW$ is not sufficient to ensure a vanishing nonlocal Euler-Lagrange expression. We exhibit a smooth convolution-type counterexample.

</details>


### [141] [A cubic closed string field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14670)
*Harold Erbin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a cubic closed bosonic string field theory using a Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation, introducing an infinite tower of auxiliary k-string fields Σₖ to convert higher-order vertices into cubic couplings and kinetic terms; the resulting action is cubic, gauge-invariant, and recovers the original non-polynomial action at tree level via Gaussian integration, while satisfying extended genus-zero IBL∞ relations when the string products form a cyclic L∞ algebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To circumvent the long-standing no-go theorem of Sonoda and Zwiebach (1990) that forbids a cubic formulation of closed bosonic string field theory with a single string field, symmetric vertex, and canonical propagator.

Method: Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation introducing auxiliary k-string fields Σₖ (entering at order gₛ^{2k−2}), turning quartic+ vertices into quadratic kinetic terms and cubic interactions; triangular structure enabling tree-level recovery of original action via Gaussian integration; analysis of worldsheet realization via OPE and entangled two-string states; verification of extended IBL∞ relations linked to cyclic L∞ algebras.

Result: Explicit construction of a consistent, gauge-invariant cubic closed string field theory with auxiliary fields Σₖ; proof that no new physical states arise generically where auxiliary kinetic operators are invertible; worldsheet interpretation of Σ₂ as an entangled two-string state reducing quartic interaction to asymmetric cubic gluing; extension of IBL∞ relations to include zero-input/zero-output operations iff string products form a cyclic L∞ algebra.

Conclusion: The no-go theorem is evaded not by violating its assumptions but by enlarging the field space with auxiliary fields; the resulting cubic framework preserves unitarity and locality on the worldsheet in a generalized sense, provides a computationally tractable formulation for perturbative closed string amplitudes, and establishes a precise algebraic correspondence between cyclic L∞ structures and extended IBL∞ geometry in genus-zero SFT.

Abstract: The action of closed bosonic string field theory is non-polynomial, and for more than thirty years a no-go theorem of Sonoda and Zwiebach (1990) has excluded a cubic formulation with a single string field, a symmetric vertex and the canonical propagator. We evade it with a Hubbard--Stratonovich transformation and provide an explicit construction of a cubic closed string field theory at all orders. The transformation introduces an infinite tower of auxiliary $k$-string fields $\Sigma_k$, entering at order $g_s^{2 k - 2}$, which turn the vertices with four or more legs into kinetic terms and cubic couplings. The action is cubic, at most quadratic in each auxiliary field, and triangular: eliminating the auxiliary fields by Gaussian integrals recovers the original action order by order at tree level, under invertibility and kernel conditions on the auxiliary kinetic operators. The original gauge symmetry lifts to the cubic action with no new parameter, and we argue that the auxiliary fields add no physical state at generic momentum, wherever their kinetic operators are invertible. On the worldsheet, $\Sigma_2$ is an entangled two-string state: by an operator product expansion, the quartic form reduces to a gluing of two asymmetric cubic vertices dressed by the motion of the local coordinates and defined on states without level matching. We expect this picture to generalize to higher orders, defining a gluing of patches carrying $k$ punctures each. The string vertices, read as products with several outputs, satisfy an extended form of the genus-zero $\mathrm{IBL}_\infty$ relations, including operations with no input or no output, exactly when the string products form a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra. Finally, we compare our action with the bar--cobar construction of Chiaffrino, Mamade and Zwiebach.

</details>


### [142] [On Tunnel Fusion and Yangian $R$-matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14793)
*Dmitry Galakhov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the role of the upper-triangular factor U from the Gauss decomposition of the rational R-matrix in Yangian symmetry, linking algebraic (factorization twist/universalizer), physical (Berry phase in SUSY quiver QFT), and geometric (renormalized stable envelope) interpretations; it derives recursion relations for tunneling amplitudes, reveals channel intertwining, cancellations, and wall-crossing, focusing on Y(sl₂) (higher-spin/non-evaluation) and Y(sl_{n+1}) (fundamental).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify algebraic, physical, and geometric perspectives on the Gauss factor U of the rational R-matrix, and to understand how integrability (via YBE) constrains tunneling amplitudes in SUSY quiver QFT — particularly how amplitude representations change across parameter walls.

Method: Analysis of the Gauss decomposition R = LDU of the rational R-matrix for simple Lie algebras; interpretation of U as a factorization twist (algebraic), Berry phase from SUSY quiver QFT (physical), and renormalized Maulik–Okounkov stable envelope (geometric); derivation of recursion relations for collective tunneling amplitudes; study of YBE flatness conditions and wall-crossing behavior in parameter space.

Result: U serves as a universalizer conjugating a pseudo-coproduct to the true Yangian coproduct; its physical realization as a Berry phase implies YBE follows from SUSY-induced flatness; tunneling amplitudes satisfy novel recursion relations; different channels are intertwined, causing fine cancellations and wall-crossing — same amplitude expressed via distinct channel sums depending on parameters.

Conclusion: The Gauss factor U is a unifying object bridging representation theory, supersymmetric QFT, and enumerative geometry; integrability constraints manifest physically as delicate amplitude relations and wall-crossing, revealing deep connections between Yangian symmetry, Berry geometry, and quiver moduli spaces.

Abstract: XXX spin-chain Hamiltonians can be derived within the $R$-matrix formalism. For a simple Lie algebra $\mathfrak{g}$, the rational $R$-matrix $R$ intertwines the coproduct $\Delta$ in the Yangian $\mathsf{Y}(\mathfrak{g})$. $R$ admits a particular Gauss decomposition $R=LDU$, where the upper-triangular matrix $U$ can be obtained from the lower-triangular matrix $L$. We treat $U$ in three ways. Algebraically, $U$ is known as a factorization twist. It plays the role of a ``universalizer'': it conjugates a non-universal pseudo-coproduct $\mathscr{D}$ in the molten crystal chain basis to the true Yangian coproduct $\Delta=U^{-1}\mathscr{D} U$. Physically, $U$ can be obtained as a Berry phase in SUSY quiver QFT for a quiver associated with the Dynkin diagram of $\mathfrak{g}$. In this case, $U$ is calculated as a sum over tunneling amplitudes. Geometrically, $U$ can be interpreted as a renormalized Maulik-Okounkov stable envelope localized at a fixed point. In the physical setting, the Yang-Baxter equation (YBE), which ensures integrability, follows from the flatness of the Berry connection, which in turn follows from supersymmetry. However, the YBE imposes subtle constraints on the tunneling amplitudes, which we examine here. We construct recursion relations that allow higher collective amplitudes to be obtained from more elementary ones. We observe that different tunneling channels are in fact intertwined, leading to finer cancellations among amplitudes and to wall-crossing phenomena: depending on the parameters, the same amplitude is represented as a sum over different channels. We focus on $\mathsf{Y}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ with higher-spin and non-evaluation representations, as well as on the fundamental representation of $\mathsf{Y}(\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1})$.

</details>


### [143] [Casimir Attraction and Repulsion in a Magnetic Domain-Wall Background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14799)
*Inés Cavero-Peláez,Beatriz Gómez-López,Jose M. Muñoz-Castañeda,Gonzalo Sancho-Garrido*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop Casimir interaction between two δ–δ′ plates embedded in a fixed sine-Gordon kink (Pöschl–Teller) background, modeling transverse fluctuations of an S² sigma model / easy-axis antiferromagnet with a domain wall. It finds exact factorization of the background-dressed determinant, orientation-dependent sign of interaction per mode, and non-monotonic position- and separation-dependent attraction/repulsion — with energy and force changing sign at different locations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological backgrounds (e.g., kinks) modify quantum vacuum (Casimir) forces between localized boundary conditions (δ–δ′ plates), particularly in models with internal symmetry (S² sigma model) and no classical plate–wall coupling.

Method: One-loop effective action computation using zeta-function regularization; spectral analysis of background-dressed fluctuation operators; exact factorization of the relative functional determinant via Gel’fand–Yaglom method; sign analysis of individual frequency modes and integrated energy/force.

Result: For v ≥ 2|w|, operators are non-negative; strict stability (v > 2|w|) excludes zero modes. The relative determinant factorises into position-dependent effective plate strengths. Interaction energy and force exhibit distinct sign-change loci: e.g., for v=1.05, w=0.5, d=1.5, energy changes sign at c ≈ −0.44, 2.63; force at c ≈ −0.30, 2.00. Identical oriented plates switch from attraction near the kink to repulsion away from it under rigid translation.

Conclusion: The Casimir interaction in topological backgrounds is highly nontrivial: energy and force decouple in sign behavior, requiring force-based interpretation of attraction/repulsion; background geometry induces tunable, position-sensitive quantum forces even without classical coupling.

Abstract: We study the one-loop Casimir interaction between two $\delta$--$\delta'$ plates in the fixed one-soliton Pöschl--Teller background of the sine-Gordon kink. The transverse fluctuation problem is the Gaussian truncation of an $S^2$ sigma model, equivalently an easy-axis antiferromagnet with a domain wall, in which the plates exert no classical force on the wall. For $v\ge2|w|$ the fluctuation operators are non-negative, and strictly stable couplings, $v>2|w|$, also exclude zero modes, so the quadratic field theory is consistent at the fixed sine-Gordon mass. The background-dressed relative determinant factorises exactly into effective, gradient-dependent strengths of the two plates, and the sign of each frequency channel of the interaction energy is fixed by their orientation. Two identical, equally oriented, strictly stable plates switch from attraction near the kink to repulsion away from it under a rigid translation at fixed couplings and separation. The interaction energy and the force conjugate to the separation neither share their sign everywhere nor change sign at the same positions: there are regions of negative energy and repulsive force, so attraction and repulsion must be read from the force. For such plates with $w\neq0$ the force is repulsive at short and attractive at large separation, and the interaction energy has a minimum at a separation that depends on the position of the pair. For $v=1.05$, $w=0.5$ and $d=1.5$ the energy changes sign at $c\simeq-0.44$ and $2.63$, and the force at $c\simeq-0.30$ and $2.00$.

</details>


### [144] [Solitons of the intermediate long wave equation with residue source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15072)
*Yuto Ishii,Atsushi Nakamula,Soma Yoshida*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the intermediate long-wave (ILW) equation using Sato hyperfunctions, introduces a residue-extended ILW system with self-consistent sources arising from zeros of tau-functions inside integration contours, defines a residue index to classify soliton sectors, constructs exact multi-soliton solutions (including mixed elevation–depression types), and reveals an exponential spectral decay rate tied to complex singularity distances—vanishing at odd-multiple boundaries between residue sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in principal-value integrals of the bilinear ILW equation by rigorously interpreting them via Sato hyperfunctions, and to systematically incorporate contributions from tau-function zeros crossing integration contours—thereby generalizing the ILW framework to include self-consistent sources and richer soliton taxonomy.

Method: Hyperfunction theory (Sato framework) for rigorous treatment of principal-value integrals; contour-based residue analysis of tau-function zeros; construction of exact tau-functions via Hirota's bilinear method; Fourier-space spectral analysis of soliton solutions; classification via residue index defined per analytic strip.

Result: Derivation of a residue-extended ILW system with self-consistent sources; classification of soliton sectors (elevation/depression, higher branches) by integer-valued residue index; explicit one- and multi-soliton tau-functions with factorised interactions; additivity and collisional invariance of residue index (under no contour-crossing); exponential wavenumber decay of soliton spectra with rate governed by distance to nearest complex singularity; spectral rate vanishes at odd-multiple boundaries between residue sectors—signaling sector transitions.

Conclusion: The residue index provides a topological invariant for ILW solitons within the hyperfunction formulation, unifying elevation and depression solitons into a coherent hierarchy; the spectral decay rate serves as a quantitative, observable marker of residue-sector transitions, bridging analytic structure, integrability, and physical soliton behavior.

Abstract: We revisit the bilinear formulation of the intermediate long-wave (ILW) equation by interpreting its principal-value integrals in the framework of Sato hyperfunctions. When the tau-functions have no zeros inside the relevant contours, the conventional source-free ILW equation is recovered. When zeros enter the contours, their residue contributions must instead be retained, leading naturally to a residue-extended ILW system with self-consistent sources determined by the tau-functions themselves. We introduce a residue index that counts the zeros enclosed in each analytic strip and use it to classify the resulting soliton sectors. The conventional elevation-soliton belongs to the zero-residue sector, whereas depression-solitons and higher elevation branches generally carry non-zero residue indices. Exact one- and multisoliton tau-functions are constructed, including mixed elevation--depression configurations with factorised two-body interactions. Their residue indices are additive and remain invariant during collisions, provided that no zero crosses a contour boundary. A Fourier-space analysis shows that the soliton spectra decay exponentially at large wavenumbers, with a decay rate determined by the distance of the nearest complex singularity from the real axis. This rate vanishes as an odd-multiple boundary between adjacent residue sectors is approached, providing a direct spectral signature of the transition between them.

</details>


### [145] [BPS invariants from resurgent topological string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15480)
*Jie Gu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews the conjectural link between resurgence in topological string free energies and BPS invariants in type II string theory on Calabi-Yau threefolds, identifying Borel singularities with D-brane central charges and Stokes constants with generalized DT invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify and consolidate evidence for a deep conjectural correspondence between the resurgent structure of topological string perturbation theory and enumerative BPS invariants in string compactifications.

Method: Review and synthesis of resurgence theory, holomorphic anomaly equations, exact WKB, blowup equations, Stokes automorphisms, flat coordinates, dual partition functions, and Kontsevich-Soibelman wall-crossing applied to topological strings on Calabi-Yau threefolds.

Result: A coherent framework is established where non-perturbative topological string amplitudes are encoded via trans-series solutions; Borel singularities align with integral periods of D-brane central charges, and Stokes constants reproduce BPS indices (generalized Donaldson-Thomas invariants) across local and compact geometries.

Conclusion: The resurgence-BPS correspondence is strongly supported by multiple independent consistency checks, yet lacks a general proof; key open problems include rigorous derivation, physical interpretation of non-perturbative completions, and extension to generic moduli spaces.

Abstract: We review recent progress on the conjectural relation between the resurgent structure of topological string free energies and BPS invariants of type II string theory compactified on the same Calabi-Yau threefold. In this picture, Borel singularities are located at integral periods associated with central charges of stable D-brane bound states, while Stokes constants encode their BPS indices, or generalized DT invariants. After introducing resurgence and perturbative topological strings, we explain how trans-series solutions of the holomorphic anomaly equations determine non-perturbative amplitudes and illustrate the proposed correspondence in local and compact geometries. We discuss the behavior under changes of polarization and moduli, and review supporting arguments based on exact WKB analysis, blowup equations, the Stokes automorphisms of flat coordinates and dual partition functions, and Kontsevich-Soibelman wall-crossing. We conclude with open questions concerning a general proof, the interpretation of non-perturbative amplitudes, and the construction of non-perturbative completions.

</details>


### [146] [Extension of Poincare superalgebra via hidden symmetries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15946)
*Ümit Ertem*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the Poincaré superalgebra by incorporating Killing-Yano forms and conformal Killing-Yano forms alongside Killing spinors, constructing a symmetry superalgebra that unifies geometric hidden symmetries with spinorial supersymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Poincaré superalgebra by incorporating higher-degree geometric symmetries—specifically Killing-Yano and closed conformal Killing-Yano forms—as extensions of the odd (fermionic) part, while preserving the even (bosonic) part as the space of Killing spinors.

Method: The authors define appropriate Lie and graded Lie brackets between Killing vector fields, special odd Killing-Yano forms, special even closed conformal Killing-Yano forms, and Killing spinors; they verify closure and graded Jacobi identities to establish a superalgebra structure.

Result: A consistent extension of the Poincaré superalgebra is constructed, where the odd part is enlarged from Killing vectors and their spinorial representations to include special Killing-Yano and closed conformal Killing-Yano forms, and the even part remains the space of Killing spinors.

Conclusion: Hidden symmetries encoded in Killing-Yano geometry naturally extend supersymmetry algebras, yielding a richer symmetry superalgebra that generalizes the Poincaré superalgebra in curved spacetimes admitting such structures.

Abstract: Isometries of a manifold and special types of spinors called Killing spinors which are related to Killing vector fields constitute a symmetry superalgebra structure corresponding to Poincare superalgebra. The antisymmetric generalization of Killing vector fields to higher degree forms which are called Killing-Yano forms are considered. The odd part of the Poincare superalgebra is extended from the space of Killing vector fields and their rotational representation to the space of special odd Killing-Yano forms and special even closed conformal Killing-Yano forms. The even part is again corresponds to the space of Killing spinors. By defining the relevant brackets, we show that hidden symmetries and Killing spinors together define an extension of the Poincare superalgebra.

</details>


### [147] [Holographic Wilsonian RG of Yang-Mills theory in AdS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16074)
*Soner Albayrak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the stochastic quantization–Wilsonian renormalization duality to non-abelian gauge theories in AdS space, identifying the radial coordinate as stochastic time; it establishes that the Langevin distribution equals the product of UV/IR radial wavefunctions at cutoff ε = t, interprets the process as a Doob transform, refines the stochastic–holographic dictionary (e.g., radial field ↔ Zwanziger’s gauge-fixing function, radial gauge ↔ Parisi–Wu quantization), and derives closed-form holographic Wilsonian couplings (triple- and quadruple-trace) matching known gluon correlators and flat-space YM amplitudes in appropriate limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the equilibrium-evolution duality between stochastic quantization and Wilsonian RG to non-abelian gauge theories in curved spacetime—specifically AdS—where the radial direction plays the role of stochastic time, thereby deepening the holographic interpretation of RG flow and gauge fixing.

Method: Analytic derivation using stochastic quantization (Langevin dynamics) coupled with holographic Wilsonian RG in AdS₄; explicit identification of radial evolution with stochastic processes; Doob transform analysis; matching of bulk gauge conditions to stochastic gauge-fixing prescriptions (Zwanziger, Parisi–Wu); computation of finite-cutoff holographic effective action via boundary-bulk propagators and Witten diagrams.

Result: 1) Exact all-order identification of Langevin distribution with UV × IR radial wavefunction product at cutoff ε = t; 2) Radial field component identified as Zwanziger’s stochastic gauge-fixing function; 3) Radial gauge ↔ Parisi–Wu quantization; bulk gauge A_z = α⁻¹∂_i A_i ↔ Zwanziger’s gauge; 4) Closed-form expressions for triple- and quadruple-trace couplings in holographic Wilsonian action; 5) Infrared limit reproduces standard gluon correlators; total-energy pole recovers flat-space Yang–Mills amplitudes.

Conclusion: The stochastic–holographic duality holds robustly for non-abelian gauge theories in AdS, unifying stochastic quantization, Wilsonian RG, and holography; the radial evolution is intrinsically stochastic and governed by a Doob-transformed ground-state process; the refined dictionary enables precise mapping between bulk gauge choices and stochastic gauge-fixing schemes, and the derived finite-cutoff couplings provide a concrete framework for holographic RG with manifest gauge invariance and UV/IR factorization.

Abstract: In this work, we extend the equilibrium-evolution duality between stochastic quantization and Wilsonian renormalization to non-abelian gauge theories in anti-de Sitter space, for which the radial coordinate of AdS plays the role of the stochastic time. We show, to all orders in the fields for bulk theories without explicit radial dependence, that the Langevin distribution at time $t$ is the product of the ultraviolet and infrared radial wavefunctions at the cutoff $\epsilon=t$. We therefore identify this Langevin process as the ground-state (Doob) transform of the radial evolution. By explicit calculation, we also extend the existing dictionary in the stochastic-renormalization duality: the radial component of the bulk field is Zwanziger's stochastic gauge-fixing function; radial gauge is Parisi-Wu quantization; and the bulk gauge $A_z=\alpha^{-1}\partial_iA_i$ is Zwanziger's gauge. We also obtain the expressions for the triple-trace and quadruple-trace couplings of the holographic Wilsonian action at finite cutoff in closed form; in the infrared, they reduce to the known gluon correlators and, at the total-energy pole, to flat-space Yang-Mills amplitudes as expected. Although our explicit results are provided at tree-level and derived in AdS$_4$, we also comment on the de Sitter counterpart of these statements and on the loop corrections.

</details>


### [148] [Generalized Kosmann derivative and symmetry operators on Dirac structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16309)
*Ümit Ertem,Özgür Açık,Özgür Kelekçi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a spinorial symmetry framework for metric Dirac structures in H-twisted exact Courant algebroids, establishing intrinsic geometric and algebraic structures—including Killing–Yano identities, symmetry operators from special forms, and Lie (super)algebra actions—rooted in generalized metrics and Killing spinors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify generalized isometries, hidden spinorial symmetries, and flux-sensitive Lie-algebroid geometry by constructing an intrinsic, connection-independent framework for metric Dirac structures in H-twisted exact Courant algebroids.

Method: Uses generalized metrics to induce Riemannian Lie-algebroid geometry on Dirac structures; defines and compares Kosmann–Dorfman and Kosmann derivatives for metric-preserving sections; derives KY/CKY integrability conditions intrinsically; constructs symmetry operators from special KY/CKY forms; computes Hermitian bilinears of Killing spinors; analyzes explicit examples (S²×S¹ with rank-two anchor and S³ graph Dirac structure) to verify algebraic structures.

Result: Proves exact intertwining between positive-spinor Kosmann–Dorfman and intrinsic Kosmann derivatives; derives intrinsic KY and CKY integrability identities; constructs Killing-spinor-preserving operators from odd special KY and even special closed CKY forms; obtains such forms from Hermitian bilinears of imaginary-Killing-number spinors; exhibits explicit spin connection and transverse C∞(S¹,ℂ²) Killing-spinor space on S²×S¹; shows that intrinsic Killing sections act via Lie algebra representation; reveals failure of odd Jacobi identity for Hermitian-current bracket on S³, remedied by adjoining an even phase generator to form a Lie superalgebra.

Conclusion: The framework establishes a robust, flux-aware, spinorial symmetry theory for metric Dirac structures, unifying geometric, algebraic, and analytic aspects—particularly linking generalized isometries, hidden symmetries, and twisted Courant geometry through intrinsic Lie-algebroid and superalgebraic structures.

Abstract: We develop a spinorial symmetry framework for metric Dirac structures in $H$-twisted exact Courant algebroids. A generalized metric equips a Dirac structure with an intrinsic Riemannian Lie-algebroid geometry and identifies it isometrically with the positive subbundle of the generalized tangent bundle. For sections preserving the generalized metric, we prove an exact intertwining between the positive-spinor Kosmann--Dorfman action and the intrinsic Kosmann derivative. The comparison is independent of the auxiliary compatible generalized connection. Within the intrinsic geometry we derive Killing--Yano (KY) and conformal Killing--Yano (CKY) integrability identities, construct Killing-spinor-preserving operators from odd special KY and even special closed CKY forms, and obtain these forms from Hermitian bilinears of Killing spinors with imaginary Killing number. We illustrate the framework on a global Dirac structure over $S^2\times S^1$ with nontrivial $H$-flux class and rank-two anchor. Its spin connection and symmetry operators are explicit, and its Killing-spinor space is a transverse family $C^\infty(S^1,\mathbb C^2)$. Finally, intrinsic Killing sections act on Killing spinors by a Lie algebra representation. For a graph Dirac structure over $S^3$, the odd Jacobi identity of the Hermitian-current bracket fails by a phase, and adjoining a single even phase generator yields a Lie superalgebra. These results connect generalized isometries, hidden spinorial symmetries, and flux-sensitive Lie-algebroid geometry.

</details>


### [149] [Finite cyclic monodromy in BMS3 gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16823)
*Evangelos Melas*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies induced data for 3D asymptotically flat BMS gravity on hyperbolic Virasoro orbits with winding, analyzing stabilizers via the Hill operator's developing map; it identifies a discrete cyclic inducing label (unlike massive BMS particles), derives Poincaré helicity ladders, constructs locally flat winding geometries with hyperbolic Lorentz holonomy, excludes such orbits from the smooth real periodic near-horizon current model, and obtains monodromy-resolved orbital functionals—yet no unitary realization on the full orbit is provided, leaving quantum interpretation conjectural.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure of induced representations and symmetry realizations in 3D asymptotically flat BMS gravity, particularly on nontrivial hyperbolic Virasoro orbits with winding that lack constant supermomentum, and to clarify the role of discrete versus continuous labels in quantum gravity contexts.

Method: Uses the developing map of the associated Hill operator to compute stabilizers; analyzes formal smooth equivariant sections restricted to global Lorentz orbits; derives finite cyclic equivariance conditions at rotationally symmetric points; constructs locally flat geometries with hyperbolic Lorentz holonomy; applies monodromy resolution to obtain orbital functionals; examines projected momentum classification (timelike vs. others) and helicity ladder structure.

Result: Stabilizer in spin double cover is ℝ × ℤ_{2n} (line × even-order cyclic group); discrete cyclic label is independent of continuous parameter; finite cyclic equivariance emerges at symmetric points; timelike-projected momentum yields Poincaré helicity ladder with non-invariant rest energy; existence of locally flat winding BMS geometries with hyperbolic holonomy is proven; smooth real periodic near-horizon current model excludes these orbits; closed-form selection rules and orbital functionals for finite monodromy are obtained.

Conclusion: The analysis reveals a novel discrete inducing label in BMS representation theory tied to finite rotational symmetry, distinguishing winding hyperbolic orbits from massive BMS particles; however, a full unitary realization on the infinite-dimensional orbit remains open, and the physical meaning of cyclic sectors in quantum gravity is still conjectural.

Abstract: We investigate inducing data for three dimensional asymptotically flat BMS gravity on hyperbolic Virasoro orbits with winding, which contain no constant supermomentum, and the restriction of formal smooth equivariant sections to global Lorentz orbits. Using the developing map of the associated Hill operator we rederive their stabilizer and show that, in the spin double cover, it is the direct product of a line with a cyclic group of even order. The character of the cyclic factor is therefore a discrete inducing label independent of the continuous one, unlike for the massive BMS particles, where it is fixed by the spin. At points with the corresponding finite rotational symmetry, restriction of formal smooth equivariant sections to a global Lorentz orbit yields the finite cyclic equivariance condition, while an independently defined unitary global branch realizes the associated finite difference equation. At points with timelike projected momentum we derive the resulting ladder of Poincare helicities, show that the projected rest energy is not an orbit invariant, construct locally flat winding BMS geometries with hyperbolic Lorentz holonomy, prove that the smooth real periodic near horizon current model excludes these winding orbits, and obtain selection rules and closed form expressions for finite monodromy resolved orbital functionals. A unitary realization on the full infinite dimensional orbit is not constructed, and the interpretation of the cyclic sectors in quantum gravity remains conjectural.

</details>


### [150] [Infinite spin superparticles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17006)
*Eugenia Boffo,Martin Cederwall*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs infinite spin representations of the super-Poincaré algebra via a higher-derivative superparticle action on super-Minkowski space in dimensions 3, 4, 6, and 10, and employs super-twistor transforms to derive first-quantised spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and understand infinite-spin (continuous-spin) representations of the super-Poincaré algebra—exotic massless states beyond standard higher-spin theories—in various spacetime dimensions.

Method: Formulates a higher-derivative superparticle action on super-Minkowski background; applies super-twistor transforms to map phase-space variables; extracts unitary irreducible representations and identifies first-quantised spectra from the transformed system.

Result: Explicit construction of infinite-spin supermultiplets for the super-Poincaré algebra in D = 3, 4, 6, and 10; derivation of their first-quantised spectra via super-twistor correspondence.

Conclusion: Infinite-spin representations admit consistent supersymmetric realisations in multiple critical dimensions, and super-twistor methods provide an effective framework for their spectral analysis and classification.

Abstract: We obtain infinite spin representations of the super-Poincaré algebra from a higher-derivative superparticle action on a super-Minkowski background in 3, 4, 6 and 10 dimensions. Super-twistor transforms are performed, and used to deduce first-quantised spectra.

</details>


### [151] [Twisting IKKT with branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17270)
*Fabian Hahner,Natalie M. Paquette*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies supersymmetric twists of the IKKT matrix model, identifying seven total classes (two at finite N, five new ones at infinite N), linking them to D-brane backgrounds in type IIB supergravity and BCOV theory via open-closed duality; it also reconstructs MNS localization using BV formalism with quadratic antifield terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the conjectural IKKT/type IIB string theory correspondence by systematically classifying all possible supersymmetric twists—including both dynamical and kinematical supercharges—and to understand how they map to known supergravity and topological string structures.

Method: Classification of supersymmetric twists using Lie algebraic analysis of the IKKT model’s supersymmetry algebra; incorporation of non-commuting backgrounds (D-brane geometries); BV formalism treatment including higher central extensions; expansion around non-commutative vacua to derive worldvolume theories; use of open-closed duality to relate twists to deformations of BCOV theory; explicit reconstruction of Moore–Nekrasov–Shatashvili localization via antifield completion.

Result: Seven supersymmetric twist classes identified: two previously known (finite-N), five new (infinite-N, tied to non-commuting D-brane backgrounds); all equivalence classes of flat-space IIB supergravity twists are realized, albeit with modified orbit stratification due to a higher central extension in the IKKT BV algebra; emergent worldvolume theories include 4D BF theory on D3-branes; all twists correspond to BCOV deformations; Spin(7) twist yields full BV reconstruction of MNS localization with quadratic antifield terms.

Conclusion: The IKKT model supports a rich structure of supersymmetric twists that faithfully encode key aspects of type IIB supergravity and topological string theory—particularly through D-brane geometry, open-closed duality, and BV quantization—thereby strengthening its status as a non-perturbative formulation of IIB strings.

Abstract: Motivated by the conjectural relationship between the IKKT matrix model and type IIB string theory, we classify supersymmetric twists of the matrix model, allowing for both dynamical and kinematical supercharges. Beyond the two twists available at any finite $N$, which we studied in our previous work, we find five additional classes at infinite rank which require non-commuting backgrounds associated with D-branes in IIB supergravity. Together, these twists realize representatives of every equivalence class of twisting supercharges in flat-space type IIB supergravity recently classified by the first author; however, the precise form of the orbit stratification differs. This difference is related to a higher central extension of the IIB superalgebra that appears in our treatment of the IKKT model in the BV formalism. Expanding around the non-commutative backgrounds gives worldvolume theories on products of A- and B-branes, including four-dimensional BF theory on an emergent D3-brane. We relate all these twists to deformations of BCOV theory (the closed string field theory of the topological B-model) using the open-closed duality of classical string field theory. For one of the twists with Spin$(7)$ symmetry, we also show the localization of the IKKT partition function by Moore, Nekrasov, and Shatashvili can be directly reconstructed with the BV formalism, with their auxiliary field completion encoded by quadratic antifield terms. Throughout, we provide self-contained treatments of the formal techniques we employ.

</details>


### [152] [Triple-Massive-Exchange Bispectrum of Quasi-Single-Field Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15660)
*Xingang Chen,Zhong-Zhi Xianyu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides an exact, non-perturbative analytic solution for the triple-exchange bispectrum in quasi-single-field inflation using Mellin-Barnes representation applied to the mixed propagator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The triple-exchange bispectrum in scalar quasi-single-field inflation had remained analytically unsolved across its full parameter space despite advances in cosmological collider physics.

Method: Direct application of the Mellin-Barnes representation to the mixed propagator between massless and massive fields, yielding a convergent double series solution.

Result: An exact, non-perturbative, and physically transparent analytic expression for the triple-exchange bispectrum, valid for general mass and mixing strength.

Conclusion: The method is generalizable and resolves a long-standing problem in primordial non-Gaussianity computation, enhancing predictive power in cosmological collider physics.

Abstract: The triple-exchange bispectrum of the scalar quasi-single-field inflation model has resisted analytic evaluation across its full parameter space since its advent, despite rapid progress in cosmological collider physics. In this Letter, we solve it for general mass and mixing strength as a convergent double series, by applying the Mellin-Barnes representation directly to the mixed propagator between the massless and massive modes. The result is exact, non-perturbative, and physically transparent; the method is broadly applicable.

</details>


### [153] [Magnetic-slope crossovers in the QCD bulk-to-shear viscosity ratio](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15929)
*Aalok Misra,Shivam S. Kushwah,Keshav Dasgupta,Charles Gale,Sangyong Jeon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper predicts a double sign reversal in the magnetic-field dependence of the longitudinal bulk-to-shear viscosity ratio ζ∥/η∥ across the QCD phase transition, using a top-down MQGP-inspired holographic model, kinetic-theory identities with appropriate quasiparticles (quark–gluon plasma above T₀, pion gas below), and consistency checks against lattice QCD equation-of-state data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-time transport coefficients like viscosities under magnetic fields are inaccessible to lattice QCD; thus, first-principles predictions for how B alters dissipation in quark–gluon matter are lacking. The paper addresses this gap by combining holography, kinetic theory, and lattice constraints.

Method: Combines (i) a top-down MQGP-inspired M/string-theory construction of a QCD-like thermal plasma, (ii) a kinetic-theory identity within a single-mode quasiparticle truncation, (iii) compatibility matching to lattice equation-of-state (e.g., speed of sound), and (iv) cross-checks with bottom-up VQCD in the deconfined phase.

Result: Predicts two sign reversals in ∂B(ζ∥/η∥) vs. T: one below T₀ (conditional on a pion-gas rate inequality) at T₁ < T₀, and another above T₀ at T₂ > T₀; confirmed qualitatively by both holographic and kinetic approaches, and consistent with lattice speed-of-sound data.

Conclusion: The magnetic-field slope of ζ∥/η∥ is not monotonic across the QCD crossover—it flips sign twice, reflecting distinct microscopic carrier physics (pions vs. quarks/gluons) and thermodynamic structure; this provides a testable, normalization-insensitive signature for B-dependent transport near T₀.

Abstract: How a magnetic field alters dissipation in quark--gluon matter is almost unconstrained from first principles, since real-time transport is not directly accessible to lattice simulation. Using a top-down MQGP-inspired M/string-theory construction of a QCD-like thermal plasma, together with a kinetic-theory identity within a specified single-mode quasiparticle truncation and a compatibility construction using available lattice equation-of-state data, we propose a normalization-insensitive sign prediction that the field slope of the longitudinal bulk-to-shear viscosity ratio \zeta_\parallel/\eta_\parallel reverses sign twice with temperature---below and above the deconfinement pseudo-critical temperature T_0, at T_1<T_0<T_2 (the lower reversal T_1 conditional on a stated pion-gas rate inequality). A kinetic-theory identity with phase-appropriate carriers, a quark--gluon plasma above T_0 and a chiral pion gas below, reproduces the same pattern, and lattice speed-of-sound data can be shown to be consistent as well. The bottom-up VQCD in the deconfined phase supports a negative slope for large temperatures; the slope crossover is not resolvable from the available \zeta_\parallel/s, \eta_\parallel/s plots.

</details>


### [154] [Silent Heavy Fields: An Exclusion Rule for the Cosmological Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16029)
*Shuntaro Aoki,Zhehan Qin,Masahide Yamaguchi,Yuhang Zhu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exclusion rule for vanishing cosmological collider signals from heavy fields during inflation, covering two classes of diagrams with de Sitter invariant heavy sectors coupling covariantly to a single external field; the rule is proven for exchange diagrams to all loop orders via spectral decomposition and field redefinition, and extended to one-loop polygons; previously claimed approximate signals in these classes must be revisited, while a nonvanishing triangle loop signal is computed for the first time in a chemical-potential-motivated toy model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy fields during inflation may remain 'silent' in primordial correlators despite dynamical evolution — creating blind spots for the cosmological collider probe; the paper aims to identify precise conditions under which such signals vanish.

Method: Spectral decomposition of the heavy-sector two-point function combined with a covariant field redefinition to prove vanishing of oscillatory signals for exchange diagrams to all loop orders; extension to one-loop polygons via matching of oscillatory contributions; explicit computation of triangle loop signal in a toy model with chemical potential.

Result: An exact exclusion rule is derived: cosmological collider signals vanish when a de Sitter invariant heavy sector couples covariantly to a single external field at one vertex, for both exchange diagrams (all loops) and one-loop polygons; previously reported approximate signals in these classes are invalidated; a leading nonvanishing triangle loop signal is computed for the first time.

Conclusion: The exclusion rule reveals fundamental limitations of the cosmological collider as a probe of heavy fields, necessitating reinterpretation of prior results in the identified diagram classes; the existence of a nonvanishing triangle signal demonstrates that not all configurations are suppressed, opening new avenues for model-building and observational discrimination.

Abstract: The cosmological collider uses oscillatory signatures in primordial correlators to probe heavy fields during inflation. This probe has blind spots, as heavy fields can keep ticking in time while remaining silent in the correlators. In this letter, we derive an exclusion rule under which cosmological collider signals vanish. The rule covers two classes of diagrams where a de Sitter invariant heavy sector couples covariantly to a single external field at one vertex. We first prove it for exchanges mediated by the two-point function of the heavy sector to all loop orders, using a spectral decomposition followed by a field redefinition. The result then extends to one-loop polygons by matching their oscillatory contributions onto those of the first class. The approximate signals of previously studied processes that fall into these classes must therefore be revisited. As a nonvanishing example, we derive the leading signal of a triangle loop for the first time in a toy model motivated by chemical potentials.

</details>


### [155] [Anti-periodic Gravitino and Dirac Effective Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16047)
*Karim Benakli*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an effective superspace operator for Dirac gravitino masses in 5D $N=1$ supergravity on $S^1/\mathbb{Z}_2$, using gauge-invariant gravitino curvature superfields and a nonlocal $\Box^{-1}$ kernel; Dirac masses emerge as poles in two-point functions of these superfields, with explicit matching to fermionic components of $N=1$ superspace invariants on a radion-auxiliary background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize Dirac gravitino masses—enabled by a preserved $U(1)_R$ symmetry that forbids Majorana masses—within a consistent $N=1$ superspace effective field theory framework, particularly in extra-dimensional supergravity.

Method: Constructs a spin-$3/2$ bilinear operator in $N=1$ superspace using gravitino curvature superfields; employs a nonlocal inverse d'Alembertian kernel ($\Box^{-1}$); analyzes minimal 5D supergravity compactified on $S^1/\mathbb{Z}_2$ with Scherk-Schwarz-induced anti-periodic boundary conditions; computes two-point functions of curvature superfields sourced at orbifold fixed points; separates physical and gauge modes to obtain a $16\times16$ fermionic kernel; matches its entries to fermionic components of two $N=1$ superspace invariants, including spinor contractions and normalization, on a background with nonzero radion auxiliary field.

Result: The Dirac gravitino mass arises as poles in the two-point functions of gauge-invariant gravitino curvature superfields; the associated quadratic fermionic kernel is explicitly realized as a $16\times16$ matrix whose elements precisely reproduce the fermionic content (including spinor index structure and normalization) of two known $N=1$ superspace invariants.

Conclusion: A consistent $N=1$ superspace description of Dirac gravitino masses is achievable via nonlocal curvature superfield operators; the construction is concretely realized in 5D orbifold supergravity with Scherk-Schwarz breaking, validating the superspace effective approach and linking Kaluza-Klein mass generation to superspace invariant structure.

Abstract: Introducing a preserved $U(1)_R$ symmetry forbids gravitini Majorana masses while allowing a Dirac mass. We investigate how a Dirac mass for gravitini can be described by an effective operator in $N=1$ superspace, in analogy with the well-known Dirac gaugino mass. We find that the corresponding spin-$3/2$ bilinear operator can be expressed in terms of gauge-invariant gravitino curvature superfields coupled through a nonlocal kernel involving an inverse d'Alembertian. We study this construction in minimal five-dimensional supergravity compactified on $S^1/\mathbb Z_2$. The gravitino satisfies anti-periodic boundary conditions induced by a Scherk-Schwarz twist. The physical Kaluza-Klein Dirac masses then appear as poles in the two-point functions of the curvature superfields associated with sources localized at the endpoints. Separation of physical and gauge modes relates these propagators to a quadratic fermionic kernel represented by a $16\times16$ matrix. Its elements match the fermionic components of two invariants written in $N=1$ superspace, including their spinor contractions and normalization. This matching is carried out on a fixed background with a nonzero radion auxiliary field.

</details>


### [156] [Matching lattice and continuum chiral determinants at finite volume](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16544)
*Zhengwen Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves agreement between two lattice formulations of chiral gauge theories—Craig's overlap measure and Hsu–Witten’s Weyl determinant with η-invariant phase fixing—on a flat 4-torus with one SU(5) generation, under smooth gapped paths in the continuum limit, after normalization and subtraction of a local Yang-Mills counterterm whose coefficient is explicitly computed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether distinct lattice realizations of chiral fermions—specifically Craig's gauge-invariant overlap and Hsu–Witten’s reconstructed Weyl determinant with η-invariant phase—yield physically equivalent quantum weights in the continuum limit, especially in the presence of topological and geometric constraints.

Method: Rigorous spectral analysis of Dirac operators on bounded smooth families of gauge fields; uniform convergence estimates for logarithmic variations of fermion determinants; derivation and verification of reconstruction bounds for Craig’s interpolation; extension of Hsu’s small-field comparison to finite-amplitude fields; explicit computation of counterterm coefficients via vacuum polarization and η-invariant analysis; numerical validation in divergent-vacuum-expansion regimes.

Result: Exact agreement of relative fermion weights in the continuum limit along smooth gapped paths after counterterm subtraction; explicit uniform convergence rates for log-variations; proven reconstruction bounds for Craig’s interpolation; matching phase variations even under Hsu’s reflection-positive reconstruction with flat collars (though counterterm coefficient enhanced by profile-dependent factor); logarithmic correction to modulus scaling in hypercharge backgrounds, analytically tied to free overlap vacuum polarization.

Conclusion: The two lattice chiral fermion formulations are equivalent in the continuum limit for SU(5) on T⁴, modulo a local, calculable Yang-Mills counterterm—establishing robustness of the η-invariant phase prescription and validating overlap-based constructions beyond perturbation theory.

Abstract: Recent formulations of lattice chiral gauge theories define a complex fermion weight either through Craig's gauge-invariant overlap measure or, following Hsu and Witten, through a continuum Weyl determinant on a reconstructed gauge connection, with its phase fixed by the five-dimensional $\eta$-invariant. For one $SU(5)$ generation on a flat four-torus, we prove that their relative weights agree in the continuum limit along smooth gapped paths, after fixing a normalization and subtracting a local Yang-Mills counterterm, whose coefficient we determine. On bounded smooth families a uniform Dirac gap suffices: the vacuum expansion need not converge. We obtain explicit uniform convergence rates for the logarithmic variations and, for Craig's interpolation, prove the reconstruction bounds that the matching requires, extending Hsu's small-field comparison to finite amplitude.
