For Hsu's reflection-positive reconstruction with flat collars, the phase variations still match, while the logarithmic coefficient of the quadratic counterterm is enhanced by a calculable, profile-dependent factor. In a hypercharge background on which the vacuum expansion diverges, numerical comparisons at the input gauge field support quadratic convergence of the phase, whereas the modulus acquires a logarithmic correction to quadratic scaling, whose coefficient follows analytically from the free overlap vacuum polarization.

</details>


### [157] [Is Spin Slow in Hot QCD?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16684)
*Zi-Hao Liu,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Light-quark spin in quark-gluon plasma damps rapidly—at rate ~g⁴T ln(1/g)—due to helicity-conserving soft scatterings, contradicting assumptions of slow relaxation needed for hydrodynamic treatment; no slow spin mode emerges, even with global rotation or stronger coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether quark spin can be treated as a hydrodynamic variable in hot QCD, which requires slow (long-lived) spin relaxation compared to typical transport timescales.

Method: Exact evaluation of the full leading-order collision operator in kinetic theory to compute spin relaxation rate, accounting for helicity conservation, soft small-angle scatterings, and multi-mode damping structure.

Result: Spin damping occurs at rate g⁴T ln(1/g), comparable to vector current damping—not suppressed by mass or helicity flips; spatial spin locks to momentum, enabling rapid reorientation via soft scatterings; damping is multi-modal, not describable by a single relaxation time; global rotation induces only reversible precession.

Conclusion: Spin cannot be promoted to a hydrodynamic variable in quark-gluon plasma under standard conditions, as no slow, dissipative spin mode exists—even enhanced coupling worsens separation from hydrodynamic timescales.

Abstract: Promoting spin to a hydrodynamic variable requires slow relaxation, as mass-suppressed helicity flips in hot QCD suggest. Using the full leading-order collision operator, we show instead that light-quark spin damps at the transport rate $g^4T\ln(1/g)$, nearly as fast as vector currents. Since spatial spin locks to momentum, helicity-conserving soft, small-angle scatterings readily rotate it. Damping involves multiple kinetic modes beyond a single relaxation time, global rotation adds only reversible precession, and stronger coupling further disfavors a slow spin mode in quark-gluon plasma.

</details>


### [158] [Gauge Theory Bootstrap with varying $N_c$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16824)
*Rafael Cordoba,Yifei He,Martin Kruczenski,Derek Ping,Jignesh Mohanty,Emil Alegria*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the Gauge Theory Bootstrap to study asymptotically free gauge theories across varying numbers of colors $N_c$ (3–12), extracting low-energy hadronic observables (scattering lengths, effective ranges, LECs $\bar{l}_i$, and resonance properties) and analyzing their $N_c$ scaling—confirming expected $1/N_c$ behavior for $\rho$/$f_2$ mesons but anomalous growth for the $\sigma$, supporting both conventional quark-model expectations and prior unitarized chiral perturbation theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Gauge Theory Bootstrap framework on asymptotically free gauge theories by systematically varying the microscopic parameter $N_c$, and to examine how low-energy strong dynamics—including resonance spectra and chiral low-energy constants—scale with $N_c$, thereby probing the validity of large-$N_c$ counting rules and distinguishing between conventional $q\bar{q}$ and exotic resonance interpretations.

Method: Gauge Theory Bootstrap: a non-perturbative S-matrix approach imposing analyticity, unitarity, crossing symmetry, and Regge asymptotics; $N_c$ is incorporated via sum rules and asymptotic form factors. Scattering amplitudes are constrained to extract scattering lengths, effective ranges, chiral low-energy constants $\bar{l}_i$, and resonance pole positions (masses and widths) for $\sigma$, $\rho$, and $f_2$ in the range $3 \leq N_c \leq 12$.

Result: For $3 \leq N_c \leq 12$: (i) $\rho$ and $f_2$ masses vary weakly with $N_c$; their widths scale as $\sim 1/N_c$, consistent with conventional $q\bar{q}$ mesons; (ii) $\sigma$ mass and width both increase with $N_c$, matching predictions from unitarized $\chi$PT; (iii) low-energy constants $\bar{l}_i$ and effective range parameters show quantifiable $N_c$ dependence.

Conclusion: The Gauge Theory Bootstrap successfully reproduces expected large-$N_c$ scaling for established resonances ($\rho$, $f_2$) while capturing the atypical $N_c$-dependence of the $\sigma$, reinforcing its utility for first-principles exploration of strongly coupled gauge dynamics without Lagrangian input.

Abstract: We use the Gauge Theory Bootstrap to study asymptotically free gauge theories with a varying number of colors $N_c$. The parameter $N_c$ enters the computation through the sum rules and asymptotic form factors, allowing us to tune this microscopic parameter directly and examine the corresponding low-energy strongly coupled dynamics. For $3\le N_c\le 12$, we extract the scattering lengths, effective ranges, and low-energy constants $\bar{l}_i$, and study their $N_c$ dependence. We also obtain the $\sigma$, $\rho$, and $f_2$ resonances for the same range of $N_c$. The $\rho$ and $f_2$ masses depend only weakly on $N_c$, and their widths decrease as $N_c$ increases, consistent with the $1/N_c$ scaling expected for conventional $q\bar{q}$ mesons. In contrast, the $\sigma$ meson mass and width both increase with $N_c$, in agreement with a previous unitarized $\chi$PT analysis. These results provide a further test of the Gauge Theory Bootstrap for studying asymptotically free gauge theories.

</details>


### [159] [Galilean cosmologies in two dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14636)
*Daniel Grumiller,Kai-Yu Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a general 2D Galilean dilaton gravity theory inspired by Carroll black holes, showing its solutions describe cosmological spacetimes and that the infinite-speed-of-light limit yields Galilean extremal surfaces linking singularities; entropy is given by the dilaton at that surface.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the structure of gravity in the ultra-relativistic (Carrollian) limit by constructing the most general two-dimensional Galilean dilaton gravity theory, bridging insights from Carroll black holes and non-Lorentzian geometry.

Method: Constructs the most general diffeomorphism- and Weyl-invariant action for Galilean dilaton gravity in 2D; analyzes classical solutions, their geometric structure (including limiting behavior as c → ∞), and computes thermodynamic quantities such as entropy via Wald’s formalism adapted to Galilean symmetry.

Result: The solution space generically describes cosmological (rather than stationary black hole) spacetimes; the bifurcation surface of Carroll black holes becomes a Galilean extremal surface in the c → ∞ limit; entropy equals the dilaton field evaluated at that extremal surface.

Conclusion: Galilean dilaton gravity provides a consistent non-Lorentzian framework where black hole thermodynamics survives in a geometrically modified form—entropy remains governed by a local extremal surface, now Galilean—supporting the universality of horizon thermodynamics beyond Lorentzian settings.

Abstract: Inspired by Carroll black holes, we consider the most general theory of Galilean dilaton gravity in two dimensions. We find generically that the solution space describes cosmological spacetimes. From a limiting perspective, these Galilean geometries connect the white and black hole singularities through the bifurcation surface. The latter turns into a Galilean extremal surface in the limit of infinite speed of light. We also discuss aspects of Galilean tachydynamics and find that the entropy is given by the dilaton evaluated at the Galilean extremal surface.

</details>


### [160] [Time Advances and Dispersive Constraints in Dressed Gravitational Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14721)
*Ziyu Dong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a low-energy group time advance in 4D gravitational scattering at leading eikonal order with full soft dependence, using a finite-scale sharp dressing profile; it argues that dispersive constraints and high-energy boundedness forbid analytic crossing, linking this to energy-growing internal scales from string diffusion and nonlinear graviton rescattering, and suggests joint hard–soft detection to observe the effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile apparent time advances in gravitational scattering with fundamental principles like causality, unitarity, and analyticity—particularly in quantum gravity where soft radiation and dressing ambiguities complicate the definition of asymptotic states and time delays.

Method: Eikonal approximation in 4D general relativity with dressed (Faddeev–Kulish) asymptotic states; sharp IR-finite dressing profile with controllable soft scale; dispersive analysis (absorption positivity) to constrain analytic structure; incorporation of string-inspired diffusion and nonlinear graviton rescattering effects beyond leading soft theorems.

Result: A finite, calculable low-energy group time advance emerges at leading eikonal order with full soft dependence; analytic crossing of the amplitude is excluded by dispersive matching and high-energy boundedness, implying intrinsic energy-dependent internal scales; joint detection of hard particles and coherent soft radiation preserves interference and enables observability of the advance.

Conclusion: The derived time advance is physical and consistent with causality when properly accounting for dressing and soft coherence; its non-analytic crossing behavior signals breakdown of naive perturbative analyticity due to quantum gravitational effects like stringy diffusion and strong-field graviton dynamics—supporting a UV-sensitive, intrinsically nonlocal effective description.

Abstract: We derive a low energy group time advance in 4D dressed gravitational scattering at leading eikonal order with full soft dependence for a sharp dressing profile of finite soft scale. Dispersive matching of nonnegative absorption excludes the amplitude's analytic crossing with high energy boundedness. This reflects internal scales growing with energy via string diffusion in quantum gravity and high energy nonlinear graviton rescattering, coupled to soft clouds beyond leading soft order. Interference preserving joint detection of hard particles and coherent soft radiation may reveal the advance.

</details>


### [161] [Self-gravitating spinning mesonic tubes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14772)
*Alex Giacomini,Marcela Lagos,Seung Hun Oh,Aldo Vera*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs new regular, spinning, pulsed gyratonic solutions for the Einstein–non-linear sigma and Einstein–Skyrme models coupled to Maxwell electrodynamics in 4D; solutions feature tube-like energy concentration, no curvature singularities, and—when the Skyrme term is included—require asymptotically de Sitter spacetimes and exhibit non-zero topological charge density despite vanishing total charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable, singularity-free, rotating solitonic configurations (mesonic tubes) in Einstein-matter theories that incorporate both pion dynamics (via SU(2) sigma model) and baryon structure (via Skyrme term), while consistently coupling to electromagnetism and angular momentum.

Method: Analytical construction of exact or self-consistent gyratonic (axisymmetric, null-propagating, angular-momentum-carrying) solutions in 4D general relativity; uses minimally coupled SU(2) chiral Lagrangians with and without the Skyrme term, plus Maxwell field; imposes regularity, asymptotic flatness (for sigma model) or asymptotic de Sitter (for Skyrme model), and smooth matter profiles.

Result: 1) Regular, asymptotically flat, spinning pulsed gyratonic solutions for the Einstein–SU(2) sigma–Maxwell system; 2) For the Einstein–Skyrme–Maxwell system, regular solutions exist only in asymptotically de Sitter spacetimes; 3) All solutions exhibit finite-energy, tube-localized energy density and non-zero topological charge density despite zero total topological charge.

Conclusion: Gyratonic mesonic tubes provide a consistent framework for modeling spinning, localized hadronic excitations in gravitational settings; the necessity of de Sitter asymptotics for Skyrmions highlights a fundamental interplay between higher-derivative terms, gravity, and cosmological boundary conditions—suggesting possible implications for skyrmionic dark matter or early-universe soliton formation.

Abstract: In this paper, we construct new self-gravitating solutions for the four-dimensional $SU(2)$ Einstein non-linear sigma model and the Einstein-Skyrme model, both minimally coupled to the Maxwell field, which describes spinning pulsed mesonic tubes. These solutions are gyratonic in the sense that they are characterized by a propagation axis and possess angular momentum. The gyratonic solutions are described by a smooth matter distribution, which is free of curvature singularities. The maximum of the energy density is concentrated on a tube around the propagation axis. We first consider the $SU(2)$ non-linear sigma model minimally coupled to General Relativity and the Maxwell field, constructing asymptotically flat, spinning, pulsed gyratonic solutions. Subsequently, we include the Skyrme term in the analysis. In this case, we show that in order to maintain the resolvability of the matter field equations, the spacetime must be asymptotically de Sitter. It is also shown that, despite the fact that the total topological charge is zero, these solutions possess a non-zero topological charge density.

</details>


### [162] [Spectra and Central Charges of Gauged Curvature Squared Supergravities in Five Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15444)
*Erol Aksoy,Cagatay Okyay,Mehmet Ozkan,Cemal Berfu Senisik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes off-shell gauged 5D N=2 supergravity extended by curvature-squared invariants (Weyl², Ricci², R², Gauss–Bonnet), classifying spectra into supermultiplets around AdS₅ vacua, identifying unitarity conditions, computing central charges a, c and flavor charge exactly, linking vacuum selection to a-maximization, kinetic terms to c and flavor charge, critical spin-2/graviton degeneracy to zeros of c, and verifying c-extremization for AdS₃×Σ₂ backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine how higher-derivative curvature-squared corrections affect the physical spectrum, unitarity, and holographic central charges in off-shell 5D N=2 gauged supergravity — particularly whether such extensions admit stable, ghost-free supersymmetric vacua compatible with AdS/CFT.

Method: Exact analysis in coupling constants while retaining all auxiliary fields; spectral decomposition into 5D supermultiplets around maximally supersymmetric AdS₅ vacua; quadratic fluctuation analysis for ghost/instability detection; exact computation of Weyl anomaly coefficients a, c and flavor central charge via holographic renormalization or supersymmetric localization; application of a-maximization and c-extremization principles to identify consistent vacua and 2D central charges.

Result: R² invariant yields a ghost-free spectrum with a long vector multiplet in a finite coupling range; Weyl² and Ricci² invariants introduce unavoidable ghosts/instabilities via long spin-2 multiplets; Gauss-Bonnet adds no new degrees of freedom — its quadratic action is a rescaling of the two-derivative theory, fixing the Ricci² spectrum; vacuum equation coincides with a-maximization; graviton and vector kinetic terms ∝ c and flavor charge; spin-2/graviton degeneracy occurs at c = 0; AdS₃×Σ₂ central charges match c-extremization, but unitarity fails generically — only in part of parameter space do spin-2 KK towers have positive norm and bounded energy.

Conclusion: Only the Ricci scalar squared invariant admits a finite, ghost-free parameter regime compatible with unitary 5D supergravity and holography; the Gauss-Bonnet term preserves spectral structure but constrains couplings; central charges fully encode dynamics and vacuum selection, reinforcing deep links between supersymmetric variational principles (a-maximization, c-extremization) and consistency of higher-derivative supergravity.

Abstract: We study the spectra and the central charges of off-shell gauged five-dimensional $\mathcal N=2$ supergravity extended by the Weyl squared, Ricci tensor squared and Ricci scalar squared invariants, and by their Gauss-Bonnet combination. Working exactly in the couplings and keeping all auxiliary fields, we organize the spectra around the maximally supersymmetric AdS$_5$ vacuum into supermultiplets. With the Ricci scalar squared invariant, the spectrum gains a long vector multiplet and is free of ghosts in a finite range of the coupling, while with the Weyl squared or Ricci tensor squared invariant, it gains a long spin-2 multiplet and always contains ghosts or instabilities. The Gauss-Bonnet combination adds no new modes: around its supersymmetric vacuum, the quadratic action is a rescaled copy of that of the two-derivative theory, and this fixes the spectrum of the Ricci tensor squared extension. We also compute the central charges $a$ and $c$ and the flavor central charge exactly in the couplings. The vacuum equation is $a$-maximization, and the kinetic terms of the massless graviton and vector multiplets are proportional to $c$ and to the flavor central charge, so that the critical points, where the massive spin-2 multiplet becomes degenerate with the graviton multiplet, are zeros of $c$. Finally, around supersymmetric AdS$_3\times\Sigma_2$ backgrounds of the Gauss-Bonnet extension, the two-dimensional central charges are reproduced by $c$-extremization, while the spectrum is generically not unitary and contains towers of states whose energies stay bounded at large Kaluza-Klein levels. In part of the parameter space, the tower of spin-2 multiplets has positive norm, so that the spectrum has no finite density of states.

</details>


### [163] [How to find regular black-hole islands in the swamp](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15795)
*Astrid Eichhorn,Pedro G. S. Fernandes,Verena Koeder*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper investigates how higher-curvature corrections in effective field theories of gravity can gradually resolve black-hole singularities, imposing monotonicity constraints on metric deviations from Schwarzschild to identify 'islands' of regularity—exemplified at curvature order eight—and tests whether string theory and asymptotically safe gravity satisfy these conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum-gravity-motivated higher-curvature corrections progressively eliminate black-hole singularities within effective field theory, without abrupt regularization, and to constrain the allowed parameter space using physically motivated monotonicity criteria.

Method: Imposes a monotonicity condition on metric function deviations from Schwarzschild geometry as a proxy for gradual singularity resolution; derives necessary conditions for 'less singular' behavior on and outside the horizon; identifies an 'island' of coefficient space satisfying five concrete regularity conditions at order R^4 (i.e., eighth-derivative) in the gravitational effective action.

Result: Five explicit regularity conditions are formulated; they define a bounded, non-empty region ('island') in the space of dimensionless eight-derivative couplings; string theory (type IIA/B, heterotic) and asymptotically safe gravity are found to lie inside or near this island, suggesting compatibility with gradual singularity resolution.

Conclusion: Gradual singularity resolution is compatible with monotonic metric deformation assumptions, yielding testable constraints on effective gravity; the existence of a finite 'island' at order R^4 supports the idea that UV-complete theories like string theory and asymptotically safe gravity may naturally realize such regular black-hole geometries.

Abstract: An infinite tower of higher-curvature corrections is expected to lead to regular black-hole solutions. We assume that this regular behavior does not abruptly arise, but gradually builds up with each successive order of the effective field theory of gravity. In addition, we assume that the effective field theory comes with a single scale of singularity-resolving new physics. We translate this assumption into a monotonicity requirement for deviations of the metric functions from a Schwarzschild black hole. Under these assumptions, conditions can be formulated which trace the increase of ``less singular'' black-hole behavior on and outside of the black-hole horizon within the regime of validity of the effective field theory. These conditions constrain the effective field theory coefficients. To exemplify this construction, we motivate five specific conditions and find a corresponding island of black hole regularity conditions at order eight in the effective field theory. We also explore whether string theory as well as asymptotically safe gravity lie on this island.

</details>


### [164] [Cosmological correlators and kinematic flow on complex Hartle-Hawking saddles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15868)
*Vikramaditya Mondal,Shinji Mukohyama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes cosmological correlators on complex Lorentzian saddle-point geometries (e.g., Hartle-Hawking) for a conformally coupled scalar with cubic interaction, expressing wave function coefficients as discrete spectral sums over the three-sphere; these sums are encoded in Srivastava-Daoust hypergeometric functions satisfying recursion/difference equations, yielding rational, path-independent kinematic flow and explicit late-time boundary correlators up to four points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute cosmological correlators on complex Lorentzian saddle-point backgrounds (e.g., Hartle-Hawking) without explicit bulk time evolution, and to understand how they transform under kinematic shifts ('kinematic flow') in a closed universe with positive cosmological constant.

Method: Analyzes the gravitational path integral over homogeneous, isotropic, closed metrics; constructs wave function coefficients for a conformally coupled scalar with cubic interaction around Hartle-Hawking saddles; expands in eigenmodes on S³; identifies summands with coefficients from the Einstein static universe; expresses results via Srivastava-Daoust multi-variable hypergeometric functions; derives recursion and difference equations for Feynman-Witten tree diagrams (1- and 2-site); obtains correlators by solving discrete kinematic flow governed by upper-triangular rational transformation matrices.

Result: Wave function coefficients are discrete spectral sums over S³, expressible as Srivastava-Daoust functions satisfying universal recursions; 1- and 2-site tree diagrams obey closed difference equations; kinematic flow is finite-dimensional, rational, upper-triangular, and path independent; explicit two-, three-, and four-point cosmological correlators are obtained; a difference equation for the four-point function is derived at the late-time conformal boundary.

Conclusion: Cosmological correlators on complex Hartle-Hawking saddles admit a purely algebraic, discrete kinematic description—free of continuous time evolution—via hypergeometric spectral sums and rational difference equations, establishing a computable, path-independent framework for boundary correlators in de Sitter-like quantum cosmology.

Abstract: The Lorentzian gravitational path integral over homogeneous and isotropic metrics in a closed universe with positive cosmological constant is dominated by complex saddle points, such as the Hartle-Hawking saddles. We ask how to compute cosmological correlators on such complex backgrounds, and how they respond to the shifts of the kinematic variables---the kinematic flow---without tracking the bulk time evolution explicitly. We, specifically, study a conformally coupled scalar test field with a cubic interaction around these saddles. We find that the wave function coefficients can be written as discrete spectral sums with the summands containing the corresponding coefficients in the Einstein static universe, which are obtainable in analogy with the polytopes program for flat spacetime. Whereas the wave function coefficients of the flat FLRW case are given by integrals over shifted kinematics, the discrete spectrum on the three-sphere for Hartle-Hawking saddles produces sums. We identify the coefficients, which depend on the final hypersurface through a phase fixed by its size, with Srivastava-Daoust multi-variable hypergeometric functions. They satisfy recursion relations for any number of sites, under which the sums collapse into rational functions. For the one- and two-site Feynman-Witten tree diagrams, we derive difference equations in the kinematic variables which close on a finite set of sums, giving a discrete kinematic flow. The transformation matrices are upper triangular with rational elements, and satisfy a consistency condition which makes the flow path independent. We then obtain the two-, three- and four-point cosmological correlators. At the late-time conformal boundary, we derive an explicit difference equation for the four-point function.

</details>


### [165] [Cosmological Dressing Rules for Massive Scalars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15870)
*Jonathan Gräfe,Matthias Nowinski,Ivo Sachs,Siva Pawan Venna*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes the equivalence of different Schwinger-Keldysh formulations of de Sitter correlation functions as spectral representations of a common Sturm-Liouville problem, and derives explicit dressing rules for scalar fields using Euler-integral auxiliary propagators compatible with flat-space diagrammatics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify seemingly distinct Schwinger-Keldysh formulations (harmonic analysis vs. dressed flat-space correlators) of de Sitter correlation functions under a common mathematical framework and enable systematic flat-space-inspired computation.

Method: Identifies the underlying Sturm-Liouville structure shared by different Schwinger-Keldysh representations; derives dressing rules for arbitrary-mass scalars by solving this eigenvalue problem, yielding auxiliary propagators expressed as Euler integrals.

Result: Demonstrates formal equivalence of harmonic and dressed-flat-space Schwinger-Keldysh representations; provides explicit, computationally tractable dressing rules with Euler-integral auxiliary propagators applicable to general scalar masses.

Conclusion: The diverse Schwinger-Keldysh representations are unified as spectral decompositions of a single Sturm-Liouville operator; the derived Euler-integral dressing rules bridge de Sitter QFT and flat-space diagrammatic techniques.

Abstract: The Schwinger-Keldysh formulation of de Sitter correlation functions comes in different guises including harmonic analysis and dressed flat space correlators. We observe that these are equivalent spectral representations of the same Sturm-Liouville problem. Starting from the latter we derive dressing rules for scalar fields of arbitrary mass with auxiliary propagators in the form of Euler integrals that are accessible to flat space diagrammatic tools.

</details>


### [166] [Exact Infrared Triangle in Gravity with Long-Range Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15921)
*Sangmin Choi,Ameya Kadhe,Prahar Mitra,Andrea Puhm*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact-in-coupling equivalence between superrotation charge conservation and the massless limit of the classical logarithmic soft graviton theorem in Einstein gravity with massless matter, proposes a tail correction to displacement memory, and computes new higher-order superrotation charges corresponding to 1-loop exact corrections to the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous results on gravity coupled to massive matter by uncovering exact-in-coupling infrared structures in the massless case, particularly the role of long-range gravitational dressings and their resummation in soft theorems and memory effects.

Method: Analytic derivation using asymptotic symmetry analysis, soft theorem matching, and gravitational dressing formalism; exact treatment of gravitational coupling (i.e., non-perturbative in G_N) via classical and quantum infrared resummation techniques.

Result: 1) Exact equivalence between novel superrotation charge conservation and the massless limit of the classical logarithmic soft graviton theorem; 2) A proposed formula for the tail contribution to displacement memory induced by long-range dressings; 3) New higher-order superrotation charges, exact in G_N, corresponding to 1-loop exact corrections to the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft graviton theorem.

Conclusion: Long-range gravitational dressings in massless Einstein gravity resum exactly in the coupling, leading to novel universal infrared structures—superrotation charges, logarithmic soft theorems, and memory tails—that fundamentally differ from the massive-matter case and encode all-order quantum corrections in a classical framework.

Abstract: Einstein gravity coupled to massless matter exhibits universality in the infrared. In the presence of long-range interactions we show that a novel superrotation charge conservation is equivalent to the massless limit of the classical logarithmic soft graviton theorem, and we propose a formula for the corresponding tail to the displacement memory. These results, which extend earlier work on gravity coupled to massive matter in a qualitatively different way, are exact in the gravitational coupling and reveal that long-range gravitational dressings resum. We also compute higher-order corrections to the known superrotation charge which are exact in the gravitational coupling and would correspond to a 1-loop exact correction to the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft graviton theorem.

</details>


### [167] [Imaging the two-sided black hole interior with four-point correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15933)
*Joydeep Chakravarty*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a smeared boundary four-point function to probe local physics behind the horizon of a two-sided AdS black hole, using high-frequency wavepackets, nontrivial time ordering, and a state-dependent operator; enables imaging of interior features like null shocks and matter shells via signal jumps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an observable that probes local bulk physics behind the event horizon of a two-sided AdS black hole—regions inaccessible to conventional boundary correlators—and to detect dynamical interior structures such as null shocks and matter shells.

Method: Constructs a boundary four-point function smeared with high-frequency wavepackets; employs geodesic approximation for wavepacket propagation, non-trivial time ordering of operators, and inserts one state-dependent operator at mid-thermal-circle to access the black hole interior.

Result: The correlator factorizes into a flat-space-like scattering amplitude near a bulk interior point; interior structures (e.g., null shocks, matter shells) manifest as discontinuous jumps in the observed signal when the probed bulk point crosses them.

Conclusion: This smeared, time-ordered, state-dependent observable provides a well-defined holographic probe of local physics behind the horizon and enables 'imaging' of dynamical interior geometry—offering a concrete step toward reconstructing black hole interiors from boundary data.

Abstract: We propose a boundary four-point function smeared with wavepackets, which probes local physics behind the horizon of a two-sided AdS black hole. At high frequencies, boundary wavepackets aimed at an interior bulk point travel along geodesics and scatter locally about the bulk point, factorizing the correlator into a flat-space like scattering amplitude. To obtain this configuration, we utilize a non-trivial time ordering of operators, and also use one state-dependent operator insertion moved halfway across the thermal circle. An interesting property of this observable is that it also probes features of nontrivial states: null shocks and matter shells behind the horizon show up as jumps in the observed signal as the bulk point crosses them, thereby imaging the black hole interior.

</details>


### [168] [Self-Force and Bremsstrahlung in Finite Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15993)
*Kyungsun Lee,Piljin Yi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the self-force and Bremsstrahlung radiation for a point charge in Born–Infeld nonlinear electrodynamics, demonstrating consistency between local self-force and global radiative dissipation, and highlighting how residual nonlinear effects modify multipole structure and deviate from Maxwellian behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend classical self-force analysis—well-studied in Maxwell electrodynamics—to nonlinear electrodynamics (specifically Born–Infeld), where finite self-energy and regularized self-fields are naturally accommodated, and to resolve conceptual tensions between different formulations of the self-force.

Method: Analytical computation of both the local electromagnetic self-force (via stress-tensor integration near the worldline) and global radiative energy-momentum loss (via asymptotic field analysis and flux integrals) in Born–Infeld theory; comparison and consistency check between the two; examination of effective multipole moments induced by nonlinearity; discussion of two distinct self-force definitions in literature.

Result: The local self-force and global radiative dissipation are shown to be mutually consistent in Born–Infeld electrodynamics; nonlinearity, though asymptotically suppressed, leaves observable imprints via modified effective dipole and higher multipoles of a moving charge, leading to measurable deviations from Maxwell predictions; the two common self-force definitions (e.g., Abraham–Lorentz vs. radiation-reaction-based) are reconciled within this framework.

Conclusion: Born–Infeld electrodynamics provides a physically consistent, ultraviolet-finite setting for studying the classical self-force and radiation reaction; residual nonlinear effects persist in the dynamics despite asymptotic linearity, necessitating careful treatment of effective sources and multipole structure; the analysis clarifies foundational aspects of self-interaction in nonlinear field theories.

Abstract: We study the self-force and the accompanying Bremsstrahlung for a moving point charge in nonlinear electrodynamics with finite self-field and self-energy. The self-force in the Maxwell theory has been the subject of many studies, often by renowned physicists. After reviewing Coleman's lucid treatise for Maxwell among them, we wish to explore the same question in the Born--Infeld theory, our prototype. We independently compute the local self-force and the global radiative dissipation, and show how the two are mutually consistent. Despite the nonlinearity turning off for the asymptotic radiative field, its residual effect survives via the effective multipole moments of a moving charge, allowing deviation from Maxwell. We close with a detailed discussion about two different manifestations of the self-force in the literature and how these enter our computation. One of the appendices explores Born--Infeld ModMax as a generalization.

</details>


### [169] [Holographic Counterterms from Restricted Weyl Symmetry in Even-Dimensional Einstein-AdS Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16187)
*Giorgos Anastasiou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a symmetry-driven renormalization method for even-dimensional Einstein-AdS gravity using asymptotically-preserving infinitesimal bulk Weyl transformations; it constructs a bulk-covariant completion of the Einstein-Hilbert action via Euler density and covariant total derivatives, yielding closed-form, on-shell finite renormalized actions in all even dimensions—including explicit 10D expressions—and validates consistency with holographic renormalization for black hole geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities and divergences in the gravitational action for even-dimensional asymptotically locally AdS (AlAdS) spacetimes, leveraging boundary conformal symmetry—specifically, by exploiting infinitesimal Weyl transformations that preserve field equations asymptotically—to systematically construct a covariant, cutoff-independent renormalized action.

Method: Imposes vanishing variation of the Einstein-Hilbert density under restricted bulk Weyl transformations (with parameter Σ(X)), modulo integrable boundary terms as the radial cutoff is removed; solves resulting recursive relations for compensating terms—Euler density and hierarchy of covariant total derivatives—using factorized shifted Laplacian operators; matches normalizations to ambient construction of conformal invariants on Einstein backgrounds.

Result: Derives a closed-form, bulk-covariant renormalized action valid in arbitrary even dimensions; recovers known 4D, 6D, and 8D conformally renormalized actions; provides first explicit 10D completion involving □³; proves on-shell finiteness of Euclidean AlAdS Einstein geometries via renormalized-volume identity; confirms finite on-shell actions for 8D and 10D AdS black holes with non-conformally-flat horizons, matching Holographic Renormalization predictions.

Conclusion: The symmetry-based renormalization scheme—rooted in asymptotic Weyl invariance—is both systematic and dimensionally universal for even-dimensional Einstein-AdS gravity; it unifies and generalizes prior results, offers explicit higher-dimensional prescriptions, and establishes rigorous on-shell finiteness without ad hoc counterterms.

Abstract: We develop a symmetry-based approach to renormalization for even-dimensional Einstein-AdS gravity based on infinitesimal bulk Weyl transformations that preserve its field equations asymptotically. We determine a bulk-covariant completion of the Einstein-Hilbert density by requiring its variation under restricted Weyl transformations with parameter $\Sigma(X)$ to vanish modulo terms whose integrals remain finite as the radial cutoff is removed. This requirement induces two types of compensating terms with fixed couplings: the Euler density and a hierarchy of covariant total derivatives. The resulting recursive relations admit a factorized solution in terms of shifted Laplacians, yielding a closed-form completion in arbitrary even bulk dimensions. On Einstein backgrounds, these expressions agree, after matching normalizations, with those obtained through the ambient construction of conformal invariants. The associated renormalized-volume identity establishes on-shell finiteness for Euclidean AlAdS Einstein geometries in arbitrary even dimensions. The prescription reproduces the known conformally renormalized actions in four, six and eight dimensions and provides an explicit ten-dimensional completion involving powers of the Laplacian up to $\Box^3$. Applications to eight- and ten-dimensional AdS black holes with non-conformally-flat Einstein horizons yield finite on-shell actions in agreement with Holographic Renormalization.

</details>


### [170] [Thermodynamics within and across AdS Gauss--Bonnet holographic CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16364)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates thermal phase competition in holographic CFTs using 5D AdS Gauss–Bonnet gravity, deriving a boundary first law to identify work conjugate to the dimensionless coupling; it finds first-order transitions between CFTs with different anomaly coefficients, establishes a generalized Clapeyron relation for the coexistence line, identifies critical behavior (entropy gap ∝ |Q−Qc|^{1/2}, heat capacity divergence ∝ |T−Tc|^{-2/3}) near a vanishing critical charge, and characterizes Hawking–Page and small–large black hole coexistence surfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermal stability and phase structure depend on higher-curvature corrections (Gauss–Bonnet) in holographic CFTs, particularly how anomaly coefficients govern cross-theory competition and critical phenomena beyond Einstein gravity.

Method: Holographic thermodynamics in five-dimensional AdS Gauss–Bonnet gravity; derivation of boundary first law with unit-lapse cylinder clock; analysis of charged spherical black hole saddles; identification of coexistence lines via free energy comparison; scaling analysis near critical points; use of zero-type-D vacuum energy and fixed-radius concavity to reduce global comparison to endpoint CFTs.

Result: A first-order phase transition between CFTs at coupling endpoints governed by a generalized Clapeyron relation; emergence of a kinked cross-theory boundary when Einstein endpoint exhibits its own small–large black hole coexistence; neutral Hawking–Page line and charged small–large coexistence surface mapped; critical charge Qc → 0 at finite coupling; entropy gap closes as |Q−Qc|^{1/2}; heat capacity diverges as |T−Tc|^{-2/3} at critical charge.

Conclusion: Thermal phase structure in holographic CFTs is highly sensitive to higher-curvature couplings and anomaly coefficients; Gauss–Bonnet gravity introduces rich cross-theory competition and novel critical behavior distinct from Einstein gravity, with universal scaling exponents signaling mean-field-like criticality modified by gravitational anomalies.

Abstract: We study thermal competition within individual holographic conformal field theories (CFTs) and across theories with different anomaly coefficients in five-dimensional AdS Gauss--Bonnet gravity. We derive the work term conjugate to the dimensionless coupling from the boundary first law and use a common boundary clock to compare charged spherical saddles at fixed external source. Varying this source drives a first-order switch between the CFTs at the endpoints of the chosen coupling interval. In the unit-lapse cylinder clock with the zero-type-D vacuum energy, strict concavity at fixed radius reduces the global comparison to these two endpoints. The preferred coupling jumps across the coexistence line, whose slope is related to the entropy difference by a generalized Clapeyron relation. Where the Einstein endpoint itself undergoes small--large coexistence, a third saddle joins the competition and produces a kink in the cross-theory boundary. A compensating change of the external control preserves saddle ordering under finite energy shifts. Within fixed CFTs, we determine the neutral Hawking--Page line and the charged small--large black hole coexistence surface. The critical charge tends to zero at a finite coupling. The entropy gap closes with exponent one half, and the heat capacity diverges with exponent two thirds along a temperature sweep at the critical charge.

</details>


### [171] [Holographic viscous transport in scalar-coupled Gauss-Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16936)
*Tao Ma,Song He,Li Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests horizon-based formulas for shear and bulk viscosities in a 5D Einstein-dilaton-Gauss-Bonnet holographic model calibrated to lattice QCD. It compares horizon-conserved-current predictions against Kubo-formula results from renormalized stress-tensor correlators, finding agreement for η/s minima and validating the unmodified Eling-Oz formula for ζ/s—even with scalar-dependent coupling and finite Gauss-Bonnet α.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously test the validity of horizon-derived viscosity formulas—particularly the Eling-Oz (EO) formula for bulk viscosity—against first-principles boundary calculations (Kubo relations) in a realistic, UV-complete holographic QCD model beyond simple constant-coupling or small-α approximations.

Method: Computes renormalized retarded stress-tensor correlators in a 5D Einstein-dilaton-Gauss-Bonnet gravity model matched to lattice QCD equation of state; solves full fluctuation equations without horizon conservation assumptions; includes curvature and dilaton counterterms for UV finiteness; evaluates η/s and ζ/s via both Kubo (boundary) and horizon conserved-current methods, including exact α-dependence and scalar-dependent coupling.

Result: Horizon conserved-current method reproduces Kubo η/s for constant coupling; both methods show a minimum in η/s for scalar-dependent coupling; for ζ/s, first-order-in-α truncation underestimates peak height but preserves peak temperature; the unmodified EO formula ζ/s = (4π)⁻¹(∂φₕ/∂ln s)² accurately matches boundary results across couplings, confirming the 1/(4π) prefactor remains valid despite Gauss-Bonnet corrections to η/s.

Conclusion: The unmodified Eling-Oz formula for bulk viscosity is robust beyond constant coupling and small-α limits, and horizon prescriptions for transport coefficients are quantitatively validated against boundary Kubo results in a phenomenologically relevant holographic QCD setup.

Abstract: We test horizon formulas for the shear and bulk viscosities of a holographic plasma against viscosities extracted directly from retarded stress-tensor correlators. We work in a five-dimensional Einstein-dilaton-Gauss-Bonnet model calibrated to the lattice QCD equation of state at zero baryon density. UV-finite renormalized correlators are obtained by including the required curvature and scalar counterterms, and the fluctuation equations are solved while retaining the full dependence on the Gauss-Bonnet coupling $\alpha$, without using a horizon conservation law or an entropy-production formula. For constant coupling, the horizon conserved-current calculation reproduces the boundary Kubo results in both channels. For the scalar-dependent coupling studied here, both methods find a minimum in the shear-viscosity-to-entropy-density ratio $\eta/s$. In the bulk channel, the conserved-current calculation truncated at first order in $\alpha$ yields a lower peak height in $\zeta/s$, with the peak temperature nearly unchanged relative to the boundary result. For both couplings, the unmodified Eling-Oz (EO) formula, $\zeta/s=(4\pi)^{-1}(\partial\phi_h/\partial\ln s)^2$, reproduces the boundary result for $\zeta/s$, where $\phi_h$ is the horizon value of the scalar. The prefactor $1/(4\pi)$ is thus retained even when the Gauss-Bonnet interaction shifts $\eta/s$ from this value. These results provide a direct boundary benchmark for horizon prescriptions and support the unmodified EO formula beyond constant coupling.

</details>


### [172] [De Sitter Gravity and Emergent Time from Fusion Matrix Product States and SYK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17045)
*Shinsei Ryu,Yu-ki Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a quantum-mechanical model—realized as a matrix-product-state quantum channel, related to a chordless limit of the SYK model—that is dual to 2D de Sitter gravity; it recovers SL(2,R) boundary symmetry, Schwarzian dynamics, JT disk amplitude, and matches Bunch-Davies two-point functions, offering a concrete realization of dS/CFT in 2D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a concrete, solvable quantum mechanical dual for two-dimensional de Sitter gravity, thereby advancing the dS/CFT correspondence—a holographic framework lacking robust microscopic realizations unlike AdS/CFT.

Method: Constructs a quantum channel via a matrix product state that fuses one spin per step; takes a chordless (non-crossing) limit of the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev model; identifies emergent SL(2,R) symmetry with the future conformal boundary of dS₂; derives the Schwarzian action and JT gravity disk amplitude from a random walk interpretation; computes and compares scalar two-point functions in the Bunch-Davies vacuum.

Result: The model reproduces the SL(2,R) isometry of the future boundary of dS₂; yields the correct Schwarzian effective action and Jackiw-Teitelboim gravity disk partition function; and matches the exact Bunch-Davies two-point function for free massless scalars on dS₂.

Conclusion: A well-defined, tractable quantum-mechanical model—rooted in tensor networks and SYK-like structure—realizes dS/CFT for 2D de Sitter gravity, establishing a microscopic foundation for holography in expanding spacetimes.

Abstract: We propose a quantum-mechanical model dual to two-dimensional de Sitter (dS) gravity. It is a quantum channel, defined by a matrix product state that fuses one spin at each step, and is related to a limit of the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev model without chord crossings. Its simplest part is a random walk with an emergent $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ symmetry, which we identify with the symmetry of the future boundary of dS. It selects a time variable in which the walk obeys the Schwarzian action and yields the disk amplitude of dS Jackiw-Teitelboim gravity. As the simplest case, we consider free massless scalar fields in the fixed background of dS$_2$. We find the agreement of the two-point function in the Bunch-Davies vacuum and the matching of symmetry. Our construction provides a concrete model for the dS/CFT correspondence for two-dimensional de Sitter gravity.

</details>


### [173] [Quantum and classical thermal equilibrium for probabilistic classical fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15749)
*C. Wetterich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a formal correspondence between classical probabilistic field theories and quantum field theory, showing that free classical fields can be mapped to quantum subsystems, and that thermal equilibria (Rayleigh-Jeans vs. Planck) are dynamically selected under interactions — not dictated by fundamental principles — with numerical simulations used to distinguish asymptotic selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the physical origin of quantum statistics (e.g., Planck spectrum) versus classical statistical behavior (e.g., Rayleigh-Jeans divergence) in field theories, and to determine whether their distinction arises from fundamental axioms or from dynamical selection under interactions.

Method: Constructs a rigorous 'classical-quantum map' linking classical probability distributions of free fields to quantum density matrices; analyzes momentum dependence of energy and field correlations in classical vs. quantum thermal equilibrium; employs numerical simulations of interacting probabilistic classical fields to probe asymptotic dynamical selection.

Result: Demonstrates that free classical fields embed quantum field theory as a subsystem via a well-defined classical-quantum map; shows that Rayleigh-Jeans and Planck spectra correspond to distinct classical probability distributions; reveals that interactions break degeneracy and dynamically select one equilibrium state asymptotically, identifiable via correlation scaling in simulations.

Conclusion: The choice between classical (Rayleigh-Jeans) and quantum (Planck) thermal spectra is not foundational but emergent — determined by interaction-driven dynamics; quantum behavior can arise from classical stochastic field evolution under appropriate asymptotic conditions.

Abstract: The time evolution of the probability distribution for classical fields can be described by the quantum formalism. For free classical fields the free quantum field theory arises as a subsystem. Different probability distributions for classical fields correspond to the classical thermal equilibrium with the Rayleigh-Jeans divergence or the quantum thermal equilibrium with the Planck spectrum. The selection of the Rayleigh-Jeans or Planck spectrum is not a question of fundamental concepts. It becomes an issue of dynamical selection once interactions are present. We compare the momentum dependence of the classical energy and field correlations for the classical and quantum equilibrium. This permits to decide which equilibrium is selected as a result of asymptotic dynamics by numerical simulations of the probabilistic classical fields including interactions. For free probabilistic classical fields we construct a general ``classical-quantum map'' between the classical probability distribution and the density matrix of the quantum field theory.

</details>


### [174] [Anatomy of AdS black hole on-shell actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14688)
*Junho Hong,Sourav Roychowdhury*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper compares holographic renormalization and background subtraction methods for computing regularized on-shell actions of rotating, charged AdS black holes in gauged supergravity, focusing on the radial cutoff frame. It shows holographic renormalization in the asymptotically global AdS frame consistently yields the quantum statistical relation (up to a Casimir term in odd dimensions) across 5D–7D, grounding prior holographic comparisons on first principles; background subtraction fails in the same frame due to an identifiable mismatch involving Casimir and counterterm contributions, revealing unresolved ambiguities in cutoff surface choice. The work clarifies frame selection for conformally flat boundaries and highlights remaining ambiguities for non-conformally-flat ones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To place holographic thermodynamic comparisons—previously relying on the quantum statistical relation—on a rigorous, first-principles foundation by systematically analyzing regularization schemes (holographic renormalization vs. background subtraction) and their dependence on the choice of radial cutoff frame for rotating, charged AdS black holes in gauged supergravity.

Method: Systematic comparison of two regularization methods—holographic renormalization (using counterterms and the asymptotically global AdS frame) and background subtraction—applied to on-shell actions of rotating, electrically charged AdS$_d$ black holes with $S^1 \times S^{d-2}$ conformal boundaries in five, six, and seven dimensions; exact identification of discrepancies via analytic decomposition into Casimir energy and counterterm contributions.

Result: Holographic renormalization in the asymptotically global AdS frame satisfies the quantum statistical relation up to a parameter-independent Casimir term in odd dimensions (5D, 7D), validating prior holographic thermodynamics; background subtraction fails in that same frame, with the discrepancy precisely quantified as the sum of the Casimir term and certain counterterm contributions—exposing an unmet criterion for cutoff surface selection.

Conclusion: The asymptotically global AdS frame is uniquely suitable for holographic renormalization with conformally flat boundaries; background subtraction remains ambiguous without a principled cutoff-surface criterion; residual ambiguities persist in both methods for non-conformally-flat boundaries, limiting precision holography beyond symmetric cases.

Abstract: We revisit the regularized on-shell actions of rotating electrically charged AdS black holes of gauged supergravity via holographic renormalization and background subtraction, with a particular emphasis on the frame in which the radial cutoff is placed. For holographic renormalization, the asymptotically global AdS frame, recently employed in seven dimensions, works equally well in five and six dimensions: the renormalized on-shell action of the AdS$_d$ black holes with $S^1\times S^{d-2}$ conformal boundaries satisfies the quantum statistical relation, up to a parameter-independent Casimir term in odd dimensions, and thereby places the holographic comparisons that so far relied on this relation on a first-principles footing. Background subtraction, in contrast, fails to reproduce this result in the same frame; the mismatch, which we identify exactly in terms of the Casimir term and the counterterms, demands a criterion for the cutoff surface yet to be established. For precision holography, our results settle the choice of frame for holographic renormalization with conformally flat boundaries and delineate the ambiguities that remain in background subtraction and in holographic renormalization for boundaries that are not conformally flat.

</details>


### [175] [Solving spectral flow in AdS$_3$: from covering maps to pencils](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15070)
*Sergio Iguri,Nicolas Kovensky,Julian H. Toro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper solves the long-standing problem of computing arbitrary $n$-point correlators of spectrally flowed (winding) vertex operators in the SL(2,R) WZW model dual to string theory on AdS₃ with pure NS-NS flux, by introducing a novel 'pencil of rational maps' that uniformly unwinds all insertions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute generic $n$-point correlation functions of spectrally flowed (winding) vertex operators in the SL(2,ℝ) WZW model — a key ingredient for understanding string dynamics and holography in AdS₃ with pure NS-NS flux — extending beyond the previously known three-point results and verifying the Dei–Eberhardt four-point conjecture.

Method: Constructs and exploits a 'pencil of rational maps' from the worldsheet (Riemann sphere) to the AdS₃ boundary (also a sphere), which generically exists for arbitrary insertion points, simultaneously trivializes spectral flow (unwinds all operators), and reduces to the standard covering map in special (Hurwitz) cases; uses this map to express flowed correlators as combinations of unflowed ones via Ward identities and analytic continuation.

Result: Derives a complete, closed-form expression for all $n$-point correlators of spectrally flowed operators in the SL(2,ℝ) WZW model, valid for arbitrary windings and generic worldsheet positions; explicitly confirms the Dei–Eberhardt four-point conjecture as a special case.

Conclusion: The existence of a pencil of rational maps provides a universal geometric mechanism for handling spectral flow in AdS₃/CFT₂, fully solving the correlator problem for winding strings in pure NS-NS backgrounds and establishing a unified framework that interpolates between covering-map-based and generic correlator constructions.

Abstract: Strings in AdS$_3$ with pure NS-NS flux are described by the exactly solvable SL(2,$\mathbb{R}$) WZW model, yet correlators of winding strings, given by spectrally flowed operators, were so far known only up to three points. We obtain all of them, for any number of insertions and windings, in terms of unflowed correlators, proving the four-point conjecture of Dei and Eberhardt. The key ingredient is a pencil of rational maps from the worldsheet to the boundary which exists for generic insertion points, unwinds all insertions at once, and collapses to the covering map when the latter exists.

</details>


### [176] [$n$-Gon Correlators in $d$-dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15147)
*Yong-Xiang Su,Dongyu Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the analytic structure of scalar one-loop cosmological n-gon correlators using integrand-level reduction and a Baikov representation adapted to linear tube-energy denominators; it determines normalization factors for local leading singularities, constructs explicit dlog forms, evaluates energy discontinuities yielding elliptic integrals, and relates tube cuts to cosmological dressing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure of cosmological correlators beyond local leading singularities, particularly how integration domains influence physical observables such as discontinuities and transcendental weight.

Method: Integrand-level reduction and Baikov representation adapted to linear tube-energy denominators; evaluation of external-energy discontinuities in collinear kinematics; comparison of successive tube-energy discontinuities across dimensions; mapping to cosmological dressing representation.

Result: Explicit dlog forms for arbitrary n at d=n; unity-normalized local leading singularities; nonvanishing complete elliptic integral of the second kind from summed time orderings in the d=4 box; dimensional contrast (d=3 vs d=4) in tube-energy discontinuities; identification of correspondence between certain tube cuts and cosmological dressing.

Conclusion: Integration domains fundamentally shape the analytic structure of cosmological correlators — local leading singularities alone are insufficient; global features like discontinuities and transcendental functions (e.g., elliptic integrals) emerge only upon proper domain consideration and summation over time orderings.

Abstract: We study the analytic structure of scalar one-loop cosmological $n$-gon correlators using an integrand-level reduction and a Baikov representation adapted to linear tube-energy denominators. At spatial dimension $d=n$, we determine the algebraic factors that normalize their local leading singularities to unity and construct explicit $d\log$ forms for arbitrary $n$. For the complete box at $d=n=4$, we evaluate an external-energy discontinuity in a collinear kinematic family and find a nonvanishing complete elliptic integral of the second kind after summing all time orderings. We also compare successive tube-energy discontinuities in $d=3$ and $d=4$, and relate selected tube cuts to the cosmological dressing representation. These results illustrate the importance of integration domains in determining the analytic structure beyond local leading singularities.

</details>


### [177] [Canonical Commutation Relations and Poisson Brackets with Non-locality in Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15173)
*Yasu Gen,Hiroaki Matsunaga,Jojiro Totsuka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a string-inspired nonlocal field theory with time-shifted kinetic terms, constructs its canonical structure (symplectic form and Poisson bracket), shows the commutator matches $i\\hbar$ times the Poisson bracket at positive time separation, and reveals a discontinuous $\hbar$-jump in the commutator at the shift scale $s=\\sigma$, exposing a singular limit between nonlocal and local theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the canonical structure and quantum commutation relations in nonlocal field theories inspired by string theory—specifically those with kinetic terms coupling fields at finitely separated times—where standard local canonical quantization fails.

Method: Variational derivation of boundary term to construct a conserved symplectic form; derivation of positive-time Poisson bracket from this form; application of Schwinger--Dyson equations to relate time-ordered products to commutators; analysis of limits $\\lambda \\to 0$ (weak coupling) and $\\sigma \\to 0$ (local limit) for consistency with free field quantization.

Result: A well-defined conserved symplectic form and positive-time Poisson bracket are obtained; the equal-time commutator exhibits a finite jump $\\Delta [\\phi, \\dot{\\phi}] = i\\hbar$ precisely at $s = \\sigma$, while vanishing identically for $0 < s < \\sigma$ for any $\\lambda > 0$; the limit $\\lambda \\to 0$ at fixed $\\sigma$ does not recover the free-field commutator on $(0,\\sigma)$, but $\\sigma \\to 0$ at fixed $\\lambda$ restores locality and the standard commutator for all $s > 0$.

Conclusion: Nonlocality introduced via finite time shifts induces an intrinsic scale $\\sigma$ that governs the support of the commutator, making the local limit singular: quantum causality (nonvanishing commutator) emerges only beyond the shift scale, and the Planck-scale parameter $\\hbar$ manifests as a discontinuity—not a smooth deformation—of the commutator across $s = \\sigma$.

Abstract: We study the canonical structure of a string-inspired model in which the kinetic term couples fields at times separated by a finite shift $\sigma$, with a coupling $\lambda$ to a fixed background. From the boundary term in the variation of the action, we construct a conserved symplectic form and derive the positive-time Poisson bracket. Using the Schwinger--Dyson relation, we show that at positive time separation $s$ the commutator equals $i\hbar$ times this bracket. The canonical normalization $\hbar$ appears as a jump of the commutator expectation value at $s=\sigma$, which is equal time for shifted variables with a local kinetic term; for $0<s<\sigma$ the commutator vanishes for every $\lambda>0$. Hence $\lambda\to0$ at fixed $\sigma$ does not reproduce the free commutator on this interval, whereas $\sigma\to0$ at fixed $\lambda$ recovers the local commutator at every fixed $s>0$.

</details>


### [178] [Simplicity of the Three-Loop Six-Point ABJM Amplitude](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15193)
*Hanyu Fang,Chia-Kai Kuo,Kai Yan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper computes the three-loop six-point amplitude in planar ABJM theory using negative geometries, finding it is fully determined by lower-loop data and a simple coefficient (−π²), with no three-loop correction to the collinear anomalous dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure and simplicity of scattering amplitudes in planar ABJM theory, particularly whether iterative or hidden symmetries govern higher-loop behavior analogous to N=4 SYM.

Method: Uses negative geometries to construct and evaluate the dual-conformally invariant integrand of the three-loop six-point amplitude; isolates the bipartite chain contribution, analyzes its infrared finiteness and local finiteness, and reconstructs the full amplitude including disconnected lower-loop products.

Result: The integrated three-loop bipartite chain is infrared finite and equals −π² times the one-loop amplitude; the collinear anomalous dimension receives no three-loop correction; the full three-loop amplitude is completely fixed by lower-loop data and the coefficient −π².

Conclusion: The six-point ABJM amplitude exhibits unexpected simplicity at three loops, suggesting an iterative odd-even loop relation and hinting at deeper integrability or geometric structure underlying ABJM amplitudes.

Abstract: We compute the three-loop six-point amplitude in planar ABJM theory using negative geometries. At this order, only a single bipartite chain contributes to the logarithm of the amplitude, and we find that its dual-conformally invariant integrand is locally finite. The integrated chain is therefore infrared finite and, remarkably, equals exactly $-\pi^2$ times the one-loop amplitude. Hence the collinear anomalous dimension, like the cusp, receives no three-loop correction. Restoring the disconnected products of lower-loop contributions, which carry all the infrared divergences, we obtain the full three-loop amplitude, fixed entirely by lower-loop data and the coefficient $-\pi^2$. These results reveal an unexpected simplicity of the six-point ABJM amplitude and suggest an iterative relation between its odd- and even-loop terms.

</details>


### [179] [D(-1)/D7 bound states in supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15423)
*Gongrui Cheng,Klaas Parmentier,Thomas Van Riet*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs D(-1)/D7 bound states in Euclidean type IIB supergravity, resolving prior issues with supersymmetry and smearing by classifying codimension-two supersymmetric solutions via two holomorphic functions under a complex constraint; it identifies a distinguished solution with constant dilaton interpolating between asymptotic vacuum and a controlled $\mathbb{R} \times S^1 \times \mathbb{T}^8$ scaling limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unresolved issues in prior supergravity constructions of D(-1)/D7 bound states—specifically, ambiguities in ten-dimensional Euclidean supersymmetry and reliance on partial smearing to codimension one—while providing a fully localized, supersymmetric description aligned with the open-string instanton realization in 8D super Yang–Mills theory.

Method: Systematic classification of local codimension-two supersymmetric solutions in Euclidean type IIB supergravity; reduction of supersymmetry equations to holomorphic data (two holomorphic functions + one complex constraint); consistency checks with D7 worldvolume analysis and D3-brane flux quantization.

Result: A complete local classification of codimension-two supersymmetric D(-1)/D7 bound states; identification of a distinguished solution with constant dilaton exhibiting smooth interpolation from asymptotically flat vacuum to a controlled near-core geometry $\mathbb{R} \times S^1 \times \mathbb{T}^8$; confirmation that candidate solutions require complementary D3-brane fluxes consistent with D7-brane worldvolume theory.

Conclusion: The construction provides the first fully localized, supersymmetric, and ten-dimensionally consistent Euclidean supergravity description of D(-1)/D7 bound states, bridging open-string instanton physics in 8D SYM with closed-string geometry, and revealing novel scaling limits with toroidal compactifications.

Abstract: We construct D($-$1)/D7 bound states in Euclidean type IIB supergravity motivated by the string-theory setup of Billò et al., whose open-string description realizes instantons in eight-dimensional super Yang--Mills theory. Previous supergravity constructions either left unresolved issues concerning ten-dimensional Euclidean supersymmetry or relied on partial smearing to codimension one. We classify the local codimension-two solutions by showing that the supersymmetric field equations reduce to two holomorphic functions subject to a single complex constraint. The candidate bound-state solutions require complementary D3-brane fluxes, consistent with the D7 worldvolume analysis. A particularly interesting solution has constant dilaton and connects the vacuum at infinity to a controlled scaling limit in which the geometry becomes $\mathbb R\times S^1\times\mathbb T^8$.

</details>


### [180] [A ménage à quatre without strings attached](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15424)
*Iosif Bena,Gongrui Cheng,Klaas Parmentier,Dimitrios Toulikas,Thomas Van Riet*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a supersymmetric D0-D8-D4-D4' quadruple bound state in type II supergravity where Hanany-Witten strings are absent due to a quadratic flux constraint; it is realized both as a bulk supergravity solution and as a domain-wall solution in the non-Abelian DBI action of D8-branes, with matching Killing spinors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between individual Hanany-Witten string production by crossing D-brane pairs and the possibility of forming stable, string-free multi-brane bound states—particularly for systems involving D0, D8, and two orthogonal D4-branes.

Method: Constructs an explicit supergravity background satisfying the equations of motion and Bianchi identities for the D0-D8-D4-D4' configuration; imposes the no-Hanany-Witten-string condition (vanishing net NSNS electric source) leading to a quadratic flux constraint; derives the system as a supersymmetric solution of the non-Abelian DBI action for coincident D8-branes, interpreting the fluxed stack as a BPS domain wall separating two distinct but compatible supersymmetric vacua.

Result: A fully consistent, supersymmetric quadruple bound state without attached HW strings; derivation of a quadratic constraint among RR and NSNS fluxes ensuring absence of HW strings; matching of bulk Killing spinors with those of the DBI solution; identification of the D8-stack as a domain wall carrying precisely the charge difference between the two adjacent backgrounds.

Conclusion: The Hanany-Witten effect is not inevitable in multi-brane intersections: global consistency and supersymmetry allow for composite bound states where local string creation is cancelled topologically and dynamically via flux quantization and domain-wall structure—demonstrating a novel mechanism for brane stabilization beyond pairwise interactions.

Abstract: When two D-branes with eight mixed Dirichlet-Neumann directions cross, a fundamental string is created. This is the Hanany-Witten (HW) effect. In supergravity, the string shows up as an electric NSNS B-field sourced by the RR fields of the two branes. We show that two brane pairs, each of which would create an HW string on its own, can combine into a quadruple bound state with no HW strings attached. We explicitly construct a background with the fluxes of such a D0-D8-D4-D4' quadruple. Requiring no HW strings imposes a quadratic relation among the fluxes. We also realize this bound state as a supersymmetric solution of the non-Abelian DBI action of the D8-branes, whose Killing spinors match those of the bulk supergravity solution. The fluxed D8 stack acts as a domain wall between two supersymmetric D0-D8-D4-D4' backgrounds. Each background satisfies the no-string constraint, and the jumps in the fluxes give the charges carried by the stack.

</details>


### [181] [Radiation from scattering on multiple shockwaves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15468)
*Tim Adamo,Anton Ilderton,Hannah Kingham*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper exactly computes radiation from a charged probe scattering on multiple null electromagnetic shockwaves, recovering the Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) suppression for radially symmetric shocks (e.g., ultraboosted Coulomb fields), and reveals stronger LPM suppression for impulsive plane waves in the source-free case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond standard approximations (linearized scatterers, high-energy probes, soft photons) in modeling the LPM effect by constructing an exactly solvable multi-scattering setup in QED.

Method: Exact computation of elastic and single-photon emission amplitudes for a charged particle scattering on multiple null electromagnetic shockwaves; radiation density is doubly-averaged over transverse positions and lightfront time; analysis performed for radially symmetric shock profiles and impulsive plane waves.

Result: Exact recovery of LPM suppression for radially symmetric transverse shock profiles (including ultraboosted Coulomb field) under general kinematics; discovery of enhanced spectral suppression in the LPM regime for impulsive/ultra-short plane waves (source-free case).

Conclusion: The shockwave framework provides an exactly solvable model for multi-scattering radiation that generalizes the LPM effect beyond standard approximations, revealing stronger interference-induced suppression in idealized plane wave backgrounds.

Abstract: It is well known that radiation produced by scattering an electron off multiple sources depends on the length of the mean free path between these scatterings. When this is sufficiently small, the spectrum is suppressed due to destructive interference, a famous result known as the Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) effect. This is usually modelled approximately, with scattering centres treated linearly (e.g., as screened Coulomb potentials), high-energy probes and soft emitted photons. In this paper, we consider a scenario where multiple scatterings can be computed exactly: charged probe scattering on multiple null electromagnetic shockwaves. Here, the amplitudes for elastic scattering and single photon emission can be determined exactly, for arbitrary kinematics, in terms of the shock profiles in the transverse plane. We use these amplitudes to compute the radiation density (doubly-averaged over shock locations in the transverse plane and lightfront time) in the regimes of independent and interfering scatterings. For shock profiles which are radially symmetric in the transverse plane (including the ultraboosted Coulomb field), we recover the known LPM suppression, albeit with more general kinematics. In addition, our setup allows us to consider the source-free case of multiple scattering on impulsive/ultra-short plane waves, where we find much stronger suppression of the spectrum in the LPM regime.

</details>


### [182] [Numerical Thermodynamics of AdS Black Holes with Higher-Order Mass Corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15470)
*Saad Eddine Baddis,Adil Belhaj,Hajar Belmahi,Yassine Hassouni,Maryem Jemri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs new asymptotically AdS black hole solutions with a higher-order mass deformation term, and numerically investigates their thermodynamic properties—including stability, P–v criticality, and phase transitions—using CUDA-based parallel simulations for n = 3, 4, 5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend black hole solutions in asymptotically anti-de Sitter spacetimes by incorporating insights from noncommutative geometry and quintessence, enabling richer thermodynamic behavior via a generalized mass deformation.

Method: Analytical construction of black hole metrics with a $\frac{aM^{n-1}}{r^n}$ deformation term; numerical thermodynamic analysis via CUDA-accelerated parallel programming simulations for specific integer values of $n$ (3, 4, 5).

Result: Numerical evidence for thermodynamic stability regimes, $P$-$v$ critical points, and first- or second-order phase transitions depending on $n$ and parameter choices.

Conclusion: The higher-order mass deformation induces novel critical phenomena and phase structure in asymptotically AdS black holes, bridging concepts from quantum gravity-inspired geometry and dark energy models.

Abstract: Inspired by noncommutative geometry and quintessence, we construct a new class of asymptotically anti-de Sitter black hole solutions characterized by a higher-order mass deformation term $\frac{aM^{n-1}}{r^n}$ involving two parameters, $a$ and $n$. Due to analytical difficulties, we approach the underlying thermodynamics through parallel programming simulations using CUDA codes. By considering models with $n=3$, $n=4$, and $n=5$, we numerically study certain thermodynamic properties, including the stability, the $P$-$v$ criticality, and the phase transitions.

</details>


### [183] [Holographic dual of the 2d Ising model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15484)
*Kazumi Okuyama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a holographic duality between the 2D Ising model on a torus and a Turaev-Viro topological quantum field theory (TQFT) on a solid torus, with bulk geometry partitioned into core, sandwich, and bedding surface regions; defect states, string-net boundary data, and height variables are geometrically realized via discretized 2-skeleton interpolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise holographic correspondence for the critical 2D Ising model using topological quantum field theory tools—specifically, to geometrize its degrees of freedom (spins, domain walls, fusion diagrams) in a bulk Turaev-Viro TQFT framework.
