Method: Constructs a holographic dual by decomposing the solid torus bulk into three regions: (1) a core region preparing a topological defect state |𝒟⟩ on boundary 𝔻; (2) a sandwich region evaluating the Turaev-Viro state sum ⟨𝒟|Π|ℱ⟩, where |ℱ⟩ is the Aasen-Fendley-Mong string-net state on physical boundary 𝔹; and (3) a bedding surface 𝕊 hosting Ising height variables; the bulk is discretized by a 2-skeleton P interpolating 𝒟 and ℱ.

Result: A geometric realization of the 2D Ising model’s critical structure in terms of Turaev-Viro TQFT: domain walls correspond to defects, spins map to height variables on 𝕊, and the string-net ℱ encodes fusion data; absence of defect reduces P to the worldsheet of ℱ.

Conclusion: The Ising model on a torus admits a rigorous holographic description as a Turaev-Viro TQFT on a solid torus, with spatially separated bulk regions encoding distinct physical structures—defect preparation, state overlap computation, and spin/height variable localization—unifying lattice, topological, and continuum perspectives.

Abstract: We propose a holographic dual of the 2d Ising model on a torus as the Turaev-Viro TQFT on a solid torus. The bulk spacetime is divided into the ``core'' region and the ``sandwich'' region. The core region prepares a defect state $|\mathcal{D}\rangle$ on the topological boundary $\mathbb{D}$. The sandwich region computes the Turaev-Viro state sum $\langle\mathcal{D}|\Pi|\mathcal{F}\rangle$ between the state $|\mathcal{D}\rangle$ on $\mathbb{D}$ and the state $|\mathcal{F}\rangle$ on the physical boundary $\mathbb{B}$. The fusion diagram (or string-net) $\mathcal{F}$ on $\mathbb{B}$ is given by the Aasen-Fendley-Mong construction. There is also a ``bedding surface'' $\mathbb{S}$ between $\mathbb{D}$ and $\mathbb{B}$, and the height variables (or Ising spins inside/outside of the domain wall) are naturally living on $\mathbb{S}$. The bulk spacetime is discretized by the 2-skeleton $P$ which interpolates $\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathcal{F}$. When the defect $\mathcal{D}$ is absent, the 2-skeleton $P$ ends on the bedding surface $\mathbb{S}$ and $P$ is interpreted as the bulk worldsheet of the string-net $\mathcal{F}$.

</details>


### [184] [1-loop partition function of 11d supergravity on $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb{Z}_k$: matching $A(k)$ in ABJM free energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15510)
*Stefan A. Kurlyand,Arkady A. Tseytlin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves a mismatch between the 1-loop supergravity partition function on AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ and the exact ABJM free energy A(k) by identifying and including a previously omitted contribution from 3-form fluctuations built from harmonic 2-forms on Euclidean AdS₄ — not captured in standard KK reduction — and shows that, after ℤₖ projection and zeta-regularization, the full 1-loop result matches localization. It extends this method to the 3d ADHM theory dual to AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₚ, incorporating localized modes from the 7d SU(p) vector multiplet on the singular fixed locus to reproduce the corresponding A(k)-like function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the discrepancy between the 1-loop supergravity free energy on AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ (computed via standard KK reduction) and the exactly known function A(k) from ABJM localization; and to generalize the resolution to the ADHM theory dual to the orbifold AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₚ with conical singularity.

Method: Identifies missing 1-loop contributions from square-integrable harmonic 2-forms on Euclidean AdS₄ tensored with S⁷ modes for the 3-form field; combines them with the standard KK spectrum expressed via superconformal characters; applies ℤₖ (or ℤₚ) orbifold projection; evaluates total 1-loop free energy using zeta-function regularization; includes localized KK modes of the 7d SU(p) vector multiplet supported on the fixed locus AdS₄×S³ for the ADHM case.

Result: The corrected 1-loop supergravity free energy on AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ fully reproduces the exact localization result A(k); similarly, the extended computation for AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₚ — including localized vector multiplet modes — reproduces the ADHM-theory counterpart of A(k).

Conclusion: Standard KK reduction misses non-normalizable or boundary-localized degrees of freedom (e.g., harmonic 2-forms on AdS₄ for the C₃-field); their inclusion is essential for precision holography at the 1-loop level in M-theory dualities involving orbifolds; this provides a systematic framework for matching quantum gravity corrections to field-theoretic localization results.

Abstract: The function $A(k)$ appearing in the large $N$ expansion of free energy of ABJM theory on $S^3$ is known exactly from localization. An earlier calculation of the 1-loop partition function of 11d supergravity on $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb Z_k$, based on the standard KK spectrum on $S^7$, did not reproduce it. We identify a missing 1-loop supergravity contribution as that of the 3-form field fluctuations built from square-integrable harmonic 2-forms on Euclidean $\mathrm{AdS}_4$ tensored with $S^7$ modes, which are not captured by the KK spectrum. Adding it to the KK tower contribution expressed in terms of superconformal characters and applying the $\mathbb{Z}_k$ projection, the total expression simplifies, and its evaluation in a zeta-function prescription reproduces the expression for $A(k)$. We also apply a similar approach to the 3d ADHM theory whose M-theory dual is the orbifold $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb{Z}_p$, which has singularity along the fixed locus $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^3$, supporting a 7d $SU(p)$ vector multiplet. Adding the localized KK modes of this multiplet to the $\mathbb{Z}_p$-projected supergravity spectrum gives a 1-loop free energy that reproduces the ADHM localization counterpart of $A(k)$.

</details>


### [185] [Schwinger-Unruh Interplay and Mode-Resolved Entanglement of Charged Scalar Fields in Rindler Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15623)
*Shagun Kaushal,Himanshu Parihar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates entanglement properties of a charged scalar field in Rindler spacetime under parallel electric and magnetic fields, focusing on pairwise and cross-horizon correlations; it finds that cross-wedge antiparticle–particle entanglement is conditional on charge and squeezing parameters, while intra-wedge entanglement persists with Schwinger squeezing, and global wedge entanglement is carried by collective Gaussian modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how external electromagnetic fields—particularly parallel E and B fields—affect quantum entanglement structure of relativistic charged fields in accelerated (Rindler) frames, especially across causal horizons and Landau-quantized transverse modes.

Method: Analyzes a charged scalar field in Rindler spacetime with parallel E and B fields; constructs the global vacuum in the negative-frequency sector; derives an outgoing four-mode Gaussian state; computes logarithmic negativity and quantum mutual information for specific mode pairs; applies Gaussian Schmidt decomposition to identify collective entangled modes across the right–left wedge partition.

Result: Cross-wedge antiparticle–particle pairs are entangled only if q₂ < tanh r_U and can become separable while retaining nonzero mutual information; intra-wedge particle–antiparticle pairs remain entangled for nonzero Schwinger squeezing; global right–left wedge entanglement is encoded in two collective Gaussian modes, irrespective of separability of individual mode pairs.

Conclusion: Entanglement in this setting is highly mode- and vacuum-structured: electromagnetic fields induce Landau quantization and modify effective mass, leading to nontrivial, parameter-dependent separability transitions; global entanglement survives local separability via collective modes, highlighting the necessity of mode-resolved Gaussian analysis beyond bipartite subsystems.

Abstract: We study pairwise and cross-horizon correlations of a charged scalar field in Rindler spacetime in the presence of parallel electric and magnetic fields. The transverse magnetic field produces Landau levels, while the longitudinal mode problem follows the charged-scalar Rindler construction with the effective mass $M_n^2=m^2+(2n+1)|qB|$. For a specified global vacuum in the negative-frequency sector, we obtain the outgoing four-mode Gaussian state and calculate logarithmic negativity and quantum mutual information for selected mode pairs. We find that the cross-wedge antiparticle-particle pair is entangled only when $q_2<\tanh r_U$. It can therefore become separable although its mutual information remains nonzero. The intra-wedge particle-antiparticle pair remains entangled for nonzero Schwinger squeezing. Using the Gaussian Schmidt decomposition, we show that the entanglement across the complete right--left wedge partition is carried by a pair of collective modes, even when the selected outgoing cross-wedge pair becomes separable. Using the Gaussian Schmidt decomposition, we show that the entanglement across the complete right--left wedge partition is carried by a pair of collective modes, even when the selected outgoing cross-wedge pair becomes separable. We discuss the Landau-level dependence and the limits of these mode-resolved conclusions, without identifying the vacuum-dependent mixing parameter with a detector temperature.

</details>


### [186] [Entropy and charge transport limit Mpemba preparation in holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15654)
*Yu Shi,Sheng Long*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates Mpemba relaxation and its thermodynamic preparation cost in a holographic superconductor with full backreaction, revealing entropy-order reversal under boundary drives, identifying an O(ε⁴) horizon entropy reserve near critical slow states, and deriving a lower bound ε⁻² on preparation time due to horizon regularity and charge transport constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand non-equilibrium thermal relaxation—specifically the Mpemba effect—and quantify the minimal energetic and temporal cost of preparing such anomalous relaxation paths in strongly coupled quantum systems modeled holographically.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of asymptotically AdS black holes with scalar hair and full gravitational backreaction; analysis of horizon area/entropy evolution under boundary source quenches; perturbative expansion around critical slow branches; covariant flux analysis linking scalar field dynamics, charge transport, and horizon entropy production.

Result: 1) Boundary drives can invert post-quench horizon entropy ordering at fixed final energy and charge; 2) Near critical slow states, quadratic horizon area response cancels, yielding an O(ε⁴) entropy reserve tied to both superconducting advantage and normal-phase slow-tail weight; 3) Preparation time for suppressing leading fields scales as ≥ ε⁻² under horizon regularity and uniform radial control; 4) Charge transport bounds the scalar flux responsible for horizon entropy, tightening the time bound.

Conclusion: The Mpemba effect in holographic superconductors is constrained by a fundamental trade-off between initial field amplitude and preparation cost: the small entropy reserve (O(ε⁴)) limits how rapidly the system can be driven into a Mpemba-prepared state, with unavoidable ε⁻² scaling in time and associated flux cost imposed by bulk causality, regularity, and gauge-invariant current conservation.

Abstract: We study Mpemba relaxation and its preparation cost in a holographic superconductor with full backreaction. At matched final energy and charge, boundary drives can reverse the ordering of the remaining horizon entropy after source removal. In separate numerical comparisons, some protocols reach a given tolerance earlier even when driving and waiting times are included. To examine the limits of preparation, we consider critical states that already carry a slow scalar field and exterior charge. Along the critical slow branch at fixed energy and charge, horizon displacement cancels the quadratic area response, leaving an entropy reserve of $O(\varepsilon^4)$ for an initial scalar amplitude $\varepsilon$. The same coefficient connects this reserve to the entropy advantage of the nearby superconducting equilibrium and the entropy weight of the slow tail on the normal side. This limited reserve constrains how quickly the existing field can be removed. For releases that suppress the leading horizon field and exterior charge, the preparation time grows at least as $\varepsilon^{-2}$ under horizon regularity and uniform radial control. Charge transport constrains the same covariant scalar flux that produces horizon entropy, raising the minimum flux cost and strengthening the time bound.

</details>


### [187] [Harmonic superspace and Kaluza-Klein reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15782)
*Nikita Zaigraev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Kaluza-Klein reduction to harmonic superspace to construct off-shell superfield formulations of massive BPS multiplets in 4D, N=2 (and subsequently N=1), preserving manifest supersymmetry and Grassmann analyticity via deformed harmonic derivatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain gauge-invariant, off-shell superfield descriptions of massive higher-spin BPS multiplets in 4D, N=2 and N=1 supersymmetry—beyond standard on-shell or component approaches—leveraging the structural advantages of harmonic superspace for encoding gauge symmetry.

Method: Deforms harmonic derivatives by mass terms within harmonic superspace to implement Kaluza-Klein reduction from 5D, N=1 to 4D while preserving off-shell N=2 supersymmetry and Grassmann analyticity; applied to free Maxwell theory and linearized Poincaré supergravity.

Result: Obtains unconstrained off-shell harmonic-superspace actions for the massive BPS 4D, N=2 vector and supergravity multiplets, featuring analytic Stückelberg-type gauge symmetries; shows compatibility with reformulation in 4D, N=1 superspace.

Conclusion: The deformed harmonic-derivative KK reduction provides a systematic, technically simple route to gauge-invariant off-shell superfield formulations of massive BPS higher-spin multiplets in 4D, N=2 and paves the way for analogous N=1 constructions.

Abstract: We extend the Kaluza-Klein reduction procedure to the framework of harmonic superspace and apply it to the construction of off-shell superfield formulations of supersymmetric gauge multiplets. The reduction is implemented by a deformation of the harmonic derivatives by mass terms, while ensuring that manifest off-shell supersymmetry and Grassmann analyticity are preserved at all stages. As a first application, we reduce the free \(5D,\mathcal{N}=1\) Maxwell theory and obtain an off-shell superfield description of the BPS \(4D,\mathcal{N}=2\) massive vector supermultiplet. The resulting formulation naturally incorporates an analytic Stückelberg-type gauge symmetry. We then reduce linearized \(5D,\mathcal{N}=1\) Poincaré supergravity and derive the unconstrained harmonic-superspace action for the BPS \(4D,\mathcal{N}=2\) massive supergravity multiplet. The technical simplicity of the procedure stems from the structure of gauge theories in harmonic superspace, where the gauge symmetry is naturally encoded in analytic prepotentials and harmonic derivatives. The approach presented in this paper opens a direct route to gauge-invariant off-shell superfield formulations of massive higher-spin $4D,\mathcal{N}=2$ BPS multiplets. Furthermore, the resulting theories can be reformulated in $4D,\mathcal{N}=1$ superspace, opening the way to gauge-invariant off-shell descriptions of $4D,\mathcal{N}=1$ massive higher-spin theories.

</details>


### [188] [Swing Surfaces in AdS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15854)
*Jacqueline Caminiti,Sabrina Pasterski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes holographic entanglement entropy in the flat limit of AdS₃/CFT₂, showing the Ryu-Takayanagi (RT) formula lacks a well-defined flat-space limit due to issues with the state-operator map; it constructs an AdS₃ uplift of the 'swing' prescription and interprets swing surfaces as computing mutual information between nearly null boundary intervals in specific pure CFT states—linked to deep bulk scattering—and relates them to the open problem of interpreting black hole inner horizons information-theoretically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in defining holographic entanglement entropy in flat-space holography by understanding the fate of Ryu-Takayanagi surfaces in the flat limit of AdS₃/CFT₂ and establishing a rigorous information-theoretic foundation for the proposed 'swing surfaces'.

Method: Analytic continuation and limiting procedures applied to AdS₃ geometry and RT surfaces; construction of an explicit AdS₃ uplift of the swing prescription; computation of mutual information for nearly null boundary intervals in pure CFT₂ states; comparison with black hole inner horizon geometry and entanglement structure in AdS₃.

Result: The standard RT formula does not admit a smooth flat-space limit; swing surfaces are shown to compute boundary mutual information in special pure CFT₂ states, with gravitational duals involving deep-bulk scattering; swing surfaces are formally linked to black hole inner horizons, suggesting their information-theoretic interpretation is tied to that long-standing problem.

Conclusion: Swing surfaces provide a viable flat-holography analog of RT surfaces, but their information-theoretic meaning is inherently tied to non-perturbative bulk physics—specifically, mutual information of null-separated regions and the unresolved interpretation of inner horizons—highlighting structural differences between AdS and flat holography.

Abstract: We study holographic entanglement entropy in the flat limit of AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$, and clarify the relation between Ryu-Takayanagi surfaces and the swing surfaces proposed for flat-space holography. We show that the ordinary RT formula does not admit a well-defined flat-space limit and connect this to subtleties of the state-operator map in the flat limit. We then construct an AdS$_3$ uplift of the swing prescription and develop its corresponding information-theoretic interpretation through two complementary approaches. First, we show that the swing surface length computes the mutual information between suitably chosen nearly null boundary intervals in a special class of pure CFT states. The gravity interpretation of this result involves scattering processes which take place deep inside of the AdS$_3$ bulk. Second, we reduce the problem of finding an information-theoretic interpretation for swing surfaces to the more general problem of finding an information-theoretic interpretation of black hole inner horizons in holography.

</details>


### [189] [$α'^2$-corrections to the bosonic string from an action-level double copy map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15862)
*Eric Lescano,Agustina Pereyra Grau,Jesús A. Rodríguez*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a double-copy description of higher-derivative corrections (Riem² and Riem³) in the bosonic string effective action, showing that matching the Riem³ vertex requires both an F³ gauge-theory operator and a deformed color-to-kinematics map—leading uniquely to a left–right symmetric (YM + F³) ⊗ (YM + F³) cubic action, which correctly reproduces key string-theoretic couplings at α′ and α′² orders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive bosonic string α′-corrections (particularly Riem² and Riem³) from gauge-theory building blocks via the double copy, addressing the limitations of standard color–kinematics duality for higher-derivative gravity vertices.

Method: Constructs a cubic-order double-copy action by introducing: (i) a higher-derivative gauge-theory Lagrangian with an F³ operator (up to field redefinitions), and (ii) a deformed color-to-kinematics map replacing structure constants with a three-momentum tensor; solves matching conditions between gravity and gauge-theory amplitudes in transverse-traceless gauge, up to field redefinitions.

Result: The Riem³ vertex cannot be obtained with the standard color–kinematics replacement; the combined constraints on the F³ coupling and map deformation admit a unique double-root solution, yielding a left–right symmetric (YM + F³) ⊗ (YM + F³) action; this automatically reproduces the α′-order Riem H² coupling, vanishing α′² six-derivative HHH couplings, and absence of odd-H interactions.

Conclusion: The cubic bosonic string effective action—including its α′ and α′² corrections—is fully captured by a deformed double copy based on YM + F³, establishing a precise, action-level correspondence between constrained higher-derivative gauge theory and string-derived gravity, with left–right symmetry emerging as a necessary consequence of consistency.

Abstract: The low-energy effective action of the bosonic string contains higher-derivative corrections of the form Riem$^2$ and Riem$^3$ to the Einstein--Hilbert action, together with the associated Kalb--Ramond couplings. We construct an action-level double copy description of these corrections up to cubic order in the fields. Two ingredients are required: a higher-derivative gauge-theory Lagrangian, which up to field redefinitions is controlled by a single $F^3$ coupling, and a deformation of the color-to-kinematics replacement of the structure constants by the corresponding three-momentum tensor. We show that no higher-derivative gauge-theory operator can produce the Riem$^3$ vertex with the standard replacement rule, and that the matching conditions for the gauge-theory coupling and the deformation of the map admit a unique solution, a double root, which renders the cubic action left--right symmetric, of the form $(\mathrm{YM}+F^3)\otimes(\mathrm{YM}+F^3)$. Without further input, the resulting map reproduces the cubic $\mathrm{Riem}\,H^2$ coupling of the bosonic string at order $\alpha'$, the absence of six-derivative cubic Kalb--Ramond couplings at order $\alpha'^2$, and the absence of interactions odd in the Kalb--Ramond field. The comparison is performed in transverse-traceless gauge, up to field redefinitions.

</details>


### [190] [Subsystem Flow: Quantum Error Correction, Petz Recovery, and Ergodic Inclusions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15887)
*Yidong Chen,Nima Lashkari,Kwing Lam Leung*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bridge between quantum error correction (QEC) and modular theory in quantum many-body systems and QFT, introducing 'modular autonomy' as a criterion for subsystem protection, defining the 'subsystem flow' to quantify deviation from autonomy, linking it to relative-entropy loss and Petz recovery, constructing 'inclusion parent Hamiltonians', and proposing their spectral properties as probes of quantum chaos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify concepts from quantum error correction—specifically, protection against subsystem erasure—with modular theory in local quantum systems and QFT, by interpreting physical subsystem inclusions $\mathcal{N} \subset \mathcal{M}$ in a reference state $|\Omega\rangle$ as potential QEC codes, and to characterize when such inclusions inherently support error correction without explicit encoding.

Method: Uses Tomita–Takesaki modular theory, relative entropy, Petz recovery maps, and $L^2$-nuclearity; defines modular autonomy via invariance under modular dynamics; introduces subsystem flow as a derivative measure; constructs inclusion parent Hamiltonians via analytic continuation; analyzes ergodicity, history algebras, persistent cores, and dissipative semigroups; applies results to standard inclusions in QFT.

Result: Modular autonomy is equivalent to existence of a reference-preserving QEC code for $\mathcal{N}$ against erasure of $\mathcal{M}'$; subsystem flow quantifies relative-entropy loss infinitesimally; Petz maps arise naturally from analytic continuation; inclusion parent Hamiltonians generically evolve $\mathcal{N}$ outside $\mathcal{M}$, sometimes generating $\mathcal{M} \vee \mathcal{M}'$; standard QFT subregion inclusions are ergodic; $L^2$-nuclearity implies discrete spectrum of parent Hamiltonians and spectral gap for the associated dissipative semigroup.

Conclusion: Modular dynamics encode intrinsic error-correcting structure in quantum many-body systems; the subsystem flow and inclusion parent Hamiltonians provide rigorous, state-dependent diagnostics of information localization, dynamical generation, and chaotic behavior—offering a new framework to diagnose quantum chaos via modular spectral data.

Abstract: In quantum error correction (QEC), the goal is to engineer an encoding that protects logical information against the erasure of a physical subsystem. In local many-body quantum systems, by contrast, nature provides a subsystem inclusion $\mN\subset \mM$ in a fixed reference state $\ket{\Omega}$. Asking whether the inclusion admits a reference-preserving code that protects subsystem $\mN$ against erasure of the complement is equivalent to asking whether $\mN$ is preserved under the modular dynamics of $\mM$ in this state (modular autonomy). We define the subsystem flow, which measures the departure from this error correction regime. Away from autonomy, the infinitesimal flow quantifies relative-entropy loss, while the history algebra and persistent core describe which observables the modular orbit generates and retains. We call an inclusion ergodic when modular orbits generate everything and retain nothing. Analytic continuation of subsystem flow yields Petz recovery maps and a family of positive inclusion parent Hamiltonians. We show that, in general, evolving with these parent Hamiltonians allows $\mN$ to leave $\mM$, and in some cases, it can generate the entire $\mM\vee\mM'$. Inclusions with a common cyclic and separating vector generate everything, and standard inclusions of factors, such as those of nested subregions in QFT, are ergodic. Moreover, the $L^2$-nuclearity property implies that the spectrum of parent Hamiltonians is discrete. We define a dissipative semigroup by repeating the Petz recovery map, whose fixed-point algebra is the modular persistent core and whose spectrum is gapped under the assumption of $L^2$-nuclearity. We conjecture that the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of inclusion parent Hamiltonians can be used to define local spectral measures of quantum chaos.

</details>


### [191] [Multiparticle residues of open-string tachyon amplitudes from DDF sewing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16280)
*Dripto Biswas,Qu Cao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes maximal simultaneous residues of color-ordered tachyon disk amplitudes in critical open bosonic string theory using cubic DDF vertex sewing, reducing the sum over physical intermediate states to Gaussian contractions; it yields determinant-based residue formulas with exact mode cutoffs, recovers Veneziano coefficients at four points via Ward identities, and provides explicit matrices for higher-point residues (5- and 6-point), linking the construction to Grunsky operators and Koba–Nielsen top forms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute maximal simultaneous residues of tachyon disk amplitudes in open bosonic string theory—beyond low-order expansions—and establish a rigorous, operator-theoretic framework connecting DDF formalism, sewing, and conformal field theory structures like Grunsky operators and Koba–Nielsen forms.

Method: Sewing cubic Del Giudice–Di Vecchia–Fubini (DDF) vertices on planar cubic trees; Gaussian contraction over physical intermediate states with level-dependent finite mode cutoffs; coefficient extraction from determinant × exponential of quadratic form in sewing variables; use of cubic local coordinate maps and stress-tensor Ward identity at four points; operator, coordinate-map, and measure identities to relate to Grunsky theory and Koba–Nielsen top forms.

Result: Exact residue formulas expressed as determinants times exponentials of quadratic forms; full recovery of Veneziano pole coefficients at four points; explicit matrix representations for five-point double residue and six-point comb/snowflake residues; consistency checks against Koba–Nielsen expansions; identification of the determinant as the vacuum sewing factor of transverse bosons.

Conclusion: The DDF sewing approach provides a unified, level-resolved, and coordinate-invariant method to compute maximal residues of tachyon amplitudes; it bridges vertex-operator algebra, conformal sewing, and moduli-space geometry, laying groundwork for generalizing to higher genus or superstrings and clarifying the role of Grunsky operators in string amplitudes.

Abstract: We compute the maximal simultaneous residues of color-ordered tachyon disk amplitudes in the critical open bosonic string by summing all physical intermediate states. Sewing cubic Del Giudice--Di Vecchia--Fubini (DDF) vertices reduces this sum to Gaussian contractions for any planar cubic tree, with the internal levels specified independently. Each internal level fixes an exact finite mode cutoff. The residue then follows by coefficient extraction in the sewing variables from a determinant prefactor times the exponential of a quadratic form in the sources. With consistent sewing conventions, the local-coordinate scales cancel between the vertices and the sewing metric. At four points, cubic local maps and a one-boson stress-tensor Ward identity give an all-order resummation that recovers the Veneziano pole coefficients. The same construction gives explicit matrices for the five-point double residue and the six-point comb and snowflake, with low-level checks against independent Koba--Nielsen expansions. The determinant is the vacuum sewing factor of the transverse bosons. We formulate the operator, coordinate-map and measure identities needed to relate this construction to Grunsky operators and the full higher-point Koba--Nielsen top forms.

</details>


### [192] [Accelerating solitons in Rindler space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16568)
*Huiquan Li,Jianyong Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that integrable relativistic field theories, like the sine-Gordon model, retain integrability in Rindler spacetime; it analyzes how classical SG solitons appear to accelerating Rindler observers—revealing horizon-localized partial solitons with fractional topological charge and nontrivial energy exchange across the Rindler horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how integrability and soliton structure persist under acceleration (i.e., in non-inertial frames), and how classical solitons behave from the perspective of uniformly accelerating observers in Rindler space—particularly regarding causality, horizon localization, and energy flow.

Method: Analytical study of the classical sine-Gordon equation in Rindler coordinates; derivation and interpretation of soliton solutions using coordinate transformation from Minkowski to Rindler frame; analysis of topological charge distribution, worldline dynamics, and energy-momentum flux across the Rindler horizon.

Result: SG solitons in Rindler space appear as horizon-confined partial solitons with fractional topological charge to each Rindler observer; their trajectories describe deceleration to rest followed by re-acceleration toward the horizon; energy is exchanged with the horizon—higher-energy solitons absorb energy from it, lower-energy ones radiate energy into it.

Conclusion: Integrability and soliton physics survive in accelerated frames; Rindler observers perceive only causal subsets of global solitons, leading to emergent horizon-localized excitations with modified energetics and topology—suggesting deep links between soliton dynamics, horizons, and energy transfer in curved or non-inertial backgrounds.

Abstract: Relativistic nonlinear field theories that are integrable in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime remain integrable in Rindler space. We investigate the properties of classical sine-Gordon (SG) solitons in Rindler space. Accelerating observers in each of the two Rindler wedges can see only a portion of the full soliton in Minkowski spacetime; this portion itself forms a soliton carrying a partial topological charge. The soliton solution admits the following interpretation: a soliton incoming from the horizon decelerates to stop at some point, then falls back and accelerates to the speed of light toward the horizon. Energy can flow into and out of the Rindler horizon. The higher energy soliton gains energy from the horizon while the lower one loses energy.

</details>


### [193] [Discrete anomaly inflow from SymTFT of $U(1)$ global symmetry: the modified Villain resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16696)
*Napat Poovuttikul,K. Sapmee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a lattice SymTFT for anomalous U(1) symmetry with coefficient k using modified Villain formulation and explicit Majorana fermions to realize spin structure for odd k; derives R- and F-symbols on bulk and gapped boundary; confirms Gu-Wen relation for odd k without bosonic shadow; recovers Z₂ mod-4 anomaly and fermionic stacking; classifies gaugable Z_d subgroups of U(1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive junction data of global anomalies for continuous symmetries beyond group cohomology, starting from anomaly inflow—specifically, to realize and characterize the SymTFT of an anomalous U(1) symmetry on the lattice, including proper treatment of spin structure dependence for odd k.

Method: Uses modified Villain formulation of U(1) lattice gauge theory; introduces explicit lattice Majorana fermions to encode spin structure for odd k; identifies Majorana modes at symmetry line junctions; computes lattice algebra of symmetry lines and junctions; extracts R- and F-symbols in bulk and on gapped boundary; restricts to Z₂ subgroup to verify mod-4 anomaly via mapping torus and fermionic stacking; analyzes gauging of Z_d ⊂ U(1) subgroups.

Result: Constructs explicit lattice SymTFT for U(1)ₖ anomaly; shows Majorana fermions reside precisely at U(1) symmetry line junctions; derives consistent R- and F-symbols; proves boundary F-symbol satisfies Gu-Wen relation for odd k intrinsically (no bosonic shadow); reproduces Z₂ mod-4 phase and stacking rule; classifies gaugable Z_d subgroups and determines resulting symmetry groups.

Conclusion: The lattice SymTFT framework successfully captures continuous symmetry anomalies—including spin-structure dependence and junction data—directly from microscopic lattice construction, enabling systematic study of global anomalies beyond discrete group cohomology; provides foundation for generalizing anomaly classification to higher-form and non-invertible symmetries.

Abstract: We construct a lattice SymTFT for a $U(1)$ global symmetry with anomaly coefficient $k$ using the modified Villain formulation, in which the spin structure required for odd $k$ is realised explicitly by lattice Majorana fermions. We demonstrate that the same Majorana fermions sit at the junctions of the symmetry lines, exactly where the fusion of two $U(1)$ elements wraps around the circle. From the lattice algebra of lines and junctions we derive the $R$- and $F$-symbols in the bulk and on the gapped boundary, and show that for odd $k$ the boundary $F$-symbol obeys the Gu-Wen relation for the $U(1)$ symmetry defect, without passing through a bosonic shadow. Restricting to $\mathbb{Z}_2\subset U(1)$, we reproduce the mod-4 anomalous phase from a mapping torus and the fermionic stacking rule. As a simple application of this SymTFT, we also determine which subgroups $\mathbb{Z}_d\subset U(1)$ can be gauged and the symmetry of the resulting theories. This is a first step towards deriving, from the anomaly inflow of a continuous symmetry, the junction data of global anomalies beyond group cohomology.

</details>


### [194] [Covariant Wigner--Weyl calculus, non-dissipative transport and the chiral anomaly in inhomogeneous systems with nontrivial momentum-space topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17178)
*M.A.Zubkov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews and extends the covariant Wigner–Weyl calculus for fermions in non-Abelian gauge fields, unifying formalism and applications to chiral separation, quantum Hall effects, Fermi-surface invariants, chiral magnetic effect, and anomaly factorization — with new results on gauge-invariant symbols, exact anomaly decomposition, Luttinger–Ward identity, Moyal $N_3$ interpretation, residue-based invariant evaluation, and index-pairing formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematize and extend the covariant Wigner–Weyl calculus for fermions in non-Abelian gauge fields, especially for spatially inhomogeneous systems with nontrivial momentum-space topology, and to resolve open questions regarding anomaly factorization, stability under interactions, and geometric interpretations of topological invariants.

Method: Covariant phase-space (Wigner–Weyl) formalism for fermionic fields in non-Abelian gauge backgrounds; symbolic calculus on noncommutative phase space; index-theoretic and residue-based evaluation of topological invariants; analysis of principal symbols, fibre degrees, and non-elliptic model structures; derivation of Luttinger–Ward identity for renormalized currents; continuum and lattice regularization of Fermi-surface invariants.

Result: New characterizations: (i) operators with gauge-field-independent covariant symbols; (ii) ordering independence of integrated anomaly; (iii) exact anomaly factorization into instanton number and $N_3$ on closed 4-manifolds without homotopy assumptions; (iv) Luttinger–Ward identity underlying renormalized-velocity current; (v) identification of Moyal $N_3$ with principal symbol's fibre degree; (vi) residue evaluation of Fermi-surface invariant in both continuum and lattice settings; (vii) equilibrium chiral magnetic effect in non-Hamiltonian models; (viii) index-pairing form of chiral separation invariant.

Conclusion: The covariant Wigner–Weyl framework provides a unified, geometric, and robust description of topological transport phenomena in interacting, inhomogeneous, non-Abelian fermionic systems; key anomalies and invariants admit exact, coordinate- and regularization-independent formulations rooted in noncommutative geometry and index theory.

Abstract: We review, systematize and extend the covariant Wigner--Weyl calculus for fermions in non-Abelian gauge fields and its applications to systems inhomogeneous in coordinate space with nontrivial momentum-space topology: the non-Abelian chiral separation and quantum Hall effects, their stability under interactions, the Fermi-surface invariant of inhomogeneous systems and the non-elliptic models that carry it, with their equilibrium chiral magnetic effect, the factorization of the chiral anomaly into the instanton number and the momentum-space invariant $N_3$, and the noncommutative-geometric form of the invariants. Previous results are brought to common conventions, with hypotheses stated and notions defined. New results: the characterization of the operators whose covariant symbol is gauge-field independent and the ordering independence of the integrated anomaly; the anomaly factorization on a closed four-manifold without the homotopy hypothesis, the factorization into instanton number and $N_3$ holding exactly when the untwisted index vanishes; the Luttinger--Ward identity behind the renormalized-velocity current and the continuum conditions replacing the compactness of the Brillouin zone in the non-renormalization theorems; the identification of the Moyal $N_3$ of the chiral separation effect with the fibre degree of the principal symbol; the residue evaluation of the Fermi-surface invariant of the non-elliptic models, in the continuum and on a lattice, and the equilibrium current of the conserved charge along the magnetic field that it implies -- a chiral magnetic effect permitted by the non-Hamiltonian form of these models -- which the model with spherical Fermi surfaces trades for a chiral separation effect and a single-Weyl anomaly, and a modification of it restores at the price of the anomaly; and the index-pairing form of the chiral separation invariant.

</details>


### [195] [Love numbers for the Myers-Perry geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17261)
*Rhucha Deshpande,Oleg Lunin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes tidal response parameters—specifically Love numbers and a logarithmic running parameter—for scalar fields in Myers-Perry black hole spacetimes in arbitrary dimensions, distinguishing their dependence on horizon structure and boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the tidal deformability of higher-dimensional rotating black holes and clarify the physical origin and horizon sensitivity of scalar Love numbers versus the logarithmic running parameter.

Method: Asymptotic expansion of the scalar wave equation in Myers-Perry geometry; analytical computation of tidal response coefficients; comparison across multiple horizon boundary condition approximations (e.g., ingoing wave, stretched horizon, near-horizon limits).

Result: The logarithmic running parameter is universal—insensitive to horizon microstructure—and explicitly derived in arbitrary D; scalar Love numbers are non-zero and depend critically on horizon boundary conditions, with values computed under several consistent approximations.

Conclusion: Scalar tidal responses in Myers-Perry black holes exhibit a clean separation: the running parameter is geometric and universal, while Love numbers encode horizon physics and require careful treatment of boundary conditions—challenging the notion that black hole Love numbers universally vanish.

Abstract: We analyze Love numbers for scalar fields in the Myers-Perry geometry in arbitrary dimensions. The tidal responses of black holes are specified by two constants which encode a potential logarithmic running and the Love number. We argue that the parameter specifying the running is not sensitive to the structure of the horizon, and we explicitly compute this parameter for Myers-Perry black holes in arbitrary dimensions by utilizing the asymptotic expansion of the wave equation. The Love numbers rely on the details of the boundary conditions at the horizon, and we compute them using several distinct approximations.

</details>


### [196] [A precision bound for gravitational transseries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17274)
*Ji Hoon Lee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a precision bound on large-N transseries of holographic observables above the black hole threshold, rooted in D-brane instanton data and resurgence theory; it develops large-N resurgence methods capturing e^{-N^2} gravitational saddle asymptotics, identifies an analytic obstruction to transseries resummation under a growth assumption on D-brane instanton sums, and validates the framework using the 1/16-BPS index in N=4 SYM, revealing a thermal-AdS/black-hole duality in D-brane instanton counts that reproduces D3-brane actions on the black hole.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the limits of predictability of holographic observables at finite N via large-N transseries, particularly above the black hole threshold where non-perturbative effects (e.g., D-brane instantons) become essential and conventional large-N expansions break down.

Method: Applies Écalle’s resurgence theory to large-N holography; constructs and analyzes transseries incorporating both e^{-N} (standard instantons) and e^{-N^2} (gravitational saddle) asymptotics; imposes analyticity constraints and growth conditions on D-brane instanton sums to identify resummation obstructions; performs explicit computation and duality analysis of the 1/16-BPS index in N=4 SYM on thermal AdS vs. black hole backgrounds.

Result: Derives a precision bound for large-N transseries determined by D-brane instanton data; identifies an analytic obstruction to Borel–Écalle resummation arising from factorial growth of instanton coefficients under a physically motivated growth assumption; discovers a novel duality exchanging D-brane instanton numbers between thermal AdS and black hole geometries, whose action matching confirms consistency with D3-brane physics in the black hole background.

Conclusion: The large-N transseries of holographic observables has an intrinsic finite-N precision limit set by non-perturbative D-brane data; resurgence analysis reveals that this limit stems from an analytic obstruction tied to e^{-N^2} gravitational saddles; the observed thermal-AdS/black-hole duality suggests a deeper structural role for such dualities in quantum gravity, possibly linked to double-cone wormhole contributions.

Abstract: We propose that the large $N$ transseries of a holographic observable above the black hole threshold admits a precision bound, a limit on how precisely the transseries can determine the observable at finite $N$, set by the data of its D-brane instanton sectors. In doing so, we develop methods in large $N$ resurgence, based on the work of Écalle, that capture the $e^{-N^2}$ asymptotics of gravitational saddles beyond the $e^{-N}$ instantons. Under an assumption on the growth of the D-brane instanton sums that we expect in the black hole phase, we find an analytic obstruction to the resummation of the large $N$ transseries. This obstruction sets the precision bound. As our main example and consistency check, we study the $\frac{1}{16}$-BPS index of $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills and its black hole. We derive relations between the D-brane instanton data on thermal AdS and on the black hole, in the form of a duality that exchanges the numbers of D-brane instantons on the two backgrounds. In the index, these relations reproduce the actions of D3-branes wrapped on the black hole. We conclude with our expectations for generic situations and their relation to the double-cone wormhole.

</details>


### [197] [The $(5,5)$ String](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17275)
*Justin Kaidi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies an N=(2,1) heterotic string with a four-dimensional target space and massless spectrum; it proposes a purely cubic self-dual gauge theory where all string states—including gravity—are gauge fields of a Lie superalgebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent N=(2,1) heterotic string without requiring null currents, preserving four-dimensional target space and yielding only massless states—thereby enabling a simplified, fully geometric field-theoretic description of the full closed string spectrum.

Method: Constructs the string via right-movers on R^{2,2} and left-movers on the II_{3,3} lattice; derives the bosonic action via Kutasov-Martinec determinant duality to a self-dual metric + self-dual Maxwell system; extends to supersymmetry using a single superfield; identifies charged state couplings with structure constants of a physical-state Lie superalgebra.

Result: All physical states form an infinite, massless tower with sixteen supercharges; neutral sector yields a self-dual gravitational–gauge action; charged sector couplings match those of a Lie superalgebra whose generators are the left-moving physical states; suggests a unified cubic field theory where gravity emerges from the gauge algebra.

Conclusion: The classical target-space theory is conjectured to be a purely cubic, self-dual gauge theory based on a Lie superalgebra of physical string states—unifying gravity and gauge fields intrinsically within the gauge structure.

Abstract: We study an $N=(2,1)$ heterotic string with right-movers on $\mathbb R^{2,2}$ and left-movers of signature $(5,5)$, obtained by adjoining the lattice $\mathrm{II}_{3,3}$. Unlike the standard $N=(2,1)$ strings it requires no null current, and its target space remains four-dimensional. All of its states, an infinite tower with sixteen supercharges, are massless. For the neutral bosons, the Kutasov-Martinec determinant action is dual to a cubic action for a self-dual metric coupled to self-dual Maxwell fields, and we complete it with fermions using a single superfield. For the charged states we give evidence that the couplings are the structure constants of a Lie superalgebra formed by the physical states of the left-movers. We are thus led to propose that the classical target theory is a self-dual gauge theory for this superalgebra: every state of the string is a gauge field, and gravity is part of the gauge algebra. This would be a purely cubic field theory of all the modes of a closed string.

</details>


### [198] [Bound States of Two D0-Branes and the Three-Graviton Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17325)
*Sergio Sanjurjo,Themistocles Zikopoulos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Numerical solution of the SU(2) BFSS matrix model ground state reproduces the three-graviton coupling with high accuracy, confirms its supersymmetry protection, derives it from non-renormalized one-loop D0-brane interaction, and gives a representation-theoretic argument for excited bound states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the ground-state wavefunction of the SU(2) BFSS matrix model—identified via the BFSS conjecture with the eleven-dimensional graviton carrying two units of momentum—and extract physical observables such as the three-graviton coupling, testing consistency with M-theory and supersymmetric non-renormalization.

Method: Numerical solution of the ground state of the SU(2) BFSS matrix quantum mechanics; asymptotic analysis of the wavefunction’s power-law tail; supersymmetric protection analysis via representation theory and perturbative one-loop calculation in the D0-brane effective action.

Result: High-accuracy numerical reproduction of the Herderschee–Maldacena three-graviton coupling from the asymptotic wavefunction tail; proof that this coupling is protected from quantum corrections by supersymmetry; derivation of its exact value from the non-renormalized one-loop D0-brane interaction; a representation-theoretic characterization of recently proposed excited bound states.

Conclusion: The BFSS matrix model correctly encodes key M-theoretic physics—including graviton scattering amplitudes—via its non-perturbative ground state, and supersymmetry ensures robustness of low-energy effective couplings against quantum corrections.

Abstract: We numerically solve for the ground state of the $SU(2)$ BFSS matrix model for M-theory. This state corresponds to the zero-energy bound state of two D0-branes or, by the BFSS conjecture, to the eleven-dimensional graviton with two units of momentum along the M-theory circle. The wavefunction exhibits a strongly coupled core, as well as an asymptotic power-law tail where the D0-branes are well separated. The coefficient of this power-law decay is the three-graviton coupling, previously computed by Herderschee and Maldacena, which our numerical solution reproduces with high accuracy. We also show that this coupling is protected by supersymmetry in the matrix model, and derive its value from the non-renormalized one-loop interaction of the two D0-branes. Lastly, we provide a simple representation-theoretic argument regarding the recently proposed excited bound states of the model.

</details>


### [199] [Decoupling Dynamics: A Tale of Two Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17327)
*Andrea Antinucci,Oscar Lewis*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a holographic framework linking Symmetry Topological Field Theories (SymTFTs) of a d-dimensional QFT before and after dynamical gauging of a continuous Abelian symmetry, showing they arise as distinct topological limits of a single (d+1)-dimensional dynamical holographic gauge theory, with boundary conditions selecting the limit; it provides a quantum Hilbert-space-level definition via bulk limits and photon truncation, and analyzes both anomalous and non-anomalous cases—including full quantum treatment in (2+1)D bulk and recovery of non-invertible chiral symmetry SymTFT in 4D with mixed axial-vector anomaly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the puzzle that dynamical gauging of continuous symmetries changes the SymTFT — unlike finite symmetries, which leave it invariant — by embedding the process into a unified holographic framework where SymTFTs emerge as different topological limits.

Method: Constructs a (d+1)-dimensional dynamical holographic gauge theory whose boundary is the d-dimensional QFT; identifies SymTFTs as distinct topological limits selected by boundary conditions corresponding to ungaged vs. dynamically gaged phases; defines SymTFTs quantum-mechanically via infrared limits of bulk Hilbert spaces and operator algebras, combined with photon (massless gauge field) truncation to isolate topological data.

Result: Demonstrates that pre- and post-gauging SymTFTs are not independent but descend from the same bulk theory under different boundary-condition–driven topological limits; provides explicit quantum definitions of symmetry defects, charges, and anomalies in terms of truncated bulk Hilbert spaces and operators; fully solves the (2+1)D bulk case quantum mechanically; recovers the non-invertible chiral symmetry SymTFT for a 4D boundary theory with mixed axial-vector anomaly after vector symmetry gauging.

Conclusion: Dynamical gauging of continuous Abelian symmetries does not break the holographic SymTFT paradigm — rather, it reconfigures the boundary condition of a universal (d+1)D dynamical holographic theory, selecting a new topological limit; this reconciles continuity of the SymTFT framework with symmetry breaking and enables precise quantum characterization of generalized symmetries and anomalies across gauging transitions.

Abstract: The symmetries of a $d$-dimensional QFT are known to be captured by a topological theory in one dimension higher, the Symmetry Topological Field Theory (SymTFT), which is invariant under gauging finite symmetries of the QFT, but not under dynamical gauging of continuous symmetries. We show that the distinct SymTFTs of a $d$-dimensional QFT before and after dynamical gauging of a continuous Abelian symmetry arise as different topological limits of the same dynamical holographic gauge theory in $(d+1)$-dimensions. Gauging changes the boundary condition of this dynamical theory and thereby selects a different topological limit, explaining why the associated SymTFT changes. We give a fully quantum definition of these SymTFTs at the level of Hilbert spaces and operators by combining suitable limits of the bulk holographic theory with photon truncation. This construction clarifies how topological symmetry defects, charges, and anomalies survive while the dynamical information carried by currents is discarded. We study non-anomalous and anomalous examples, providing a complete quantum analysis for $(2+1)$-dimensional bulks. For a four-dimensional boundary theory with a mixed axial-vector anomaly, we recover the SymTFT of the non-invertible chiral symmetry that remains after gauging the vector symmetry.

</details>
